1.7.5
. The
\Gamma_{v}
-action on
\pi_{1}(G)
factors through some finite quotient
\Gamma_{v}^{\prime}
of
\Gamma_{v}
, and we have an exact sequence
\coh_{1}(\Gamma_{v}^{\prime},\pi_{1}(G))\to K_{\Gamma_{v}}\to\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{v}}
.
Since the image of
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})
in
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{v}}
is the torsion element
\beta_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})
, and since
\coh_{1}(\Gamma_{v}^{\prime},\pi_{1}(G))
is torsion, the image of
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})
in
K_{\Gamma_{v}}
is torsion. We denote this image by
\bar{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})\in K_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}.
Similarly we define
\bar{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})\in K_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}.
Let

\Delta:=(\bar{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})-\bar{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}))_{v\neq p,\infty}\in\bigoplus_{v\neq p,\infty}K_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}.

Then
\Delta
is sent to
a-a^{\prime}
by the horizontal map in (
1.7
). By the above discussion, the image of
a-a^{\prime}
under (
1.7.8.1
) is equal to the image of
\Delta
under the composite map

\bigoplus_{v\neq p,\infty}K_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}\to K_{\Gamma,\tors}\to\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}/\mathbb{Q}),

where the last map is the quotient map as in Proposition
1.7.3
. On the other hand, by the construction of the Kottwitz invariant, the image of
\Delta
in
\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}/\mathbb{Q})
is
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})-\alpha({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
. Hence the image of
a-a^{\prime}
under (
1.7.8.1
) is
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})-\alpha({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
.
∎

1.7.9
.

Let
u:{\mathfrak{c}}{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
be an isomorphism between Kottwitz parameters. Then the isomorphism
\Int(u):I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is an inner twisting, and in particular it induces an isomorphism of abelian groups

(1.7.9.1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}(I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}),G;\mathbb{A}/\mathbb{Q})\mathfrak{E}(I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}),G;\mathbb{A}/\mathbb{Q})

The following result justifies that the Kottwitz invariant is indeed an “invariant”.

Proposition 1.7.10
.

The isomorphism (
1.7.9.1
) takes
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
to
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

We write
I_{0}
and
I_{0}^{\prime}
for
I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}})
and
I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
. Let
K=\ker(\pi_{1}(I_{0})\to\pi_{1}(G))
and
K^{\prime}=\ker(\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\prime})\to\pi_{1}(G))
. The inner twisting
\Int(u):I_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\prime}
induces a
\Gamma
-equivariant isomorphism
\pi_{1}(I_{0})\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\prime})
, which we denote by
f
. Then
f
restricts to an isomorphism
KK^{\prime}
. Moreover, if we identify the two sides of (
1.7.9.1
) with quotients of
K_{\Gamma,\tors}
and
K^{\prime}_{\Gamma,\tors}
respectively as in Proposition
1.7.3
, then (
1.7.9.1
) is induced by
f:KK^{\prime}
.

Let
\omega\in\mathfrak{D}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{Q})
be the class of the cocycle
(u^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}u)_{\rho\in\Gamma}
. For each place
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
, we denote by
\beta_{v}(\omega)
the image of
\omega
under the composite map

\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{0})\xrightarrow{\ab^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}}\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{0}).

Using the isomorphism
\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{0})\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}(\pi_{1}(I_{0}))\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}
in Proposition
1.1.9
, we also view
\beta_{v}(\omega)
as an element of
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}
. We claim that for each place
v
, the image of
\beta_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})+\beta_{v}(\omega)
under the isomorphism
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{v}}\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\prime})_{\Gamma_{v}}
induced by
f
equals
\beta_{v}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
.

Our claim for
v\notin\left\{p,\infty\right\}
follows from the following commutative diagram, which is a special case of
[
Bor98
, Lem. 3.15.1]

\textstyle{\mathfrak{D}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{v})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\ab^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{D}(I_{0}^{\prime},G;\mathbb{Q}_{v})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\ab^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{v})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{v})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{E}(I_{0}^{\prime},G;\mathbb{Q}_{v})}

Here
u_{*}
is the component at
v
of the bijection (
1.6.13.1
),
c
is the translation map
x\mapsto x-\beta_{v}(\omega)
, and
d
is the group isomorphism induced by the inner twisting
\Int(u):I_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I^{\prime}_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

Similarly, the bijection
u_{*}:\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\prime})
as in (
1.6.13.2
) fits in the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{I_{0}}}
\textstyle{\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{I_{0}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\prime})_{\Gamma_{p}}}

where
c
is the translation map
x\mapsto x-\beta_{p}(\omega)
. To see this, we use the fact that if we choose
u_{0}
as in §
1.6.13
, then the image of
u_{0}^{-1}{\vphantom{\sigma}}{}^{\sigma}u_{0}\in I_{0}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
in
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}
under
\kappa_{I_{0}}
equals
\beta_{p}(\omega)
; see
[
Kot85
, Rmk. 5.7]
and
[
RV14
, Rmk. 2.2 (iv)]
. Our claim for
v=p
follows from the above commutative diagram.

Now we prove the claim for
v=\infty
. As in §
1.7.5
, we choose
(T,h)
to define
\beta_{\infty}({\mathfrak{c}})
and choose
(T^{\prime},h^{\prime})
to define
\beta_{\infty}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
. Without loss of generality we may assume that
T^{\prime}=\Int(k)(T)
and
h=\Int(k)\circ h
for some
k\in G(\mathbb{R})
. Since elliptic maximal tori transfer between inner forms, there exists
j\in I_{0}(\mathbb{C})
such that the map
\Int(uj):T_{\mathbb{C}}\to I^{\prime}_{0,\mathbb{C}}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
and has image
T^{\prime}
. We have a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.33855pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.33855pt\raise 30.2971pt\raise 7.29166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.54166pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Int(uj)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.91493pt\raise-4.34427pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.33855pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.33855pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{*}(T^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.91493pt\raise-36.36209pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.69789pt\raise-25.90672pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.26445pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 79.26445pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\prime})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where the vertical maps are the natural quotient maps. Let
n:=(uj)^{-1}k\in G(\mathbb{C})
. Then
n
is in the normalizer of
T
in
G
, and
\Int(uj)^{-1}(\mu_{h^{\prime}})=\Int(n)(\mu_{h})
. Let
\Delta_{\infty}
be the image of
\Int(n)(\mu_{h})-\mu_{h}
under
X_{*}(T)\to\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{\infty}}
. Then by the above discussion we know that the image of
\beta_{\infty}({\mathfrak{c}})+\Delta_{\infty}
under
f:\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{\infty}}\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\prime})_{\Gamma_{\infty}}
equals
\beta_{\infty}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
. On the other hand, by
[
Kot90
, Lem. 5.1]
, we have
\Delta_{\infty}=\beta_{\infty}(\omega)
. Our claim for
v=\infty
follows.

We have proved the claim. Now for each place
v
, since
\beta_{v}(\omega)
maps to zero in
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{v}}
, there exists
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}(\omega)\in K
lifting
\beta_{v}(\omega)
. By the claim, we may choose the lifts
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})
and
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
as in §
1.7.5
, in such a way that
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})+\widetilde{\beta}_{v}(\omega)
maps to
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}({\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
under
f:\pi_{1}(I_{0})\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\prime})
. To complete the proof, it remains to show that the element

\Omega:=\sum_{v}\widetilde{\beta}_{v}(\omega)\in K

is sent to zero under

K\to K_{\Gamma}=K_{\Gamma,\tors}\to\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}/\mathbb{Q}).

In fact, we show that there is a way to choose
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}(\omega)
such that the image of
\Omega
in
K_{\Gamma}
is already zero. Pick an element
\Omega^{\prime}\in\coh^{0}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0}\to G)
whose image along the surjection
\coh^{0}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0}\to G)\to\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathfrak{D}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{Q})
is
\omega
. Write
(\Omega^{\prime}_{v})_{v}
for the image of
\Omega^{\prime}
under the composite map (see Proposition
1.1.9
)

(1.7.10.1)

\displaystyle\coh^{0}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0}\to G)\to\coh^{0}_{\ab}(\mathbb{A},I_{0}\to G)\cong\bigoplus_{v}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}(K)\subset\bigoplus_{v}K_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}.

Then for each
v
,
\Omega^{\prime}_{v}\in K_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}
is a lift of
\beta_{v}(\omega)\in\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{v}}
. Thus we may and shall choose the lift
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}(\omega)\in K
of
\beta_{v}(\omega)
such that
\widetilde{\beta}_{v}(\omega)
is a lift of
\Omega_{v}^{\prime}
. In this case, to show that
\Omega
is sent to zero in
K_{\Gamma}
, it suffices to note that the composition of (
1.7.10.1
) with the natural map

\bigoplus_{v}K_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}\to K_{\Gamma,\tors}
is zero. Indeed, by Proposition
1.1.9
, this composition is identified with the composition

\coh^{0}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0}\to G)\to\coh^{0}_{\ab}(\mathbb{A},I_{0}\to G)\to\coh^{0}_{\ab}(\mathbb{A}/\mathbb{Q},I_{0}\to G),

which is zero as desired.
∎

1.7.11
.

Let
{\mathfrak{c}}=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])\in{\mathfrak{KP}}
, and write
I_{0}
for
I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}})
. Assume that
[b]\in\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
is basic. This is the case, for example, when
{\mathfrak{c}}
is
p^{n}
-admissible, by Corollary
1.6.12
. Recall that the notion of inner forms and isomorphisms between inner forms are given in Definition
1.2.2
. The image of
a
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},I_{0}^{\ad})
determines an inner form
I_{v}
of
I_{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
up to isomorphism for each place
v\neq p,\infty
. The basic element
[b]\in\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
determines an inner form
I_{p}
of
I_{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
up to isomorphism, namely
I_{p}:=J_{b}^{I_{0}}
for a decent representative
b
of
[b]
(see §
1.4.3
). Finally, let
(T,h)
be as in §
1.7.5
. Then
\Int(h(i))
induces a Cartan involution on
(I_{0}/Z_{G})_{\mathbb{R}}
, from which we obtain an inner form
I_{\infty}
of
I_{0}
over
\mathbb{R}
that is anisotropic modulo
Z_{G,\mathbb{R}}
. The isomorphism class of this inner form
I_{\infty}
depends only on
{\mathfrak{c}}
.

Proposition 1.7.12
.

In the situation of §
1.7.11
, assume that the Kottwitz invariant
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
is zero. Then there exists an inner form
I=I({\mathfrak{c}})
of
I_{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}
, unique up to isomorphism between inner forms, such that its localization over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
is isomorphic to
I_{v}
as inner forms of
I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
for each place
v
.

Proof.

The uniqueness follows from the Hasse principle for
I_{0}^{\ad}
. To prove the existence, for each place
v
we denote by
{\eta}_{v}
the cohomology class in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{0}^{\ad})
corresponding to the inner form
I_{v}
(cf. Remark
1.2.3
). By
[
Kot86
, Prop. 2.6]
(cf.
[
Bor98
, Thm. 5.16]
) we have an exact sequence of pointed sets

\displaystyle\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0}^{\ad})\to\bigoplus_{v}\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{0}^{\ad})\xrightarrow{m}\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma,\tors}.

Here
m
is defined as follows. For each place
v
, let
m_{v}
be the composite

\displaystyle\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{0}^{\ad})\xrightarrow{\ab^{1}}\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{0}^{\ad})\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}(\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad}))\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma_{v},\tors},

(note that these maps are all isomorphisms for
v
finite)
and let
i_{v}
be the natural map

\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}\to\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma,\tors}.
Then
m:=\sum_{v}i_{v}\circ m_{v}
.

We only need to prove that

\sum_{v}i_{v}\circ m_{v}(\eta_{v})=0.

For each
v
, we claim that
m_{v}(\eta_{v})
equals the image of
\beta_{v}({\mathfrak{c}})
under
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{v}}\to\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma_{v}}=\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}
. Indeed, this statement is non-trivial only for
v\in\left\{p,\infty\right\}
. For
v=p
, there is a canonical bijection between
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{0}^{\ad})
and the set of basic elements of
\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\ad})
. If we identify
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{0}^{\ad})
with
\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma_{p}}=\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}
, then this bijection is a section of the Kottwitz map
\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\ad})\to\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma_{p}}
. Moreover, this bijection sends
\eta_{p}
to the image of
[b]
in
\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\ad})
. For more details see the end of
[
RV14
, §2.1]
. The claim for
v=p
follows. For
v=\infty
, let
(T,h)
be as in §
1.7.5
, and let
\bar{T}:=T/Z_{G,\mathbb{R}}
. Let
\bar{h}
(resp.
\bar{\mu}_{h}
) be the composition of
h:\mathbb S\to T
(resp.
\mu_{h}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to T_{\mathbb{C}}
) with
T\to\bar{T}
. Since
\bar{T}
is anisotropic, we have
\tatecoh^{-1}(\Gamma_{\infty},X_{*}(\bar{T}))=X_{*}(\bar{T})_{\Gamma_{\infty}}
, and the Tate–Nakayama isomorphism
\tatecoh^{-1}(\Gamma_{\infty},X_{*}(\bar{T}))\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},\bar{T})
is induced by the map

\displaystyle X_{*}(\bar{T})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Z^{1}(\mathbb{R},\bar{T})

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\longmapsto(1\mapsto 1,\tau\mapsto\lambda(-1)),

where
\tau
denotes the complex conjugation. By definition,
\eta_{\infty}
is represented by the cocycle
(1\mapsto 1,\tau\mapsto\bar{h}(i))
, whereas
\beta_{\infty}({\mathfrak{c}})
is represented by
\mu_{h}\in X_{*}(T)
. Thus to verify our claim it suffices to check that
\bar{h}(i)=\bar{\mu}_{h}(-1)
. This follows from the equality
h(i)=\mu_{h}(-1)w_{h}(i)
, where
w_{h}
is the weight cocharacter of
h
and factors through
Z_{G,\mathbb{R}}
.

Write
K
for
\ker(\pi_{1}(I_{0})\to\pi_{1}(G))
.
By the above claim,
\sum_{v}i_{v}\circ m_{v}(\eta_{v})
is equal to the image of
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
under the composite map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}/\mathbb{Q})\cong

\displaystyle\frac{K_{\Gamma,\tors}}{\bigoplus_{v}\ker(K_{\Gamma_{v},\tors}\to\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{v}})}\to\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma,\tors}\to\pi_{1}(I_{0}^{\ad})_{\Gamma,\tors},

where the first isomorphism is as in Proposition
1.7.3
, and the second map is induced by the inclusion
K\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(I_{0})
. Since
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})=0
, we have
\sum_{v}i_{v}\circ m_{v}(\eta_{v})=0
, as desired.
∎

1.8.
Stating the point counting formula

1.8.1
.

Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum, and let
p
be a prime number. We assume that
G
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and fix a reductive model
{\mathcal{G}}
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. In the sequel we shall call such a quadruple
(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
an
unramified Shimura datum
. Let
E
be the reflex field of
(G,X)
, and let
{\mathfrak{p}}
and
q=p^{r}
be as in §
1.6.1
. In the current case
E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, so we identify
E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
with
\mathbb{Q}_{p^{r}}
. We write
K_{p}
for the hyperspecial subgroup
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

We fix notations for Hecke algebras. For each compact open subgroup
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
,
let

{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}){\sslash}K^{p})
be the Hecke algebra of
\mathbb{C}
-valued smooth compactly
supported
K^{p}
-bi-invariant distributions on
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Let
{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}){\sslash}K^{p})_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the
\mathbb{Q}
-subalgebra of
{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}){\sslash}K^{p})
consisting of distributions that are rational on characteristic functions of compact open subgroups of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Elements of
{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}){\sslash}K^{p})
can be represented as
f^{p}dg^{p}
, where
f^{p}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-valued smooth compactly supported
K^{p}
-bi-invariant function on
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and
dg^{p}
is a Haar measure on
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
assigning rational volumes to compact open subgroups. Elements of
{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}){\sslash}K^{p})_{\mathbb{Q}}
can similarly be represented as
f^{p}dg^{p}

Fix an irreducible algebraic representation
\xi
of
G
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
that factors through
G^{c}
. Fix a prime number
\ell\neq p
, and view
\xi
as a representation over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
. As explained in §
1.5.8
, we have the
{\rm Gal}(\overline{E}/E)\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-module

\coh^{i}_{c}(\Sh_{\overline{E}},\xi),

which is admissible as an
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-module. For each compact open subgroup
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, we have the induced action of
{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\sslash K^{p})_{\mathbb{Q}}
on the admissible
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-module
\coh^{i}_{c}(\Sh_{\overline{E}},\xi)^{K_{p}}
. Fix a decomposition subgroup
D_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\subset{\rm Gal}(\overline{E}/E)
at
{\mathfrak{p}}
, and fix an element
\Phi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\in D_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
that lifts the geometric
q
-Frobenius.

For
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
f^{p}dg^{p}\in\mathcal{H}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}){\sslash}K^{p})_{\mathbb{Q}}
, we define

T(\Phi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{m},f^{p}dg^{p}):=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}{\rm tr}\,\bigg({\Phi}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m}\times(f^{p}dg^{p})\mid\coh_{c}^{i}(\Sh_{\overline{E}},\xi)^{K_{p}}\bigg)\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}.

Our goal in the rest of this subsection is to state a conjectural formula for the above quantity. In what follows we keep
f^{p}dg^{p}
fixed.

1.8.2
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and let
n=mr
. Fix
\mathfrak{c}=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
, satisfying
\alpha(\mathfrak{c})=0
. As in §
1.6.16
,
{\mathfrak{c}}
gives rise to a classical Kottwitz parameter
(\gamma_{0},\gamma,\delta)\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\clsc}(p^{n})
of degree
n
, well defined up to equivalence. Let
I({\mathfrak{c}})
be the global inner form of
I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}})
as in Proposition
1.7.12
. Let

R:=\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
, and we view
\delta
as an element of
R(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
\theta
be the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-automorphism of
R
corresponding to the arithmetic
p
-Frobenius
\sigma\in{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
R_{\delta\rtimes\theta}
denote the fixed subgroup of
R
under the automorphism
\Int(\delta)\circ\theta
.

Note that the
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-group
G_{\gamma}^{0}
is isomorphic to
I({\mathfrak{c}})_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
, and the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-group
R_{\delta\rtimes\theta}^{0}
is isomorphic to
I({\mathfrak{c}})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Moreover, these isomorphisms are canonical up to inner automorphisms defined over
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
respectively. Choose Haar measures
di^{p}
on
I(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
di_{p}
on
I(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. They can be transported to
G_{\gamma}^{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
R_{\delta\rtimes\sigma}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
respectively in an unambiguous way. We denote the resulting Haar measures on
G_{\gamma}^{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
R_{\delta\rtimes\sigma}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
still by
di^{p}
and
di_{p}
.

Since
r|n
, and since
G
is quasi-split over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(as it is unramified), the Hodge cocharacters
\mu_{h}
of
h\in X
determine a
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
-conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}}
, cf.
[
Kot84a
, §1.3]
. The
negative
of this conjugacy class of cocharacters (i.e., with all members replaced by their inverses) further determines a
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
-double coset in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
via the Cartan decomposition, and we denote the characteristic function of this double coset by
\phi_{n}:G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})\to\left\{0,1\right\}
(cf.
[
Kot84a
, §2.1]
).
We define

\displaystyle O(\mathfrak{c},m,f^{p}dg^{p},di_{p}di^{p}):=O_{\gamma}(f^{p}dg^{p})TO_{\delta}(\phi_{n})\in\mathbb{C},

where
O_{\gamma}(f^{p}dg^{p})
is the orbital integral

\int\limits_{G_{\gamma}^{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}f^{p}(g^{-1}\gamma g)\frac{dg^{p}}{di^{p}},

and
TO_{\delta}(\phi_{n})
is the twisted orbital integral

\int\limits_{R_{\delta\rtimes\theta}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\backslash R(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\phi_{n}(r^{-1}\delta\theta(r))\frac{dr_{p}}{di_{p}},

with
dr_{p}
the Haar measure on
R(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
giving volume
1
to
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
.

Remark 1.8.3
.

As the notation suggests, the dependence of
O({\mathfrak{c}},m,f^{p}dg^{p},di_{p}di^{p})
on the two Haar measures
di_{p}
and
di^{p}
is only via the product measure
di_{p}di^{p}
on
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Moreover, if
di_{p}di^{p}
is rational on compact open subgroups, then
O({\mathfrak{c}},m,f^{p}dg^{p},di_{p}di^{p})
lies in
\mathbb{Q}
. To see this, we may assume that
f^{p}=1_{K^{p}aK^{p}}
for a compact open subgroup
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and some
a\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and that each of
dg^{p},di_{p},di^{p}
is rational on compact open subgroups. We then know that
O_{\gamma}(f^{p}dg^{p})
lies in
\mathbb{Q}
by adapting
[
Kot05
, (3.4.1)]
from the local setting to the adelic setting.
10
10

10

Our definition of the orbital integral equals
[G_{\gamma}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}):G_{\gamma}^{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})]
times the adelic integral analogous to
[
Kot05
, (3.4.1)]
.
Similarly, we have
TO_{\delta}(\phi_{n})\in\mathbb{Q}
by a formula similar to
[
Kot05
, (3.4.1)]
, cf. the proof of
[
ZZ20
, Lem. 4.2.3]
.

Remark 1.8.4
.

Up to normalizations of the Haar measures on
G_{\gamma}^{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
R_{\delta\rtimes\theta}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, the dependence of
O({\mathfrak{c}},m,f^{p}dg^{p},di_{p}di^{p})
on
{\mathfrak{c}}
is only via
(\gamma,\delta)
. However for later purposes it is important to normalize these Haar measures by choosing a Haar measure on
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Note that the classical Kottwitz parameter
(\gamma_{0},\gamma,\delta)
alone does not determine the global inner form
I({\mathfrak{c}})
of
G_{\gamma_{0}}^{0}
(unless
G_{\mathrm{der}}
is simply connected), so this way of normalization only makes sense with the presence of
{\mathfrak{c}}
.

Lemma 1.8.5
.

Let
I
be a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
, and let
Z
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
Z_{I}
. Assume that
I/Z
is anisotropic over
\mathbb{R}
. Then for any open subgroup
U\subset Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
I(\mathbb{Q})U
is a closed subgroup of
I(\mathbb{A}_{f}).

Proof.

We write
\bar{I}
for
I/Z
.
Since
\bar{I}
is anisotropic over
\mathbb{R}
, and since
\bar{I}(\mathbb{Q})
is discrete in
\bar{I}(\mathbb{A})
, we know that
\bar{I}(\mathbb{Q})
is a discrete (and hence closed) subgroup of
\bar{I}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Let
f
denote the map
I(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\bar{I}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Let
V:=f^{-1}(\bar{I}(\mathbb{Q}))
. Then
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})=f^{-1}(\left\{1\right\})
is open in
V
, and
V
is closed in
I(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Since
U
is open in
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, it is open in
V
. Hence
I(\mathbb{Q})U
is an open subgroup of
V
, and therefore closed in
V
. We have seen that
V
is closed in
I(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, so
I(\mathbb{Q})U
is closed in
I(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
∎

1.8.6
.

Keep the setting of §
1.8.2
. As in §
1.5.6
, for each compact open subgroup
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
we write
Z_{K_{p}K^{p}}
for
Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K_{p}K^{p}
, and write
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}
for
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{p}K^{p}
. Since
Z:=Z_{G}
and
I:=I({\mathfrak{c}})
satisfy the assumptions in Lemma
1.8.5
, we know that
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{Q})Z_{K_{p}K^{p}}
is a closed subgroup of
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Recall from
[
Bor63
, Thm. 5.1]
that
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{A}_{f})/U
is finite for every compact open subgroup
U\subset I(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. It follows that
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{Q})Z_{K_{p}K^{p}}\backslash I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is compact Hausdorff. We equip
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
with the Haar measure
di_{p}di^{p}
, and equip
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{Q})Z_{K_{p}K^{p}}
with the Haar measure that gives volume
1
to its open subgroup
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{Q})Z_{K_{p}K^{p}}
. Then
I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{Q})Z_{K_{p}K^{p}}\backslash I({\mathfrak{c}})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
has finite volume under the quotient measure, and we denote this volume by

c_{1}({\mathfrak{c}},K^{p},di_{p}di^{p}).

We also define

c_{2}({\mathfrak{c}})=c_{2}(\gamma_{0}):=\left|\Sha_{G}(\mathbb{Q},G_{\gamma_{0}}^{0})\right|.

Note that the product

c_{1}({\mathfrak{c}},K^{p},di_{p}di^{p})O({\mathfrak{c}},m,f^{p}dg^{p},di_{p}di^{p})

is independent of
di_{p}di^{p}
. Combined with Remark
1.8.3
, this implies that the above product lies in
\mathbb{Q}
. In the sequel, we shall denote this product simply by

c_{1}({\mathfrak{c}},K^{p})O({\mathfrak{c}},m,f^{p}dg^{p})\in\mathbb{Q}.

1.8.7
.

Let
\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)
be the set of stable conjugacy classes of semi-simple,
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic elements of
G(\mathbb{Q})
. (This is well defined, since
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic maximal tori transfer between inner forms of reductive groups over
\mathbb{R}
.) We fix a compact open subgroup
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
K_{p}K^{p}
is neat and such that
f^{p}
is
K^{p}
-bi-invariant. We fix a subset
\Sigma_{K^{p}}
of
G(\mathbb{Q})
such that each
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}
-translation-orbit in
\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)
is represented by exactly one element of
\Sigma_{K^{p}}
.

For each
\gamma_{0}\in\Sigma_{K^{p}}
, we write
{\mathfrak{KP}}(\gamma_{0})
for the set of
{\mathfrak{c}}\in{\mathfrak{KP}}
whose first component is
\gamma_{0}
.

For any reductive group
H
over
\mathbb{Q}
and any
\epsilon\in H(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ss}}
, we know that
(H_{\epsilon}/H_{\epsilon}^{0})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
is isomorphic to a subgroup of the abelian group
\pi_{1}(H_{\mathrm{der}})
by
[
Ste75
, Cor. 2.16 (a)]
. It follows that
H_{\epsilon}/H_{\epsilon}^{0}
is a finite commutative algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. We define

\displaystyle\iota_{H}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle:=[H_{\epsilon}(\mathbb{Q}):H_{\epsilon}^{0}(\mathbb{Q})],

\displaystyle\bar{\iota}_{H}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle:=\left|(H_{\epsilon}/H_{\epsilon}^{0})(\mathbb{Q})\right|.

Conjecture 1.8.8
.

For all sufficiently large integers
m
(in a way depending on
f^{p}dg^{p}
), we have

(1.8.8.1)

T(\Phi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{m},f^{p}dg^{p})\\
=\sum_{\gamma_{0}\in\Sigma_{K^{p}}}\bar{\iota}_{G}(\gamma_{0})^{-1}c_{2}(\gamma_{0}){\rm tr}\,\xi(\gamma_{0})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathfrak{c}}\in{\mathfrak{KP}}(\gamma_{0})\cap{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\adm}(p^{n})\\
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})=0\end{subarray}}c_{1}({\mathfrak{c}},K^{p})O({\mathfrak{c}},m,f^{p}dg^{p}).

Remark 1.8.9
.

On the right hand side of (
1.8.8.1
), each summand indexed by
\gamma_{0}
is of the form
{\rm tr}\,\xi(\gamma_{0})
(which lies in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
) times a rational number, by the discussion at the end of §
1.8.6
. Hence the right hand side of (
1.8.8.1
) in fact lies in the smallest number field containing
\left\{{\rm tr}\,\xi(\gamma_{0})\mid\gamma_{0}\in\Sigma_{K^{p}}\right\}
.

Remark 1.8.10
.

When
G_{\mathrm{der}}
is simply connected and
Z_{G}
is cuspidal, the right hand side of (
1.8.8.1
) recovers the formula conjectured by Kottwitz in
[
Kot90
, §3]
. We have formulated the conjecture only for
m
sufficiently large, in anticipation of the fact that the local terms in the Grothendieck–Lefschetz–Verdier formula are equal to the naive local terms (i.e., Deligne’s conjecture) only for
m
sufficiently large. For applications, it is important (and usually sufficient) to know that (
1.8.8.1
) holds for all sufficiently large
m
, not just all sufficiently divisible
m
.

2.
Variants of the Langlands–Rapoport Conjecture

2.1.
The formalism of Galois gerbs

The Langlands–Rapoport Conjecture, in its original form in
[
LR87
]
, is formulated using Galois gerbs.
In this subsection we recall the basic definitions in the formalism of Galois gerbs. We mainly follow
[
Kis17
, §3.1]
, while we make some corrections (see especially Remark
2.1.2
) and provide some complementary explanations.

In the following, let
k^{\prime}/k
be a Galois extension of fields of characteristic zero.

Definition 2.1.1
.

By a
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois gerb
, we mean a pair
(G,\mathfrak{G})
, where
G
is a connected linear algebraic group over
k^{\prime}
, and
\mathfrak{G}
is an extension of topological groups

1\to(G(k^{\prime}),\text{discrete topology})\to\mathfrak{G}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\to 1

satisfying the following conditions.

(i)

For each
g\in\mathfrak{G}
, there is a
k^{\prime}
-group isomorphism
g^{\alg}:\tau^{*}G\to G
, where
\tau
is the image of
g
in
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
, such that the conjugation action of
g
on
G(k^{\prime})
is given by
G(k^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\tau^{*}G)(k^{\prime})\xrightarrow{g^{\alg}}G(k^{\prime})
.

(ii)

There exists a continuous group theoretic section of
\mathfrak{G}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
defined on an open subgroup of
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
.

We often write
\mathfrak{G}
for a
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois gerb
(G,\mathfrak{G})
, and write
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
for
G
, called the
kernel
of
\mathfrak{G}
. A
morphism
between two
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois gerbs
\mathfrak{G}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{2}
is a pair
(\phi^{\Delta},\phi)
consisting of a
k^{\prime}
-homomorphism
\phi^{\Delta}:\mathfrak{G}_{1}^{\Delta}\to\mathfrak{G}_{2}^{\Delta}
and a continuous homomorphism
\phi:\mathfrak{G}_{1}\to\mathfrak{G}_{2}
satisfying the following conditions.

(a)

\phi
commutes with the maps
\mathfrak{G}_{i}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
.

(b)

The restriction of
\phi
to
\mathfrak{G}_{1}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})
is given by
\phi^{\Delta}
.

We write
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
for the category of
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois gerbs.

Remark 2.1.2
.

The assumption in Definition
2.1.1
that
G
is connected is missing in
[
Kis17
, §3.1]
, and should be added. This assumption implies that
G(k^{\prime})
is Zariski dense in
G
(see
[
Bor91
, Cor. 18.3]
). In particular, each
g\in\mathfrak{G}
uniquely determines the isomorphism
g^{\alg}
, which is vital for various constructions. Another consequence is that for a morphism
(\phi^{\Delta},\phi):\mathfrak{G}_{1}\to\mathfrak{G}_{2}
between
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois gerbs,
\phi^{\Delta}
is uniquely determined by
\phi
. For this reason we can view
\phi
alone as a morphism
\mathfrak{G}_{1}\to\mathfrak{G}_{2}
.

Remark 2.1.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
. Then all continuous group theoretic sections of
\mathfrak{G}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
defined on open subgroups of
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
belong to the same germ. Moreover, this germ can be extended to a continuous set theoretic section of
\mathfrak{G}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
defined on the whole
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
.

Definition 2.1.4
.

Let
G
be a connected linear algebraic group over
k
. Let
\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be the split extension
G(k^{\prime})\rtimes{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
, where
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
acts naturally on
G(k^{\prime})
. Then
(G_{k^{\prime}},\mathfrak{G}_{G})\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, and it is called the
neutral
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois gerb
associated with
G
.

Definition 2.1.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
. For each
g\in\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})
, conjugation by
g
induces an automorphism
\Int(g)
of
\mathfrak{G}
. Let
\phi,\psi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}
be two morphisms in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
. We say that
\phi
and
\psi
are
conjugate
(or
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})
-conjugate
, for clarity), if there exists
g\in\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})
such that
\phi=\Int(g)\circ\psi
.

2.1.6
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}
be a morphism in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
. A
k
-algebraic group
I_{\phi}
is defined in
[
Kis17
, §3.1.1, Lem. 3.1.2]
11
11

11

The assumption in
[
Kis17
, Lem. 3.1.2]
that the target of
\phi
is a neutral gerb is not needed.
. We have a canonical identification between
I_{\phi,k^{\prime}}
and the centralizer
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}
of
\im(\phi^{\Delta})
in
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
. Under this identification,
I_{\phi}(k)
is the group of
g\in\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})
such that
\Int(g)\circ\phi=\phi
.
Moreover, if we choose a continuous set theoretic section
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\to\mathfrak{H},\tau\mapsto q_{\tau}
of
\mathfrak{H}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
, then the action of
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
on
I_{\phi}(k^{\prime})\cong\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}(k^{\prime})
with respect to the
k
-form
I_{\phi}
is induced by conjugation by
\phi(q_{\tau})
inside
\mathfrak{G}
.

In fact, the axioms for
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois-gerbs guarantee that the above description of the
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
-action on
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}(k^{\prime})
can be naturally upgraded to a
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois descent datum that gives the
k
-form
I_{\phi}
of the
k^{\prime}
-group
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}
. We refer the reader to the proof of
[
Kis17
, Lem. 3.1.2]
for more details. Here we only remark that the cocycle condition for the descent datum amounts to the fact that for all
\tau,\rho\in{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
,
\phi(q_{\tau}q_{\rho}q_{\tau\rho}^{-1})
lies in
(\im\phi^{\Delta})(k^{\prime})
, and hence lies in the center of
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}
.

If
\zeta:\mathfrak{K}\to\mathfrak{H}
and
\phi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}
are two morphisms in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, then the inclusion
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{(\phi\circ\zeta)^{\Delta}}
induces an injective
k
-homomorphism

(2.1.6.1)

\displaystyle I_{\phi}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi\circ\zeta}.

If
\phi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
is a morphism in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
with
G
a connected linear algebraic group over
k
, then
I_{\phi}
contains
Z_{G}
as a
k
-subgroup.

Remark 2.1.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, and let
\phi
be the identity
\mathfrak{G}\to\mathfrak{G}
. Then
I_{\phi}
is a canonical
k
-form of
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}=Z_{\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}}
. In particular, if
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
is a torus, then we have a canonical
k
-form of
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
.

2.1.8
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be a morphism in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, where the target is a neutral gerb associated with a reductive group
G
over
k
. We assume that
\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}
is a torus. In this situation we define
k
-groups
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
and
\widetilde{I}_{\phi}
that are closely related to
I_{\phi}
.

Let
M:=G_{k^{\prime},\phi^{\Delta}}
. Then
M
is a
k^{\prime}
-subgroup of
G_{k^{\prime}}
whose base change to an algebraic closure of
k^{\prime}
becomes a Levi subgroup. Let
M^{\dagger}:=M\cap G_{\mathrm{der},k^{\prime}}
, and let
\widetilde{M}
be the inverse image of
M^{\dagger}
in
G_{\mathrm{sc},k^{\prime}}
. Then
M
,
M^{\dagger}
, and
\widetilde{M}
are reductive groups over
k^{\prime}
, and the natural maps
\widetilde{M}\to M^{\dagger}\to M
induce isomorphisms between the respective adjoint groups.

The usual conjugation action of
G(k^{\prime})
on
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(k^{\prime})
together with the natural action of
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
on
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(k^{\prime})
gives rise to an action of
\mathfrak{G}_{G}=G(k^{\prime})\rtimes{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
on
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(k^{\prime})
, which we denote by
\Int_{G}^{G_{\mathrm{sc}}}
. Choose a continuous set theoretic section
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\to\mathfrak{H},\tau\mapsto q_{\tau}
of
\mathfrak{H}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
. We define
\widetilde{I}_{\phi}
to be the
k
-form of
\widetilde{M}
corresponding to the following
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
-action on
\widetilde{M}(k^{\prime})
: Each
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
acts by
\Int_{G}^{G_{\mathrm{sc}}}(\phi(q_{\tau}))
. More precisely, just as the definition of
I_{\phi}
via Galois descent discussed in §
2.1.6
, this Galois action can be naturally upgraded to a
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois descent datum on
\widetilde{M}
. The cocycle condition in the current context amounts to the requirement that for all
\tau,\rho\in{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
,
\Int_{G}^{G_{\mathrm{sc}}}(\phi(q_{\tau}q_{\rho}q_{\tau\rho}^{-1}))
acts trivially on
\widetilde{M}
. This is indeed true, because
\phi(q_{\tau}q_{\rho}q_{\tau\rho}^{-1})
lies in
Z_{M}
, and any element of
Z_{M}
acts trivially on
\widetilde{M}
via
\Int_{G}^{G_{\mathrm{sc}}}
. Using the same principle, one sees that the Galois descent datum does not depend on the choice of
\tau\mapsto q_{\tau}
. Thus we obtain the
k
-group
\widetilde{I}_{\phi}
canonically.

In the same way we define a
k
-form
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
of
M^{\dagger}
. The natural
k^{\prime}
-homomorphism
\widetilde{M}\to M^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow M
induce
k
-homomorphisms
\widetilde{I}_{\phi}\to I_{\phi}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}
between reductive groups. Note that the composite
k^{\prime}
-homomorphism
I_{\phi,k^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow G_{k^{\prime}}\to G^{\ab}_{k^{\prime}}
is defined over
k
, and its kernel is naturally identified with
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
.

2.1.9
.

Let
l^{\prime}/l
be another Galois extension of fields of characteristic zero, equipped with compatible embeddings
k\hookrightarrow l
and
k^{\prime}\hookrightarrow l^{\prime}
. In this situation we have the
pull-back
functor

(2.1.9.1)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{PB}}:\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}rb(l^{\prime}/l).

We explain its definition.

We first define
{\mathrm{PB}}
of an object. Let
\mathfrak{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, with kernel
G
. We have a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 1\to G(k^{\prime})\to\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}^{0}\to{\rm Gal}(l^{\prime}/l)\to 1,

where
\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}^{0}
is the fiber product
\mathfrak{G}\times_{{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)}{\rm Gal}(l^{\prime}/l)
in the category of topological groups. The above short exact sequence is in fact an extension of topological groups, which follows easily from Remark
2.1.3
.

Given any
g\in\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}^{0}
with image
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(l^{\prime}/l)
, the conjugation action of
g
on
G(k^{\prime})
is induced by a
k^{\prime}
-isomorphism
(\tau|_{k^{\prime}})^{*}GG
uniquely determined by
g
(i.e., the isomorphism
h^{\alg}
, where
h
is the image of
g
in
\mathfrak{G}
). The last
k^{\prime}
-isomorphism induces an
l^{\prime}
-isomorphism
u_{g}:\tau^{*}(G_{l^{\prime}})G_{l^{\prime}}
, and in particular an automorphism of
G(l^{\prime})
given by

G(l^{\prime})=G_{l^{\prime}}(l^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\tau^{*}G_{l^{\prime}})(l^{\prime})\xrightarrow{u_{g}}G_{l^{\prime}}(l^{\prime})=G(l^{\prime}).

In this way we obtain an action of
\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}^{0}
on
G(l^{\prime})
via group automorphisms.

Let
\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}
be the quotient group
(G(l^{\prime})\rtimes\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}^{0})/\left\{x\rtimes x^{-1}\mid x\in G(k^{\prime})\right\}
. We shall denote elements of
\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}
by
[x,(u,\tau)]
, for
x\in G(l^{\prime}),(u,\tau)\in\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}^{0}
. We have a short exact sequence

(2.1.9.2)

\displaystyle 1\to G(l^{\prime})\xrightarrow{x\mapsto[x,(1,1)]}\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}\xrightarrow{[x,(u,\tau)]\mapsto\tau}{\rm Gal}(l^{\prime}/l)\to 1.

We equip
\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}
with the quotient topology of the product topology on
G(l^{\prime})\rtimes\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{l^{\prime}/l}
. (As always,
G(l^{\prime})
has the discrete topology.) Then
\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}
is a topological group, and (
2.1.9.2
) is an extension of topological groups. One checks that
(G_{l^{\prime}},\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l})\in\mathcal{G}rb(l^{\prime}/l)
. We define
{\mathrm{PB}}(\mathfrak{G})
to be
(G_{l^{\prime}},\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l})
.

We now define
{\mathrm{PB}}
of a morphism. Given any morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{G}\to\mathfrak{H}
in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, we define
{\mathrm{PB}}(\phi)
to be
(\phi^{\Delta},\phi_{l^{\prime}/l}):{\mathrm{PB}}(\mathfrak{G})\to{\mathrm{PB}}(\mathfrak{H})
, where
\phi_{l^{\prime}/l}
is given by

\displaystyle\phi_{l^{\prime}/l}:\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathfrak{H}_{l^{\prime}/l}

\displaystyle[x,(u,\tau)]

\displaystyle\longmapsto[\phi^{\Delta}(x),(\phi(u),\tau)],\quad x\in G(l^{\prime}),(u,\tau)\in\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{l^{\prime}/l}.

This concludes the definition of the functor
{\mathrm{PB}}
.

Note that for
\mathfrak{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, there is a canonical group homomorphism

\displaystyle\varsigma_{\can}^{\mathfrak{G}}:{\rm Gal}(l^{\prime}/lk^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l},\quad\tau\longmapsto[1,(1,\tau)],

which is a section of
\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}\to{\rm Gal}(l^{\prime}/l).

Lemma 2.1.10
.

Keep the setting of §
2.1.9
, and assume in addition that
l=k
. Let
\mathfrak{G},\mathfrak{H}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
.
A morphism
\psi:{\mathrm{PB}}(\mathfrak{G})\to{\mathrm{PB}}(\mathfrak{H})
is of the form
{\mathrm{PB}}(\phi)
for some morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{G}\to\mathfrak{H}
if and only if
\psi\circ\varsigma_{\can}^{\mathfrak{G}}=\varsigma_{\can}^{\mathfrak{H}}
. Moreover, when this is the case,
\phi
is unique.

Proof.

The “only if” part is trivial. We show the “if” part.

From the hypothesis on
\psi
, it follows that
\psi^{\Delta}
is the base change to
l^{\prime}
of a
k^{\prime}
-homomorphism
\phi^{\Delta}:\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}\to\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}
, and that
\psi[1,(u,\tau)]
is of the form

[1,(\phi(u),\tau)]
for some function
\phi:\mathfrak{G}\to\mathfrak{H}
. We now check that
(\phi^{\Delta},\phi)
is a morphism in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
. In fact, only the continuity of
\phi
is non-obvious. For this, we observe that the map
\mathfrak{H}^{0}_{l^{\prime}/l}\to\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}(l^{\prime})\rtimes\mathfrak{H}^{0}_{l^{\prime}/l},(u,\tau)\mapsto(1,u,\tau)
is continuous and open, since
\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}(l^{\prime})
is discrete. Hence the induced injective map
\mathfrak{H}^{0}_{l^{\prime}/l}\to\mathfrak{H}_{l^{\prime}/l}
is also continuous and open, i.e., a homeomorphism onto its image. It follows that the map
\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{l^{\prime}/l}\to\mathfrak{H}^{0}_{l^{\prime}/l},(u,\tau)\to(\phi(u),\tau)
is continuous. The continuity of
\phi
then follows from the openness of the map
{\rm Gal}(l^{\prime}/l)\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
.

Given that
(\phi^{\Delta},\phi)
is a morphism, it is clear that
\psi={\mathrm{PB}}(\phi)
.

Finally, we show that if
\psi={\mathrm{PB}}(\phi)
then
\phi
is unique. Suppose
\phi_{1}
also satisfies the condition. Then

\psi[1,(u,\tau)]=[1,(\phi(u),\tau)]=[1,(\phi_{1}(u),\tau)],

and in particular
\phi(u)=\phi_{1}(u)
, for all
(u,\tau)\in\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}^{0}
. Since the projection
\mathfrak{G}_{l^{\prime}/l}^{0}\to\mathfrak{G},(u,\tau)\mapsto u
is surjective, we have
\phi=\phi_{1}
.
∎

Definition 2.1.11
.

Let
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
be the category whose objects are projective systems
(\mathfrak{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
indexed by directed sets
(I,\leq)
and whose morphisms are given by

\Hom_{{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)}((\mathfrak{G}_{i})_{i\in I},(\mathfrak{H}_{j})_{j\in J}):=\varprojlim_{j\in J}\varinjlim_{i\in I}\Hom_{\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)}(\mathfrak{G}_{i},\mathfrak{H}_{j}).

Objects of
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
are called
pro-
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois gerbs
. We view
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
naturally as a full subcategory of
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
. When we are in the situation of §
2.1.9
, the pull-back functor (
2.1.9.1
) naturally extends to a functor
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)\to{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(l^{\prime}/l)
, which we still call
pull-back
.

2.1.12
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}=(\mathfrak{G}_{i})_{i\in I}\in{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
. Then we can take the projective limit
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}:=\varprojlim_{i}\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\Delta}
in the category of affine
k^{\prime}
-group schemes, and take the projective limit
\mathfrak{G}^{\topo}:=\varprojlim_{i}\mathfrak{G}_{i}
in the category of topological groups.
If
\phi:\mathfrak{G}\to\mathfrak{H}
is a morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
, then
\phi
naturally induces a homomorphism of affine
k^{\prime}
-group schemes
\phi^{\Delta}:\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}\to\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}
, and a continuous homomorphism
\phi^{\topo}:\mathfrak{G}^{\topo}\to\mathfrak{H}^{\topo}
. In the sequel, if
d
is an element of
\mathfrak{G}^{\topo}
, we shall simply write
d\in\mathfrak{G}
. Also, we shall simply write
\phi(d)\in\mathfrak{H}
for
\phi^{\topo}(d)\in\mathfrak{H}^{\topo}
.

The following definition generalizes Definition
2.1.5
.

Definition 2.1.13
.

Let
\mathfrak{H}=(\mathfrak{H}_{i})_{i\in I}\in{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k),\mathfrak{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, and let
\phi,\psi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}
be two morphisms in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
. We say that
\phi
and
\psi
are
conjugate
(or
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})
-conjugate
) if there exists
g\in\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})
such that
\psi=\Int(g)\circ\phi
as morphisms in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
.

2.1.14
.

Let
\mathfrak{H}=(\mathfrak{H}_{i})_{i\in I}\in{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k),\mathfrak{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, and let
\phi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}
be a morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
. We now define
I_{\phi}
, generalizing the definition in §
2.1.6
.

Choose
i_{0}\in I
such that
\phi
is induced by a morphism
\phi_{i_{0}}:\mathfrak{H}_{i_{0}}\to\mathfrak{G}
. For each
i\in I
with
i\geq i_{0}
, let
\phi_{i}
be the composition
\mathfrak{H}_{i}\to\mathfrak{H}_{i_{0}}\xrightarrow{\phi_{i_{0}}}\mathfrak{G}
. For
j\geq i\geq i_{0}
, there is a natural
k
-homomorphism
I_{\phi_{i}}\to I_{\phi_{j}}
as in (
2.1.6.1
), whose base change to
k^{\prime}
is identified with the inclusion map
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi_{i}^{\Delta}}\to\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi_{j}^{\Delta}}
. For sufficiently large
i
, the decreasing subgroups
\im(\phi_{i}^{\Delta})
of
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
stabilize, since
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
is noetherian. Hence
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi_{i}^{\Delta}}
and
I_{\phi_{i}}
also stabilize. We can thus define

\displaystyle\im(\phi^{\Delta})

\displaystyle:=\im(\phi_{i}^{\Delta}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}

\displaystyle:=\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}_{i}},

\displaystyle I_{\phi}

\displaystyle:=I_{\phi_{i}},

for
i\in I
sufficiently large. Clearly these definitions are independent of the initial choices of
i_{0}
and
\phi_{i_{0}}
.

By construction,
I_{\phi,k^{\prime}}
is canonically identified with
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}_{\phi^{\Delta}}
. It is also easy to see that
I_{\phi}(k)
precisely consists of those
g\in\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})
such that
\Int(g)\circ\phi=\phi
(as morphisms in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)
).

If
\mathfrak{G}=\mathfrak{G}_{G}
for some reductive group
G
over
k
, and if
\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}_{i}
are tori for all
i\in I
, then we also extend the definitions of
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
and
\widetilde{I}_{\phi}
in §
2.1.8
to the present case, in the obvious way. In this case, each of
I_{\phi},I_{\phi}^{\dagger},\widetilde{I}_{\phi}
is a reductive group. The group
I_{\phi}
has the same absolute rank as
G
, and contains
Z_{G}
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup.

2.1.15
.

Let
\mathfrak{H},\mathfrak{G},\phi
be as at the beginning of §
2.1.14
. Given a continuous 1-cocycle
a=(a_{\rho})\in Z^{1}(k^{\prime}/k,I_{\phi}(k^{\prime}))
, there is a morphism
a\phi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}
defined as follows. Choose
i\in I
such that
\phi
is induced by some
\phi_{i}:\mathfrak{H}_{i}\to\mathfrak{G}
and such that
I_{\phi}=I_{\phi_{i}}
. Denote by
\pi
the structural map
\mathfrak{H}_{i}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
. We define

a\phi_{i}:\mathfrak{H}_{i}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{G},\quad g\longmapsto a_{\pi(g)}\phi_{i}(g).

Here, we view
a_{\pi(g)}\in I_{\phi}(k^{\prime})
as an element of
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}(k^{\prime})\subset\mathfrak{G}
via the canonical embedding
I_{\phi,k^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
. Then
a\phi_{i}
is a morphism in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
, and we define
a\phi
to be the morphism induced by
a\phi_{i}
. This definition is independent of choices.

Lemma 2.1.16
.

In the setting of §
2.1.15
, the map
a\mapsto a\phi
is a bijection from
Z^{1}(k^{\prime}/k,I_{\phi}(k^{\prime}))
to the set of morphisms
\phi^{\prime}:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}
such that
\phi^{\prime,\Delta}=\phi^{\Delta}
. Moreover, for
a,a^{\prime}\in Z^{1}(k^{\prime}/k,I_{\phi}(k^{\prime}))
, we have
a\phi
is conjugate to
a^{\prime}\phi
if and only if
a
is cohomologous to
a^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
is finitely presented over
k^{\prime}
, the natural map

\varinjlim_{i}\Hom_{k^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{H}_{i}^{\Delta},G_{k^{\prime}})\longrightarrow\Hom_{k^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta},G_{k^{\prime}})

is a bijection. Here
\Hom_{k^{\prime}}
denotes the set of homomorphisms of
k^{\prime}
-group schemes. The lemma then reduces to the case where
\mathfrak{H}
is in
\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
. In this case the proof is exactly the same as the proof of part (2) of
[
Kis17
, Lem. 3.1.2]
.
∎

Definition 2.1.17
.

For
a
and
\phi
as in §
2.1.15
, we call
a\phi
the
twist of
\phi
by
a
. Similarly, for a class
\beta\in\coh^{1}(k^{\prime}/k,I_{\phi}(k^{\prime}))
, we define the
twist of
\phi
by
\beta
, denoted by
\phi^{\beta}
, to be the conjugacy class of
a\phi
where
a
is any cocycle representing
\beta
. This is well defined by Lemma
2.1.16
.
12
12

12

Since we define
\phi^{\beta}
to be the whole conjugacy class, its members are not necessarily of the form
a\phi
for any
a\in Z^{1}(k^{\prime}/k,I_{\phi}(k^{\prime}))
.
We shall sometimes also write
\phi^{\beta}
for an unspecified member of this conjugacy class.

2.1.18
.

We explain how to obtain Galois gerbs from Reimann’s explicit cocycle construction in
[
Rei97
, App. B]
. We first sketch the idea behind the construction informally. Let
\mathfrak{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
. Suppose that there is a continuous set theoretic section
\varsigma:{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\to\mathfrak{G}
of
\mathfrak{G}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
such that
\varsigma(\rho)\varsigma(\tau)\varsigma(\rho\tau)^{-1}
lies in
Z_{\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}}(k^{\prime})
for all
\rho,\tau\in{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
. Then the isomorphisms
(\varsigma(\tau))^{\alg}:\tau^{*}\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
for
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
form a
k^{\prime}/k
-Galois descent datum. Let
G
be the corresponding
k
-form of
\mathfrak{G}^{\Delta}
. Then the isomorphism class of
\mathfrak{G}
can be recovered from
G
and the map
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\times{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\to Z_{G}(k^{\prime}),(\rho,\tau)\mapsto\varsigma(\rho)\varsigma(\tau)\varsigma(\rho\tau)^{-1}
, which is in fact a continuous
2
-cocycle.

We now give the formal construction. First we define a category
{\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)
. The objects are pairs
(G,z)
, where
G
is a connected linear algebraic group over
k
, and
z=(z_{\rho,\tau})
is a continuous
2
-cocycle
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\times{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\to Z_{G}(k^{\prime})
satisfying
z_{1,1}=1
. A morphism
(G^{\prime},z^{\prime})\to(G,z)
is a pair
(\phi^{\Delta},f)
, where
\phi^{\Delta}:G^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\to G_{k^{\prime}}
is a homomorphism of
k^{\prime}
-groups, and
f=(f_{\rho})
is a continuous
1
-cochain
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\to G(k^{\prime})
satisfying

\displaystyle z_{\rho,\tau}f_{\rho}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}f_{\tau}f_{\rho\tau}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\phi^{\Delta}(z^{\prime}_{\rho,\tau}),

\displaystyle\Int(f_{\rho})\circ\rho^{*}(\phi^{\Delta})

\displaystyle=\phi^{\Delta},

for all
\rho,\tau\in{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
. (In particular,
f_{1}=1
.) The composition of morphisms is given by

(\phi^{\Delta},f)\circ(\psi^{\Delta},h):=(\phi^{\Delta}\circ\psi^{\Delta},(\phi^{\Delta}(h_{\rho})f_{\rho})_{\rho}).

We define a fully faithful functor

(2.1.18.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}:{\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k).

Let
(G,z)\in{\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)
. To define
{\mathcal{E}}(G,z)
, we let
\mathfrak{G}:=G(k^{\prime})\times{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
, equipped with the product topology. Define a binary operation on
\mathfrak{G}
by

(g,\rho)\cdot(h,\tau):=(g\rho(h)z_{\rho,\tau},\rho\tau).

Then
(\mathfrak{G},\cdot)
is a topological group, and the maps
G(k^{\prime})\to\mathfrak{G},g\mapsto(g,1)
and
\mathfrak{G}\to{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k),(g,\rho)\mapsto\rho
make
\mathfrak{G}
a topological group extension of
{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
by
G(k^{\prime})
. Then
(G_{k^{\prime}},\mathfrak{G})\in\mathcal{G}rb(k^{\prime}/k)
. (To check condition (ii) in Definition
2.1.1
, use that
z_{\rho,\tau}=1
for all
\rho,\tau
sufficiently close to
1
.) We define
{\mathcal{E}}(G,z)
to be
(G_{k^{\prime}},\mathfrak{G})
.

For a morphism
(\phi^{\Delta},f):(G^{\prime},z^{\prime})\to(G,z)
in
{\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)
, we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}(\phi^{\Delta},f):{\mathcal{E}}(G^{\prime},z^{\prime})=G^{\prime}(k^{\prime})\times{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(G,z)=G(k^{\prime})\times{\rm Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)

\displaystyle(g^{\prime},\rho)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\phi^{\Delta}(g^{\prime})f_{\rho},\rho).

This completes the definition of the functor
{\mathcal{E}}
. We omit the proof that
{\mathcal{E}}
is fully faithful, since this fact will not be used.

Analogous to Definition
2.1.11
, we consider the category
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)
of pro-objects in
{\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)
indexed by directed sets. The functor (
2.1.18.1
) naturally extends to a functor

(2.1.18.2)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}:{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(k^{\prime}/k)

which is also fully faithful.

In
[
Rei97
, App. B]
, various
affine groupoids
are defined, which are needed for the correct formulation of the Langlands–Rapoport Conjecture. There is a functor from
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)
to the category of affine
k^{\prime}/k
-groupoids, and Reimann obtains the desired groupoids by constructing explicit objects in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{R}}(k^{\prime}/k)
(for suitable
k^{\prime}/k
). In the present paper, we shall not need affine groupoids, but we shall import Reimann’s explicit constructions and obtain pro-Galois gerbs via the functor (
2.1.18.2
). This is the same as the point of view taken in
[
Kis17
]
.

2.2.
The Dieudonné gerb and the quasi-motivic gerb

2.2.1
.

Fix a prime
p
. We recall the definition of the
Dieudonné gerb
in terms of the functor (
2.1.18.2
), cf.
[
Rei97
, pp. 109–110]
. For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, let
\kappa_{n}:{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathbb{Z}
be the unique continuous function satisfying

\kappa_{n}(\sigma^{i},\sigma^{j})=\lfloor i/n\rfloor+\lfloor j/n\rfloor-\lfloor(i+j)/n\rfloor,\quad\forall i,j\in\mathbb{Z}.

Here
\sigma
denotes the arithmetic
p
-Frobenius as usual. Let
\mathcal{D}_{n}
be the object in
{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
given by

(\mathbb{G}_{m},(p^{\kappa_{n}(\rho,\tau)})_{\rho,\tau}).
For
n,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
with
n|n^{\prime}
, let
\lambda_{n,n^{\prime}}:{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathbb{Z}
be the unique continuous function satisfying

\lambda_{n,n^{\prime}}(\sigma^{i})=\lfloor i/n^{\prime}\rfloor n^{\prime}/n-\lfloor i/n\rfloor,\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{Z}.

Let
\delta_{n^{\prime},n}:\mathcal{D}_{n^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{D}_{n}
be the morphism in
{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
given by

(x\mapsto x^{n^{\prime}/n},(p^{\lambda_{n,n^{\prime}}(\rho)})_{\rho}).

Then
(\mathcal{D}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}
equipped with the transition morphisms
\delta_{n^{\prime},n}
is an object in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{R}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Applying the functor (
2.1.18.2
) to
(\mathcal{D}_{n})_{n}
, we obtain an object
\mathfrak{D}=(\mathfrak{D}_{n})_{n}=(\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{D}_{n}))_{n}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. This is called the
Dieudonné gerb
.

We denote by
\mathbb D
the pro-torus
\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
over
\Spec\mathbb{Z}
, where for
n|n^{\prime}
the transition map from the
n^{\prime}
-th
\mathbb{G}_{m}
to the
n
-th
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is
z\mapsto z^{n^{\prime}/n}
. We have

\mathfrak{D}^{\Delta}=\mathbb D_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}.
By construction, the set underlying
\mathfrak{D}_{n}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\times{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Using this we define a canonical element

d_{\sigma,n}:=(p^{-\lfloor 1/n\rfloor},\sigma)\in\mathfrak{D}_{n},

and a canonical map

\varsigma_{n}:{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}_{n},\quad\rho\longmapsto(1,\rho),

which is a continuous section of
\mathfrak{D}_{n}\to{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Clearly
\kappa_{n}(\sigma^{i},\sigma^{j})=0
when
i,j
are divisible by
n
, so
\kappa_{n}
vanishes on
{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})\times{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
by continuity. It follows that
\varsigma_{n}
is a group homomorphism. For future use, we compute:

(2.2.1.1)

\displaystyle d_{\sigma,n}^{n}=(p^{-\lfloor 1/n\rfloor}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}p^{\kappa_{n}(\sigma^{i},\sigma)},\sigma^{n})=(p^{-1},\sigma^{n})=p^{-1}\varsigma_{n}(\sigma^{n}),\quad\forall n.

(2.2.1.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\delta_{n,n^{\prime}})(d_{\sigma,n^{\prime}})=(p^{-\lfloor 1/n^{\prime}\rfloor\lambda_{n,n^{\prime}}(\sigma)},\sigma)=(p^{-\lfloor 1/n\rfloor},\sigma)=d_{\sigma,n},\quad\forall n|n^{\prime}.

(2.2.1.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\delta_{n,n^{\prime}})(\varsigma_{n^{\prime}}(\rho))=(p^{\lambda_{n,n^{\prime}}(\rho)},\rho)=(1,\rho)=\varsigma_{n}(\rho),\quad\forall n|n^{\prime},\forall\rho\in{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n^{\prime}}}).

By (
2.2.1.2
), the system
(d_{\sigma,n})_{n}
defines an element
d_{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{D}^{\topo}
.

Definition 2.2.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}_{p}\in{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the pull back of
\mathfrak{D}
. For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, let
\mathfrak{G}_{p,n}\in{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the pull back of
\mathfrak{D}_{n}
.
13
13

13

In
[
Kis17
, §3.1.6]
, our
\mathfrak{G}_{p,n}
is denoted by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{G}}_{p}^{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}}
.
Thus
\mathfrak{G}_{p}=(\mathfrak{G}_{p,n})_{n}
.

Definition 2.2.3
.

Let
G
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
\mathfrak{G}_{G}\in\mathcal{G}rb(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\in\mathcal{G}rb(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the associated neutral gerbs. A morphism
\theta:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is called
unramified
, if it is the pull-back of a morphism
\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}}}:\mathfrak{D}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. By the obvious generalization of Lemma
2.1.10
,
\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
is uniquely determined by
\theta
. For general
\theta
, we write
\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
for the set of
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
such that
\Int(g^{-1})\circ\theta
is unramified.

Lemma 2.2.4
.

Keep the notation of Definition
2.2.3
. The following statements hold.

(i)

For any morphism
\theta:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, the set
\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
is a
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor, where
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
multiplies on the right.

(ii)

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{D}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
be a morphism. For sufficiently divisible
n
, we have
\phi(d_{\sigma})^{n}=\phi_{n}^{\Delta}(p^{-1})\rtimes\sigma^{n}\in\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
, where
\phi_{n}
is a morphism
\mathfrak{D}_{n}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
inducing
\phi
.

Proof.

By the discussion in §
2.1.9
, the fact that
\mathfrak{G}_{p}
is the pull-back of
\mathfrak{D}
gives rise to a canonical homomorphism
\varsigma:\Gamma_{p,0}={\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\to\mathfrak{G}_{p}^{\topo}
, which is a section of
\mathfrak{G}_{p}^{\topo}\to\Gamma_{p}
. By (the obvious generalization of) Lemma
2.1.10
, a morphism
\theta:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
is unramified if and only if
\theta(\varsigma(\tau))=1\rtimes\tau
for all
\tau\in\Gamma_{p,0}
.

For part (i), we write
\theta(\varsigma(\tau))=a_{\tau}\rtimes\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, for
\tau\in\Gamma_{p,0}
. Then
(a_{\tau})_{\tau}\in Z^{1}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}},G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}))
. By the previous paragraph, an element
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
lies in
\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
if and only if
g^{-1}a_{\tau}\tau(g)=1
for all
\tau\in\Gamma_{p,0}
. By Steinberg’s theorem, such a
g
exists since
G
is connected. It is then clear that
\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
is a
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor.

We now show part (ii). First pick
n
such that
\phi
is induced by a morphism
\phi_{n}:\mathfrak{D}_{n}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{\mathrm{ur}}
. Since
\phi_{n}
is continuous, there exists an open subgroup
U
of
{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
such that
\phi_{n}(\varsigma_{n}(\rho))=1\rtimes\rho
for all
\rho\in U
. We may assume that
U={\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n^{\prime}}})
for some
n^{\prime}
divisible by
n
. Using (
2.2.1.3
), we may replace
n
by
n^{\prime}
and assume that
n=n^{\prime}
. We then have

\phi(d_{\sigma})^{n}=\phi_{n}(d_{\sigma,n}^{n})=\phi_{n}(p^{-1}\varsigma_{n}(\sigma^{n}))=\phi_{n}^{\Delta}(p^{-1})\rtimes\sigma^{n},

where the second equality is by (
2.2.1.1
).
∎

Definition 2.2.5
.

Let
G
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For any unramified morphism
\theta:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, we define
b_{\theta}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
by the formula
\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}}}(d_{\sigma})=b_{\theta}\rtimes\sigma
.

Proposition 2.2.6
.

Let
G
be a reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and let
\theta:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an unramified morphism. The following statements hold.

(i)

Viewing
\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},\Delta}:\mathbb D_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\to G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}
as a fractional cocharacter of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}
, we have
\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},\Delta}=-\nu_{b_{\theta}}
. Moreover
b_{\theta}
is decent (see §
1.4.1
).

(ii)

There are natural
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-isomorphisms
J_{b_{\theta}}\cong I_{\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\cong I_{\theta}
.

(iii)

Let
\beta\in\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\theta})
. Then there is a member
\theta^{\prime}
of the conjugacy class
\theta^{\beta}
(see Definition
2.1.17
) satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

The morphism
\theta^{\prime}:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
is unramified.

(b)

By part (ii), we view
\beta
as an element of
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},J_{b_{\theta}})
. Then the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
b_{\theta^{\prime}}
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
is given by the twist of
b_{\theta}
by
\beta
, as in §
1.4.4
.

Proof.

(i)
Choose
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
is induced by a morphism
\theta_{n}:\mathfrak{D}_{n}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
. Let
\nu_{n}:=\theta_{n}^{\Delta}
. Then
\nu_{n}
is a cocharacter of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}
, and
\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},\Delta}=n^{-1}\nu_{n}
. Up to enlarging
n
, we may assume that
\nu_{n}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}
.

By Lemma
2.2.4
(iii), up to enlarging
n
we have

\displaystyle b_{\theta}\sigma(b_{\theta})\cdots\sigma^{n-1}(b_{\theta})=\nu_{n}(p^{-1})\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}).

We conclude that
\nu_{b_{\theta}}=-n^{-1}\nu_{n}=-\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},\Delta}
, and that
b_{\theta}
is
n
-decent.

(ii)
As in
[
RZ96
, Cor. 1.14]
, the fact that
b_{\theta}
is decent implies that

J_{b_{\theta}}(R)=\left\{g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}},\nu_{b_{\theta}}}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\mid gb_{\theta}=b_{\theta}\sigma(g)\right\},

for any
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra
R
. In view of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}},\nu_{b_{\theta}}}=G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}},\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},\Delta}}
, the above description of
J_{b_{\theta}}
agrees with the explicit description of
I_{\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}
as in §
2.1.6
. The natural isomorphism
I_{\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\cong I_{\theta}
arises from the fact that they are the same
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-form of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}},\theta^{{\mathrm{ur}},\Delta}}
.

(iii)
By Steinberg’s theorem,
\beta
is represented by a cocycle

a=(a_{\rho})\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p},J_{b}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})).

Viewing
a
as in
Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\theta})
, we define
\theta^{\prime}:=a\theta
, which is in the conjugacy class
\theta^{\beta}
. Then
\theta^{\prime}
is unramified, and we have
b_{\theta^{\prime}}=a_{\sigma}b
, which implies condition (b).
∎

2.2.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}
be a reductive group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, with generic fiber
G
. Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and let
\mu
be a cocharacter of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}}.

For each morphism
\theta:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we recall the definition of a set
X_{\mu}(\theta)
and a bijection
\Phi:X_{\mu}(\theta)\to X_{\mu}(\theta)
as in
[
Kis17
, §3.3.3]
. For each
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
, we write
b_{g}
for
b_{\Int(g^{-1})\circ\theta}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Define

Y_{\mu}(\theta):=\left\{g\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)\mid b_{g}\in\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})p^{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\right\}.

The group
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
acts on
Y_{\mu}(\theta)
via right multiplication, and we define

X_{\mu}(\theta):=Y_{\mu}(\theta)/\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}).

By the Cartan decomposition, the subset
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})p^{\mu}{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
depends on
\mu
only via the
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}})
-conjugacy class of
\mu
, and so the same holds for
Y_{\mu}(\theta)
and
X_{\mu}(\theta)
.

Consider the map

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}:\mathcal{UR}(\theta)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{UR}(\theta)

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\longmapsto gb_{g}\sigma(b_{g})\cdots\sigma^{r-1}(b_{g}).

For
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
we have
b_{\widetilde{\Phi}(g)}=\sigma^{r}(b_{g})
. It follows that
\widetilde{\Phi}
restricts to a map
Y_{\mu}(\theta)\to Y_{\mu}(\theta)
.
If we fix an element
g_{0}\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
and use it to identify the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
with
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, then the map
\widetilde{\Phi}
becomes the map
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),g\mapsto b_{g_{0}}\sigma(b_{g_{0}})\cdots\sigma^{r-1}(b_{g_{0}})\sigma^{r}(g)
. This shows that
\widetilde{\Phi}
restricts to a
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-equivariant bijection
Y_{\mu}(\theta)\to Y_{\mu}(\theta)
. Hence
\widetilde{\Phi}
induces a bijection

\Phi:X_{\mu}(\theta)X_{\mu}(\theta),

which we call the
p^{r}
-Frobenius
.

The isomorphism class of the
\Phi^{\mathbb{Z}}
-set
X_{\mu}(\theta)
depends on
\theta
only through the conjugacy class of
\theta
. Moreover, after fixing an element
g_{0}\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
, from the previous paragraph we know that the map
G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})\to G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}),g\mapsto g_{0}^{-1}g
induces a bijection from
X_{\mu}(\theta)
to the
affine Deligne–Lusztig set

\displaystyle X_{\mu}(b_{g_{0}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\mid g^{-1}b_{g_{0}}\sigma(g)\in\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})p^{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\right\}

(2.2.7.1)

\displaystyle\left\{g\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\mid g^{-1}b_{g_{0}}\sigma(g)\in\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})p^{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\right\},

on which
\Phi
acts by
g\mapsto b_{g_{0}}\sigma(b_{g_{0}})\cdots\sigma^{r-1}(b_{g_{0}})\sigma^{r}(g)
. The second line is the usual definition of an affine Deligne–Lusztig set found in the literature, and we have a natural map (
2.2.7.1
) induced by the inclusion
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\hookrightarrow G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. That this map is a bijection follows from Lemma
1.6.8
and the functoriality of the Cartan decomposition.

2.2.8
.

We keep fixing a prime
p
. For each finite prime
v\neq p
, let

\mathfrak{G}_{v}:=\Gamma_{v}\in\mathcal{G}rb(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}).

Let

\mathfrak{G}_{\infty}\in\mathcal{G}rb(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R})

be the Weil group of
\mathbb{R}
, and let

\mathfrak{G}_{p}\in{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})

be as in Definition
2.2.2
.

Consider the pro-torus
\Res_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}:=\varprojlim_{L}\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
over
\mathbb{Q}
, where
L
runs through the set of finite Galois extensions of
\mathbb{Q}
contained in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
, ordered by inclusion, and the transition maps are the norm maps. The neutral gerbs
\mathfrak{G}_{\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}\in\mathcal{G}rb_{\mathbb{Q}}
for different
L
form a projective system, i.e., an object in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. We denote this object by
\mathfrak{G}_{\Res_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}
.

For each object
\mathfrak{G}
(resp. morphism
\phi
) in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
, we denote its pull-back in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v})
by
\mathfrak{G}(v)
(resp.
\phi(v)
), for each place
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
. Here the pull-back functor is defined with respect to our fixed embedding
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}
. Reimann
[
Rei97
, §B.2]
has constructed a
quasi-motivic Galois gerb
, which is an object

\mathfrak{Q}\in{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}),

equipped with a morphism
\zeta_{v}:\mathfrak{G}_{v}\to\mathfrak{Q}(v)
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v})
for each place
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
, and a morphism
\psi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{\Res_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. More precisely, Reimann constructs in the proof of
[
Rei97
, Thm. B.2.8]
versions of
\mathfrak{Q}
,
\zeta_{v}
, and
\psi
in the categories
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{R}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
and
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{R}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v})
. We transport his constructions via the functors (
2.1.18.2
). The unique characterization of the tuple
(\mathfrak{Q},(\zeta_{v})_{v},\psi)
is delicate to state. We omit this and refer the reader to
loc. cit.
and
[
Kis17
, Thm. 3.1.9]
.

By construction,
\mathfrak{Q}
is given by a projective system
(\mathfrak{Q}^{L})_{L}
in
\mathcal{G}rb_{\mathbb{Q}}
, indexed by the set of finite Galois extensions
L/\mathbb{Q}
contained in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
, ordered by inclusion. For each
L
, we have
\mathfrak{Q}^{L,\Delta}=Q^{L}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
, where
Q^{L}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-torus explicitly described in
[
Rei97
, §B.2]
. (Here
Q^{L}
agrees with the canonical
\mathbb{Q}
-form of
\mathfrak{Q}^{L,\Delta}
as in Remark
2.1.7
.) If
L\subset L^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
, then the transition morphism
\mathfrak{Q}^{L^{\prime}}\to\mathfrak{Q}^{L}
is surjective, and its kernel
Q^{L^{\prime}}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to Q^{L}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. We write
Q
for the pro-torus
(\varprojlim_{L}Q^{L})
over
\mathbb{Q}
. Since the projective system
(\mathfrak{Q}^{L})_{L}
is indexed by a countable set and since the transition morphisms are surjective, we conclude that the projections
\mathfrak{Q}^{\topo}\to\mathfrak{Q}^{L}
are surjective. (See §
2.1.12
for the notation
\mathfrak{Q}^{\topo}
.)

For
v\in\left\{p,\infty\right\},
we denote the group scheme homomorphism
\zeta_{v}^{\Delta}:\mathfrak{G}_{v}^{\Delta}\to\mathfrak{Q}(v)^{\Delta}
(see §
2.1.12
) by
\nu(v)
. By construction,
\nu(v)
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
. Thus we have
\nu(p):\mathbb D_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to Q_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and
\nu(\infty):\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}\to Q_{\mathbb{R}}
.

2.2.9
.

Let
T
be a torus over
\mathbb{Q}
, and let
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
. Let
L/\mathbb{Q}
be a finite Galois extension contained in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
\mu
is defined over
L
. Then
\mu
induces a
\mathbb{Q}
-homomorphism

\mu_{*}:\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mu}\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}T\xrightarrow{\N_{L/\mathbb{Q}}}T.

We obtain a morphism
\Psi_{T,\mu}:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{T}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
via the composition

\mathfrak{Q}\xrightarrow{\psi}\mathfrak{G}_{\Res_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}\to\mathfrak{G}_{\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}\xrightarrow{\mu_{*}}\mathfrak{G}_{T}.

This is independent of the choice of
L
.

Lemma 2.2.10
.

Let
\theta=\Psi_{T,\mu}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{T}(p)
. Choose
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
, and let
[b]\in\B(T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
be the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
b_{\Int(g^{-1})\circ\theta}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
in
T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
(which is well defined, by Lemma
2.2.4
(i)). Then
\kappa_{T}([b])\in X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p}}
is equal to the image of
-\mu\in X_{*}(T)
.

Proof.

The proof reduces to the “universal case”, where
T=\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and the map
\mu_{*}:\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\to T
is the identity. In this case,
X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p}}
is torsion free, and therefore the homomorphism
\iota:X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p}}\to X_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
induced by taking averages of
\Gamma_{p}
-orbits in
X_{*}(T)
is injective. For each
[b^{\prime}]\in\B(T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, we have
\iota(\kappa_{T}([b^{\prime}]))=\nu_{b^{\prime}}
by
[
Kot85
, §2.8]
. Hence to prove the lemma it suffices to prove that
\iota(\mu)=-\nu_{b}
. By Proposition
2.2.6
(i), we have
-\nu_{b}=(\Int(g^{-1})\circ\theta)^{{\mathrm{ur}},\Delta}=\theta^{\Delta}
. By
[
Kis17
, (3.1.11)]
,
\theta^{\Delta}
is indeed equal to
\iota(\mu)
, as desired.
14
14

14

A similar argument is made in the proof of
[
Kis17
, Lem. 3.4.2]
in order to determine
\kappa_{T}([b])
for
T=\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
. There
T
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, so the cited result
[
RR96
, Thm. 4.2 (ii)]
is valid. Our present argument does not need this unramifiedness assumption.

∎

2.3.
Strictly monoidal categories

2.3.1
.

Let
G,H
be two strictly monoidal categories (which we always assume to be small). By a
strictly monoidal functor

G\to H
, we mean a functor that strictly respects the monoidal structures. By a
monoidal isomorphism
between two strictly monoidal functors
\phi,\psi:G\to H
, we mean an isomorphism of functors
{\mathcal{A}}:\phi\psi
such that for any two objects
g_{1},g_{2}\in G
the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\phi(g_{1}\otimes g_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{A}}(g_{1}\otimes g_{2})}
\textstyle{\psi(g_{1}\otimes g_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\phi(g_{1})\otimes\phi(g_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{A}}(g_{1})\otimes{\mathcal{A}}(g_{2})}
\textstyle{\psi(g_{1})\otimes\psi(g_{2})}

Every group can be naturally viewed as a strictly monoidal category, where the only morphisms are the identities. For two groups
G
and
H
, the set of strictly monoidal functors
G\to H
is the same as the set of group homomorphisms
G\to H
. More generally, each crossed module
(\widetilde{H}\to H)
also determines a strictly monoidal category denoted by
H/\widetilde{H}
; see
[
Kis17
, §3.2.1]
. When
(\widetilde{H}\to H)
is a crossed module, each element
h\in H
induces via conjugation a strictly monoidal functor
\Int(h):H/\widetilde{H}\to H/\widetilde{H}
.

2.3.2
.

Now let
G
be a group and
(\widetilde{H}\xrightarrow{\varrho}H)
be a crossed module. We denote the structural action of
H
on
\widetilde{H}
by
\Int
. Consider two strictly monoidal functors
\phi,\psi:G\to H/\widetilde{H}
. If
{\mathcal{A}}:\phi\psi
is an isomorphism of functors, then for each
q\in G
the isomorphism
{\mathcal{A}}(q):\phi(q)\psi(q)
corresponds to an element
{\mathcal{A}}(q)\in\widetilde{H}
. Thus we may view
{\mathcal{A}}
as a function
G\to\widetilde{H}
. In this way, there is a one-to-one correspondence between monoidal isomorphisms
{\mathcal{A}}:\phi\psi
and functions
{\mathcal{A}}:G\to\widetilde{H}
satisfying

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(qr)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{A}}(q)\cdot\Int(\phi(q))({\mathcal{A}}(r)),

\displaystyle\varrho({\mathcal{A}}(q))\cdot\phi(q)

\displaystyle=\psi(q),\quad\forall q,r\in G.

When
G
