is equipped with a topology, we shall call a monoidal isomorphism
{\mathcal{A}}:\phi\psi

continuous
, if the corresponding function
{\mathcal{A}}:G\to\widetilde{H}
is continuous with respect to the given topology on
G
and the discrete topology on
\widetilde{H}
.

2.3.3
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero, and let
\bar{k}
be a fixed algebraic closure. Let
G
be a reductive group over
k
, and let
\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be the associated neutral gerb in
\mathcal{G}rb(\bar{k}/k)
. As in
[
Kis17
, §3.2.2]
, we have a crossed module
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\bar{k})\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, and we denote the corresponding strictly monoidal category
\mathfrak{G}_{G}/G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\bar{k})
by
\mathfrak{G}_{G/G_{\mathrm{sc}}}
. We have a canonical strictly monoidal functor
\mathfrak{G}_{G}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G/G_{\mathrm{sc}}}
.

Lemma 2.3.4
.

Keep the setting and notation of
§
2.3.3
. Let
\mathfrak{H}=(\mathfrak{H}_{i})_{i\in I}
be an object in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\bar{k}/k)
such that the projections
\mathfrak{H}^{\topo}\to\mathfrak{H}_{i}
are surjective and such that
\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}_{i}
are tori for all
i\in I
. Let
\phi:\mathfrak{H}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be a morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\bar{k}/k)
. Let
a\in Z^{1}(k,I_{\phi})
and let
\phi^{\prime}=a\phi
(see §
2.1.15
). Let
\phi_{\widetilde{\ab}}
(resp.
\phi^{\prime}_{\widetilde{\ab}}
) be the composite strictly monoidal functor

\mathfrak{H}^{\topo}\xrightarrow{\phi^{\topo}\text{ (resp.~$\phi^{\prime,\topo}$)}}\mathfrak{G}_{G}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{G}_{G/G_{\mathrm{sc}}}.

Then there exist
g\in G(\bar{k})
and a continuous monoidal isomorphism
\phi_{\widetilde{\ab}}\Int(g)\circ\phi^{\prime}_{\widetilde{\ab}}
, if and only if the class of
a
in
\coh^{1}(k,I_{\phi})
lies in the image of
\coh^{1}(k,\widetilde{I}_{\phi})\to\coh^{1}(k,I_{\phi})
. Here
\widetilde{I}_{\phi}
is defined in §
2.1.8
and §
2.1.14
.

Proof.

By the assumption that
\mathfrak{H}^{\topo}\to\mathfrak{H}_{i}
are surjective, the lemma reduces to the case where
\mathfrak{H}\in\mathcal{G}rb(\bar{k}/k)
, which we now assume. Write
M
for
G_{\bar{k},\phi^{\Delta}}
. Let
\varrho
denote the natural map
G_{\mathrm{sc}}\to G
, and let
\widetilde{M}:=\varrho^{-1}(M)\subset G_{\mathrm{sc},\bar{k}}
. Write
\pi
for the structural map
\mathfrak{H}\to{\rm Gal}(\bar{k}/k)
. For each
q\in\mathfrak{H}
, we write
\phi(q)=g_{q}\rtimes\pi(q)
, with
g_{q}\in G(\bar{k})
.

Assume there exist
g\in G(\bar{k})
and a continuous monoidal isomorphism
{\mathcal{A}}:\phi_{\widetilde{\ab}}\Int(g)\circ\phi_{\widetilde{\ab}}^{\prime}
.
As discussed in §
2.3.2
,
{\mathcal{A}}
can be viewed as a continuous function
\mathfrak{H}\to G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\bar{k})

satisfying

(2.3.4.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(qr)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{A}}(q)\cdot\Int(\phi(q))({\mathcal{A}}(r)),

(2.3.4.2)

\displaystyle\varrho({\mathcal{A}}(q))\cdot\phi(q)

\displaystyle=\Int(g)[a_{\pi(q)}\phi(q)],\quad\forall q,r\in\mathfrak{H}.

Decompose
g
as
g=g^{\prime}z
, with
g^{\prime}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\bar{k}),z\in Z_{G}(\bar{k})
.
Fix a lift
\widetilde{g}\in G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\bar{k})
of
g^{\prime}
, and define a continuous map
{\mathcal{B}}:\mathfrak{H}\to G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\bar{k})
by

{\mathcal{B}}(q):=\widetilde{g}^{-1}{\mathcal{A}}(q)\cdot\Int(\phi(q))(\widetilde{g})\in G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\bar{k}),\quad\forall q\in\mathfrak{H}.

Then by (
2.3.4.1
), we have

(2.3.4.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}(qr)={\mathcal{B}}(q)\cdot\Int(\phi(q))({\mathcal{B}}(r)),\quad\forall q,r\in\mathfrak{H}.

By (
2.3.4.2
), we have

\varrho({\mathcal{B}}(q))=(g^{\prime})^{-1}\cdot[\varrho({\mathcal{A}}(q))\cdot\phi(q)]\cdot g^{\prime}\phi(q)^{-1}\\
=za_{\pi(q)}\phi(q)g^{-1}\cdot g^{\prime}\phi(q)^{-1}=za_{\pi(q)}g_{q}\pi(q)z^{-1}\pi(q)^{-1}g_{q}^{-1},

i.e.,

(2.3.4.4)

\displaystyle\varrho({\mathcal{B}}(q))=z{\vphantom{{\pi(q)}}}^{\pi(q)}z^{-1}a_{\pi(q)},\quad\forall q\in\mathfrak{H}.

From (
2.3.4.4
), we see that

(2.3.4.5)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}(q)\in\widetilde{M}(\bar{k}),~\forall q\in\mathfrak{H};

(2.3.4.6)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}(q)\in Z_{G_{\mathrm{sc}}}(\bar{k}),~\forall q\in\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}(\bar{k}).

Using (
2.3.4.3
) and (
2.3.4.5
), we see that the map
{\mathcal{B}}|_{\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}(\bar{k})}:\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}(\bar{k})\to Z_{G_{\mathrm{sc}}}(\bar{k})
is a group homomorphism. Since
\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}(\bar{k})
is a divisible abelian group and
Z_{G_{\mathrm{sc}}}(\bar{k})
is finite, we have
{\mathcal{B}}|_{\mathfrak{H}^{\Delta}(\bar{k})}\equiv 1
. Combining the last fact with (
2.3.4.3
), we see that
{\mathcal{B}}(q)
depends only on
\pi(q)
, i.e.,
{\mathcal{B}}=B\circ\pi
for a continuous map
B:{\rm Gal}(\bar{k}/k)\to\widetilde{M}(\bar{k})
. Now by the definition of the
k
-form
\widetilde{I}_{\phi}
of
\widetilde{M}
, the relation (
2.3.4.3
) precisely means that
B\in Z^{1}(k,\widetilde{I}_{\phi})
. Note that the inclusion of
\bar{k}
-groups
Z_{G,\bar{k}}\hookrightarrow M
induces an inclusion of
k
-groups
Z_{G}\to I_{\phi}
. Hence (
2.3.4.4
) implies that the class of
a
in
\coh^{1}(k,I_{\phi})
equals the image of the class of
B
in
\coh^{1}(k,\widetilde{I}_{\phi})
.

Conversely, assume the class of
a
in
\coh^{1}(k,I_{\phi})
lies in the image of
\coh^{1}(k,\widetilde{I}_{\phi})
. Then there exist
g\in I_{\phi}(\bar{k})
and
B\in Z^{1}(k,\widetilde{I}_{\phi})
such that

a_{\tau}=g\cdot\varrho(B(\tau))\cdot\Int(g_{\tau})({\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}g^{-1}),\quad\forall\tau\in{\rm Gal}(\bar{k}/k).

Decompose
g
as
g=\varrho(\widetilde{g})z^{-1}
, with
\widetilde{g}\in\widetilde{M}(\bar{k})
and
z\in Z_{G}(\bar{k})
. Then we can absorb
\widetilde{g}
into
B
by replacing each
B(\tau)
with
\widetilde{g}B(\tau)\Int(g_{\tau})({\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}\widetilde{g}^{-1})
. Hence we may assume that
g=z^{-1}
. Let
{\mathcal{B}}:=B\circ\pi:\mathfrak{H}\to\widetilde{M}(\bar{k})
. Then
{\mathcal{B}}
is continuous and satisfies (
2.3.4.3
) and (
2.3.4.4
). Note that the relation (
2.3.4.4
) can also be written as

(2.3.4.7)

\displaystyle\varrho({\mathcal{B}}(q))\cdot\phi(q)=\Int(z)[a_{\pi(q)}\phi(q)]

(since
{\vphantom{{\pi(q)}}}{}^{\pi(q)}z=\Int(\phi(q))(z)
). By (
2.3.4.3
) and (
2.3.4.7
),
{\mathcal{B}}
is a continuous monoidal isomorphism
\phi_{\widetilde{\ab}}\Int(z)\circ\phi^{\prime}_{\widetilde{\ab}}
.
∎

Remark 2.3.5
.

By the discussion in §
2.2.8
, the assumptions on
\mathfrak{H}
in Lemma
2.3.4
are satisfied by the quasi-motivic Galois gerb
\mathfrak{Q}\in{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
.

2.4.
Admissible morphisms for an unramified Shimura datum

2.4.1
.

Let
(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
be an unramified Shimura datum as in §
1.8.1
. Let
E
be the reflex field, and let
{\mathfrak{p}}
and
q=p^{r}
be as in §
1.6.1
. We will use the following notation throughout the paper. For each field extension
F/E
such that
G_{F}
is quasi-split, the Hodge cocharacters
\mu_{h}
attached to
h\in X
determine a
G(F)
-conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G_{F}
. We denote this conjugacy class by
\bbmu_{X}(F)
. Now inside
\bbmu_{X}(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{r}})
, there is a canonical
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}})
-conjugacy class consisting of those cocharacters in
\bbmu_{X}(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{r}})
that extend to cocharacters of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}}
. We denote this
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}})
-conjugacy class by
\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
.

A choice of
x\in X
gives rise to a morphism

\xi_{\infty}:\mathfrak{G}_{\infty}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{G}_{G}(\infty)

in
\mathcal{G}rb(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R})
, whose conjugacy class depends only on
X
. See
[
Kis17
, §3.3.5]
for the explicit construction. For a finite prime
v
unequal to
p
, let

\xi_{v}:\mathfrak{G}_{v}=\Gamma_{v}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{G}_{G}(v)=G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})\rtimes\Gamma_{v}

be the natural section. Then
\xi_{v}
is a morphism in
\mathcal{G}rb(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v})
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be a morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. (Here
\mathfrak{Q}
is defined with respect to the fixed
p
.) For each place
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
except
p
, we define

X_{v}(\phi):=\left\{g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})\mid\Int(g)\circ\xi_{v}=\phi(v)\circ\zeta_{v}\right\}.

We define

X_{p}(\phi):=X_{-\mu}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})

for
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
. Here the right hand side is as in §
2.2.7
, and it is independent of the choice of
\mu
. We have the
p^{r}
-Frobenius
\Phi:X_{p}(\phi)X_{p}(\phi)
.

Definition 2.4.2

(cf.
[
Kis17
, §3.3.6]
)
.

Keep the setting of §
2.4.1
. A morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
is called
admissible
, if the following conditions are satisfied.

(i)

Let
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Let
\psi_{\mu_{\widetilde{\ab}}}:\mathfrak{Q}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{G}_{G/G_{\mathrm{sc}}}
be the associated strictly monoidal functor, defined in
[
Kis17
, §3.3.1]
. Let
\phi_{\widetilde{\ab}}
be the composite strictly monoidal functor
\mathfrak{Q}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathfrak{G}_{G}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G/G_{\mathrm{sc}}}
. We require that there exist
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and a continuous monoidal isomorphism
{\mathcal{A}}:\Int(g)\circ\psi_{\mu_{\widetilde{\ab}}}\phi_{\widetilde{\ab}}.

(ii)

For each place
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
,
X_{v}(\phi)\neq\emptyset
.

Remark 2.4.3
.

Condition (i) in Definition
2.4.2
is a correction of condition (1) in
[
Kis17
, §3.3.6]
in that we add the requirement that
{\mathcal{A}}
should be continuous. The results on admissible morphisms in
[
Kis17
, §3.4]
, especially the statement and proof of
[
Kis17
, Prop. 3.4.11]
, are only correct with the present definition.

Remark 2.4.4
.

Given a morphism
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
, whether it is admissible depends only on its conjugacy class. We may thus speak of admissible conjugacy classes of morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
.

Remark 2.4.5
.

As is pointed out by Reimann
[
Rei97
, App. B]
, the original construction of the quasi-motivic gerb
\mathfrak{Q}
in
[
LR87
]
is incorrect. As such, the definitions and results in
[
LR87
, §5]
about “admissible morphisms” do not directly apply to the objects defined in Definition
2.4.2
. Nevertheless, most of the content of
loc. cit.
can be modified to suit the corrected definition of
\mathfrak{Q}
. In the sequel, we shall cite
loc. cit.
only for those technical results that are essentially independent of the actual construction of
\mathfrak{Q}
.

2.4.6
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an arbitrary morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. By condition (ii) in
[
Rei97
, Def. B2.7]
, there exists a continuous cocycle
\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}:\Gamma\to G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
that induces the morphisms
\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l}
for all finite primes
l\neq p
, in the following sense. We have a canonical map
\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p}=\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\to\overline{\mathbb Q}_{l}
given by the projection
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\to\mathbb{Q}_{l}
and the fixed embedding
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\to\overline{\mathbb Q}_{l}
. Denote the composition

\Gamma_{l}\hookrightarrow\Gamma\xrightarrow{\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}}G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})\to G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{l})

by
\zeta_{\phi,l}
. Then for each
\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{l}=\Gamma_{l}
we have

(\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l})(\tau)=\zeta_{\phi,l}(\tau)\rtimes\tau\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\rtimes\Gamma_{l}.

If we choose an arbitrary
\mathbb{Z}
-structure on
G
, then for almost all primes
l\neq p
the set
X_{l}(\phi)
contains integral points in
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
(and is
a fortiori
non-empty). Indeed, for almost all
l
, the chosen
\mathbb{Z}
-structure on
G
has connected smooth reduction at
l
, and
\zeta_{\phi,l}
is induced by a continuous unramified cocycle
{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{l}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{l})\to G(\mathbb{Z}_{l}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. It is a standard result (see for instance
[
PR94
, p. 294, Thm. 6.8’]
) that any such cocycle is a coboundary, and this precisely means that
X_{l}(\phi)
contains integral points.

2.4.7
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism. On choosing a
\mathbb{Z}
-structure on
G
, we form the restricted product

X^{p}(\phi):=\prod^{\prime}_{l\notin\left\{p,\infty\right\}}X_{l}(\phi)

with respect to the subsets of integral elements of the
X_{l}(\phi)
(cf. §
2.4.6
). Clearly
X^{p}(\phi)
is independent of the choice of
\mathbb{Z}
-structure, and is a
G(\mathbb A^{p}_{f})
-torsor under right multiplication. (It is non-empty since
\phi
is admissible.) Equivalently, with the notation in §
2.4.6
,
X^{p}(\phi)
is the right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-torsor consisting of
x\in G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
such that

x^{-1}\cdot\zeta_{\phi}^{p,\infty}(\tau)\cdot{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}x=1,\quad\forall\tau\in\Gamma.

We now define

X(\phi):=X_{p}(\phi)\times X^{p}(\phi),

which is equipped with the action of
\Phi^{\mathbb{Z}}\times G(\mathbb A_{f}^{p})
. We still call
\Phi
the
q
-Frobenius
on
X(\phi)
.

By definition,
X(\phi)
is a subset of
G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\times G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
. Under the canonical embedding
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
, we let the group
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
act on
G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\times G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
via left multiplication. This induces a left action of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on
X(\phi)
. For each
\tau\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we set

S_{\tau}(\phi):=\varprojlim_{K^{p}}I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}\backslash X(\phi)/K^{p},

where
K^{p}
runs through the compact open subgroups of
G(\mathbb A^{p}_{f})
, and
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}
denotes the image of

I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\xrightarrow{\Int(\tau)}I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}).

Then
S_{\tau}(\phi)
inherits the action of
\Phi^{\mathbb{Z}}\times G(\mathbb A_{f}^{p})
. When
\tau=1
, we write
S(\phi)
for
S_{\tau}(\phi)
.

2.5.
Integral models and the Langlands–Rapoport Conjecture

2.5.1
.

Keep the setting and notation of §
2.4.1
. Let
K_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and let
\Sh_{K_{p}}=\Sh_{K_{p}}(G,X)
be the inverse limit

\varprojlim_{K^{p}}\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}(G,X),

where
K^{p}
runs through compact open subgroups of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. This inverse limit exists as an
E
-scheme, since the transition maps are finite. The right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action on
\Sh_{K_{p}}
induced by the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-action on
\Sh(G,X)
is admissible in the sense of Definition
1.5.1
.

Definition 2.5.2
.

By a
smooth integral model
of
\Sh_{K_{p}}
, we mean a scheme
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
extending
\Sh_{K_{p}}
, equipped with an admissible right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action extending the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action on
\Sh_{K_{p}}
.
15
15

15

The adjective “smooth” refers to the smoothness requirement in condition (ii) in Definition
1.5.1
for the admissible
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
. The scheme
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
is typically not locally of finite presentation over
\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
.
When
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
is given, we write
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
for
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}/K^{p}
for all sufficiently small compact open subgroups
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

The following theorem is proved in
[
Kis10
]
for
p>2
, and in
[
KMP16
]
for
p=2
.

Theorem 2.5.3

(
[
Kis10
,
KMP16
]
)
.

If
(G,X)
is of abelian type, then there exists a smooth integral model of
\Sh_{K_{p}}
. This model is uniquely characterized by the extension property as detailed in
[
Kis10
, §2.3.7]
.

Remark 2.5.4
.

In
[
Kis10
]
and
[
KMP16
]
, it is not explicitly verified that the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action on the integral model satisfies the separatedness in condition (ii) and condition (iii) in Definition
1.5.1
. The former follows from the facts that the Siegel modular schemes at finite levels are separated over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, that normalization maps and closed immersions are separated, and that taking finite free quotients preserve separatedness. For the latter, see
[
LS18
, §3]
for an explanation.

2.5.5
.

Now fix a prime
\ell\neq p
and fix an irreducible
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-representation
\xi
of
G
that factors through
G^{c}=G/Z_{ac}
, as in §
1.5.8
. Suppose a smooth integral model
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
is given. As explained in §
1.5.2
, for sufficiently small compact open subgroups
U^{p}\subset K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, the map
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}U^{p}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
is finite étale Galois and the Galois group is identified with the maximal quotient of
K^{p}/U^{p}
that acts faithfully on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}U^{p}}
. Since
\Sh_{K_{p}U^{p}}
is dense in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}U^{p}}
, we see that the last group is identified with
{\rm Gal}(\Sh_{K_{p}U^{p}}/\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}})
. Thus by (
1.5.8.1
), we have

{\rm Gal}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}U^{p}}/\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}})\cong K^{p}/U^{p}Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{-,(p)},

where
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{-,(p)}
is the image of
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{-}
under the projection
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Hence
{\rm Gal}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}/\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}})
as defined in (
1.5.2.1
) is the quotient of
K^{p}
by the closure of
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{-,(p)}
in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. (Actually
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{-,(p)}
is already closed in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, because
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{-}
is compact.) By Lemma
1.5.7
, (the closure of)
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{-,(p)}
is contained in
Z_{ac}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
when
K^{p}
is sufficiently small. We now view
\xi
as a continuous representation of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
via the projection
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
. Then for all sufficiently small
K^{p}
, the restriction
\xi|_{K^{p}}
factors through
{\rm Gal}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}/\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}})
by the above discussion. Thus as in §
1.5.2
, for each sufficiently small
K^{p}
we obtain a lisse
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-sheaf
{\mathscr{L}}_{\xi,K^{p}}
on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
, and for each geometric point
x
of
\Spec\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
we have the admissible
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-module

\coh^{i}_{c}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p},x},\xi):=\varinjlim_{K^{p}}\coh^{i}_{c}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p},x},{\mathscr{L}}_{\xi,K^{p}}).

When
x=\Spec\overline{E}
, the above is identified with
\coh^{i}_{c}(\Sh_{\overline{E}},\xi)^{K_{p}}
. Moreover, we have a canonical adjunction morphism

(2.5.5.1)

\displaystyle\coh_{c}^{i}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p},\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},\xi)\longrightarrow\coh_{c}^{i}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p},\overline{E}},\xi)\cong\coh^{i}_{c}(\Sh_{\overline{E}},\xi)^{K_{p}},

which is
{\rm Gal}(\overline{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}/E_{{\mathfrak{p}}})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant. Here
{\rm Gal}(\overline{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}/E_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
acts on the left via the quotient
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
, and acts on the right via the embedding into
{\rm Gal}(\overline{E}/E)
.

Definition 2.5.6
.

We say that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
has
well-behaved
\coh^{*}_{c}
, if (
2.5.5.1
) is an isomorphism for all choices of
\ell\neq p
,
\xi
, and
i
.

Theorem 2.5.7

(
[
LS18
, Cor. 4.6]
)
.

The canonical smooth integral model in Theorem
2.5.3
has well-behaved
\coh^{*}_{c}
.

Recall that for each admissible morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, we have defined in §
2.4.7
a set
S(\phi)
equipped with an action of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and a
q
-Frobenius
\Phi
, where
q
is the residue cardinality of
{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Conjecture 2.5.8

(Langlands–Rapoport)
.

There exists a smooth integral model
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
of
\Sh_{K_{p}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
which has well-behaved
\coh^{*}_{c}
and for which there is a bijection

\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}){}\coprod_{\phi}S(\phi)

compatible with the actions of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and the
q
-Frobenius
\Phi
. Here
\phi
runs through a set of representatives for the conjugacy classes of admissible morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
.

In the rest of this section, we formulate a variant of the above conjecture, which we call “the Langlands–Rapoport–
\tau
Conjecture”.

2.6.
Preparations for the Langlands–Rapoport–
\tau
Conjecture

In this subsection we develop the prerequisites for our formulation of the Langlands–Rapoport–
\tau
Conjecture.

2.6.1
.

Using that the projections
\mathfrak{Q}^{\topo}\to\mathfrak{Q}^{L}
are surjective (see §
2.2.8
), for each
\tau\in\Gamma
we can choose a lift
q_{\tau}\in\mathfrak{Q}^{\topo}
of
\tau
. We fix such a choice in the sequel. We first study a “well-positioned” condition for morphisms from
\mathfrak{Q}
to neutral gerbs. Let
G
be an arbitrary reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
. Recall from §
2.2.8
that
\mathfrak{Q}^{\Delta}
has the canonical
\mathbb{Q}
-structure
Q
.

Definition 2.6.2
.

A morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
is called
günstig gelegen
(to be abbreviated as
gg
)
16
16

16

This terminology comes from Langlands–Rapoport
[
LR87
, §5]
. However, the definition of günstig gelegen morphisms given by Langlands–Rapoport uses the elements
\delta_{n}
, which do not directly make sense with the corrected definition of
\mathfrak{Q}
.
, if
\phi^{\Delta}:\mathfrak{Q}^{\Delta}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Lemma 2.6.3
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be a morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. For each
\tau\in\Gamma={\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
, write
\phi(q_{\tau})=g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau
, with
g_{\tau}\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then
\phi
is gg if and only if
g_{\tau}
lies in
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\phi^{\Delta}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
for each
\tau\in\Gamma
. If
\phi
is gg, then the canonical
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-isomorphism
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}G_{\phi^{\Delta},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is an inner twistings between the underlying
\mathbb{Q}
-groups.

Proof.

Assume that
\phi
is induced by a morphism
\phi_{0}:\mathfrak{Q}^{L}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
in
\mathcal{G}rb(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. Let
y\in Q^{L}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})=\mathfrak{Q}^{L,\Delta}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and
\tau\in\Gamma
be arbitrary. We denote the image of
q_{\tau}\in\mathfrak{Q}^{\topo}
in
\mathfrak{Q}^{L}
still by
q_{\tau}
. Then

(2.6.3.1)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\Delta}({\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}y)=\phi_{0}(q_{\tau}yq_{\tau}^{-1})=(g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau)\phi_{0}^{\Delta}(y)(g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau)^{-1}=g_{\tau}{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}[\phi_{0}^{\Delta}(y)]g_{\tau}^{-1}.

Now
\phi^{\Delta}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
if and only if
\phi_{0}^{\Delta}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
(since the transition maps in the pro-torus
Q=\varprojlim_{L}Q^{L}
are all surjective). By (
2.6.3.1
), this is equivalent to the condition that each
g_{\tau}
centralizes
\im(\phi_{0}^{\Delta})
. Since
\im(\phi_{0}^{\Delta})=\im(\phi^{\Delta})
, the last condition is equivalent to the condition that each
g_{\tau}
lies in
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\phi^{\Delta}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
.

Now if
\phi
is gg, then for each
\tau\in\Gamma
the
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-isomorphism
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}G_{\phi^{\Delta},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
differs from its
\tau
-twist by composition with
\Int(g_{\tau})
. Since
g_{\tau}\in G_{\phi^{\Delta}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, this means that the
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-isomorphism is an inner twisting.
∎

Definition 2.6.4
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be a morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. By a
G
-rational maximal torus in
I_{\phi}
, we mean a maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
(defined over
\mathbb{Q}
) such that the composite embedding
T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\phi^{\Delta}}\ \hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Remark 2.6.5
.

In Definition
2.6.4
,
T
is necessarily a maximal torus in
G
. This is because
\mathfrak{Q}^{\Delta}
is a pro-torus, and as a result
I_{\phi}
is a reductive group having the same absolute rank as
G
, cf. §
2.1.14
.

Lemma 2.6.6
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be a morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
such that
I_{\phi}
contains a
G
-rational maximal torus
T
. Then
\phi
is gg. Moreover, let
f
denote the
\mathbb{Q}
-embedding underlying
T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\phi^{\Delta}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Then
\phi
factors as
\mathfrak{Q}\xrightarrow{\phi_{T}}\mathfrak{G}_{T}\xrightarrow{f}\mathfrak{G}_{G}
.

Proof.

For each
\tau\in\Gamma
, define
g_{\tau}\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
as in Lemma
2.6.3
. For
t\in T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, we have

f({\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}t)=g_{\tau}{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}[f(t)]g_{\tau}^{-1},

by the definition of the
\mathbb{Q}
-structure of
I_{\phi}
; see §
2.1.6
and §
2.1.8
. Since
f
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, we have
f({\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}t)={\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}[f(t)]
. Hence
g_{\tau}
commutes with
f(T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}))
. Since
f(T)
is a maximal torus in
G
(see Remark
2.6.5
), we have
g_{\tau}\in f(T)(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. We conclude that
\phi
is gg by Lemma
2.6.3
. Moreover, since
Z_{I_{\phi}}\subset T
and since
\phi^{\Delta}
factors through the center of
G_{\phi^{\Delta}}
, we know that
\phi^{\Delta}
factors through
f(T)\subset G
. We have already seen that each
g_{\tau}
lies in
f(T)(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. It follows that
\phi
factors through
f:\mathfrak{G}_{T}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
.
∎

Lemma 2.6.7
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism. Then the
\mathbb{R}
-group
I_{\phi(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}}
is an inner form of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
. Moreover, the
\mathbb{R}
-groups
(I_{\phi}/Z_{G})_{\mathbb{R}}=I_{\phi(\infty)}/Z_{G,\mathbb{R}}
and
I_{\phi(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}}/Z_{G,\mathbb{R}}
are both anisotropic.

Proof.

As in (
2.1.6.1
) we have an
\mathbb{R}
-embedding
I_{\phi(\infty)}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}}
. Since
\phi
is admissible,
\phi(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}
is conjugate to
\xi_{\infty}
. Hence there is an
\mathbb{R}
-isomorphism
I_{\phi(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}}I_{\xi_{\infty}}
induced by
\Int(g)
for some
g\in G(\mathbb{C})
. As discussed in
[
Kis17
, §3.3.5]
,
I_{\xi_{\infty}}
is the inner form of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
with anisotropic adjoint group. The lemma follows. ∎

2.6.8
.

We now return to the setting of §
2.4.1
. Thus we have an unramified Shimura datum
(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
and the notion of admissible morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
.

Proposition 2.6.9
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism. For each maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, there exists
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\Int(g)(T)
is a
G
-rational maximal torus in
I_{\Int(g)\circ\phi}
.

Proof.

This is essentially proved by Langlands–Rapoport, when they prove
[
LR87
, Lem. 5.23]
. We sketch the argument, as the precise statement of the proposition is not explicit in
[
LR87
]
.
17
17

17

The only information about
\mathfrak{Q}
used in this argument is the fact that
\mathfrak{Q}^{\Delta}
is a pro-torus with surjective transition maps. Hence the validity of this argument is unaffected by Reimann’s correction of the definition of
\mathfrak{Q}
.

Let
\psi:G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}G^{*}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
be an inner twisting from
G
to a fixed quasi-split reductive group
G^{*}
over
\mathbb{Q}
. Then the
G^{*}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugacy class of the composite embedding
\iota:T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\xrightarrow{\psi}G^{*}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is stable under
\Gamma
. By
[
Kot82
, Cor. 2.2]
, we can modify
\psi
by an inner automorphism to arrange that
\iota
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Let
T^{*}=\iota(T)
. Then
T^{*}
is a maximal torus in
G^{*}
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, and we have a
\mathbb{Q}
-isomorphism
\iota:TT^{*}
. We now check that
T^{*}
transfers to
G
locally at all places
v
, or equivalently, that some
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
-conjugate of
T
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-torus in
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
. For
v=p
, this follows from the assumption that
G
is unramified, and hence quasi-split, over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For
v=\infty
, this follows from the fact that
T^{*}/Z_{G^{*}}\cong T/Z_{G}
is anisotropic over
\mathbb{R}
(Lemma
2.6.7
). For
v\notin\left\{\infty,p\right\}
, pick
u_{v}\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
such that
\Int(u_{v}^{-1})\circ\phi(v)\circ\zeta_{v}=\xi_{v}
, which exists since
\phi
is admissible. Then the canonical embedding
I_{\Int(u_{v})\circ\phi(v),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
, and hence
\Int(u_{v})(T_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}})
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-maximal torus in
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
, as desired.

Since
T^{*}
transfers to
G
locally and is elliptic over
\mathbb{R}
, it transfers globally to
G
by
[
LR87
, Lem. 5.6]
. This means there exists
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\Int(g)(T)
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-maximal torus in
G
and such that the isomorphism
\Int(g)\circ\psi^{-1}:T^{*}\to\Int(g)(T)
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. It follows that
\Int(g)(T)
is a
G
-rational maximal torus in
I_{\Int(g)\circ\phi}
.
∎

Corollary 2.6.10
.

Every admissible morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
is conjugate to a gg morphism.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.6.9
,
\phi
is conjugate to a morphism
\phi^{\prime}:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
such that
I_{\phi^{\prime}}
contains a
G
-rational maximal torus.
By Lemma
2.6.6
,
\phi^{\prime}
is gg.
∎

2.6.11
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism. By Corollary
2.6.10
, the set
{\mathcal{W}}:=\left\{g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\mid\Int g\circ\phi\text{ is gg}\right\}
is non-empty. Using Lemma
2.6.3
, one checks that the canonical embedding
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
and
{\mathcal{W}}
together form an inner transfer datum from
I_{\phi}
to
G
(Definition
1.2.4
). We thus obtain a canonical map

(2.6.11.1)

\displaystyle\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\longrightarrow\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},G),

and we define
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\subset\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})
as in §
1.2.5
.

Proposition 2.6.12
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism, and
\beta\in\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi}).
Then
\phi^{\beta}
(see Definition
2.1.17
) is admissible if and only if
\beta
belongs to
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})
.

Proof.

The “if” part is proved in
[
Kis17
, Lem. 4.5.6]
, under the assumption that
Z_{G}^{0}
is cuspidal. Below we give complete proofs of both directions of the implication, taking into account the correction of the definition of admissible morphisms mentioned in Remark
2.4.3
. Since the admissibility condition is invariant under conjugacy, we use Corollary
2.6.10
to reduce to the case where
\phi
is gg. We now assume that
\phi
is gg and fix a cocycle
a\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})
representing
\beta
.

Step 1.
We show that
\beta
has zero image under the composite

(2.6.12.1)

\displaystyle\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\xrightarrow{\ab^{1}{Q}}\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\xrightarrow{(\ref {eq:transfer I_phi})}\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},G)

if and only if there exist
h\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and a continuous monoidal isomorphism
\Int(h)\circ\phi_{\widetilde{\ab}}(a\phi)_{\widetilde{\ab}}
. (See Definition
2.4.2
for the notation.) By Lemma
2.3.4
and Remark
2.3.5
, the latter condition is equivalent to asking that
\beta
comes from
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{I}_{\phi}).
We have a natural exact sequence of pointed sets

\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{I}_{\phi})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{I}_{\phi}\to I_{\phi}),

where
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{I}_{\phi}\to I_{\phi})
is the Galois cohomology of the crossed module
(\widetilde{I}_{\phi}\to I_{\phi})
of
\mathbb{Q}
-groups; see
[
Bor98
, §3]
. (The crossed module structure is the one inherited from the crossed module
G_{\mathrm{sc}}\to G
.) The natural map
(Z_{G_{\mathrm{sc}}}\to Z_{G})\to(\widetilde{I}_{\phi}\to I_{\phi})
is a quasi-isomorphism of crossed modules, and therefore
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{I}_{\phi}\to I_{\phi})
is naturally isomorphic to
\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},G)
. The composition
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{I}_{\phi}\to I_{\phi})\cong\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},G)
is equal to (
2.6.12.1
). This proves the desired statement.

Step 2.
We show that
\beta
has trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi})
if and only if
\phi(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}
is conjugate to
(a\phi)(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}
. By Lemma
2.1.16
, the latter condition is equivalent to the vanishing of the image of
\beta
under

\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi})=\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi(\infty)})\xrightarrow{\dagger}\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}}),

where
\dagger
is induced by the
\mathbb{R}
-inclusion
I_{\phi(\infty)}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}}
. Thus the desired statement boils down to
\dagger
having trivial kernel, which follows from Lemma
2.6.7
and
[
Kis17
, Lem. 4.4.5]
.

Step 3.
We show that if
\beta
has zero image under (
2.6.12.1
), then
\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l}
is conjugate to
(a\phi)(l)\circ\zeta_{l}
for all finite primes
l\neq p
. For this, it suffices to show that
\beta
has trivial image under the composite map

(2.6.12.2)

\displaystyle\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},I_{\phi})=\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},I_{\phi(l)})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},I_{\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l}}).

Since
\phi
is admissible,
{\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l}}
is conjugate to
\xi_{l}
. It follows that the canonical
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-embedding
I_{\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}}
is an inner twisting between
\mathbb{Q}_{l}
-groups. This induces a canonical isomorphism

(2.6.12.3)

\displaystyle\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},I_{\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l}})\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},G).

Now we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref {eq:enlarge v})}
\scriptstyle{(\ref {eq:coh pol})}
\textstyle{\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},I_{\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ab^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}_{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},I_{\phi(l)\circ\zeta_{l}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref {eq:v})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
localization
\textstyle{\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},G)}

which implies the desired statement.

Step 4.
We show that if
\beta
has zero image under (
2.6.12.1
), then
X_{p}(a\phi)\neq\emptyset
. We fix
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
as in §
2.4.1
. Let
\theta:=\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p}
, and let
\theta^{\prime}:=(a\phi)(p)\circ\zeta_{p}
. By definition
X_{p}(\phi)=X_{-\mu}(\theta)
and
X_{p}(a\phi)=X_{-\mu}(\theta^{\prime})
.

Fix an arbitrary
g_{0}\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
, and write
\theta_{0}
for
\Int(g_{0}^{-1})\theta
. Thus
\theta_{0}
is unramified. Now
\Int(g_{0}^{-1})
induces a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-isomorphism
I_{\theta}I_{\theta_{0}}
. Let
\beta_{0}
denote the image of
\beta
under the composite

\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\phi})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\theta})\xrightarrow{\Int(g_{0}^{-1})}\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\theta_{0}}).

Then
\theta^{\prime}
belongs to the conjugacy class of
\theta_{0}^{\beta_{0}}
.

By Proposition
2.2.6
(iii), the conjugacy class
\theta_{0}^{\beta_{0}}
contains an unramified member
\theta_{0}^{\prime}
such that
b^{\prime}:=b_{\theta_{0}^{\prime}}
is obtained from
b:=b_{\theta_{0}}
by twisting by
\beta_{0}
. Since
G
is quasi-split over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we can apply Proposition
1.4.5
to conclude that
\nu_{b}=\nu_{b^{\prime}}
, and that
\kappa_{G}(b^{\prime})-\kappa_{G}(b)
is the image of
\beta_{0}
in
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}
. Our assumption on
\beta
implies that the last image is zero.
Hence we have
[b]=[b^{\prime}]
in
\B(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, by Kottwitz’s classification (see §
1.4.2
).

Since
\theta_{0}
(resp.
\theta^{\prime}_{0}
) is an unramified member in the conjugacy class of
\theta
(resp.
\theta^{\prime}
), by the discussion in §
2.2.7
, we have
X_{-\mu}(\theta)\cong X_{-\mu}(b)
, and
X_{-\mu}(\theta^{\prime})\cong X_{-\mu}(b^{\prime})
. Since
[b]=[b^{\prime}]
, we have
X_{-\mu}(b)\cong X_{-\mu}(b^{\prime})
. Thus the non-emptiness of
X_{-\mu}(\theta)
implies the non-emptiness of
X_{-\mu}(\theta^{\prime})
.

The proof of the proposition is completed by combining the above four steps. (The “only if” part follows from Steps 1 and 2 alone.)
∎

2.6.13
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism. We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}).

We have a natural map

\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}(\phi),

and it is surjective by weak approximation (see
[
PR94
, Thm. 7.8]
) applied to
I_{\phi}^{\ad}
.

The boundary map arising from the short exact sequence
1\to Z_{I_{\phi}}\to I_{\phi}\to I_{\phi}^{\ad}\to 1
induces an isomorphism of pointed sets
\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)\cong\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Since
\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cong\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is an abelian group, we have a canonical abelian group structure on
\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)
.

Lemma 2.6.14
.

The surjection
\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)\to\mathcal{H}(\phi)
induces an abelian group structure on
\mathcal{H}(\phi)
. Moreover, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}},I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}};\mathbb{Q})}

where the rows are isomorphisms. Here the right vertical arrow is induced by the inclusion
\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and the bottom arrow is induced by the boundary map
\delta:I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})\cong\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})
arising from the short exact sequence
1\to Z_{I_{\phi}}\to I_{\phi}\to I_{\phi}^{\ad}\to 1
.

Proof.

First note that
\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}},I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}};\mathbb{Q})
is a subgroup of
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}})
, so the quotient on the lower right corner of the diagram is defined. Now the boundary map
\delta
induces a bijection
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and maps
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})\subset I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
onto the image of
\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}},I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}};\mathbb{Q})\to\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})
(since we have a surjective boundary map
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})\to\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}},I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}};\mathbb{Q})
associated with the short exact sequence
1\to Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}}\to I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}\to I_{\phi}^{\ad}\to 1
). The lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 2.6.15
.

The subset
\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}},I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}};\mathbb{Q})
of
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}})
(which is a subgroup) is equal to the kernel of the composite map of pointed sets

\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}).

Proof.

By the Kneser–Harder–Chernousov Theorem (see
[
Bor98
, Thm. 5.0.3]
), the localization map
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\mathrm{sc}})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\mathrm{sc}})
is a bijection. The lemma follows.
∎

2.6.16
.

Let
\mathcal{AM}=\mathcal{AM}(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
be the set of all admissible morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
.
On this set we define an equivalence relation
\approx
by declaring
\phi_{1}\approx\phi_{2}
if and only if
\phi_{1}^{\Delta}
is
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugate to
\phi_{2}^{\Delta}
. Clearly
\approx
is weaker than the equivalence relation defined by conjugacy among admissible morphisms. By Lemma
2.1.16
, we know that
\phi_{1}\approx\phi_{2}
if and only if there exists a (necessarily unique)
\beta\in\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})
such that
\phi_{2}
belongs to the conjugacy class
\phi_{1}^{\beta}
. Moreover, when this is the case, we know that
\beta
lies in
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{1}})
by Proposition
2.6.12
.

As a consequence of Lemma
2.6.14
and Lemma
2.6.15
, we know that for
\phi\in\mathcal{AM}
, the abelian group
\mathcal{H}(\phi)
depends only on the groups
Z_{I_{\phi}},Z_{I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc}}},I_{\phi,\mathrm{sc},\mathbb{R}}
and the maps between them. The same is true for the abelian group
\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)\cong\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Now if
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\mathcal{AM}
are such that
\phi_{1}\approx\phi_{2}
, then for any
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\Int(g)\circ\phi_{1}^{\Delta}=\phi_{2}^{\Delta}
, the
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-isomorphism
\Int(g):G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\phi_{1}^{\Delta}}G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\phi_{2}^{\Delta}}
induces an inner twisting
\comp_{g}:I_{\phi_{1},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I_{\phi_{2},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
between
\mathbb{Q}
-groups. Clearly the equivalence class (Definition
1.2.1
) of the inner twisting
\comp_{g}
is independent of the choice of
g
. It follows that
Z_{I_{\phi_{1}}},Z_{I_{\phi_{1},\mathrm{sc}}}
are canonically identified with
Z_{I_{\phi_{2}}},Z_{I_{\phi_{2},\mathrm{sc}}}
respectively. If we let
\comp_{g,\mathrm{sc}}:I_{\phi_{1},\mathrm{sc},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I_{\phi_{2},\mathrm{sc},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
be the inner twisting induced by
\comp_{g}
, then the equivalence class of
\comp_{g,\mathrm{sc}}
is also independent of
g
. Moreover,
\comp_{g,\mathrm{sc}}\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{C}
is the composition of an
\mathbb{R}
-isomorphism with an inner automorphism defined over
\mathbb{C}
. This is because if we let
\beta
be the element of
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{1}})
such that
\phi_{2}\in\phi_{1}^{\beta}
, then the class of
\comp_{g,\mathrm{sc}}
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{1}}^{\ad})
is the image of
\beta
, and hence has trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi_{1}}^{\ad})
. Thus
I_{\phi_{1},\mathrm{sc},\mathbb{R}}
is canonically identified with
I_{\phi_{2},\mathrm{sc},\mathbb{R}}
up to inner automorphisms defined over
\mathbb{R}
.
From this analysis, we see that there are canonical abelian group isomorphisms

\displaystyle\comp_{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}}:

\displaystyle~\mathcal{H}(\phi_{1})\mathcal{H}(\phi_{2})

\displaystyle\comp^{\mathfrak{E}^{p}}_{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}}:

\displaystyle~\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi_{1})\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi_{2})

which depend only on
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
. These maps commute with the natural surjections
\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi_{i})\to{\mathcal{H}}(\phi_{i})
. For any three
\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}\in\mathcal{AM}
such that
\phi_{1}\approx\phi_{2}\approx\phi_{3}
, we have the following cocycle relations

\displaystyle\comp_{\phi_{2},\phi_{3}}\circ\comp_{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\comp_{\phi_{1},\phi_{3}},\quad\comp_{\phi_{1},\phi_{1}}=\id_{\mathcal{H}(\phi_{1})};

\displaystyle\comp^{\mathfrak{E}^{p}}_{\phi_{2},\phi_{3}}\circ\comp^{\mathfrak{E}^{p}}_{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\comp^{\mathfrak{E}^{p}}_{\phi_{1},\phi_{3}},\quad\comp^{\mathfrak{E}^{p}}_{\phi_{1},\phi_{1}}=\id_{\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi_{1})}.

We now view
\mathcal{AM}=\mathcal{AM}(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
as a discrete topological space, and define sheaves of abelian groups
\mathfrak{E}^{p}=\mathfrak{E}^{p}_{(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})}
and
{\mathcal{H}}={\mathcal{H}}_{(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})}
on
\mathcal{AM}
whose stalks at each
\phi\in\mathcal{AM}
are
\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)
and
{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
respectively. We have a surjective homomorphism
\mathfrak{E}^{p}\to{\mathcal{H}}
. The above discussion implies that
\mathfrak{E}^{p}
and
{\mathcal{H}}
are the pull-backs of unique (up to unique isomorphism) sheaves of abelian groups
\mathfrak{E}^{p}_{\approx}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\approx}
on the quotient space
\mathcal{AM}/{\approx}
. The surjective homomorphism
\mathfrak{E}^{p}\to{\mathcal{H}}
is the pull-back of a unique surjective homomorphism
\mathfrak{E}^{p}_{\approx}\to{\mathcal{H}}_{\approx}
.

The quotient map
\mathcal{AM}\to\mathcal{AM}/{\approx}
factors through
\mathcal{AM}/\mathrm{conj}
, the set of conjugacy classes of admissible morphisms. We let
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{conj}}
(resp.
\mathfrak{E}^{p}_{\mathrm{conj}}
) be the pull-back of
{\mathcal{H}}_{\approx}
(resp.
\mathfrak{E}^{p}_{\approx}
) to
\mathcal{AM}/\mathrm{conj}
.

Definition 2.6.17
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\left\{\mathfrak{E}^{p},{\mathcal{H}}\right\}
, we denote by
\Gamma({\mathcal{F}})
the group of global sections of the sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
on
\mathcal{AM}
. For
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{F}})
, we write
\underline{\tau}(\phi)\in{\mathcal{F}}(\phi)
for the germ of
\underline{\tau}
at each
\phi\in\mathcal{AM}
. We denote by
\Gamma({\mathcal{F}})_{0}
(resp.
\Gamma({\mathcal{F}})_{1}
) the subgroup of
\Gamma({\mathcal{F}})
consisting of those global sections that descend to global sections of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\approx}
over
\mathcal{AM}/{\approx}
(resp. global sections of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathrm{conj}}
over
\mathcal{AM}/\mathrm{conj}
). Thus
\Gamma({\mathcal{F}})_{0}\subset\Gamma({\mathcal{F}})_{1}\subset\Gamma({\mathcal{F}})
.

2.6.18
.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{AM}
. The boundary map
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{I_{\phi}})
arising from the short exact sequence
1\to Z_{I_{\phi}}\to I_{\phi}\to I_{\phi}^{\ad}\to 1
induces a map

(2.6.18.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\phi)=I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{I_{\phi}})/\Sha_{I_{\phi}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi}}).

Indeed, since
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{R})
is compact (by Lemma
2.6.7
), it is connected (see
[
Bor91
, §24.6]
). Hence the map
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{R})\to I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{R})
is onto, and the boundary map
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{R})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},Z_{I_{\phi}})
is trivial. In particular, the image of
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi}})
lies in
\Sha^{\infty}_{I_{\phi}}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi}})
, and it follows that (
2.6.18.1
) is well defined. We have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
2.6.18.1
)
\textstyle{\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{I_{\phi}})/\Sha_{I_{\phi}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi}})}

where the left vertical arrow is the natural surjection and the right vertical arrow is induced by the inclusion
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},Z_{I_{\phi}})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{I_{\phi}})
.

Definition 2.6.19
.

Let
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})
, and
\underline{\sigma}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})
.

(i)

We say that
\underline{\tau}
is
tori-rational
, if for each
\phi\in\mathcal{AM}
and for each maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
, the image of
\underline{\tau}(\phi)
is trivial under the composite map

(2.6.19.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\phi)\xrightarrow{(\ref {eq:second coh real'n of H(phi)})}\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{I_{\phi}})/\Sha_{I_{\phi}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi}})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},T)/\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T).

(ii)

We say that
\underline{\sigma}
is
tori-rational
, if for each
\phi\in\mathcal{AM}
and for each maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
, the image of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi)
is trivial under the composite map

\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)\cong\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\subset\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},Z_{I_{\phi}})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},T)\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},T)/\Sha_{G}^{\infty,p}(\mathbb{Q},T).

In the above,
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T)
denotes the kernel of the composite map
\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T)\to\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})\to\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},G)
, and similarly for
\Sha_{G}^{\infty,p}(\mathbb{Q},T)
; see §
1.2.5
and §
2.6.11
.

The next lemma relates the two notions of tori-rationality for elements of
\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})
and of
\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})
.

Lemma 2.6.20
.

Let
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})
. The following statements are equivalent.

(i)

\underline{\tau}
is tori-rational.

(ii)

The section
\underline{\tau}
has a lift
\underline{\sigma}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})
along the natural surjection
\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})\to\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})
such that
\underline{\sigma}
is tori-rational.

(iii)

Every
\underline{\sigma}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})
lifting
\underline{\tau}
is tori-rational.

Proof.

The implication (ii)
\Rightarrow
(i) follows from the commutative diagram (
2.6.18
). Obviously (iii)
\Rightarrow
(ii). It remains to show (i)
\Rightarrow
(iii).

Let
\underline{\sigma}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})
be a lift of
\underline{\tau}
. For each
\phi\in\mathcal{AM}
, we have a natural surjection
\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})\to{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
as in Lemma
2.6.14
. Fix an element
\epsilon_{\phi}\in\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})
lifting
\underline{\tau}(\phi)
. Then the image of
\underline{\tau}(\phi)
under (
2.6.18.1
) is represented by
\epsilon_{\phi}
.

By the commutative diagram (
2.6.18
), the image of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi)
under
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},Z_{I_{\phi}})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{I_{\phi}})

equals the sum of
\epsilon_{\phi}
and the image of some
\upsilon_{\phi}\in\Sha^{\infty}_{I_{\phi}}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{I_{\phi}})
. For each maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
, by tori-rationality of
\underline{\tau}
there exists an element
\beta_{\phi,T}\in\Sha^{\infty}_{G}(\mathbb{Q},T)
whose image in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},T)
equals that of
\epsilon_{\phi}
. Let
\beta_{\phi,T}^{\prime}\in\Sha^{\infty}_{G}(\mathbb{Q},T)
be the sum of
\beta_{\phi,T}
and the image of
\upsilon_{\phi}
in
\Sha^{\infty}_{I_{\phi}}(\mathbb{Q},T)\subset\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T)
. Then the image of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi)
under

\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},T)

equals that of
\beta^{\prime}_{\phi,T}
. It follows that
\beta^{\prime}_{\phi,T}
lies in
\Sha_{G}^{\infty,p}(\mathbb{Q},T)
, and that the image of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi)
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},T)/\Sha_{G}^{\infty,p}(\mathbb{Q},T)
is trivial, as desired.
∎

2.7.
The Langlands–Rapoport–
\tau
Conjecture

2.7.1
.

Let

(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
be an unramified Shimura datum. Let
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})_{1}
(Definition
2.6.17
). For each admissible morphism
\phi:\mathbb{Q}_{f}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, we set

S_{\underline{\tau}}(\phi):=S_{\widetilde{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)}(\phi),

where
\widetilde{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is any lift of
\underline{\tau}(\phi)\in{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
, and
S_{\widetilde{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)}(\phi)
is defined as in §
2.4.7
. The isomorphism class of the
\Phi{Z}\times G(\mathbb A_{f}^{p})
-set
S_{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)
is independent of the choice of
\widetilde{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)
. Moreover, from the assumption that
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})_{1}
, it follows that the isomorphism class of the
\Phi{Z}\times G(\mathbb A_{f}^{p})
-set
S_{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)
depends on
\phi
only via its conjugacy class.

We write

\mathsf{LR}(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}},\underline{\tau})
for the modification of Conjecture
2.5.8
where each
S(\phi)
is replaced by
S_{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)
.

Combined with Theorem
2.5.3
and Theorem
2.5.7
, the main result of
[
Kis17
]
can be stated as follows.

Theorem 2.7.2
.

Let
(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
be an unramified Shimura datum such that
(G,X)
is of abelian type. Assume
p>2
. Then there exists
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})_{1}
such that the statement
\mathsf{LR}(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}},\underline{\tau})
holds.

We refer the reader to Theorem
6.2.4
below for a more precise version of the above theorem. There we will also explain that the assumption
p>2
can be removed. In the following conjecture, we impose better control of
\underline{\tau}
than the condition that
\underline{\tau}
belongs to
\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})_{1}
.

Conjecture 2.7.3

(Langlands–Rapoport–
\tau
)
.

For each unramified Shimura datum
(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
, there exists a tori-rational element
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})_{0}
such that the statement
\mathsf{LR}(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}},\underline{\tau})
holds.

Theorem 2.7.4
.

Conjecture
2.7.3
implies Conjecture
1.8.8
.

We devote the next section to the proof of Theorem
2.7.4
. Note that Conjecture
2.7.3
is weaker than Conjecture
2.5.8
, as the latter asserts that
\underline{\tau}
can be taken to be trivial.
In view of Theorem
2.7.4
, Conjecture
2.7.3
is a viable substitute for Conjecture
2.5.8
for applications to computing zeta functions and
\ell
-adic cohomology.

Theorem
2.7.2
is weaker than Conjecture
2.7.3
in that no extra control of
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})_{1}
is provided. In Part
II
we shall improve on Theorem
2.7.2
and prove Conjecture
2.7.3
in the case of abelian type.

3.
Langlands–Rapoport–
\tau
implies point counting

Throughout §
3
, we fix an unramified Shimura datum
(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
, and keep the notation
E,{\mathfrak{p}},q=p^{r}
as in §
2.4.1
. Our goal is to prove Theorem
2.7.4
.

3.1.
Semi-admissible and admissible Langlands–Rapoport pairs

Definition 3.1.1
.

By a
Langlands–Rapoport pair
(
LR pair
), we mean a pair
(\phi,\epsilon)
, where
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
is a morphism in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, and
\epsilon
is an element of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q}).

We call such a pair
(\phi,\epsilon)

semi-admissible
, if
\phi
is admissible. We denote by
\mathcal{LRP}
the set of all LR pairs, and by
\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
the subset of semi-admissible LR pairs.

Remark 3.1.2
.

If
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
, then
\epsilon
is semi-simple. This is because
(I_{\phi}/Z_{G})(\mathbb{R})
is anisotropic by Lemma
2.6.7
.

3.1.3
.

The group
G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
acts on the set
\mathcal{LRP}
by
conjugation
in the following sense. If
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
and
g\in G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
, then
\Int(g)(\phi,\epsilon):=(\Int(g)\circ\phi,\Int(g)\epsilon)
is also an element of
\mathcal{LRP}
. We write

\langle\mathcal{LRP}\rangle:=\mathcal{LRP}/G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\text{-conjugacy}.

For
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
, we denote by

\langle\phi,\epsilon\rangle\in\langle\mathcal{LRP}\rangle

the
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugacy class of
(\phi,\epsilon)
. The subset
\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}\subset\mathcal{LRP}
is stable under
G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
-conjugacy, and we write

\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}\rangle:=\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}/G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\text{-conjugacy}.

3.1.4
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
. Let
\theta=\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p}:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}(p)
, and choose
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
(see Definition
2.2.3
and Lemma
2.2.4
). Let
b_{g}:=b_{\Int(g^{-1})\circ\theta}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
(see Definition
2.2.5
), and let
\epsilon_{g}:=\Int(g^{-1})(\epsilon)\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Since
\epsilon
is semi-simple (Remark
3.1.2
), so is
\epsilon_{g}
.

We have
\epsilon_{g}\in I_{\Int(g)^{-1}\circ\theta}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and hence
\epsilon_{g}\in J_{b_{g}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by Proposition
2.2.6
. Also, by the same proposition,
b_{g}
is decent. It then also follows that
\epsilon_{g}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, as
J_{b_{g}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
(see §
1.4.3
). We let

\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon):=\left\{(b_{g},\epsilon_{g})\mid g\in\mathcal{UR}(\theta)\right\}.

Let
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
act on
\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon)
on the left by
h\cdot(b,\epsilon^{\prime}):=(hb\sigma(h)^{-1},h\epsilon^{\prime}h^{-1})
. Since
\mathcal{UR}(\theta)
is a
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-torsor, the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-action on
\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon)
is transitive.

3.1.5
.

Fix a positive integer
m
, and let
n=mr
. (Recall that
q=p^{r}
is the residue cardinality of
{\mathfrak{p}}
.) We will define
q^{m}
-admissible LR pairs
, which will serve to describe
\mathbb{F}_{q^{m}}
-points of the Shimura variety.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
, and let
(b,\epsilon^{\prime})\in\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon)
. Recall from §
2.2.7
and §
2.4.1
that the set
X_{p}(\phi)
is identified with the set
X_{-\mu}(b)
, where
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
. The action of
\Phi
on
X_{p}(\phi)
corresponds to the left multiplication by
\Phi_{b}:=(b\rtimes\sigma)^{r}
on
X_{-\mu}(b)
. The action of
\epsilon
on
X_{p}(\phi)
corresponds to the left multiplication by
\epsilon^{\prime}
on
X_{-\mu_{X}}(b)
. Let

X_{p}(\phi,\epsilon,q^{m}):=\left\{x\in X_{p}(\phi)\mid\epsilon x=\Phi^{m}x\right\}.

Then we have an identification

\displaystyle X_{p}(\phi,\epsilon,q^{m})\cong X_{-\mu}(b,\epsilon^{\prime},q^{m}):=\left\{x\in X_{-\mu}(b)\mid\epsilon^{\prime}x=\Phi_{b}^{m}x\right\}.

This motivates the following definition.

Definition 3.1.6
.

We say that an element
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
is
q^{m}
-admissible
, if for one (and hence every) element
(b,\epsilon^{\prime})
of
\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon)
, we have

\left\{x\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\mid\epsilon^{\prime}x=\Phi_{b}^{m}x\right\}\neq\emptyset.

We denote by
\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})
the set of
q^{m}
-admissible elements of
\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
. This subset is stable under
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugacy, and we write

\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})\rangle:=\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})/G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\text{-conjugacy}.

Lemma 3.1.7
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})
, and let
(b,\epsilon^{\prime})\in\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon)
. Then there exists
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
satisfying the following conditions.

(i)

The fractional cocharacter
t\nu_{b}
is a cocharacter of
G
defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p^{t}}
.

(ii)

We have
\epsilon^{\prime t}=p^{nt\nu_{b}}k
, for
k
lying in some conjugate of
{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
in
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.

Proof.

We have seen in §
3.1.4
that
b
is decent. Take
t
such that
b
is
t
-decent. Then condition (i) is already satisfied. Also by assumption there exists
x\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
such that

\epsilon^{\prime}x=\Phi_{b}^{m}x.

Since
\epsilon^{\prime}
commutes with
\Phi_{b}=(b\rtimes\sigma)^{r}
, we have

\epsilon^{\prime t}x=\Phi_{b}^{mt}x.

By Lemma
1.6.8
, we can replace
t
by a multiple, and assume that
\sigma^{t}x=x.
Let
s=tn=tmr
. Then

\epsilon^{\prime t}x=\Phi_{b}^{mt}x=(b\rtimes\sigma)^{s}x=b{\vphantom{b}}^{\sigma}b{\vphantom{b}}^{\sigma^{2}}b\cdots{\vphantom{b}}^{\sigma^{s-1}}bx.

Therefore

(3.1.7.1)

\displaystyle k:=(b{\vphantom{b}}^{\sigma}b{\vphantom{b}}^{\sigma^{2}}b\cdots{\vphantom{b}}^{\sigma^{s-1}}b)^{-1}\epsilon^{\prime t}\in x{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})x^{-1}.

Finally, since
b
is
t
-decent we have
k=p^{-nt\nu_{b}}{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{t}
. This proves condition (ii).
∎

Recall from §
2.2.8
that we have a homomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-group schemes
\nu(p):\mathbb D_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to Q_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

Proposition 3.1.8
.

Let
(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1}),(\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2})\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})
. Suppose
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{2}
as elements of
G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
. Then
\phi_{1}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(p)=\phi_{2}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(p)
as homomorphisms
\mathbb D_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
.

Proof.

As in §
3.1.4
, for
i=1,2
, we choose
g_{i}\in\mathcal{UR}(\phi_{i}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
, and let
(b_{i},\epsilon_{i}^{\prime})
be the element of
\cls_{p}(\phi_{i},\epsilon_{i})
associated with
g_{i}
. By Lemma
3.1.7
, we can find
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that

{\epsilon^{\prime}_{i}}^{t}=p^{nt\nu_{b_{i}}}k_{i},

where
k_{i}
lies in some
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-conjugate of
{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
, for
i=1,2
.
Since
{\epsilon^{\prime}_{i}}
commutes with
b_{i}\rtimes\sigma
, we know that
{\epsilon^{\prime}_{i}}^{t}
commutes with
nt\nu_{b_{i}}
. Also
k_{i}
lies in a bounded subgroup of
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Applying Lemma
1.6.10
to
F=\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, we see that
nt\nu_{b_{i}}
is the unique cocharacter
\nu
of
G
over
\overline{\breve{\mathbb}{Q}}_{p}
commuting with
{\epsilon_{i}^{\prime}}^{t}
such that
{\epsilon_{i}^{\prime}}^{-t}p^{\nu}
lies in a
G(\overline{\breve{\mathbb}{Q}}_{p})
-conjugate of a bounded subgroup of
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
Let
g=g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}\in G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. Then we have
\epsilon^{\prime}_{2}=\Int(g)\epsilon^{\prime}_{1}
. By the above-mentioned uniqueness of
nt\nu_{b_{i}}
with respect to
{\epsilon^{\prime}_{i}}^{t}
, we have

(3.1.8.1)

\displaystyle\nu_{b_{2}}=\Int(g)\circ\nu_{b_{1}}.

By Proposition
2.2.6
(i) we have

(3.1.8.2)

\displaystyle-\nu_{b_{i}}=\left(\Int(g_{i}^{-1})\circ\phi_{i}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}\right)^{\Delta}=\Int(g_{i}^{-1})\circ\phi_{i}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(p).

Comparing (
3.1.8.1
) with (
3.1.8.2
), we have
\phi_{1}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(p)=\phi_{2}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(p)
as desired.
∎

Recall from §
2.2.8
that we have a homomorphism of
\mathbb{R}
-group schemes
\nu_{\infty}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Q_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Lemma 3.1.9
.

Let
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be two admissible morphisms. Then
\phi_{1}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(\infty)=\phi_{2}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(\infty)
.

Proof.

By condition (ii) in Definition
2.4.2
with
v=\infty
, we know that
\phi_{i}(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty}
is conjugate to
\xi_{\infty}
. Hence
(\phi_{i}(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty})^{\Delta}=\phi_{i}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(\infty)
is conjugate to
{\xi}_{\infty}^{\Delta}
. By the definition of
\xi_{\infty}
in
[
Kis17
, §3.3.5]
,
{\xi}_{\infty}^{\Delta}
is equal to the weight cocharacter for the Shimura datum
(G,X)
, which is central in
G
(see
[
Del79
, §2.1.1]
). The lemma follows.
∎

Corollary 3.1.10
.

Let
(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1}),(\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2})\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})
. For
i=1,2
, assume that
\phi_{i}
is gg (Definition
2.6.2
), and that
\epsilon_{i}
lies in
G(\mathbb{Q})
. Then for all
g\in G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
such that
\Int(g)(\epsilon_{1})=\epsilon_{2}
, we have
\Int(g)\circ\phi_{1}^{\Delta}=\phi_{2}^{\Delta}.

Proof.

Write
H:=\left\{g\in G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})\mid\Int(g)\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{2}\right\}
. Let
g\in H
.
For all
\tau\in\Gamma
, we have
{\vphantom{g}}{}^{\tau}g\in H
, since
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in G(\mathbb{Q})
.
Applying Proposition
3.1.8
and Lemma
3.1.9
to
(\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2})
and
\Int({\vphantom{g}}^{\tau}g)(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1})
, we get

(3.1.10.1)

\displaystyle\phi_{2}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(v)=\Int({\vphantom{g}}^{\tau}g)\circ\phi_{1}^{\Delta}\circ\nu(v),\quad\text{for }v=p,\infty.

Now we apply
\tau^{-1}
to both sides of (
3.1.10.1
). As
\phi_{1}^{\Delta}
and
\phi_{2}^{\Delta}
are defined over
\mathbb{Q}
(by the gg assumption), we get

(3.1.10.2)

\displaystyle\phi_{2}^{\Delta}\circ{\vphantom{\nu}}^{\tau^{-1}}\nu(v)=\Int(g)\circ\phi_{1}^{\Delta}\circ{\vphantom{\nu}}^{\tau^{-1}}\nu(v),\quad\text{for }v=p,\infty.

By construction, for each finite Galois extension
L/\mathbb{Q}
contained in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
, the
\mathbb{Q}
-torus
Q^{L}
is split over
L
, and the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
X_{*}(Q^{L})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is generated by the
{\rm Gal}(L/\mathbb{Q})
-conjugates of the fractional cocharacters

\mathbb D_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\xrightarrow{\nu(p)}Q_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\to Q^{L}_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}

and

\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{\nu(\infty)}Q{C}\to Q^{L}_{\mathbb{C}}.

See
[
Kis17
, §3.1]
for details. Hence (
3.1.10.2
) for all
\tau
implies that
\phi_{2}^{\Delta}=\Int(g)\circ\phi_{1}^{\Delta}
.
∎

3.2.
The günstig gelegen condition

As in §
2.6.1
, for each
\tau\in\Gamma
we choose a lift
q_{\tau}\in\mathfrak{Q}^{\topo}
. For
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
, we write
I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}
for
(I_{\phi})_{\epsilon}^{0}
.

Definition 3.2.1
.

We say that an LR pair
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
is
günstig gelegen
(
to be abbreviated as gg
)
18
18

18

As in Definition
2.6.2
, this terminology comes from
[
LR87
, §5]
, but our definition is modified to suit the corrected definition of
\mathfrak{Q}
.
, if the following conditions are satisfied:

•

The embedding
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
maps
\epsilon\in I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
into
G(\mathbb{Q})
. Moreover,
\epsilon
is
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic in
G
, cf. §
1.6.1
.

•

For each
\tau\in\Gamma,
write
\phi(q_{\tau})=g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau,
with
g_{\tau}\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then
g_{\tau}
lies in
(G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\phi^{\Delta}})_{\epsilon}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})=I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
.

We denote by
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
the set of gg LR pairs.

Remark 3.2.2
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
, and let
\phi(q_{\tau})=g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau
, for
\tau\in\Gamma
. If one changes the choice of
q_{\tau}
, then
g_{\tau}
is left multiplied by a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-point of
\im(\phi^{\Delta})
, which is a central torus in
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\phi^{\Delta}}
. Hence the second condition in Definition
3.2.1
is independent of the choice of
q_{\tau}
.

3.2.3
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
. By Lemma
2.6.3
,
\phi
is gg, and the canonical
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-isomorphism
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}G_{\phi^{\Delta},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is an inner twisting between the
\mathbb{Q}
-groups
I_{\phi}
and
G_{\phi^{\Delta}}
. Moreover, in the current case this inner twisting restricts to an inner twisting between the
\mathbb{Q}
-groups
I_{\phi,\epsilon}
and
(G_{\phi^{\Delta}})_{\epsilon}
, and an inner twisting between the
\mathbb{Q}
-groups
I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}
and
(G_{\phi^{\Delta}})_{\epsilon}^{0}
.

In fact, the inner twisting between
I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}
and
(G_{\phi^{\Delta}})_{\epsilon}^{0}
can be interpreted as follows. Let
I_{0}=G_{\epsilon}^{0}\subset G
. Since
(\phi,\epsilon)
is gg,
\phi
factors as
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{I_{0}}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. We write
\phi_{I_{0}}
for
\phi
when we view it as a morphism
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{I_{0}}
. Then
\phi_{I_{0}}
is itself gg. Hence by Lemma
2.6.3
applied to
\phi_{I_{0}}
, we obtain an inner twisting between
I_{\phi_{I_{0}}}
and
(I_{0})_{(\phi_{I_{0}})^{\Delta}}=(I_{0})_{\phi^{\Delta}}
. It is easy to see that as
\mathbb{Q}
-groups we have
I_{\phi_{I_{0}}}=I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}
and
(I_{0})_{\phi^{\Delta}}=(G_{\phi^{\Delta}})_{\epsilon}^{0}
.

Definition 3.2.4
.

We write

[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
for the quotient set of
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
divided by the equivalence relation of
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugacy.
19
19

19

We caution the reader that the subset
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}\subset\mathcal{LRP}
is not stable under
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugacy.
For
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
, we denote its image in
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
by
[\phi,\epsilon]
. We denote the natural injection
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]\to\langle\mathcal{LRP}\rangle
by
\mathfrak{v}
(standing for
vergessen
).

Lemma 3.2.5
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
, and let
a=(a_{\tau})_{\tau}\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon})
. Then
(a\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
(i.e., the element
\epsilon\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
lies in the image of
I_{a\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow I_{a\phi}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\hookrightarrow G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
). Here
a\phi
is the twist of
\phi
by the cocycle
a
as in §
2.1.15
. If in addition we have
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
and
a\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})
, then
(a\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
.

Proof.

First recall that
(a\phi)^{\Delta}=\phi^{\Delta}
, and
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
and
I_{a\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
are equal as
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-subgroups of
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Write
\phi(q_{\tau})=g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau
for each
\tau\in\Gamma
. Then we have
(a\phi)(q_{\tau})=a_{\tau}g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau
. Since
\epsilon\in I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
, we have
g_{\tau}{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}\epsilon g_{\tau}^{-1}=\epsilon.
Since
\epsilon
commutes with
a_{\tau}
, we have

a_{\tau}g_{\tau}{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}\epsilon g_{\tau}^{-1}a_{\tau}^{-1}=\epsilon,

which means that
\epsilon\in I_{a\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
. Thus we have shown that
(a\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
.

To show the second statement, we need to check that
a_{\tau}g_{\tau}\in I_{a\phi,\epsilon}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
for all
\tau
. But both
g_{\tau}
and
a_{\tau}
lie in
I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, and we have
I_{a\phi,\epsilon}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})=I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. The desired statement follows.
∎

Lemma 3.2.6
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
. Let
a,b\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon})
. Then
a,b
are cohomologous in
I_{\phi,\epsilon}
if and only if
\langle a\phi,\epsilon\rangle=\langle b\phi,\epsilon\rangle
.

Proof.

Write
\phi(q_{\tau})=g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau
, for each
\tau\in\Gamma
. If
a,b
are cohomologous in
I_{\phi,\epsilon}
, then there exists
u\in I_{\phi,\epsilon}({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
such that

(3.2.6.1)

\displaystyle a_{\tau}=u^{-1}b_{\tau}g_{\tau}{\vphantom{u}}^{\tau}ug_{\tau}^{-1},\quad\forall\tau\in\Gamma.

Here
{\vphantom{\tau}}{}^{\tau}u
denotes the action of
\tau
on
u
viewed as in
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then

(a\phi)(q_{\tau})=a_{\tau}g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau=u^{-1}b_{\tau}g_{\tau}{\vphantom{u}}^{\tau}u\rtimes\tau=\Int(u^{-1})\circ(b\phi)(q_{\tau}).

Hence we have
\Int(u^{-1})\circ(b\phi)=a\phi
as they already agree on the kernel. Also
\Int(u^{-1})\epsilon=\epsilon.
Therefore
(a\phi,\epsilon)=\Int(u^{-1})(b\phi,\epsilon)
, and so
\langle a\phi,\epsilon\rangle=\langle b\phi,\epsilon\rangle
.

Conversely, assume that
\langle a\phi,\epsilon\rangle=\langle b\phi,\epsilon\rangle
. Then there exists
u\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
(a\phi,\epsilon)=\Int(u^{-1})(b\phi,\epsilon)
. Since
(a\phi)^{\Delta}=(b\phi)^{\Delta}
, we have
u\in I_{\phi,\epsilon}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Now the relation
\Int(u^{-1})\circ(b\phi)=a\phi
is equivalent to (
3.2.6.1
), which shows that
a
and
b
are cohomologous.
∎

3.2.7
.

Let
(\psi,\delta)\in\mathcal{LRP}
and
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
.
In view of Lemma
3.2.5
and Lemma
3.2.6
, we have well-defined maps

\displaystyle Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\psi,\delta})\to\mathcal{LRP},\quad a\mapsto(a\psi,\delta)

and

\displaystyle Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})\to\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}},\quad a\mapsto(a\phi,\epsilon),

which induce maps

\iota_{\psi,\delta}:\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\psi,\delta})\longrightarrow\langle\mathcal{LRP}\rangle

and

\eta_{\phi,\epsilon}:\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})\longrightarrow[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]

respectively. Moreover, by Lemma
3.2.6
the map
\iota_{\psi,\delta}
is injective.

Lemma 3.2.8
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
. The subset
\im(\eta_{\phi,\epsilon})
of
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
depends only on
[\phi,\epsilon]\in[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
.

Proof.

Suppose we have
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
such that
[\phi,\epsilon]=[\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}]
. Then
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})=\Int(g)(\phi,\epsilon)
for some
g\in G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
. We have an isomorphism
\Int(g):I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}\to I_{\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}^{0}
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. This induces a bijection

g_{*}:\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I^{0}_{\phi,\epsilon})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I^{0}_{\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}),

and we have
\eta_{\phi,\epsilon}=\eta_{\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}\circ g_{*}.
∎

Definition 3.2.9
.

For
x=[\phi,\epsilon]\in[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
, we define the subset

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{x}:=\im(\eta_{\phi,\epsilon})\subset[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}].

This is well defined by Lemma
3.2.8
.

Lemma 3.2.10
.

Let
x,y\in[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
. Then
y\in\mathcal{C}_{x}
if and only if
x\in\mathcal{C}_{y}
. In particular, subsets of the form
\mathcal{C}_{x}
form a partition of
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
.

Proof.

Let
x=[\phi,\epsilon]\in[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
. Assume that
y\in\mathcal{C}_{x}
. Then there exists
a=(a_{\tau})_{\tau}\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})
such that
y=[a\phi,\epsilon]
. We identify
I_{\phi}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
with
I_{a\phi}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. For each
\tau\in\Gamma
, let
\widehat{\tau}(\cdot)
(resp.
\check{\tau}(\cdot)
) denote the action of
\tau
on
I_{\phi}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
with respect to the
\mathbb{Q}
-structure
I_{\phi}
(resp.
I_{a\phi}
). We have

\check{\tau}(\cdot)=a_{\tau}\widehat{\tau}(\cdot)a_{\tau}^{-1}.

For each
\tau
, let
b_{\tau}:=a_{\tau}^{-1}\in I_{a\phi}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then for
\sigma,\tau\in\Gamma
we have

b_{\sigma\tau}=(a_{\sigma}\widehat{\sigma}(a_{\tau}))^{-1}=(\check{\sigma}(a_{\tau})a_{\sigma})^{-1}=b_{\sigma}\check{\sigma}(b_{\tau}),

showing that
(b_{\tau})_{\tau}\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{a\phi})
. Clearly we have
\phi=b(a\phi)
, and so
x\in\mathcal{C}_{y}
.
∎

Lemma 3.2.11
.

Let
(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1}),(\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
be elements having the same image in
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}],
and let
g\in G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
be such that
\Int(g)(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1})=(\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2})
(which exists by the first assumption). Then we have
g~{\vphantom{g}}^{\tau}g^{-1}\in G_{\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
for all
\tau\in\Gamma
. In particular,
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in G(\mathbb{Q})
are stably conjugate.

Proof.

For
i=1,2
, we write
\phi_{i}(q_{\tau})=g_{i,\tau}\rtimes\tau
. Then

g_{2,\tau}=gg_{1,\tau}{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}g^{-1}=gg_{1,\tau}g^{-1}g~{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}g^{-1},

and hence

g~{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}g^{-1}=(gg_{1,\tau}g^{-1})^{-1}g_{2,\tau}.

Since
(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1}),(\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
, we have
g_{1,\tau}\in G_{\epsilon_{1}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and
g_{2,\tau}\in G_{\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. It follows that
gg_{1,\tau}g^{-1}\in G_{\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Hence
g~{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}g^{-1}\in G_{\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, as desired.
∎

Definition 3.2.12
.

As in §
1.8.7
, let
\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)
be the set of stable conjugacy classes of semi-simple,
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic elements of
G(\mathbb{Q})
. We define the
stable conjugacy class map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{scc}:[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]\longrightarrow\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G),

sending
[\phi,\epsilon]
to the stable conjugacy class of
\epsilon
. This is well defined by Lemma
3.2.11
.

Definition 3.2.13
.

Fix
m
as in §
3.1.5
. We set

\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}:=\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}\cap\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa},\quad\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m}):=\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}\cap\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m}),

[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}]:=\mathfrak{v}^{-1}(\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}\rangle),\quad[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]:=\mathfrak{v}^{-1}(\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})\rangle).

3.2.14
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})
. Then
\phi
is gg (see §
3.2.3
), and
\epsilon\in G(\mathbb{Q})
. Hence by Corollary
3.1.10
the
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-inclusion
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
induces a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-isomorphism
I_{\phi,\epsilon,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}G_{\epsilon,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
. By this fact and by the discussion in §
3.2.3
, we obtain canonical inner twistings

\displaystyle(I^{0}_{\phi,\epsilon})_{{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}}

\displaystyle(G^{0}_{\epsilon})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},

\displaystyle(I_{\phi,\epsilon})_{{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}}

\displaystyle(G_{\epsilon})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}.

Lemma 3.2.15
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}
. Assume that
I_{\phi}
contains a
G
-rational maximal torus
T
(see Definition
2.6.4
) such that
\epsilon\in T(\mathbb{Q})
and such that
T/Z_{G}
is anisotropic over
\mathbb{R}
. Then
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
.

Proof.

The assumptions imply that
\epsilon
is a semi-simple
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic element of
G(\mathbb{Q})
. By the proof of Lemma
2.6.6
, if we write
\phi(q_{\tau})=g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau
, then
g_{\tau}
lies in
T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
for each
\tau\in\Gamma
. Note that
T\subset I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}
. Hence
g_{\tau}
lies in
I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}(\mathbb{Q})
as desired. ∎

Lemma 3.2.16
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
. Then there exists
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}):=\Int(g)(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
satisfies the following condition: The group
I_{\phi^{\prime}}
contains a
G
-rational maximal torus
T^{\prime}
such that
\epsilon^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q})
and such that
T^{\prime}/Z_{G}
is anisotropic over
\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, in this case we have
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
.

Proof.

Choose a maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
\epsilon\in T(\mathbb{Q})
. (This is possible since
\epsilon
is semi-simple; see Remark
3.1.2
.) Since
\phi
is admissible, Proposition
2.6.9
implies that there exists
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\Int(g)(T)
is a
G
-rational maximal torus in
I_{\Int(g)\circ\phi}
. We write
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})
for
\Int(g)(\phi,\epsilon)
, and write
T^{\prime}
for
\Int(g)(T)
. Then
T^{\prime}
is a
G
-rational maximal torus in
I_{\phi^{\prime}}
, and
\epsilon^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q})
. Since
\phi^{\prime}
is admissible, we know that
T^{\prime}/Z_{G}
is anisotropic over
\mathbb{R}
by Lemma
2.6.7
. Finally, we have
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
by Lemma
3.2.15
.
∎

Corollary 3.2.17
.

The injection
