\mathfrak{v}:[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]\to\langle\mathcal{LRP}\rangle
restricts to bijections
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}]\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}\rangle
and
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})\rangle
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.2.16
, every
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugacy class in
{\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}}
contains an element of
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
. Hence
\mathfrak{v}
restricts to a bijection
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}]\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}\rangle
. Since
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]
is by definition
\mathfrak{v}^{-1}(\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})\rangle)
, we see that
\mathfrak{v}
also restricts to a bijection
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]\langle\mathcal{LRP}_{\adm}(q^{m})\rangle
. ∎

3.2.18
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}.
As
I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
I_{\phi}
, we use the canonical inner transfer datum from
I_{\phi}
to
G
as in §
2.6.11
to define
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})
; see §
1.2.5
.

Proposition 3.2.19
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}.

Then
\eta_{\phi,\epsilon}^{-1}([\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}])=\Sha^{\infty}_{G}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}).

Proof.

By Proposition
2.6.12
, an element
\beta\in\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})
lies in
\eta_{\phi,\epsilon}^{-1}([\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}])
if and only if the image of
\beta
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})
lies in
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})
. Thus we only need to check that the map
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi})
has trivial kernel. Note that
(I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}){R}
and
(I_{\phi})_{\mathbb{R}}
are both reductive groups over
\mathbb{R}
, and their centers both contain
Z_{G,\mathbb{R}}
. The desired statement then follows from Lemma
2.6.7
and
[
Kis17
, Lem. 4.4.5]
. ∎

Proposition 3.2.20
.

Let
x\in[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]
, and let
e=\mathfrak{scc}(x)\in\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)
. Then

\mathcal{C}_{x}\cap[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]=\mathfrak{scc}^{-1}(e)\cap[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})].

Proof.

We only need to show the containment
\mathfrak{scc}^{-1}(e)\cap[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]\subset\mathcal{C}_{x}
. Let
y\in\mathfrak{scc}^{-1}(e)\cap[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]
be arbitrary. Write
x=[\phi_{0},\epsilon_{0}],y=[\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1}]
, for some
(\phi_{i},\epsilon_{i})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})
,
i=0,1
. Since
\mathfrak{scc}(x)=\mathfrak{scc}(y)
, there exists
g\in G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
such that

(3.2.20.1)

\displaystyle\Int(g)\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{0},

(3.2.20.2)

\displaystyle g{\vphantom{g}}^{\tau}g^{-1}

\displaystyle\in G_{\epsilon_{0}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}),\quad\forall\tau\in\Gamma.

Let
\phi^{\prime}=\Int(g)\circ\phi_{1}
. By (
3.2.20.1
), we have
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon_{0})\in\mathcal{LRP}
, and
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon_{0})
is
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugate to
(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1})
. By Corollary
3.1.10
applied to the relation (
3.2.20.1
), we have
\phi^{\prime\Delta}=\phi_{0}^{\Delta}
. By Lemma
2.1.16
we have
\phi^{\prime}=a\phi_{0}
for some
a\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{0}})
. For
i=0,1
, we write
\phi_{i}(q_{\tau})=g^{(i)}_{\tau}\rtimes\tau.

Then

(3.2.20.3)

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}(q_{\tau})=a_{\tau}g^{(0)}_{\tau}\rtimes\tau.

By the definition of
\phi^{\prime}
we have

(3.2.20.4)

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}(q_{\tau})=\Int(g)\circ\phi_{1}(q_{\tau})=g(g^{(1)}_{\tau}\rtimes\tau)g^{-1}=gg^{(1)}_{\tau}{\vphantom{g}}{}^{\tau}g^{-1}\rtimes\tau.

Comparing (
3.2.20.3
) and (
3.2.20.4
), we have

(3.2.20.5)

\displaystyle a_{\tau}=gg^{(1)}_{\tau}{\vphantom{g}}{}^{\tau}g^{-1}(g^{(0)}_{\tau})^{-1}=\Int(g)(g_{\tau}^{(1)})\cdot(g{\vphantom{g}}^{\tau}g^{-1})\cdot(g^{(0)}_{\tau})^{-1}.

Since
(\phi_{i},\epsilon_{i})
is gg, we have
g_{\tau}^{(i)}\in G_{\epsilon_{i}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Hence
\Int(g)(g_{\tau}^{(1)})
and
(g^{(0)}_{\tau})^{-1}
both lie in
G_{\epsilon_{0}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Thus by (
3.2.20.2
) and (
3.2.20.5
), we have
a_{\tau}\in G_{\epsilon_{0}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. By the discussion in §
3.2.14
, we have
(G_{\epsilon_{0}}^{0})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}=(I_{\phi_{0},\epsilon_{0}}^{0})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
, since
(\phi_{0},\epsilon_{0})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})
. Hence
a=(a_{\tau})
is a cocycle in
Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{0},\epsilon_{0}}^{0})
. It follows from Lemma
3.2.5
that the pair
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon_{0})=(a\phi_{0},\epsilon_{0})
is gg. Since this pair is
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
-conjugate to
(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1})
, we have
y=[\phi^{\prime},\epsilon_{0}]=[a\phi_{0},\epsilon_{0}]\in\im(\eta_{\phi_{0},\epsilon_{0}})=\mathcal{C}_{x}
.
∎

3.3.
Admissible morphisms and maximal tori

We first explain a result which considerably strengthens Corollary
2.6.10
.

Definition 3.3.1
.

By a
special point datum
for
(G,X)
, we mean a triple
(T,i,h)
, where
T
is a torus over
\mathbb{Q}
,
i:T\to G
is an injective
\mathbb{Q}
-homomorphism whose image is a maximal torus in
G
, and
h:\mathbb S\to T_{\mathbb{R}}
is an
\mathbb{R}
-homomorphism such that
i\circ h\in X
. We denote the set of special point data by
\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
.

Definition 3.3.2
.

Let
(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
. Let
\mu_{h}\in X_{*}(T)
be the Hodge cocharacter associated with
h
. We denote by
\phi(T,i,h)
the composite morphism

\mathfrak{Q}\xrightarrow{\Psi_{T,\mu_{h}}}\mathfrak{G}_{T}\xrightarrow{i}\mathfrak{G}_{G}

in
{\mathrm{pro}\text{-}\mathcal{G}rb}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. (See §
2.2.9
for
\Psi_{T,\mu_{h}}
.)

Theorem 3.3.3

(
[
Kis17
, Lem. 3.5.8, Thm. 3.5.11]
)
.

A morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
is admissible if and only if there exists
(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
such that
\phi
is conjugate to
\phi(T,i,h)
.

Remark 3.3.4
.

Let
(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
, and let
\phi=\phi(T,i,h)
. Then
i(T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
is contained in
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
(when they are both viewed as subgroups of
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
), and the inclusion
T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. In other words,
T
is naturally a
G
-rational maximal torus in
I_{\phi}
. By Lemma
2.6.6
,
\phi
is gg. With this observation, we see that Theorem
3.3.3
strengthens Corollary
2.6.10
.

3.3.5
.

Our next goal is to prove a more precise version of the “only if” direction in Theorem
3.3.3
. We introduce some general notation. For a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-torus
T
and a cocharacter
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)
, we write
\bar{\lambda}^{T}
, or simply
\bar{\lambda}
, for

[L:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]^{-1}\sum_{\tau\in{\rm Gal}(L/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\tau(\lambda)\in X_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q},

where
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is any finite Galois extension over which
\lambda
is defined. For an unramified reductive group
M
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we write

\displaystyle w_{M}:M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\longrightarrow\pi_{1}(M)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}=\pi_{1}(M)

for the Kottwitz homomorphism associated with the
p
-adic valuation on
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, as in §
1.3
and §
1.4.2
. (Here
\Gamma_{p,0}
acts trivially on
\pi_{1}(M)
since
M
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.)

We fix
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
as in §
2.4.1
. For each
b\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, we define
X_{-\mu}(b)
as in §
2.2.7
.

Lemma 3.3.6
.

Let
T\subset G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
be a maximal torus over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
b\in T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\subset G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
be such that
X_{-\mu}(b)
is non-empty. Then there exists
\mu_{T}\in X_{*}(T)
which is
G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
-conjugate to
\mu
and such that
\nu_{b}
is equal to
-\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T}
as elements of
X_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

This is proved by Langlands–Rapoport, when they prove
[
LR87
, Lem. 5.11]
. We recall the argument with the suitable changes in notation.

First note that
\nu_{b}
is a fractional cocharacter of
T
defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(as this holds for arbitrary
b\in T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
). Let
M
be the centralizer in
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
of the maximal
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-split subtorus of
T
. Then
M
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Levi subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
containing
T
, and
\nu_{b}
factors through the center of
M
. Up to conjugating
T
and
b
by an element of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we may assume that
M
contains a maximal torus
T^{\prime}
that is the centralizer of (the generic fiber of) a maximal
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-split torus in
{\mathcal{G}}
. In particular, there is a reductive model
{\mathcal{M}}
of
M
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that the embedding
M\hookrightarrow G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
extends to
{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}
. Without loss of generality, we may also assume that
\mu
is a cocharacter of
T^{\prime}
defined over
E_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathbb{Q}_{p^{r}}
.

Let
\Omega
(resp.
\Omega_{M}
) denote the absolute Weyl group of
G
(resp.
M
). The Cartan decompositions give rise to maps

\displaystyle c_{{\mathcal{G}}}:

\displaystyle~G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\backslash G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\Omega\backslash X_{*}(T^{\prime}),

\displaystyle c_{{\mathcal{M}}}:

\displaystyle~M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\backslash M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{M}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\Omega_{M}\backslash X_{*}(T^{\prime}).

These maps lift the Kottwitz homomorphisms
w_{G}
and
w_{M}
respectively, cf. Corollary
1.3.15
.

Now by the assumption that
X_{-\mu}(b)\neq\emptyset
, there exists
x\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
such that

c_{{\mathcal{G}}}(x^{-1}b\sigma(x))=\Omega\cdot(-\mu).

From this, it is shown on p. 178 of
[
LR87
]
that there exists
m\in M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
such that

(3.3.6.1)

\displaystyle c_{{\mathcal{M}}}(m^{-1}b\sigma(m))=\Omega_{M}\cdot(-\mu).

(The argument uses the Iwasawa decomposition and the fact that
\mu
is minuscule, cf. also the proof of
[
Kis17
, Lem. 2.2.2]
.)

We take the desired
\mu_{T}
to be any element of
X_{*}(T)
that is conjugate to
\mu\in X_{*}(T^{\prime})
by
M(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. It remains to check that
\nu_{b}=-\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T}
. Note that
\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T}
factors through the maximal
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-split subtorus of
T
, and is therefore central in
M
. We have seen that
\nu_{b}
is also central in
M
. Hence in order to check
\nu_{b}=-\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T}
, it suffices to check that
\nu_{b}
and
-\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T}
have equal image in
\pi_{1}(M)_{\mathbb{Q}}:=\pi_{1}(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
. Without loss of generality, we may replace
b
by a
\sigma
-conjugate in
T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, and assume that
b
is decent in
T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
(see §
1.4.1
). Then for sufficiently divisible
n
we have

b\sigma(b)\cdots{\sigma^{n-1}}(b)=p^{n\nu_{b}}.

For each
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
, we denote its image in
\pi_{1}(M)_{\mathbb{Q}}
by
[\lambda]
.
We compute

\displaystyle[n\nu_{b}]=w_{M}(p^{n\nu_{b}})

\displaystyle=w_{M}(b\sigma(b)\cdots\sigma^{n-1}(b))

\displaystyle=w_{M}(m)-w_{M}(\sigma^{n}(m))-[\mu+\sigma(\mu)+\cdots+\sigma^{n-1}(\mu)],

where the last equality follows from (
3.3.6.1
). (Note that the action of
\sigma
on
X_{*}(T^{\prime})
is indeed well defined as
T^{\prime}
is unramified.) We can choose
n
divisible enough such that the coset
m{\mathcal{M}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\in M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{M}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
is fixed by
\sigma^{n}
(see Lemma
1.6.8
), and such that
T^{\prime}
splits over
\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}
. Then the above relation becomes

[\nu_{b}]=-[\bar{\mu}^{T^{\prime}}].

Finally,
[\bar{\mu}^{T^{\prime}}]
is equal to the image of
\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T}
in
\pi_{1}(M)_{\mathbb{Q}}
. This is because
\mu
and
\mu_{T}
have the same image in
\pi_{1}(M)
, and the Galois actions on both
X_{*}(T)
and
X_{*}(T^{\prime})
are compatible with that on
\pi_{1}(M)
. Thus we conclude that
\nu_{b}
and
-\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T}
have the same image in
\pi_{1}(M){Q}
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 3.3.7
.

Let
T\subset G
be a maximal torus over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
T_{\mathbb{R}}
is elliptic in
G_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
i
denote the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow G
. Let
\mu_{T}\in X_{*}(T)
be such that
i\circ\mu_{T}
lies in
\bbmu_{X}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then there exist
u\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and an
\mathbb{R}
-homomorphism
h:\mathbb S\to T_{\mathbb{R}}
, satisfying the following conditions.

(i)

i^{\prime}:=\Int(u)\circ i:T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.

(ii)

\mu_{T}=\mu_{h}
.

(iii)

i^{\prime}\circ h\in X
.

Proof.

This is proved by Langlands–Rapoport, when they prove
[
LR87
, Lem. 5.12]
. In fact, in that lemma
\mu_{T}
is assumed to be of the form
\omega\mu_{h_{0}}
, where
\omega
is an element of the absolute Weyl group of
(G,T)
, and
h_{0}
is an
\mathbb{R}
-homomorphism
\mathbb S\to T_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
i\circ h_{0}\in X
. We explain why our hypothesis implies that setting. Since
T_{\mathbb{R}}
is elliptic in
G_{\mathbb{R}}
, there indeed exists an
\mathbb{R}
-homomorphism
h_{0}:\mathbb S\to T_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
i\circ h_{0}\in X
. Then
i\circ\mu_{h_{0}}
and
i\circ\mu_{T}
are conjugate by
G(\mathbb{C})
, and it follows that
\mu=\omega\mu_{h_{0}}
for some
\omega
in the absolute Weyl group.
∎

3.3.8
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism. Let
T\subset I_{\phi}
be a maximal torus over
\mathbb{Q}
. Since
(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})^{\Delta}
is a central fractional cocharacter of
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
, it can be viewed as an element of
X_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. We say that a cocharacter
\mu_{T}\in X_{*}(T)
is
\phi
-admissible
, if the composition
\mathbb{G}_{m,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\xrightarrow{\mu_{T}}T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is a cocharacter in
\bbmu_{X}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, and if
\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}=(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})^{\Delta}
as elements of
X_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.

Theorem 3.3.9
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism, and let
T\subset I_{\phi}
be a maximal torus over
\mathbb{Q}
. The following statements hold.

(i)

There exists
\mu_{T}\in X_{*}(T)
that is
\phi
-admissible in the sense of §
3.3.8
.

(ii)

Let
\mu_{T}\in X_{*}(T)
be as in (i). Then there exists a special point datum of the form
(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
, satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

We have
\mu_{T}=\mu_{h}
.

(b)

There exists
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\Int(g)\circ\phi=\phi(T,i,h)
, and such that the embedding
i:T\to G
equals the composition

T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\xrightarrow{\Int(g)}I_{\phi(T,i,h),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}.

(Here the first two maps are defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, and the second map is an isomorphism.)

Proof.

This essentially follows from the proof of
[
LR87
, Satz 5.3]
. We reproduce the argument for the convenience of the reader, and we remove the assumption in
loc. cit.
that
G_{\mathrm{der}}
is simply connected.

(i)
By Proposition
2.6.9
, we may assume that
T
is a
G
-rational maximal torus in
I_{\phi}
. By Lemma
2.6.6
,
\phi
factors through
\mathfrak{G}_{T}\subset\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. Hence the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
\mathcal{UR}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
contains elements of
T(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. We choose
t\in\mathcal{UR}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})\cap T(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
, and let
b=b_{\Int(t^{-1})\circ\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p}}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
(see Definition
2.2.5
). Then
b\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Since
\phi
is admissible, we have
X_{-\mu}(b)\neq\emptyset
for
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
. Hence by Lemma
3.3.6
we find
\mu_{T}\in X_{*}(T)
such that
\overline{\mu_{T}}^{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}=-\nu_{b}
. Finally, by Proposition
2.2.6
(i), we have
-\nu_{b}=(\Int(t)^{-1}\circ\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})^{\Delta}
, which equals
(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})^{\Delta}
. Hence
\mu_{T}
is
\phi
-admissible.

(ii)
Again by Proposition
2.6.9
, we may assume that
T
is a
G
-rational maximal torus in
I_{\phi}
. We denote by
i_{0}
the inclusion
T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
, which is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. By Lemma
2.6.7
,
i_{0}(T_{\mathbb{R}})
is an elliptic maximal torus in
G_{\mathbb{R}}
. Applying Lemma
3.3.7
, we find
u\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and an
\mathbb{R}
-homomorphism
h:\mathbb S\to T_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\Int(u)\circ i_{0}:T\to G
is still defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, such that
\mu_{T}=\mu_{h}
, and such that
\Int(u)\circ i_{0}\circ h\in X
.

We may replace
\phi
and
i_{0}
by
\Int(u)\circ\phi
and
\Int(u)\circ i_{0}
respectively, and assume that
u=1
. This does not change the property that
T
is
G
-rational, and we now have
(T,i_{0},h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
such that
\mu_{T}=\mu_{h}
.

By Lemma
2.6.6
, we may factorize
\phi
uniquely as
\phi=i_{0}\circ\phi_{T}
, where
\phi_{T}
is a morphism
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{T}
. Analogously,
\phi(T,i_{0},h)
factors as
i_{0}\circ\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}
by its definition. We claim that
\phi_{T}^{\Delta}=\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}^{\Delta}
, or equivalently
\phi^{\Delta}=\phi(T,i_{0},h)^{\Delta}
. In fact, the
\phi
-admissibility of
\mu_{T}
implies that

(3.3.9.1)

\displaystyle(\phi_{T}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})^{\Delta}=(\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})^{\Delta}

(see
[
Kis17
, (3.1.11)]
). Also, since
\phi
and
\phi(T,i_{0},h)
are both admissible (the former by assumption and the latter by the “if” direction in Theorem
3.3.3
), we have

(3.3.9.2)

\displaystyle(\phi_{T}(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty})^{\Delta}=(\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}(\infty)\circ\zeta_{\infty})^{\Delta}

by Lemma
3.1.9
. Since
\phi_{T}^{\Delta}
and
\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}^{\Delta}
are both homomorphisms
\mathfrak{Q}^{\Delta}\to T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
(for instance by Lemma
2.6.6
), the above relations (
3.3.9.1
) and (
3.3.9.2
) imply that
\phi_{T}^{\Delta}=\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}^{\Delta}
as desired (cf. the discussion on the tori
Q^{L}
in the proof of Corollary
3.1.10
).

By the claim above and by Lemma
2.1.16
, we have
\phi_{T}=a\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}
, for some element
a\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}})=Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T)
. By the admissibility of
\phi
and
\phi(T,i_{0},h)
and by Proposition
2.6.12
, the image of
a
under

Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T)\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T)\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi(T,i_{0},h)})

lies in
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi(T,i_{0},h)})
. By Lemma
2.6.7
and
[
Kis17
, Lem. 4.4.5]
, the map
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},T)\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},I_{\phi(T,i_{0},h)})
has trivial kernel. Thus the class of
a
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T)
lies in the kernel of the map
\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T)\to\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},G)
induced by
i_{0}:T\to G
. It follows that there exists
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, satisfying:

(3.3.9.3)

\displaystyle i_{0}(a_{\tau})=g^{-1}{\vphantom{\tau}}{}^{\tau}g,\quad\forall\tau\in\Gamma;

(3.3.9.4)

\displaystyle g\in G(\mathbb{R})\cdot i_{0}(T(\mathbb{C}))\subset G(\mathbb{C}).

For each
\tau\in\Gamma
, we write
\phi(T,i_{0},h)(q_{\tau})=g_{\tau}\rtimes\tau
, and
\phi(q_{\tau})=g^{\prime}_{\tau}\rtimes\tau
. (Here
q_{\tau}\in\mathfrak{Q}
is a lift of
\tau
, as always).
Because
i_{0}(a_{\tau})
and
g_{\tau}
both lie in
i_{0}(T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}))
and commute with each other, we have
g^{\prime}_{\tau}=i_{0}(a_{\tau})g_{\tau}=g_{\tau}i_{0}(a_{\tau})=g_{\tau}g^{-1}{\vphantom{\tau}}{}^{\tau}g
. Then we have

(3.3.9.5)

\displaystyle(\Int(g)\circ\phi)(q_{\tau})=gg^{\prime}_{\tau}{\vphantom{\tau}}{}^{\tau}g^{-1}\rtimes\tau=gg_{\tau}g^{-1}.

Now let
i:=\Int(g)\circ i_{0}
. By (
3.3.9.3
),
i
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-embedding
T\to G
. By (
3.3.9.4
),
i\circ h\in X
. In particular,
(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
. By (
3.3.9.5
), we have
(\Int(g)\circ\phi)(q_{\tau})=\phi(T,i,h)(q_{\tau})
. Since we also have
(\Int(g)\circ\phi)^{\Delta}=\phi(T,i,h)^{\Delta}
(because
\phi_{T}^{\Delta}=\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}^{\Delta}
), we have
\Int(g)\circ\phi=\phi(T,i,h)
. Thus
(T,i,h)
and
g
are the desired elements. ∎

3.4.
Admissible stable conjugacy classes

The goal of this subsection is to construct certain elements of the image of
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]\subset[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]
under the map
\mathfrak{scc}:[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]\to\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)
in Definition
3.2.12
. For
M
a reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we write
w_{M}:M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to\pi_{1}(M)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
for the Kottwitz homomorphism, as in §
1.4.2
. For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we denote by
\B^{(k)}(M)
the set of
\sigma^{k}
-conjugacy classes in
M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.

Lemma 3.4.1
.

Let
M
be an unramified reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
x\in M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
be in the kernel of
w_{M}
. For all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, the class of
x
in
\B^{(k)}(M)
is in the image of the natural map
\B^{(k)}(M_{\mathrm{sc}})\to\B^{(k)}(M)
.

Proof.

Write
\tau:=\sigma^{k}
. We first assume that
M_{\mathrm{der}}=M_{\mathrm{sc}}
. Consider
M^{\ab}=M/M_{\mathrm{der}}
, which is an unramified torus over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The image
\bar{x}
of
x
in
M^{\ab}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is in the kernel of
w_{M^{\ab}}
, and this kernel is the unique parahoric subgroup of
M^{\ab}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
(which is hyperspecial in this case). By Greenberg’s theorem
[
Gre63
, Prop. 3]
, we have
\bar{x}=\bar{c}\cdot{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}\bar{c}^{-1}
for some
\bar{c}\in M^{\ab}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Let
c\in M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
be a lift of
\bar{c}
, which exists because
\coh^{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},M_{\mathrm{der}})
is trivial by Steinberg’s theorem. Then
c^{-1}x{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}c\in M_{\mathrm{der}}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, which means that the class of
x
in
\B^{(k)}(M)
comes from
\B^{(k)}(M_{\mathrm{der}})=\B^{(k)}(M_{\mathrm{sc}})
.

In the general case, as in
[
Kot84a
, §3]
we take a
z
-extension

\displaystyle 1\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow H\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 1

over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, where
H
is an unramified reductive group with simply connected derived subgroup, and
Z
is an unramified induced torus contained in the center of
H
. Then we have a commutative diagram with exact rows:

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{w_{Z}}
\textstyle{H(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{w_{H}}
\textstyle{M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{w_{M}}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}

By
[
Kot97
, §7]
, the vertical arrows in the above diagram are surjective. Since
w_{M}(x)=0
, there exists
h\in\ker(w_{H})\subset H(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
that maps to
x\in M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Applying the first part of the proof to
H
, we know that the class of
h
in
\B^{(k)}(H)
comes from
\B^{(k)}(H_{\mathrm{sc}})
. But the composite map
H_{\mathrm{sc}}\to H\to M
factors through
M_{\mathrm{sc}}
. The lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 3.4.2
.

Let
M
be an unramified reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
T\subset M
be an elliptic maximal torus. Let
x\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be such that
w_{M}(x)=0\in\pi_{1}(M)
. Then some integer power of
x
lies in a compact subgroup of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}
be the connected Néron model of
T
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then the kernel of
w_{T}:T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
is
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
; see
[
Rap05
, Rmk. 2.2 (iii)]
. If
w_{T}(x^{k})=0
for some integer
k
, then
x^{k}
lies in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\cap T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})={\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, which is a compact subgroup of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Hence it remains to show that
w_{T}(x^{k})=0
for some
k
. We know that
w_{T}
maps
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
into the group of
\sigma
-invariants
(X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}})^{\sigma}
. It remains to show that the natural map

(X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}})^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}\to\pi_{1}(M)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}=\pi_{1}(M)

has torsion kernel. For this, let
A
be the maximal
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-split subtorus of
Z_{M}
. Since
T
is elliptic in
M
,
A
is also the maximal
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-split subtorus of
T
. In particular
X_{*}(A)
is identified with
X_{*}(T)^{\Gamma_{p}}
. The inclusion map
X_{*}(A)\cong X_{*}(T)^{\Gamma_{p}}\hookrightarrow X_{*}(T)
induces an isomorphism

X_{*}(A)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}})^{\sigma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}.

(The inverse map is induced by taking average over
\Gamma_{p,0}
-orbits in
X_{*}(T)
.) Thus we reduce to showing that the natural map
X_{*}(A)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\pi_{1}(M)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is injective, but this is clear.
∎

Definition 3.4.3
.

For
\epsilon\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{\mathrm{ss}}
, we let
M(\epsilon)
be the Levi subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
that is the centralizer of the maximal
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-split subtorus of the center of
G_{\epsilon}^{0}
. (Equivalently,
M(\epsilon)
is the smallest Levi subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
containing
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p},\epsilon}^{0}
.)

Definition 3.4.4
.

Let
\epsilon\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{\mathrm{ss}}
, and let
M:=M(\epsilon)
. Let
n=mr
as in §
3.1.5
. We say that
\epsilon
is
p^{n}
-admissible
, if there exists a cocharacter
\mu_{M}
of
M_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}}
satisfying the following conditions.

•

\mu_{M}\in\bbmu_{X}(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
. (Here
\bbmu_{X}(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
is well defined since
E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}=\mathbb{Q}_{p^{r}}\subset\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}
.)

•

We have

(3.4.4.1)

\displaystyle w_{M}(\epsilon)=-\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sigma^{i}[\mu_{M}]^{M}\in\pi_{1}(M),

where
[\mu_{M}]^{M}
denotes the image of
\mu_{M}
in
\pi_{1}(M)
.

The following lemma generalizes
[
LR87
, Lem. 5.17]
to the case where
G_{\mathrm{der}}
is not necessarily simply connected.

Lemma 3.4.5
.

The set of
p^{n}
-admissible elements in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{\mathrm{ss}}
is invariant under stable conjugacy over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Proof.

Evidently this set is invariant under
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-conjugacy. Now let
\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{\mathrm{ss}}
be stably conjugate and suppose that
\epsilon
is
p^{n}
-admissible. We show that
\epsilon^{\prime}
is
p^{n}
-admissible. Let
M=M(\epsilon)
. Since
M
is a Levi subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, the inclusion
M\subset G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
induces an injection
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},M)\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},G)
, cf.
[
Hai09
, §4.1]
. Also note that
G_{\epsilon}^{0}=M_{\epsilon}^{0}
. Hence we have a natural bijection
\mathfrak{D}(M_{\epsilon}^{0},M;\mathbb{Q}_{p})\mathfrak{D}(G_{\epsilon}^{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. It follows that we have a natural surjection from the set of
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-conjugacy classes in the stable conjugacy class of
\epsilon
in
M
onto the set of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-conjugacy classes in the stable conjugacy class of
\epsilon
in
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
20
20

20

Recall that these two sets are mapped onto by
\mathfrak{D}(M_{\epsilon}^{0},M;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathfrak{D}(G_{\epsilon}^{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
respectively.
Conjugating
\epsilon^{\prime}
by an element of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
if necessary, we may assume that
\epsilon^{\prime}
lies in
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and that
\epsilon^{\prime}
is stably conjugate to
\epsilon
inside
M
. Under these assumptions, we have
M=M(\epsilon^{\prime})
. To check that
\epsilon^{\prime}
is
p^{n}
-admissible, it suffices to show that
w_{M}(\epsilon)=w_{M}(\epsilon^{\prime})
. For this, note that since
\epsilon
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
are stably conjugate in
M
, they are conjugate in
M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
by Steinberg’s theorem. The desired statement follows from the fact that
w_{M}
is a group homomorphism from
M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
to an abelian group. ∎

By Lemma
3.4.5
, we have a well-defined notion of
p^{n}
-admissibility
for stable conjugacy classes in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{\mathrm{ss}}
. The following result is a generalization of one direction in
[
LR87
, Satz 5.21]
.

Theorem 3.4.6
.

Let
\epsilon\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ss}}
represent a stable conjugacy class in
\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)
whose localization over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is
p^{n}
-admissible. Then the stable conjugacy class of
\epsilon
lies in the image of
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]
under the map

\mathfrak{scc}:[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}]\to\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)
. (Here
n=mr
and
p^{n}=q^{m}
.)

Proof.

By assumption there exists an
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic maximal torus in
(G_{\epsilon}^{0})_{\mathbb{R}}
. By a theorem of Kneser
[
Kne65
]
, there exists a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-elliptic maximal torus in
(G_{\epsilon}^{0})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. It then follows from
[
LR87
, Lem. 5.10]
that there exists a maximal torus
T
in
G_{\epsilon}^{0}
such that
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
is elliptic in
(G_{\epsilon}^{0})_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
for
v=\infty
and
p
.

Let
M=M(\epsilon)
(which is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
) be as in Definition
3.4.3
, and let
\mu_{M}
be as in Definition
3.4.4
. Then
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a maximal torus in
M
. Let
\mu_{T}\in X_{*}(T)
be a conjugate of
\mu_{M}
under
M(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}).
By Lemma
3.3.7
, we find
u\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\Int(u):T\to G
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, and such that
\Int(u)\circ\mu_{T}=\mu_{h}
for some
h\in X
. Note that
\epsilon
is stably conjugate to
\Int(u)(\epsilon)
, because
u^{-1}{\vphantom{\tau}}{}^{\tau}u\in T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\subset G_{\epsilon}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
for all
\tau\in\Gamma
. Hence we may replace
\epsilon,T,M
by
\Int(u)(\epsilon),\Int(u)(T),\Int(u)(M)
respectively, and assume that
\mu_{T}=\mu_{h}
for some
h\in X
(which necessarily factors through
T_{\mathbb{R}}
).

We denote the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow G
by
i
. Then we have
(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X).
Let
\phi=\phi(T,i,h)
(Definition
3.3.2
). Then
\epsilon\in T(\mathbb{Q})\subset I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
, and we have
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}_{\sa}
by Theorem
3.3.3
. Moreover, by Remark
3.3.4
we have
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
. It remains to show that
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})
.

Since
M
is an unramified reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, it contains an unramified
and
elliptic maximal torus
T^{\prime}
(see
[
LR87
, p. 171]
or
[
DeB06
, §2.4]
). Let
\mu^{\prime}\in X_{*}(T^{\prime})
be a conjugate of
\mu_{T}
and
\mu_{M}
under
M(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. Let
b^{\prime}=\mu^{\prime}(p^{-1})\in T^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Then one immediately checks the following properties:

(i)

The element
b^{\prime}
is decent in
T^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.

(ii)

We have
w_{T^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})=-\mu^{\prime}\in X_{*}(T^{\prime})
. In particular, we have

(3.4.6.1)

\displaystyle w_{M}(b^{\prime})=-[\mu_{M}]^{M}\in\pi_{1}(M).

Since
\phi=\phi(T,i,h)=i\circ\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}
, the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
\mathcal{UR}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
contains the
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
\mathcal{UR}(\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
. Let
(b_{0},\epsilon_{0})\in\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon)
be the element associated with some
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\Psi_{T,\mu_{T}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
(see §
3.1.4
). Then
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon
, and
b_{0}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Moreover, by Lemma
2.2.10
, the element
\kappa_{T}(b_{0})\in X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p}}
equals the image of
-\mu_{T}
.

Since
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and
T^{\prime}
are both elliptic in
M
, we know that
b_{0}\in T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and
b^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
are both basic in
M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
By what we have seen about
w_{T^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})
and
\kappa_{T}(b_{0})
, we have
\kappa_{M}(b^{\prime})=\kappa_{M}(b_{0})\in\pi_{1}(M)_{\Gamma_{p}}
. It follows that
b^{\prime}
and
b_{0}
represent the same (basic) class in
\B(M)
(see
[
Kot85
, Prop. 5.6]
). Since
b_{0}
and
b^{\prime}
are decent, there exists
s\in M(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
such that
b^{\prime}=sb_{0}\sigma(s)^{-1}
(see §
1.4.1
). We then have
(b^{\prime},s\epsilon s^{-1})\in\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon)
.

We write
\epsilon^{\prime}
for
s\epsilon s^{-1}
, which is an element of
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. It remains to show that
\epsilon^{\prime-1}(b^{\prime}\rtimes\sigma)^{n}
has a fixed point in
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
. For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, let

U_{k}:=(\epsilon^{\prime})^{-k}\cdot b^{\prime}\cdot\sigma(b^{\prime})\cdots\sigma^{kn-1}(b^{\prime})\in M(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}),

so that

(3.4.6.2)

\displaystyle(\epsilon^{\prime})^{-k}(b^{\prime}\rtimes\sigma)^{kn}=U_{k}\rtimes\sigma^{kn}.

Since
\epsilon^{\prime}
is
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-conjugate to
\epsilon
, we have
w_{M}(\epsilon^{\prime})=w_{M}(\epsilon)
. Hence
w_{M}(U_{k})=0
by (
3.4.4.1
) and (
3.4.6.1
). By Lemma
3.4.1
, the class of
U_{1}
in
\B^{(n)}(M)
comes from
\B^{(n)}(M_{\mathrm{sc}})
. We claim that the class of
U_{1}
in
\B^{(n)}(M)
is basic with trivial Newton point. Given the claim, we use the fact that the only basic class in
\B^{(n)}(M_{\mathrm{sc}})
is the trivial class (
[
Kot85
, Prop. 5.4]
) to deduce that
U_{1}=c\sigma^{n}(c^{-1})
for some
c\in M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. It follows that
\epsilon^{\prime-1}(b^{\prime}\rtimes\sigma)^{n}=U_{1}\rtimes\sigma^{n}
has a fixed point in
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
, namely
c{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
. The proof of the theorem will then be finished.

To prove the claim, it suffices to find
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
e\in M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
such that

(3.4.6.3)

\displaystyle U_{1}\cdot\sigma^{n}(U_{1})\cdot\sigma^{2n}(U_{1})\cdots\sigma^{(t-1)n}(U_{1})=e\cdot\sigma^{tn}(e^{-1}).

Since
\epsilon^{\prime}
commutes with
b^{\prime}\rtimes\sigma
, it easily follows from (
3.4.6.2
) that the left hand side of (
3.4.6.3
) is equal to
U_{t}
. Fix an arbitrary reductive model
{\mathcal{M}}
of
M
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. By Greenberg’s theorem
[
Gre63
, Prop. 3]
(cf.
[
Kot84a
, Lem. 1.4.9]
), in order to find
t,e
such that
U_{t}=e\cdot\sigma^{tn}(e^{-1})
it suffices to find
t
such that
U_{t}\in{\mathcal{M}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
.

Now for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
we have

(3.4.6.4)

\displaystyle U_{k}=(\epsilon^{\prime})^{-k}p^{\lambda_{k}},

where

\lambda_{k}:=-\sum_{j=0}^{kn-1}\sigma^{j}(\mu^{\prime}).

Since
T^{\prime}
is unramified, for sufficiently divisible
k
we have
\sigma^{kn}(\mu^{\prime})=\mu^{\prime}
. In this case
\lambda_{k}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and in particular it is central in
M
by the ellipticity of
T^{\prime}
. Hence for sufficiently divisible
k
we have

s^{-1}U_{k}s=\epsilon^{-k}p^{\lambda_{k}}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Since
w_{M}(s^{-1}U_{k}s)=w_{M}(U_{k})=0
, and since
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is elliptic in
M
, we apply Lemma
3.4.2
to conclude that some power of
s^{-1}U_{k}s
lies in a compact subgroup of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. In particular, for any given neighborhood
\mathcal{N}
of
1
in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, all sufficiently divisible powers of
s^{-1}U_{k}s
lie in
\mathcal{N}
. Now observe that when
\lambda_{k}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we have
\lambda_{kl}=l\cdot\lambda_{k}
for all
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and therefore

s^{-1}U_{kl}s=(s^{-1}U_{k}s)^{l},\quad\forall l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}.

We conclude that for
\mathcal{N}
as above, we have
s^{-1}U_{k}s\in\mathcal{N}
for all sufficiently divisible
k
. If we take
\mathcal{N}
to be
(s^{-1}{\mathcal{M}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})s)\cap T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, then we see that
U_{k}\in{\mathcal{M}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
for all sufficiently divisible
k
, as desired.
∎

For a quasi-split reductive group
M
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, recall that the
degree
n
norm
is a map from the set of
\sigma
-conjugacy classes in
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
to the set of stable conjugacy classes in
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
; see
[
Kot82
, §5]
.

Lemma 3.4.7
.

Let
\epsilon\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{\mathrm{ss}}
, and let
M=M(\epsilon)
. Assume
that there exists a cocharacter
\mu_{M}
of
M_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}}
satisfying the first condition in Definition
3.4.4
and such that
\epsilon
is a degree
n
norm with respect to
M
of some
\delta\in M(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
satisfying
w_{M}(\delta)=-[\mu_{M}]^{M}
. Then
\epsilon
is
p^{n}
-admissible.

Proof.

It suffices to check (
3.4.4.1
), for the given
\mu_{M}
. First assume that
M_{\mathrm{der}}
is simply connected. Then we have
\pi_{1}(M)=\pi_{1}(M^{\ab})
. Let
\bar{\epsilon}
and
\bar{\delta}
be the images of
\epsilon
and
\delta
in
M^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
M^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
respectively. Then we have
\bar{\epsilon}=\bar{\delta}\sigma(\bar{\delta})\cdots\sigma^{n-1}(\bar{\delta})
. It follows that

(3.4.7.1)

\displaystyle w_{M}(\epsilon)=w_{M^{\ab}}(\bar{\epsilon})=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sigma^{i}(w_{M^{\ab}}(\bar{\delta}))=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sigma^{i}(w_{M}(\delta)),

which gives the desired (
3.4.4.1
).

In the general case, we take an unramified
z
-extension
1\to Z\to H\to M\to 1
as in the proof of Lemma
3.4.1
. Let
\widetilde{\delta}\in H(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
be a lift of
\delta
, and let
\widetilde{\epsilon}\in H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be a degree
n
norm of
\widetilde{\delta}
. By the identity (
3.4.7.1
) applied to
H,\widetilde{\epsilon},\widetilde{\delta}
, we have

(3.4.7.2)

\displaystyle w_{H}(\widetilde{\epsilon})=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sigma^{i}(w_{H}(\widetilde{\delta})).

Now the image of
\widetilde{\epsilon}
in
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is stably conjugate to
\epsilon
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and therefore
M(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-conjugate to
\epsilon
. Hence the image of
w_{H}(\widetilde{\epsilon})\in\pi_{1}(H)
under
\pi_{1}(H)\to\pi_{1}(M)
equals
w_{M}(\epsilon)
. Obviously the image of
w_{H}(\widetilde{\delta})\in\pi_{1}(H)
under
\pi_{1}(H)\to\pi_{1}(M)
equals
w_{M}(\delta)
. Hence from (
3.4.7.2
) we get

\displaystyle w_{M}(\epsilon)=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sigma^{i}(w_{M}(\delta)),

which gives the desired (
3.4.4.1
).
∎

In the next proposition, let the function
\phi_{n}:G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})\to\left\{0,1\right\}
be as in §
1.8.2
. Thus
\phi_{n}
is the characteristic function of the double coset
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})p^{-\mu}{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
for arbitrary
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
, in the notation of §
2.4.1
.

Proposition 3.4.8
.

Let
\epsilon\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ss}}
represent a stable conjugacy class in
\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)
. Assume that
\epsilon\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a degree
n
norm of some
\delta\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
whose
\sigma
-conjugacy class in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
intersects non-trivially with the support of
\phi_{n}
. Then the stable conjugacy class of
\epsilon
is in the image of
[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})]
under
\mathfrak{scc}
.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon^{\prime}
be a
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-conjugate of
\epsilon
such that
M(\epsilon^{\prime})
contains the generic fiber of a maximal
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-split torus
{\mathcal{A}}
in
{\mathcal{G}}
. By Theorem
3.4.6
, we only need to check that
\epsilon^{\prime}
is
p^{n}
-admissible.

Write
M
for
M(\epsilon^{\prime})
. Since
M\supset{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, we have a reductive model
{\mathcal{M}}
of
M
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that the embedding
M\hookrightarrow G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
extends to
{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}
. Let
{\mathcal{S}}
be a maximal
\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}}
-split torus in
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}}}
such that
{\mathcal{S}}
contains
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}}}
. In particular,
S:={\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}}
is a maximal
\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}
-split torus in
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}}
, and it is contained in
M_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}}
. There exists
\mu^{\prime}\in X_{*}(S)
that is
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
-conjugate to some
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
. We fix such a
\mu^{\prime}
. Note that
\phi_{n}
is the characteristic function of
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})p^{-\mu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
.

Denote by
s_{M}(\phi_{n})
the function in the unramified Hecke algebra of
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
with respect to
{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
, obtained from
\phi_{n}
via the partial Satake transform (a.k.a. normalized constant term). Now

[
Hai09
, Lem. 4.2.1]
implies that there exists
\delta_{M}\in M(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
which is
\sigma
-conjugate to
\delta
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
and whose degree
n
norm with respect to
M
is the stable conjugacy class of
\epsilon
in
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. By the descent formula
[
Hai09
, (4.4.4)]
, the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
\delta_{M}
in
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
intersects non-trivially with the support of
s_{M}(\phi_{n})
. On the other hand, one deduces from the computation of Satake transform on p. 297 of
[
Kot84a
]
that
s_{M}(\phi_{n})
is a non-negative linear combination of the characteristic functions of the double cosets
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})p^{-u\mu^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
, where
u
runs over the
\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}
-rational Weyl group of
M
in
G
(acting on
S
). Hence we may assume that
\delta_{M}
lies in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})p^{-u\mu^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
, for some
u
as above. Let
\mu_{M}:=u\mu^{\prime}
. Then
\mu_{M}
satisfies the first condition in Definition
3.4.4
, and
w_{M}(\delta_{M})=-[\mu_{M}]^{M}
. Since the stable conjugacy class of
\epsilon^{\prime}
in
M(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the degree
n
norm of
\delta_{M}
with respect to
M
, we apply Lemma
3.4.7
to conclude that
\epsilon^{\prime}
is
p^{n}
-admissible. ∎

3.5.
Constructing Kottwitz parameters

3.5.1
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
, and let
\tau\in I^{\ad}_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
be an arbitrary element. Our first goal is to assign to the triple
(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)
a Kottwitz parameter (see Definition
1.6.4
)

\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)\in{\mathfrak{KP}}.

Let
\gamma_{0}:=\epsilon,
and
I_{0}:=G_{\gamma_{0}}^{0}
. The Kottwitz parameter
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)
shall be of the form
(\gamma_{0},a,[b])
. Note that by the first condition in Definition
3.2.1
,
\gamma_{0}
is indeed a semi-simple and
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic element of
G(\mathbb{Q})
, meeting the requirement in Definition
1.6.4
.

We construct
a\in\mathfrak{D}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Fix a lift
\widetilde{\tau}\in I_{\phi}(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
of
\tau
, and let
\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}:\Gamma\to G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
be the cocycle as in §
2.4.6
. Since
(\phi,\epsilon)
is gg,
\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}
takes values in
I_{0}(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
. For each
\rho\in\Gamma
, let
t_{\rho}:=\widetilde{\tau}^{-1}\cdot{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}\in Z_{I_{\phi}}(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
. (Here
{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}
is defined with respect to the
\mathbb{Q}
-structure of
I_{\phi}
.) Then
(t_{\rho})_{\rho}
is a continuous cocycle
\Gamma\to Z_{I_{\phi}}(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
. Consider the map

\displaystyle A:\Gamma

\displaystyle\longrightarrow G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle\longmapsto t_{\rho}\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho).

Using the fact that the natural map
Z_{I_{\phi}}\to G
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
and factors through
I_{0}
, we know that
A
is a continuous cocycle
\Gamma\to I_{0}(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p}).
We claim that
A
has trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\Gamma,G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p}))
. In fact, if we denote by
\widetilde{\tau}_{G}
the image of
\widetilde{\tau}
under the canonical embedding
I_{\phi}(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f})\to G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f})
, then

t_{\rho}=\widetilde{\tau}_{G}^{-1}\cdot\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho)\cdot{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{G}\cdot\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho)^{-1}.

(Here
{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{G}
is defined with respect to the
\mathbb{Q}
-structure of
G
.) Hence

(3.5.1.1)

\displaystyle A(\rho)=\widetilde{\tau}_{G}^{-1}\cdot\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho)\cdot{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{G},\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma.

Since
\phi
is admissible, the cocycle
\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}
has trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\Gamma,G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p}))
. It follows from (
3.5.1.1
) that
A
also has trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\Gamma,G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p}))
. Now from (
3.5.1.1
) it is clear that the class of
A
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},I_{0})
is independent of the choice of
\widetilde{\tau}
. We define the desired element
a\in\mathfrak{D}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
to be the class of
A
.

Next we construct
[b]\in\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. By §
3.2.3
, we may view
\phi
as a morphism
\phi_{I_{0}}:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{I_{0}}.
We choose
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\phi_{I_{0}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
and let
b=b_{\Int(g^{-1})\circ\phi_{I_{0}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}}\in I_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
; see Definition
2.2.5
. By Lemma
2.2.4
(i), the class
[b]
of
b
in
\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
is independent of choices. We now check condition
KP0
in Definition
1.6.4
for
[b]
. By the admissibility of
\phi
, we have
X_{p}(\phi)\neq\emptyset
. Comparing §
2.2.7
and §
2.4.1
, we have
X_{p}(\phi)\cong X_{-\mu}(b)
for
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
, and so
X_{-\mu}(b)\neq\emptyset
. It immediately follows that
b
and
-\mu
have the same image in
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p}}
(cf. Corollary
1.3.15
). Thus
KP0
is satisfied by
[b]
. This finishes the construction of
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)\in{\mathfrak{KP}}.

Proposition 3.5.2
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
, and let
\tau\in I^{\ad}_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Let

\displaystyle(\gamma_{0},a,[b])

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,1),

\displaystyle(\gamma_{0},a^{\prime},[b^{\prime}])

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau).

Then
[b^{\prime}]=[b]
. The difference

a^{\prime}-a\in\mathfrak{D}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cong\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})

is equal to the image of
\tau
under the composite map

(3.5.2.1)

\displaystyle I^{\ad}_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathfrak{D}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\mathfrak{E}(Z_{I_{\phi}},I_{\phi};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}).

In particular, the dependence of
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)
on
\tau
is only through the image of
\tau
in
\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the definition of
[b]
and
[b^{\prime}]
. For the second statement, using the notation in §
3.5.1
, we know that
a
is represented by the cocycle
\rho\mapsto\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho)
whereas
a^{\prime}
is represented by the cocycle
\rho\mapsto t_{\rho}\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho)
, where
t_{\rho}
is determined by a choice of
\widetilde{\tau}
lifting
\tau
. The difference
a^{\prime}-a
in
\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},I_{0})
is then given by the image of the class of
(t_{\rho})_{\rho}
under
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},Z_{I_{\phi}})=\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},Z_{I_{\phi}})\to\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},I_{0})
. The desired statement follows.
∎

Proposition 3.5.3
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon),(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
such that
[\phi,\epsilon]=[\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}]
. Let
u\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
be an element such that
\Int(u)(\phi,\epsilon)=(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})
. Let
\tau\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and let
\tau^{\prime}\in I_{\phi^{\prime}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
be the image of
\tau
under the
\mathbb{Q}
-isomorphism
I_{\phi}^{\ad}I_{\phi^{\prime}}^{\ad}
induced by the
\mathbb{Q}
-isomorphism
\Int(u):I_{\phi}I_{\phi^{\prime}}
. Then
u
is an isomorphism

\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)\mathbf{t}(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})

in the sense of Definition
1.6.14
.

Proof.

We write
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])
and
\mathbf{t}(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})=(\gamma_{0}^{\prime},a^{\prime},[b^{\prime}])
. By definition,
\gamma_{0}=\epsilon
and
\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=\epsilon^{\prime}
. We check that
u
satisfies the three conditions in Definition
1.6.14
. Condition (i) follows from Lemma
3.2.11
.

To check condition (ii), we define
t_{\rho}
as in §
3.5.1
, with respect to
(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)
. Then the counterpart
t_{\rho}^{\prime}
with respect to
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
can be chosen to be
ut_{\rho}u^{-1}
. Now
a
is represented by the cocycle
A:\rho\mapsto t_{\rho}\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho)
, and
a^{\prime}
is represented by the cocycle
A^{\prime}:\rho\mapsto t_{\rho}^{\prime}\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi^{\prime}}(\rho)=ut_{\rho}u^{-1}\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi^{\prime}}(\rho)
. Note that
\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi^{\prime}}(\rho)=u\cdot\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho)\cdot{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}u^{-1}
, since
\phi^{\prime}=\Int(u)\circ\phi
.
Hence

A^{\prime}(\rho)=uA(\rho){\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}u^{-1},\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma.

This proved condition (ii).

To check condition (iii), we write
I_{0}
for
G_{\epsilon}^{0}
and write
I_{0}^{\prime}
for
G_{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{0}
. We choose
d\in I_{0}(\overline{\breve{\mathbb}{Q}}_{p})
such that
u_{0}:=ud^{-1}
lies in
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, as in §
1.6.13
. Choose
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\phi_{I_{0}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})\subset I_{0}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
, and
g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{UR}(\phi^{\prime}_{I_{0}^{\prime}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})\subset I_{0}^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. We may assume that
b=b_{\Int(g^{-1})\circ\phi_{I_{0}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}}
and
b^{\prime}=b_{\Int(g^{\prime-1})\circ\phi^{\prime}_{I_{0}^{\prime}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}}
.
Then we have

(3.5.3.1)

\displaystyle g^{-1}u^{-1}g^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),

since this element conjugates the unramified morphism
\Int(g^{\prime-1})\circ\phi^{\prime}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}(p)
to the unramified morphism
\Int(g^{-1})\circ\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p}:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}(p)
. For the same reason we have

(3.5.3.2)

\displaystyle b=(g^{-1}u^{-1}g^{\prime})b^{\prime}\sigma(g^{-1}u^{-1}g^{\prime})^{-1}.

The bijection
u_{*}:\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\to\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\prime})
as in (
1.6.13.2
) sends
[b]
to
[u_{0}b\sigma(u_{0})^{-1}]
, and by (
3.5.3.2
) the latter element equals
[wb^{\prime}\sigma(w)^{-1}]
, where

w=u_{0}g^{-1}u^{-1}g^{\prime}\in G(\overline{\breve{\mathbb}{Q}}_{p}).

To finish the proof it suffices to show that
w\in I^{\prime}_{0}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}).
Since
d^{-1},g^{-1}\in I_{0}(\overline{\breve{\mathbb}{Q}}_{p}),
we have
u_{0}g^{-1}u^{-1}=\Int(u)(d^{-1}g^{-1})\in I^{\prime}_{0}(\overline{\breve{\mathbb}{Q}}_{p})
. Also
g^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}_{0}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
, so
w\in I^{\prime}_{0}(\overline{\breve{\mathbb}{Q}}_{p})
. By the fact that
u_{0}\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and by (
3.5.3.1
), we have
w\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Hence
w\in I_{0}^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, as desired.
∎

3.5.4
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
. Let
I_{0}:=G_{\epsilon}^{0}.
Recall that the group
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})
is defined in §
3.2.18
. Using the canonical inner twisting between
I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0}
and
(G_{\phi^{\Delta}})_{\epsilon}^{0}\subset I_{0}
as in §
3.2.3
, we have a natural homomorphism
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})\to\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}).
Let
e=(e_{\rho})_{\rho}\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})
be a cocycle representing a class in
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})
. By Proposition
3.2.19
, we obtain
(e\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
.

Proposition 3.5.5
.

Let
(\epsilon,a,[b]):=\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,1)
, and
(\epsilon,a^{\prime},[b^{\prime}]):=\mathbf{t}(e\phi,\epsilon,1).
Then the difference
a^{\prime}-a\in\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is equal to the natural image of
e
. The elements
[b^{\prime}],[b]\in\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
have conjugate Newton cocharacters. Moreover, if
\nu_{b}
is central in
I_{0}
, then
\kappa_{I_{0}}(b^{\prime})-\kappa_{I_{0}}(b)\in\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}
is equal to the image of
e
in
\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{0})
, which is identified with
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}
as in Proposition
1.1.9
.

Proof.

By construction,
a
and
a^{\prime}
are represented by the cocycles
\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}
and
\zeta^{p,\infty}_{e\phi}
respectively. Clearly

(3.5.5.1)

\displaystyle\zeta^{p,\infty}_{e\phi}(\rho)=e_{\rho}\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}(\rho),\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma

The statement about
a^{\prime}-a
follows from this and
[
Bor98
, Lem. 3.15.1]
.

We now prove the statement about
[b]
and
[b^{\prime}]
. As in §
3.2.3
, we may view
\phi
as a morphism

\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{I_{0}},
which we denote by
\phi_{I_{0}}
. We may also view
e
as a cocycle in
Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{I_{0}}})
. Then
e\phi
is induced by
e\phi_{I_{0}}:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{I_{0}}
. By construction,
b
(resp.
b^{\prime}
) is associated with the choice of an element
g
in
\mathcal{UR}(\phi_{I_{0}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
(resp.
g^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{UR}((e\phi_{I_{0}})(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
). Let
\theta:=\Int(g)^{-1}\circ\phi_{I_{0}}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}
and let
\theta^{\prime}:=\Int(g^{\prime})^{-1}\circ(e\phi_{I_{0}})(p)\circ\zeta_{p}
, and we view both as unramified morphisms
\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{I_{0}}(p)
. Thus
b=b_{\theta}
and
b^{\prime}=b_{\theta^{\prime}}
. Now
\Int(g^{-1})
induces a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-map
I_{\phi_{I_{0}},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to I_{\theta}
. We let
e_{\theta}
denote the image of
e
under

Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{I_{0}}})\longrightarrow Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\phi_{I_{0}}})\xrightarrow{\Int(g^{-1})}Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\theta}).

By Proposition
2.2.6
(ii), we have a canonical isomorphism
I_{\theta}\cong J_{b}^{I_{0}}
. (See §
1.4.3
for the notation
J_{b}^{I_{0}}
.) We write
e_{b}
for
e_{\theta}
when we view it as an element of
Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},J_{b}^{I_{0}})
. Now
\theta^{\prime}
is in the conjugacy class of
e_{\theta}\theta
(as morphisms
\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{I_{0}}(p)
). By Proposition
2.2.6
(iii), the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
b^{\prime}
in
I_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
is the twist of
b
by
e_{b}
. In particular,
[b]
and
[b^{\prime}]
have conjugate Newton cocharacters by Proposition
1.4.5
.

If
\nu_{b}
is central, then we can apply Proposition
1.4.5
(to the group
I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
), and conclude that
\kappa_{I_{0}}(b^{\prime})-\kappa_{I_{0}}(b)
is equal to the image of
e_{b}
under

\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},J_{b}^{I_{0}})\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},J_{b}^{I_{0}})\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{0})\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}.

Here the second isomorphism is induced by the canonical inner twisting
\iota
between the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-groups
J_{b}^{I_{0}}
and
I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, as in case (ii) of §
1.4.3
. We are left to check that the above image of
e_{b}
in
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}
is equal to the image of
e
in
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}
as in the proposition. This boils down to the commutativity of the following diagram up to inner automorphisms:

\textstyle{(I_{\phi_{I_{0}}})_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\Int(g^{-1})}
\textstyle{I_{0,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}}
\textstyle{I_{\theta,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(J_{b}^{I_{0}})_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\iota}

Here the top arrow is given by the canonical inner twisting between
I_{\phi_{I_{0}}}
and
(I_{0})_{\phi^{\Delta}}\subset I_{0}
. The bottom arrow is the canonical isomorphism in Proposition
2.2.6
(ii). It suffices to check that the composition
I_{\theta,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}(J_{b}^{I_{0}})_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\xrightarrow{\iota}I_{0,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
is equal to the canonical embedding
I_{\theta,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\to I_{0,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
attached to
\theta:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{I_{0}}(p)
. This is straightforward by the proof of Proposition
2.2.6
(ii).
∎

Proposition 3.5.6
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
, and let
{\mathfrak{c}}=\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,1)\in{\mathfrak{KP}}
. Then the Kottwitz invariant
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
is trivial.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.5.3
and Proposition
1.7.10
, we may replace
(\phi,\epsilon)
by any other element
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
such that
[\phi,\epsilon]=[\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}]
, in the course of the proof. Choose a maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
\epsilon\in T(\mathbb{Q})
. (This is possible since
\epsilon
is semi-simple.) By Theorem
3.3.9
, there exists
(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
and
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\Int(g)\circ\phi=\phi(T,i,h)
, and such that
\Int(g)(\epsilon)=i(\epsilon)\in G(\mathbb{Q}).
Using Lemma
3.2.15
one checks that
(\Int(g)\circ\phi,\Int(g)(\epsilon))\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
. Hence up to replacing
(\phi,\epsilon)
by
(\Int(g)\circ\phi,\Int(g)(\epsilon))
, we may assume that
\phi=\phi(T,i,h)
, that
\epsilon\in T(\mathbb{Q})
, and that the embedding
T_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
coincides with
i
.

Write
{\mathfrak{c}}=(\gamma_{0}=\epsilon,a,[b])
. By definition (see §
2.2.9
and Definition
3.3.2
),
\phi
factors as
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, where
L/\mathbb{Q}
be a finite Galois extension contained in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
splitting
T
, and
\mathfrak{G}_{\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
is induced by a
\mathbb{Q}
-homomorphism
f:\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\to G
. In particular,
\zeta^{p,\infty}_{\phi}
is induced by cocycle
\Gamma\to(\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m})(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
and
f
. By Shapiro’s lemma and Hilbert 90, the class
a
is trivial.

Recall that
b=b_{\theta},
where
\theta=\Int(g^{-1})\circ\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p}
for some
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})\cap I_{0}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. Since
\phi
factors through
i:\mathfrak{G}_{T}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, we may take
g
to be inside
i(T)(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. Then
\theta
factors through
i:\mathfrak{G}_{T}(p)\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}(p)
, and
b\in i(T)(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Write
b=i(b_{T})
. By Lemma
2.2.10
, the element
\kappa_{T}(b_{T})\in X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p}}
is equal to the image of
-\mu_{h}\in X_{*}(T)
. Therefore, keeping the notation in §
1.7.5
, we may choose
\widetilde{\beta}_{p}({\mathfrak{c}})
to be the image of
-\mu_{h}\in X_{*}(T)
in
\pi_{1}(I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}))
(with respect to
i:T\to I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}})
). Also, we may choose
\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{c}})
to be the image of
\mu_{h}\in X_{*}(T)
in
\pi_{1}(I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}))
. We have seen that
a
is trivial, so we may choose
\widetilde{\beta}_{l}({\mathfrak{c}})
to be zero for all
l\notin\left\{p,\infty\right\}
. Then we have
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})=0
.
∎

Corollary 3.5.7
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
and let
\tau\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}).
Let
{\mathfrak{c}}=\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)\in{\mathfrak{KP}}
. Then
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
is equal to the image of
\tau
under

I^{\ad}_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\xrightarrow{(\ref {eq:tau to E(I_0,G)})}\mathfrak{E}(I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}),G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{E}(I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}),G;\mathbb{A})\to\mathfrak{E}(I_{0}({\mathfrak{c}}),G;\mathbb A/\mathbb{Q}).

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
3.5.6
, Proposition
3.5.2
, and Proposition
1.7.8
.
∎

As in §
3.1.5
, let
n
be a positive multiple of
r
.

Proposition 3.5.8
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
and
\tau\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Then
(\phi,\epsilon)
is
p^{n}
-admissible if and only if
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)
is
p^{n}
-admissible.

Proof.

Write
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)=(\epsilon,a,[b])
and write
I_{0}
for
G_{\epsilon}^{0}
. By construction,
[b]\in\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
has a representative
b\in I_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
such that
b=b_{\theta}
, where
\theta=\Int(g^{-1})\circ\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p}
for some
g\in\mathcal{UR}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})\cap I_{0}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. Since
g
commutes with
\epsilon
, we see that
(b,\epsilon)\in\cls_{p}(\phi,\epsilon)
. By
[
Kot84a
, Lem. 1.4.9]
,
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\tau)
is
p^{n}
-admissible if and only if
\epsilon^{-1}(b\rtimes\sigma)^{n}
has a fixed point in
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/G(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
. The latter condition is precisely the definition that
(\phi,\epsilon)
is
p^{n}
-admissible.
∎

Proposition 3.5.9
.

Let
{\mathfrak{c}}=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
with
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})=0
. Assume that there exists an element of
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
of the form
(\phi_{0},\gamma_{0})
. Then there exists an element of
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
of the form
(\phi,\gamma_{0})
such that
{\mathfrak{c}}=\mathbf{t}(\phi,\gamma_{0},1).

Proof.

Let
I_{0}:=G_{\gamma_{0}}^{0}
. Write
\mathbf{t}(\phi_{0},\gamma_{0},1)={\mathfrak{c}}_{0}=(\gamma_{0},a_{0},[b_{0}])\in{\mathfrak{KP}}.
By Proposition
3.5.8
, we have
{\mathfrak{c}}_{0}\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
. Hence by Corollary
1.6.12
we know that
\nu_{b_{0}}
is central in
I_{0}
and that
\nu_{b}=\nu_{b_{0}}
. In particular,
\kappa_{I_{0}}(b)
and
\kappa_{I_{0}}(b_{0})
both lie in
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}
. Consider the element

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}e_{0}&:=&(a-a_{0})&\oplus&(\kappa_{I_{0}}(b)-\kappa_{I_{0}}(b_{0}))&&\\
&\in&\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},I_{0})&\oplus&\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}&\cong&\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f},I_{0}).\end{array}

By
KP0
in Definition
1.6.4
, the element
\kappa_{I_{0}}(b)-\kappa_{I_{0}}(b_{0})
goes to zero in
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p}}
. Therefore
e_{0}\in\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb A_{f}).

Note that the image of
e_{0}
in
\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb A/\mathbb{Q})
is just
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})-\alpha({\mathfrak{c}}_{0})
. We have
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})=0
by hypothesis, and
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}}_{0})=0
by Proposition
3.5.6
. By the exact sequence (
1.7.1.1
), there exists a lift
e_{1}\in\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0})
of
e_{0}
. Let
I:=I_{\phi_{0},\gamma_{0}}^{0}
. Then we have a canonical inner twisting
I_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
as in §
3.2.14
, and hence a canonical isomorphism
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I)\cong\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0})
. Let
e_{2}\in\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I)
be the element corresponding to

e_{1}\in\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{0})
.

By Proposition
3.2.19
, we obtain an element
(e_{2}\phi_{0},\gamma_{0})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}.
We claim that
\mathbf{t}(e_{2}\phi_{0},\gamma_{0})={\mathfrak{c}}
. Write
\mathbf{t}(e_{2}\phi_{0},\gamma_{0})=(\gamma_{0},a^{\prime},[b^{\prime}])
. By Proposition
3.5.5
, we have
a^{\prime}-a_{0}=a-a_{0}
, and so
a^{\prime}=a
. As
\nu_{b_{0}}
is central in
I_{0}
, we can Proposition
3.5.5
to conclude that
\nu_{b^{\prime}}=\nu_{b_{0}}
, and that
\kappa_{I_{0}}(b^{\prime})-\kappa_{I_{0}}(b_{0})=\kappa_{I_{0}}(b)-\kappa_{I_{0}}(b_{0})
. Thus we have
[b]=[b^{\prime}]
by the classification of
\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
(see §
1.4.2
). Having checked that
\mathbf{t}(e_{2}\phi_{0},\gamma_{0})={\mathfrak{c}}
, we deduce that
(e_{2}\phi_{0},\gamma_{0})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
by Proposition
3.5.8
.
∎

3.6.
The effect of a controlled twist

Recall that Conjecture
2.7.3
predicts the existence of a
tori-rational element
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})_{0}
such that
\mathsf{LR}(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}},\underline{\tau})
holds. In this subsection we fix such a
\underline{\tau}
.
By Lemma
2.6.20
there exists a tori-rational
\underline{\sigma}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})_{0}
lifting
\underline{\tau}
. By the last assertion in Proposition
3.5.2
, we have a well-defined Kottwitz parameter
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\underline{\sigma}(\phi))
assigned to each
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
. We shall write
\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi,\epsilon)
for
\mathbf{t}(\phi,\epsilon,\underline{\sigma}(\phi))
. We establish analogues of the results in §
3.5
, for the function
\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}:\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}\to{\mathfrak{KP}}
.

As in §
3.1.5
, let
n
be a positive multiple of
r
.

Proposition 3.6.1
.

Let
(\phi,\epsilon)
and
(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})
be elements of
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
such that
[\phi,\epsilon]=[\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}]
. Let
u\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
be an element such that
\Int(u)(\phi,\epsilon)=(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})
. Then
u
is an isomorphism
\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi,\epsilon)\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}).
In particular, the isomorphism class of
\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi,\epsilon)
depends on
(\phi,\epsilon)
only through
[\phi,\epsilon]\in[\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}]
.

Proof.

Since
\underline{\sigma}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})_{0}
, we may find a lift
\tau\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi)
and a lift
\tau^{\prime}\in I_{\phi^{\prime}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
\tau
maps to
\tau^{\prime}
under the isomorphism
I_{\phi}^{\ad}I_{\phi^{\prime}}^{\ad}
induced by
\Int(u):I_{\phi}I_{\phi^{\prime}}
. The proposition then follows from Proposition
3.5.3
.
∎

Proposition 3.6.2
.

Keep the setting and notation of §
3.5.4
, and assume in addition that
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
. Let
(\epsilon,a,[b]):=\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi,\epsilon)
, and
(\epsilon,a^{\prime},[b^{\prime}]):=\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(e\phi,\epsilon)
. The following statements hold.

(i)

The difference
a^{\prime}-a\in\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is equal to the natural image of
e
.

(ii)

The elements
[b^{\prime}],[b]\in\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
are basic and have equal Newton cocharacter. The difference
\kappa_{I_{0}}(b^{\prime})-\kappa_{I_{0}}(b)\in\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}
is equal to the image of
e
in
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}
as in Proposition
3.5.5
.

(iii)

Let
e^{\prime}\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi,\epsilon}^{0})
be another cocycle representing the same cohomology class as
e
. Then we have
\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(e\phi,\epsilon)=\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(e^{\prime}\phi,\epsilon)
. (These two Kottwitz parameters are equal, not just isomorphic.)

Proof.

For (i), in view of Proposition
3.5.2
and the statement about
a^{\prime}-a
in Proposition
3.5.5
, it suffices to check that
\underline{\sigma}(\phi)
and
\underline{\sigma}(e\phi)
have the same image in
\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. But this follows easily from the fact that
\underline{\sigma}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})_{0}
.

By Proposition
3.5.2
, the components
[b]
and
[b^{\prime}]
are unaffected by
\underline{\sigma}
. Hence to prove (ii) we may assume that
\underline{\sigma}
is trivial. By Proposition
3.5.8
and Corollary
1.6.12
, we know that
[b]
is basic in
\B(I_{0})
. The remaining statements in (ii) follow from Proposition
3.5.5
.

Part (iii) follows from the previous two parts, and Kottwitz’s classification of
\B(I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
(see §
1.4.2
).
∎

For each
{\mathfrak{c}}\in{\mathfrak{KP}}
, recall that we have defined the Kottwitz invariant
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
in §
1.7.5
.

Proposition 3.6.3
.

For each
(\phi,\epsilon)\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
, we have
\alpha(\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi,\epsilon))=0
.

Proof.

Choose a maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
\epsilon\in T(\mathbb{Q})
. (This is possible since
\epsilon
is semi-simple.) By Corollary
3.5.7
and the exact sequence (
1.7.1.1
), it suffices to show that the image of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi)
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},T)
comes from
\Sha^{\infty,p}_{G}(\mathbb{Q},T)
. This follows from the fact that
\underline{\sigma}
is tori-rational.
∎

Let
n
be a positive multiple of
r
.

Lemma 3.6.4
.

Let
(\phi_{1},\epsilon_{1})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
. Let
g\in G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
be an element that stably conjugates
\epsilon_{1}
to some
\epsilon_{2}\in G(\mathbb{Q})
, i.e.,
\Int(g)(\epsilon_{1})=\epsilon_{2}
, and
g~{\vphantom{g}}^{\tau}g^{-1}\in G_{\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
for all
\tau\in\Gamma
. Define
\phi_{2}:=\Int(g)\circ\phi_{1}
. Then
(\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
.

Proof.

We only need to show that
(\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}
. Since
\epsilon_{2}\in G(\mathbb{Q})
is stably conjugate to
\epsilon_{1}
, it is
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic in
G
as
\epsilon_{1}
is (cf. §
1.8.7
). Now write
\phi_{i}(q_{\tau})=g_{i,\tau}\rtimes\tau
, for
i=1,2
. It remains to show that
g_{2,\tau}\in I_{\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, for each
\tau\in\Gamma
. As in the proof of Lemma
3.2.11
, we have

g~{\vphantom{\tau}}^{\tau}g^{-1}=[gg_{1,\tau}g^{-1}]^{-1}g_{2,\tau}.

The left hand side lies in
G_{\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
by hypothesis, and the term
gg_{1,\tau}g^{-1}
lies in
G_{\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
since
g_{1,\tau}\in G_{\epsilon_{1}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Hence we have
g_{2,\tau}\in G_{\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. By §
3.2.14
, the last group is in fact equal to
I_{\phi_{2},\epsilon_{2}}^{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}).

∎

Proposition 3.6.5
.

Let
{\mathfrak{c}}=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
. Let
(\gamma_{0},\gamma,\delta)\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\clsc}(p^{n})
be the classical Kottwitz parameter of degree
n
(up to equivalence) assigned to
{\mathfrak{c}}
as in §
1.6.16
. Assume that the
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
\delta
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
intersects non-trivially with the support of
\phi_{n}
. (Here
\phi_{n}
is as in §
1.8.2
.) Assume that
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})=0
. Then there exists an element of
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(q^{m})
of the form
(\phi,\gamma_{0})
such that
\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi,\gamma_{0})={\mathfrak{c}}
.

Proof.

Let
I_{0}:=G_{\gamma_{0}}^{0}
. Since
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a degree
n
norm of
\delta\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
, by Proposition
3.4.8
and Lemma
3.6.4
there exists an element of
\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
of the form
(\phi_{0},\gamma_{0})
. By Proposition
3.5.9
, we find
(\phi_{1},\gamma_{0})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
such that
\mathbf{t}(\phi_{1},\gamma_{0},1)={\mathfrak{c}}
.

Consider the Kottwitz parameter

(3.6.5.1)

\displaystyle(\gamma_{0},a_{1},[b_{1}]):=\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi_{1},\gamma_{0}).

By Proposition
3.5.2
, we know that
[b_{1}]=[b]
, and that
a_{1}-a
is equal to the image of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi_{1})
in
\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Fix a maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi_{1}}
such that
\gamma_{0}\in T(\mathbb{Q})
. Then by tori-rationality of
\underline{\sigma}
, the image of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi_{1})
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},T)
is equal to the image of some
\beta\in\Sha_{G}^{\infty,p}(\mathbb{Q},T)
.

Under
T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi_{1},\gamma_{0}}^{0}
the class
-\beta
determines a class in
\Sha^{\infty,p}_{G}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{1},\gamma_{0}}^{0})
. Fix a cocycle
e\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi_{1},\gamma_{0}}^{0})
representing the latter class. By Proposition
3.2.19
, we obtain
(e\phi_{1},\gamma_{0})\in\mathcal{LRP}^{\mathrm{gg}}_{\sa}
. Write
\phi
for
e\phi_{1}
, and let

(3.6.5.2)

\displaystyle(\gamma_{0},a^{\prime},[b^{\prime}]):=\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi,\gamma_{0}).

Comparing (
3.6.5.1
) and (
3.6.5.2
), we see from Proposition
3.6.2
that
a^{\prime}-a_{1}
equals the image of
-\beta

in
\mathfrak{E}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and that
[b^{\prime}]=[b_{1}]
(since
-\beta
is trivial at
p
). Thus we have
[a^{\prime}]=[a]
and
[b^{\prime}]=[b]
. Hence
\mathbf{t}_{\underline{\sigma}}(\phi,\gamma_{0})={\mathfrak{c}}
.
∎

3.7.
Proof of Theorem
2.7.4

3.7.1
.

Throughout we fix a tori-rational element
\underline{\tau}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{H}})_{0}
such that the statement
\mathsf{LR}(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}},\underline{\tau})
holds. Namely,
we have a smooth integral model
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
of
\Sh_{K_{p}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
which has well-behaved
\coh^{*}_{c}
, and we have a bijection

(3.7.1.1)

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}){}\coprod_{\phi}S_{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)

compatible with the actions of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and the
q
-Frobenius
\Phi
.

Our goal is to prove (
1.8.8.1
) for all sufficiently large
m
. First observe that in the proof we may arbitrarily replace
K^{p}
by an open subgroup. In particular, we may and shall assume that
K^{p}
is neat (see
[
Lan13
, Def. 1.4.1.8, Rmk. 1.4.1.9]
), and that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}U^{p}}
is defined for each open subgroup
U^{p}\subset K^{p}
. It follows from the neatness of
K^{p}
that
K=K_{p}K^{p}
is neat in the sense of Pink
[
Pin90
, §0.6]
.
In the sequel we write
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}
for
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K
and write
Z_{K}
for
Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K
, as in §
1.8.6
.

By linearity, we may assume that
f^{p}=1_{K^{p}g^{-1}K^{p}}
for some
g\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and that
dg^{p}
assigns volume
1
to
K^{p}
.

By Lemma
2.6.20
we fix a tori-rational
\underline{\sigma}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{E}^{p})_{0}
lifting
\underline{\tau}
. For each admissible morphism
\phi
, we fix a lift
\tau_{\phi}\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
of
\underline{\sigma}(\phi)\in\mathfrak{E}^{p}(\phi)=I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. We let
X^{p}(\phi)^{\prime}
be the
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-set whose underlying set is
X^{p}(\phi)
, but the
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action is given by the natural action pre-composed with conjugation by
\tau_{\phi}
. Thus
S_{\underline{\tau}}(\phi)
is isomorphic to

\varprojlim_{U^{p}}I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(X_{p}(\phi)\times X^{p}(\phi)^{\prime}/U^{p}).

Lemma 3.7.2
.

For each admissible morphism
\phi
, the following statements hold.

(i)

Let
\epsilon^{\prime}
be an element of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
which is conjugate under
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
to some
\epsilon\in I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
. If there exist
y^{p}\in X^{p}(\phi)/K^{p}_{g}
and
y_{p}\in X_{p}(\phi)
such that

y^{p}g\equiv\epsilon^{\prime}y^{p}g\mod K^{p},\quad\text{and }\quad\epsilon^{\prime}y_{p}=y_{p},

then
\epsilon^{\prime}\in Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}
.

(ii)

We have
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{der}}\cap Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}=\left\{1\right\}
.

Proof.

(i)
The proof follows the same idea as
[
Mil92
, Lem. 5.5]
.
We view
\epsilon
as an element of
G({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
and let
\bar{\epsilon}
be its image in
G^{\ad}({\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})
. By Lemma
2.6.7
,
\epsilon
is semi-simple, and
\bar{\epsilon}
lies in the
\mathbb{R}
-points of a compact form of
G^{\ad}_{\mathbb{R}}
. By the existence of
y^{p}
, the image of
\epsilon
under
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\to G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
has a conjugate
u
that lies in
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\subset G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
. By the existence of
y_{p}
, the image of
\epsilon
under
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\to G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
has a conjugate
v
that lies in
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. It follows that
\bar{\epsilon}
is torsion. Now let
\bar{u}
be the image of
u
in
G^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Then
\bar{\epsilon}
is conjugate to
\bar{u}
inside
G^{\ad}(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
, and so
\bar{u}
is torsion. But
\bar{u}
lies in the image of
K^{p}
under
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to G^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, which is neat. Hence at least one local component of
\bar{u}
is trivial. It follows that
\bar{\epsilon}=1
. We have thus shown that
\epsilon\in Z(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, and in particular
\epsilon=\epsilon^{\prime}
.

Since the natural embedding
Z\to I_{\phi}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
and since
\epsilon\in I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
, we have
\epsilon\in Z(\mathbb{Q})
. Now using the existence of
y^{p}
and
y_{p}
it is easy to see that
\epsilon\in Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}
.

(ii)
Clearly
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{der}}\cap Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}
is contained in the
\mathbb{Q}
-points of the center of
I_{\phi,\mathrm{der}}
, and is hence finite. Since
K
is neat,
Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}
is torsion free, and so
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{der}}\cap Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}=\left\{1\right\}
.
∎

Lemma 3.7.3
.

We keep the assumptions on
f^{p}
and
dg^{p}
in §
3.7.1
. When
m
is sufficiently large (in a way depending on
K^{p}
and
f^{p}
), we have

(3.7.3.1)

\displaystyle T(\Phi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{m},f^{p}dg^{p})=\sum_{\phi}\sum_{\epsilon}\#{\mathscr{O}(\phi,\epsilon,m,g)}\cdot{\rm tr}\,{\xi}(\epsilon),

where

•

the first summation is over a set of representatives for the conjugacy classes of admissible morphisms
\phi
.

•

for each
\phi
the second summation is over a subset of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
such that each conjugacy class in
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})/Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}
is represented exactly once.

•

the set
\mathscr{O}(\phi,\epsilon,m,g)
is defined as the quotient of

X_{p}(\phi,\epsilon,q^{m})\times\left\{y^{p}\in X^{p}(\phi)^{\prime}/K^{p}_{g}\mid y^{p}\equiv\epsilon y^{p}g\mod K^{p}\right\}

by the diagonal left action of
I_{\phi,\epsilon}(\mathbb{Q})
. Here
X_{p}(\phi,\epsilon,q^{m})
is defined in §
3.1.5
.

Proof.

Since (
2.5.5.1
) is an isomorphism by assumption,
T(\Phi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{m},f^{p}dg^{p})
is equal to

(3.7.3.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}{\rm tr}\,\bigg({\Phi}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m}\times(f^{p}dg^{p})\mid\coh_{c}^{i}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p},\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},\xi)\bigg).

Let
K^{p}_{g}:=K^{p}\cap gKg^{-1}.
We have two maps
\pi_{g},\pi_{1}:\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}_{g}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K}
, induced by the actions of
g\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
1\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
, respectively. By our specific choices of
f^{p}
and
dg^{p}
, the endomorphism
\Phi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{m}\times(f^{p}dg^{p})
of
\coh_{c}^{i}(\mathscr{S}_{K,\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},{\mathscr{L}}_{\xi,K^{p}})
is induced by the correspondence

\textstyle{\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}_{g},\mathbb{F}_{q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{g}}
\scriptstyle{F^{mr}\circ\pi_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}_{K,\mathbb{F}_{q}}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}_{K,\mathbb{F}_{q}}}

and the cohomological correspondence

(3.7.3.7)

\displaystyle\pi_{g}^{*}{\mathscr{L}}_{\xi,K^{p}}\xrightarrow{\overrightarrow{g}^{*}}{\mathscr{L}}_{\xi,K^{p}_{g}}\xrightarrow{(\overrightarrow{1}^{*})^{-1}}\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathscr{L}}_{\xi,K^{p}}\cong\pi_{1}^{*}(F^{mr})^{*}{\mathscr{L}}_{\xi,K^{p}}.

(See §
1.5.2
for
\overrightarrow{g}^{*}
and
\overrightarrow{1}^{*}
.)
Here
F
is the absolute
p
-Frobenius endomorphism, and the last isomorphism in (
3.7.3.7
