depends on the choice of a uniformizer in
F
, but the definitions of
\Upsilon_{\rho}^{\lambda}
and
\omega_{\rho,0}^{\lambda}
are independent of such a choice (up to canonical
\otimes
-isomorphism), by property (iv) in §
4.4.1
,

As in §
4.1.1
, we denote by
P_{\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}}
the
\mathfrak{S}
-scheme
\SIsom^{\otimes}(\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}},\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda})
. Since
\underline{\Aut}^{\otimes}(\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}})\cong G_{\mathfrak{S}}
by the reconstruction theorem, we know that
P_{\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}}
is a pseudo-torsor under
G_{\mathfrak{S}}
(i.e., for each
\mathfrak{S}
-scheme
S
, the set
P_{\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}}(S)
is either empty or a principal homogeneous space under
G(S)
). However, in general
\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}
may not be a fiber functor (as it may not be exact), and
P_{\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}}
may not be a
G_{\mathfrak{S}}
-torsor.

Lemma 4.4.3
.

Let
G,\rho,\lambda
be as in §
4.4.2
. Let
U
be the complement of the closed point in
\Spec\mathfrak{S}
. Then
P_{\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}}|_{U}
is a
G_{U}
-torsor over
U
.

Proof.

We write
\omega
for
\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}
.
By
[
KP18
, Thm. 3.3.2]
, the functor
\omega_{U}:L\mapsto\omega(L)|_{U}
is an exact faithful
\otimes
-functor from
{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G
to the category of vector bundles on
U
. As in §
4.1.1
, we may also regard
\omega_{U}
as a fiber-wise faithful exact functor between the fibered categories
\mathbf{Rep}~G
and
\mathbf{Bun}_{U}
, where the fibers of
\mathbf{Bun}_{U}
over
\Spec\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
\Spec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
are respectively the categories of vector bundles on
U
and on
U\times_{\Spec\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Spec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Since
P_{\omega}|_{U}
is identified with
P_{\omega_{U}}
, we know that it is a
G_{U}
-torsor by
[
Bro13
, Thm. 4.8]
.
∎

4.4.4
.

Let
G
be a smooth affine group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with connected fibers. Let
U
be the complement of the closed point in
\Spec\mathfrak{S}
. We say that
G
satisfies property KL
, if every
G
-torsor over
U
extends to
\Spec\mathfrak{S}
.
26
26

26

By descent,
G
satisfies property KL if and only if all
G
-torsors on the complement of the closed point of
\Spec W(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})[\![u]\!]
extend to
\Spec W(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})[\![u]\!]
, cf. Step 4 in the proof of
[
KP18
, Prop. 1.4.3]
. Thus property KL is intrinsic to the group
G
, and is independent of the finite extension
k/\mathbb{F}_{p}
appearing in the definition
\mathfrak{S}=W(k)[\![u]\!]
.
(Here “KL” stands for “Key Lemma”.) Since
G
is smooth with connected fibers and since the residue field of the closed point in
\Spec\mathfrak{S}
is the finite field
k
, by Lang’s theorem we know that all
G
-torsors on
\Spec\mathfrak{S}
are trivial. Thus property KL is equivalent to the property that all
G
-torsors on
U
are trivial.

It has been proved by Anschütz
[
Ans18
, Cor. 1.2]
that all parahoric group schemes
G
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
satisfy property KL, generalizing earlier results in
[
CTS79
]
and
[
KP18
]
. We will make use of this result mainly when
G
is either a reductive group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, or the connected Néron model of a torus. (In the former case this result already follows from
[
CTS79
]
, as explained in Step 5 in the proof of
[
Kis10
, Prop. 1.3.4]
.) In Corollary
4.4.16
below we will also apply the result of Anschütz to some other parahoric group schemes.

Lemma 4.4.5
.

Let
G
be a smooth affine group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with connected fibers, satisfying property KL. Let
\rho:\Gamma_{K}\to G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be a
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-valued crystalline representation, and let
\lambda\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be a point fixed by
\Gamma_{K}
. Then
\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G\to{\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
defined in §
4.4.2
is
\otimes
-isomorphic to
\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}
(non-canonically). In particular,
\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}
and
\omega_{\rho,0}^{\lambda}
are fiber functors, and
\Upsilon_{\rho}^{\lambda}
is an object in
{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{K_{0}}^{\circ}
.

Proof.

We write
\omega
for
\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}
. By Lemma
4.4.3
and by the discussion in §
4.4.4
, we know that
P_{\omega}|_{U}
is a trivial
G_{U}
-torsor. Fix a section of it over
U
. Then for each
L
in
{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G
, we obtain an isomorphism
\iota_{L}:\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}(L)|_{U}\omega(L)|_{U}
between vector bundles on
U
, which is functorial in
L
and compatible with tensor products. Since
\mathfrak{S}
is a noetherian normal domain and since the closed point in it has codimension
2
, the isomorphism
\iota_{L}
extends uniquely to an isomorphism
\widetilde{\iota}_{L}:\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}(L)\omega(L)
between finite projective
\mathfrak{S}
-modules. By the uniqueness, we know that
\widetilde{\iota}_{L}
is functorial in
L
and compatible with tensor products. Thus we have constructed a
\otimes
-isomorphism
\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}\omega
between
\otimes
-functors.

Since
\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda}
is
\otimes
-isomorphic to
\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}
, it is a fiber functor. Since
\omega^{\lambda}_{\rho,0}\cong(\omega_{\rho}^{\lambda})_{W}
, it is also a fiber functor. It follows that
\Upsilon_{\rho}^{\lambda}
is in
{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{K_{0}}^{\circ}
.
∎

4.4.6
.

Keep the setting of Lemma
4.4.5
, and take
\lambda
to be trivial. Fix a
\otimes
-isomorphism
\eta:\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}\omega_{\rho}
as in Lemma
4.4.5
. Then for
L
in
{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G,
the isomorphism
L\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathfrak{S}\omega_{\rho}(L)
induced by
\eta
carries the Frobenius on
\omega_{\rho}(L)[1/E]
to a
\varphi
-semi-linear endomorphism on
L\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathfrak{S}[1/E]
, which is of the form
\delta_{\mathfrak{S},L}\otimes\varphi
for some
\delta_{\mathfrak{S},L}\in{\rm GL}(L)(\mathfrak{S}[1/E])
. By the reconstruction theorem, the elements
\delta_{\mathfrak{S},L}
for all
L
come from a common, unique element
\delta_{\mathfrak{S}}\in G(\mathfrak{S}[1/E])
(cf. the similar argument in §
4.2.5
). If we change the choice of
\eta
, then
\delta_{\mathfrak{S}}
gets
\varphi
-conjugated by an element of
G(\mathfrak{S})
.

Let
\delta\in G(K_{0})
be the image of
\delta_{\mathfrak{S}}
under the specialization
u\mapsto 0.
Then the
G(\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}})
-
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
\delta
is independent of the choice of
\eta
, and it coincides with
{\rm inv}(\Upsilon_{\rho,0})
defined in §
4.2.5
. More precisely,
\eta
naturally induces a point
y\in Y(\Upsilon_{\rho,0})^{\circ}
, and we have
\delta=\delta_{y}
, where
\delta_{y}
is defined in §
4.2.5
.

Let
[\rho]\in\Crys_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
be the element represented by
\rho
. We can apply the map
D_{\cris}^{G}
in §
4.3.5
to
[\rho]
and obtain an element of
\RZ_{G}/{\sim}
. In particular, we obtain a cocharacter
\mu
of
G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
, well defined up to
G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
-conjugacy (cf. the discussion on the equivalence relation
\sim
in §
4.3.1
).

For brevity, we write
\pi_{1}(G)
for
\pi_{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. As in Definition
1.3.8
, we have the Kottwitz homomorphism

\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}:G(K_{0})\longrightarrow\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}

associated with the
p
-adic valuation on
K_{0}
. We write
[\mu]
for the image of
\mu
in
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}
, which depends only on
[\rho]
.

Proposition 4.4.7
.

With the notation in §
4.4.6
, we have
\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}(\delta)=[\mu].

For the proof of the proposition we need the following result which will be used again later.

Lemma 4.4.8
.

Let
f
be a non-zero irreducible element of
\mathfrak{S}
with
f(0)\in W
having
p
-adic valuation
1
. Let
v_{f}
be the
f
-adic valuation
on
\mathrm{Frac\,}\mathfrak{S}
, and let

\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{f}}:G(\mathrm{Frac\,}\mathfrak{S})\longrightarrow\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}

be the associated Kottwitz homomorphism as in Definition
1.3.8
. Let
g\in G(\mathfrak{S}[1/f])
and let
g_{0}
be the image of
g
in
G(K_{0})
via the specialization
u\mapsto 0.
Then we have
\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{f}}(g)=\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}(g_{0})
in
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}.

Proof.

Let
v_{f},v_{p}
be the discrete valuations on
\mathfrak{S}[1/fp]
attached to the primes
(f),(p)
. Let
v_{0}
be the discrete valuation on
\mathfrak{S}[1/fp]
given by

\mathfrak{S}[1/fp]\xrightarrow{u\mapsto 0}K_{0}\xrightarrow{v_{p}}\mathbb{Z}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}.

Since
f,p\in\mathfrak{S}
are prime elements, any unit
w\in\mathfrak{S}[1/fp]^{\times}
has the form
w=f^{i}p^{j}y
with
i,j\in\mathbb{Z},
and

y\in\mathfrak{S}^{\times}.
Hence we have
v_{f}(w)+v_{p}(w)=v_{0}(w)=i+j
. By Proposition
1.3.10
, for any
g\in G(\mathfrak{S}[1/fp])
we have

\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{f}}(g)+\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}(g)=\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{0}}(g)=\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}(g_{0}).

Now if
g\in G(\mathfrak{S}[1/f]),
then
g\in G(\mathfrak{S}_{(p)})
, and we have

\kappa^{v_{p}}_{G_{K_{0}}}(g)=0,

by Corollary
1.3.12
. The lemma follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.4.7
.

We write
\omega
for
\omega_{\rho}
. For each
L\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G
and
V\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
, we understand that
\Gamma_{K}
acts on
L
and
V
via
\rho
.

Let
\omega^{\prime}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G\to{\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
be the base change of
\omega
along
\varphi:\mathfrak{S}\to\mathfrak{S}
, that is,
\omega^{\prime}(L):=\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}(L)
. As in §
4.1.2
, we have a functor

\omega^{\prime}_{K}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{K}

induced by
\omega^{\prime}
. By (ii) in §
4.4.1
, the functor
\omega^{\prime}_{K}
is canonically identified with the functor
V\mapsto D_{\dR}(V)
. Note that
D_{\dR}(V)
is an admissible filtered
\varphi
-module for each
V\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
. Now the filtrations on
D_{\dR}(V)
for all
V
give rise to a
\otimes
-filtration on
\omega^{\prime}_{K}
. Since
\omega^{\prime}_{K}
is exact, and since exact sequences of admissible filtered
\varphi
-modules are automatically strict with respect to the filtrations, the
\otimes
-filtration on
\omega^{\prime}_{K}
is exact in the sense of
[
SR72
, IV, §2.1]
(cf.
[
Zie15
, §4.2]
). Now since
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
K
are fields of characteristic zero and since
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is of finite type, a theorem of Deligne (see
[
SR72
, IV, §2.4]
) implies that the filtration on
\omega_{K}^{\prime}
is induced by a cocharacter
\mu_{\dR}:\mathbb{G}_{m,K}\to\underline{\Aut}^{\otimes}(\omega^{\prime}_{K})
. The
\otimes
-isomorphism
\eta:\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}\omega
fixed in §
4.4.6
induces a
\otimes
-isomorphism
\eta^{\prime}:\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}\omega^{\prime}
via pull-back along
\varphi
. (Note that we have a canonical identification
\varphi^{*}(\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}})\cong\idfunc_{\mathfrak{S}}
.) We use
\eta^{\prime}
to identify
\underline{\Aut}^{\otimes}(\omega^{\prime}_{K})
with
G_{K}
, and thereby identify
\mu_{\dR}
with a cocharacter
\mu^{\prime}
of
G_{K}
.

We claim that
\mu^{\prime}
lies in the
G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
-conjugacy class of
\mu
, and in articular
[\mu^{\prime}]=[\mu]\in\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}
. In fact, by the definition of
\mu
, there exists a finite extension
F/K
and an element of
\RZ_{G}(F)
of the form
(\mu,\gamma)
such that the
\otimes
-functor

{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G\longrightarrow\left\{\text{finite-dimensional filtered $F$-vector spaces}\right\},\quad V\longmapsto D_{\dR}(V)

is
\otimes
-isomorphic with

(4.4.8.1)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G\xrightarrow{\mathcal{I}_{\mu,\gamma}}\mathrm{MF}_{K}^{\varphi}\xrightarrow{(\cdot)\otimes_{F_{0}}F}\left\{\text{finite-dimensional filtered $F$-vector spaces}\right\}.

Now (
4.4.8.1
) lifts
\idfunc_{F}
, and gives an (exact)
\otimes
-filtration on
\idfunc_{F}
. This
\otimes
-filtration is (tautologically) induced by the cocharacter
\mu
of
\underline{\Aut}^{\otimes}(\idfunc_{F})=G_{F}
. The claim immediately follows.

Now consider an object
L
in
{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G
. We write
\mathfrak{M}
for
\omega(L)=\mathfrak{M}(L)
, and write
L_{R}
for
L\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}R
, for any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra
R
. We write
B^{+}
for
\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}_{(E)}
, which is a
\mathfrak{S}[1/p]
-algebra, and write
B
for
B^{+}[1/E]=\mathrm{Frac\,}B^{+}
. Then
\eta^{\prime}
induces a
B
-linear isomorphism

\mathscr{F}:L_{B}\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}B.

We equip
L_{B}=L_{K}\otimes_{K}B
with the tensor product filtration of the filtration on
L_{K}
defined by
\mu^{\prime}
and the
E
-adic filtration on
B
. We equip
\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}B
with the tensor product filtration of the filtration on
\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}
and the
E
-adic filtration on
B
. Since the isomorphism (
4.4.1.1
) is induced by the filtered isomorphism (
4.4.1.2
), we know that
\mathscr{F}
is a filtered isomorphism. In particular, we have

(4.4.8.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}(\mu^{\prime}(E)^{-1}\cdot L_{B^{+}})=\mathscr{F}({\rm Fil}^{0}L_{B})={\rm Fil}^{0}(\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}B).

Here
\mu^{\prime}(E)\in G(B)
acts on
L_{B}
.

By the definition of
\delta_{\mathfrak{S}}
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{S}[1/E]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}[1/E]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\mathfrak{M}}}
\textstyle{L_{\mathfrak{S}[1/E]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}[1/E]}

Base changing from
\mathfrak{S}[1/E]
to
B
, we obtain the commutative diagram

\textstyle{L_{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\mathfrak{M}}}
\textstyle{L_{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}B}

It is easy to see that in the above diagram
\varphi_{\mathfrak{M}}
maps
{\rm Fil}^{0}(\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}B)
into
\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}B^{+}\subset\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}B
. Hence by (
4.4.8.2
) we have

\delta_{\mathfrak{S}}\cdot\mu^{\prime}(E)^{-1}\cdot L_{B^{+}}\subset L_{B^{+}}.

Since
L
is arbitrary, by the reconstruction theorem we know that the element
\delta_{\mathfrak{S}}\cdot\mu^{\prime}(E)^{-1}\in G(B)
lies in
G(B^{+})
.
Applying Corollary
1.3.15
to
F=B
and
\mathcal{O}_{F}=B^{+}
, we obtain that
\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{E}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{S}})=[\mu^{\prime}]
in
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}
, where
\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{E}}
is as in Lemma
4.4.8
. By Lemma
4.4.8
, we have
\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}(\delta)=[\mu^{\prime}]
. But we have seen that
[\mu^{\prime}]=[\mu]
. This finishes the proof.
∎

4.4.9
.

Let
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension (inside
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
). Consider
G
,
\rho
, and
\lambda
as in Lemma
4.4.5
. There is a natural
base change
functor
{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{K_{0}}^{\circ}\to{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ},D\mapsto D\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
. This induces a base change functor
{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{K_{0}}^{\circ}\to{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
. We set

\Upsilon_{\rho,{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\lambda}\in{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}

to be the base change of
\Upsilon_{\rho}^{\lambda}\in{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{K_{0}}^{\circ}
, namely,
\Upsilon_{\rho,{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\lambda}
is the composite
\otimes
-functor

{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G\xrightarrow{\Upsilon_{\rho}^{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{K_{0}}^{\circ}\xrightarrow{(\cdot)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}.

If
K^{\prime}/K
is a finite extension inside
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
and if
\rho^{\prime}
is the restriction of
\rho
to
\Gamma_{K^{\prime}}
, then the same construction gives rise to

\Upsilon_{\rho^{\prime},{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\lambda}\in{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}.

By property (iv) in §
4.4.1
, we know that
\Upsilon_{\rho^{\prime},{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\lambda}
is canonically
\otimes
-isomorphic with
\Upsilon_{\rho,{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\lambda}
. Therefore up to canonical isomorphism in
{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
, the definition of
\Upsilon_{\rho,{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\lambda}\in{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
depends on
\rho
only via its germ at
1\in\Gamma_{p}
,

Now suppose we are given a general element
[\rho]\in\Crys_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
(see §
4.3.5
). Let
\rho\in\Crys_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(K)
be a representative of
[\rho]
, where
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a finite extension inside
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
. Since the homomorphism
\rho:\Gamma_{K}\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is continuous, there is a finite extension
K^{\prime}/K
in
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
for which
\rho(\Gamma_{K^{\prime}})\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Moreover, if
\lambda\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is an arbitrarily given element, we can further enlarge
K^{\prime}
if necessary to arrange that
\lambda
is fixed by
\rho(\Gamma_{K^{\prime}})\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. (This can be achieved because the stabilizer of
\lambda
in
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is the open subgroup
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cap\lambda G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\lambda^{-1}
.) We then define

\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda}:=\Upsilon_{\rho|_{\Gamma_{K^{\prime}}},{\mathrm{ur}}}^{\lambda}\in{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}.

This definition depends only on
[\rho]\in\Crys_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
and
\lambda\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, up to canonical isomorphism.

As usual, if
\lambda
is trivial, we write
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}
for
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda}
.

4.4.10
.

Let
G
be a smooth affine group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with connected fibers, satisfying property KL. We fix
[\rho]\in\Crys_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
, and obtain
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}\in{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
as in §
4.4.9
. In §
4.2.5
, we defined the
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-
\sigma
-conjugacy class
{\rm inv}(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
attached to
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}
. Let

\delta_{{\mathrm{ur}}}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
be a representative of
{\rm inv}(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
. Let
\mu
be as in §
4.4.6
(which depends only on
[\rho]\in\Crys_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
). As usual, write
w_{G}:G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
for the Kottwitz homomorphism associated with the
p
-adic valuation on
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Then
w_{G}
is trivial on
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
(by Corollary
1.3.12
), and so
w_{G}(\delta_{{\mathrm{ur}}})
depends only on
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}
and not on the choice of
\delta_{{\mathrm{ur}}}
.

Corollary 4.4.11
.

In the setting of §
4.4.10
, the element
w_{G}(\delta_{{\mathrm{ur}}})\in\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
is equal to the image of
\mu
.

Proof.

As explained in §
4.4.9
, we can pick a
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-valued crystalline representation
\rho:\Gamma_{K}\to G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
that represents
[\rho]
. Up to replacing
K
by a finite (unramified) extension, we may assume that
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}=\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}
. We may also assume that
\delta_{{\mathrm{ur}}}=\delta
, where
\delta
is as in §
4.4.6
(defined with respect to
\rho
). The corollary then follows from Proposition
4.4.7
.
∎

Corollary 4.4.12
.

Let
T
be a cuspidal torus over
\mathbb{Q}
, and let
[\rho_{T}]
be an element of
\Mot_{T}\subset\Crys_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
(see Definition
4.3.11
). Let
\mu=\mathscr{M}_{T}([\rho_{T}])\in X_{*}(T)
, where
\mathscr{M}_{T}
is the bijection in Proposition
4.3.12
. Let
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}
be the connected Néron model of
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and let
\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]}
be the object in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}\text{-}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
associated with
[\rho_{T}]
as in §
4.4.9
. Let
\delta_{T}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
be a representative of
{\rm inv}(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})
(see §
4.2.5
). Then
\delta_{T}
lies in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
, and
w_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(\delta_{T})\in X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
is equal to the image of
\mu
.

Proof.

Since
[\rho_{T}]
lies in
\Mot(T)
, we have
\delta_{T}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
. The claim about
w_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(\delta_{T})
follows from Corollary
4.4.11
applied to
G={\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}
. Here we have used that
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}
satisfies KL; see §
4.4.4
.
∎

Remark 4.4.13
.

By Lemma
4.3.9
, the two properties satisfied by
\delta_{T}
claimed in Corollary
4.4.12
uniquely characterize the
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-orbit of
\delta
under
\sigma
-conjugation.

4.4.14
.

Keep the setting of §
4.4.10
. Let
\lambda\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be an arbitrary element. We then obtain
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}=\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{1}
and
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda}
. As in §
4.2.5
, associated with
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda}\in{G\text{-}\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
we have the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda})
, together with a
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor

Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda})^{\circ}\subset Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda}).

Thus we can canonically identify
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
with
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Similarly, we identify
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
with
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
.

There is a tautological
\otimes
-isomorphism
\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda}[1/p]\Upsilon_{[\rho]}[1/p]
, induced by the tautological isomorphism
L\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\lambda\cdot L)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
for each
L
in
{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G
. This induces a tautological isomorphism
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda})Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
. Thus we have an induced map

Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]}^{\lambda})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\longrightarrow Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),

which we identify as a map

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}).

Let
\lambda_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
be the image of
1
under the last map. Since the Kottwitz homomorphism
w_{G}:G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
is trivial on
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
(by Corollary
1.3.12
), we obtain well-defined elements
w_{G}(\lambda),w_{G}(\lambda_{0})\in\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
.

Proposition 4.4.15
.

In the setting of §
4.4.14
, we have
w_{G}(\lambda)=w_{G}(\lambda_{0})
.

Proof.

As explained in §
4.4.9
, we may pick a
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-valued crystalline representation
\rho:\Gamma_{K}\to G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
representing
[\rho]
, where
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a finite extension inside
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
, such that the
\Gamma_{K}
-action on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
induced by
\rho
fixes
\lambda
. For later purposes we shall also suitably enlarge
K
to assume that
\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}=\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
.
(As always,
K_{0}
denotes the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
inside
K
.)

As in §
4.4.2
, we have
\otimes
-functors
\omega_{\rho}
and
\omega^{\lambda}_{\rho}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G\to{\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
(defined with respect to
K
and a chosen uniformizer.) For brevity we denote them by
\omega
and
\omega^{\lambda}
respectively. By Lemma
4.4.5
,
P_{\omega}(\mathfrak{S})
and
P_{\omega^{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{S})
are non-empty. In particular,
Y(\omega)/G(\mathfrak{S})
and
Y(\omega^{\lambda})/G(\mathfrak{S})
admit canonical base points, and can both be identified with
G(\mathfrak{S}[1/p])/G(\mathfrak{S})
canonically (see §
4.1.12
). The tautological isomorphism
\omega^{\lambda}[1/p]\omega[1/p]
induces a map
Y(\omega^{\lambda})/G(\mathfrak{S})\to Y(\omega)/G(\mathfrak{S})
, which we identify as a map
G(\mathfrak{S}[1/p])/G(\mathfrak{S})\to G(\mathfrak{S}[1/p])/G(\mathfrak{S})
. Denote the image of
1
under the last map by
\lambda_{\mathfrak{S}}
. It is clear that
\lambda_{0}
is equal to the image of
\lambda_{\mathfrak{S}}
under
G(\mathfrak{S}[1/p])/G(\mathfrak{S})\to G(K_{0})/G(\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, where the first map is induced by the specialization
u\mapsto 0
.

Now write
C
for
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}
. By (iii) in §
4.4.1
, the base change
\omega^{\lambda}_{C}
of
\omega^{\lambda}
to
C
is canonically
\otimes
-isomorphic to the functor

\idfunc^{\lambda}_{C}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{C},\quad L\mapsto(\lambda\cdot L)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C.

Similarly,
\omega_{C}
is canonically
\otimes
-isomorphic to
\idfunc_{C}
. Moreover, the canonical
\otimes
-isomorphisms
\idfunc_{C}^{\lambda}\cong\omega^{\lambda}_{C}
and
\idfunc_{C}\cong\omega_{C}
are compatible with the tautological isomorphisms
\idfunc_{C}^{\lambda}[1/p]\cong\idfunc_{C}[1/p]
and
\omega^{\lambda}_{C}[1/p]\cong\omega_{C}[1/p]
. It follows that the image of
\lambda_{\mathfrak{S}}
under
G(\mathfrak{S}[1/p])/G(\mathfrak{S})\to G(C[1/p])/G(C)
is equal to the image of
\lambda
under
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to G(C[1/p])/G(C)
. Since the map
{\rm Gr}\to G(C[1/p])/G(C)
is injective, we conclude that the image of
\lambda_{\mathfrak{S}}
in
G(\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}_{(p)}[1/p])/G(\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}_{(p)})
is equal to the image of
\lambda
.

Consider the Kottwitz homomorphism

\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}:G(\mathrm{Frac\,}\mathfrak{S})\longrightarrow\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{K_{0}}}=\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}

associated with the
p
-adic valuation
v_{p}
on
\mathrm{Frac\,}\mathfrak{S}
. By Lemma
1.3.12
and by the functoriality of the Kottwitz homomorphism,
\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}
factors through a map
G(\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}_{(p)}[1/p])/G(\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}_{(p)})\to\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
, whose restriction to
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is equal to
w_{G}
. Since
\lambda_{\mathfrak{S}}
and
\lambda
have the same image in

G(\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}_{(p)}[1/p])/G(\widehat{\mathfrak{S}}_{(p)}),

we conclude that
\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}(\lambda_{\mathfrak{S}})=w_{G}(\lambda)
. Now by Lemma
4.4.8
, we have
\kappa_{G_{K_{0}}}^{v_{p}}(\lambda_{\mathfrak{S}})=w_{G}(\lambda_{0})
since
\lambda_{0}
is the image of
\lambda_{\mathfrak{S}}
under the specialization
u\mapsto 0
. Therefore
w_{G}(\lambda)=w_{G}(\lambda_{0})
as desired.
∎

Corollary 4.4.16
.

Let
F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension. Let
G_{0}
be a parahoric group scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{F}
, and let
G=\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}G_{0}.
Then
G
satisfies KL, and in particular the conclusion of Proposition
4.4.15
holds for
G.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
G
is parahoric and apply the result of Anschütz
[
Ans18
]
(recalled in §
4.4.4
). The fact that
G
is parahoric is well known; see for instance
[
HR20
, Prop. 4.7]
.
∎

4.5.
Crystalline representations factoring through a maximal torus

4.5.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}
be a parahoric group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and write
G
for
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. (Note the change of notations from §
4.4
.) We fix
[\rho]\in\Crys_{G}
. Let
T\subset G
be a maximal torus. We assume that

[\rho]
is equal to the image of an element
[\rho_{T}]\in\Crys_{T}
under the natural injection
\Crys_{T}\hookrightarrow\Crys_{G}
. Let
[\rho^{\ab}]
be the image of
[\rho_{T}]
under
\Crys_{G}\to\Crys_{G^{\ab}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}
(resp.
{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}
)
be the connected Néron model of
T
(resp.
G^{\ab}
) over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. As recalled in §
4.4.4
, the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-group schemes
{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ},{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}
satisfy KL. As in §
4.4.9

we obtain

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{[\rho]}

\displaystyle\in{\mathcal{G}}\text{-}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ},

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]}

\displaystyle\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}\text{-}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ},

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]}

\displaystyle\in{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}\text{-}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}.

As in §
4.2.5
we obtain the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
, the
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})
, and the
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]})
. For
*\in\left\{\Upsilon_{[\rho]},\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]},\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]}\right\}
, we have the set of integral points
Y(*)^{\circ}\subset Y(*)
.

By definition,
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
depends only on the fiber functor

(v\circ\Upsilon_{[\rho]})[1/p]:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}.

Similarly,
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})
depends only on the fiber functor

(v\circ\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})[1/p]:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}T\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}.

Observe that
(v\circ\Upsilon_{[\rho]})[1/p]
is canonically isomorphic to the composition

{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Res}}{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}T\xrightarrow{(v\circ\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})[1/p]}{\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}},

as they are both identified with the functor
V\mapsto D_{\cris}(V)\otimes_{K_{0}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}
, where we view
V
as a
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/K)
-representation via
[\rho]
(for a sufficiently large finite extension
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
). Hence we obtain a canonical map
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})\to Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
, which is equivariant for the
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-action on the two sides. In particular this map is injective.

Similarly, we obtain a natural map
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})\to Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]})
which is equivariant with respect to
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\to G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, and a natural map
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})\to Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]})
which is equivariant with respect to
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\to G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
.

Since the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow G
does not necessarily extend to a map
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}\rightarrow{\mathcal{G}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p},

one cannot expect that the map
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})\to Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
sends integral points to integral points. Nevertheless, we have the following compatibility result.

Proposition 4.5.2
.

The natural maps

Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})\longrightarrow Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]})

and

Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})\longrightarrow Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]})

send integral points to integral points. If we assume that
{\mathcal{G}}
is a reductive group scheme, then the image of
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})^{\circ}
in
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
is contained in
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})^{\circ}\cdot G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
.

Proof.

The first statement follows immediately from the functoriality of the constructions, and the fact that the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-homomorphisms
T\to G^{\ab}
and
G\to G^{\ab}
extend to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-homomorphisms
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}\to{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}
and
{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}
respectively. The second statement follows from the first statement once we know that the map
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})^{\circ}\to Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]})^{\circ}
(provided by the first statement) is surjective. For the last fact, it suffices to observe that the map
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\to{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
is surjective when
{\mathcal{G}}
is a reductive group scheme. In fact, in this case we even know that
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})\to{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}})
is surjective for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, by Lang’s theorem applied to
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{der},\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}}}
which is smooth over
\mathbb{Z}_{p^{n}}
and has connected fibers.
∎

Remark 4.5.3
.

Let
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a large enough finite extension such that
[\rho_{T}]
is induced by
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-valued crystalline representation
\rho_{T}:{\rm Gal}(\overline{K}/K)\to{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
which factors through
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cap{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
\rho
(resp.
\rho^{\ab}
) be the induced
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-valued (resp.
{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-valued) crystalline representation. Let
K_{0}
be the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
inside
K
. Then we have the variant of Proposition
4.5.2
, where
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho_{T}]})
,
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho]})
,
Y(\Upsilon_{[\rho^{\ab}]})
, and
G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
are replaced by the
T(K_{0})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{\rho_{T}})
, the
G(K_{0})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{\rho})
, the
G^{\ab}(K_{0})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{\rho^{\ab}})
, and
G_{\mathrm{der}}(K_{0})
respectively.

5.
Shimura varieties of Hodge type

5.1.
Abelian schemes and related structures on the Shimura variety

5.1.1
.

Throughout this section we keep the following setting. Let
(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
be an unramified Shimura datum as in §
2.4.1
, and assume that
(G,X)
is of Hodge type. Let
E\subset\mathbb{C}
be the reflex field of
(G,X)
, viewed as a subfield of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
via our fixed embedding
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
. Let
{\mathfrak{p}}
be the prime of
E
determined by the embedding
E\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
. We write
K_{p}
for
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Since
{\mathcal{G}}
is smooth over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, its Hopf algebra
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{G}}}({\mathcal{G}})
injects into
\mathcal{O}_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. The intersection
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{G}})\cap\mathcal{O}_{G}(G)
inside
\mathcal{O}_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
has the natural structure of a Hopf algebra over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, and defines a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-group scheme
G_{(p)}
. Thus
G_{(p)}
is the unique (up to unique isomorphism) reductive group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
whose generic fiber is identified with
G
and whose base change to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is identified with
{\mathcal{G}}
.

We fix an embedding of Shimura data
(G,X)\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(V{Q}),S^{\pm})
, where
V{Q}
is a symplectic space over
\mathbb{Q}
, and
(\mathrm{GSp}(V{Q}),S^{\pm})
is the corresponding Siegel Shimura datum. As in
[
Kis17
, §1.3.3]
, we may choose the symplectic space
V_{\mathbb{Q}}
and the embedding
G\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GSp}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})
suitably such that the latter is induced by a closed embedding of
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-group schemes
G_{(p)}\hookrightarrow{\rm GL}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})
, for some self-dual
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
in
V_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
27
27

27

This uses
[
Kis10
, Lem. 2.3.1]
and Zarhin’s trick. In the former result, there is an extra assumption on
G
when
p=2
. However this extra assumption can be removed, as explained in the proof of
[
KMP16
, Lem. 4.7]
.
We fix such choices of
V_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
G\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GSp}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})
, and
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. For any
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-algebra
R
we write
V_{R}
for
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}R
. We write
\mathrm{GSp}
for the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group
\mathrm{GSp}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})
, and write
\mathbb{K}_{p}
for the compact open subgroup
\mathrm{GSp}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
of
\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Thus the embedding
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
maps
K_{p}
into
\mathbb{K}_{p}
.

As in
[
Kis17
, §1.3.6]
, we fix once and for all a collection of tensors
(s_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}}\subset V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}^{\otimes}
such that the image of
G_{(p)}\hookrightarrow{\rm GL}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})
is the scheme-theoretic stabilizer of these tensors. In the sequel, we shall assume that the collection
(s_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}}
is maximal, i.e., it consists of all elements of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}^{\otimes}
that are stabilized by
G_{(p)}
.

Let
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
be the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear dual of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. We shall view it as a representation of
{\mathcal{G}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, i.e., the contragredient of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. Similarly, we view the
\mathbb{Q}
-linear dual
V^{*}{Q}
of
V_{\mathbb{Q}}
as a representation of
G
over
\mathbb{Q}
. We view
(s_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}}
also as tensors over
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
or
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Lemma 5.1.2
.

For any Shimura datum
(G,X)
of Hodge type, the following statements hold.

(i)

The
\mathbb{Q}
-tori
Z_{G}^{0}
and
G^{\ab}
are cuspidal (see Definition
1.5.4
).

(ii)

The anti-cuspidal part
(Z_{G}^{0})_{ac}
of
Z_{G}^{0}
(see Definition
1.5.4
) is trivial.

(iii)

Let
(T,i,h)
be a special point datum for
(G,X)
(see Definition
3.3.1
). Then
T
is cuspidal, and
i(T_{\mathbb{R}})
is an elliptic maximal torus in
G_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Let
w
be the weight cocharacter of
(G,X)
. Since
(G,X)
is of Hodge type, we know that
w
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, and that
\Int(h(\sqrt{-1}))
induces a Cartan involution on
G_{\mathbb{R}}/w(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}})
for each
h\in X
(by directly checking the similar properties for the Siegel Shimura datum). Recall from
[
Del79
, §2.1.1]
that
w
is central. Thus
(Z_{G}^{0})_{\mathbb{R}}/w(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}})
is anisotropic. Since
w(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}})
is defined and split over
\mathbb{Q}
, we see that
Z_{G}^{0}
is cuspidal. Since
G^{\ab}
is isogenous to
Z_{G}^{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}
, it is also cuspidal. This proves (i). Statement (ii) follows from (i) in view of Lemma
1.5.5
. For (iii), we have
i\circ h\in X
, and the Cartan involution
\Int((i\circ h)(\sqrt{-1}))
on
G_{\mathbb{R}}/w(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}})
restricts to the identity on
i(T_{\mathbb{R}})/w(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}})
. Hence
i(T_{\mathbb{R}})/w(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}})
is anisotropic, and the desired statements follow.
∎

5.1.3
.

For each compact open subgroup
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(resp.
\mathbb{K}\subset\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
), we write
\Sh_{K}
(resp.
\Sh_{\mathbb{K}}
) for the Shimura variety
\Sh_{K}(G,X)
(resp.
\Sh_{\mathbb{K}}(\mathrm{GSp},S^{\pm})
). Below we recall the construction of the canonical smooth integral model
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
of
\Sh_{K_{p}}=\varprojlim_{K^{p}}\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}
in Theorem
2.5.3
.

Fix once and for all a neat compact open subgroup
K^{p}_{1}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
whose image in
\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is a neat compact open subgroup
\mathbb{K}^{p}_{1}
. We write
\mathscr{K}^{p}
for the set of open subgroups of
K^{p}_{1}
. Clearly all members of
\mathscr{K}^{p}
are neat. Let
K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}
. By
[
Kis10
, Lem. 2.1.2]
, there exists an open subgroup
\mathbb{K}^{p}\subset\mathbb{K}^{p}_{1}
such that the image of
K^{p}
in
\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is contained in
\mathbb{K}^{p}
and such that the natural map

\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}\longrightarrow\Sh_{\mathbb{K}_{p}\mathbb{K}^{p}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}E

is a closed embedding of
E
-schemes.

Now
\Sh_{\mathbb{K}_{p}\mathbb{K}^{p}}
has a canonical model
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{K}_{p}\mathbb{K}^{p}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, which represents the usual Siegel moduli problem. We define
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
to be the normalization of the closure of
\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}
inside
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{K}_{p}\mathbb{K}^{p}}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
. (It is shown in
[
Xu20
]
that taking the normalization is redundant.) By the main results of
[
Kis10
]
and
[
KMP16
]
,
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
, and the inverse limit
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}=\varprojlim_{K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}}\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}
is the canonical smooth integral model of
\Sh_{K_{p}}
. For each
K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}
, we have
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}=\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}/K^{p}
, so the notation here is consistent with that in Definition
2.5.2
.

In the sequel, we write

K_{1}:=K_{p}K^{p}_{1}.

Over
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}
we have an abelian scheme up to prime-to-
p
isogeny
{\mathcal{A}}
that is the pull-back of the universal abelian scheme up to prime-to-
p
isogeny over
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{K}_{p}\mathbb{K}^{p}_{1}}
. For any
\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
-scheme
Y
and any
\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
-morphism
x:Y\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}
, we denote by
{\mathcal{A}}_{x}
the pull-back of
{\mathcal{A}}
along
x
. For any
Y
as above and any
\mathcal{O}_{E,({\mathfrak{p}})}
-morphism
x:Y\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
, we again write
{\mathcal{A}}_{x}
for the pull-back of
{\mathcal{A}}
along the composite map
Y\xrightarrow{x}\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}
.

5.1.4
.

For a scheme
Y
and a prime number
l
, we write
\Lisse_{\mathbb{Z}_{l}}(Y)
(resp.
\Lisse_{\mathbb{Q}_{l}}(Y)
) for the
\otimes
-category of lisse
\mathbb{Z}_{l}
-sheaves (resp. lisse
\mathbb{Q}_{l}
-sheaves) on
Y
. We define the category of lisse
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-sheaves
\Lisse_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}(Y)
to be the
\mathbb{Q}
-isogeny category associated with the product category of
\Lisse_{\mathbb{Z}_{l}}(Y)
for all primes
l\neq p
. Thus
\Lisse_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}(Y)
is an
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category, with unit object given by the product of the unit objects in
\Lisse_{\mathbb{Z}_{l}}(Y)
. For
R\in\{\mathbb{Z}_{l},\mathbb{Q}_{l},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\}
, we denote the unit objects in
\Lisse_{R}(Y)
by
R
. More generally, given any finite free
R
-module
W
we still write
W
for the “constant sheaf” in
\Lisse_{R}(Y)
represented by
W
.

By Lemma
1.5.7
, (
1.5.8.2
), and Lemma
5.1.2
(ii), we have

{\rm Gal}(\Sh/\Sh_{K_{1}})\cong K_{1}.

For each
W\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{G}}
, we can view
W
as a continuous
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representation of
{\rm Gal}(\Sh/\Sh_{K_{1}})
via
{\rm Gal}(\Sh/\Sh_{K_{1}})=K_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{proj}}K_{p}={\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. By the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear variant of the construction in
[
HT01
, §III.3]
(the
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
-linear version was used in §
1.5.8
), we can attach to the
{\rm Gal}(\Sh/\Sh_{K_{1}})
-representation
W
a lisse
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-sheaf
\mathbb L_{p}(W)
on
\Sh_{K_{1}}
. This construction defines a faithful exact
\otimes
-functor

(5.1.4.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb L_{p}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{G}}\longrightarrow\Lisse_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sh_{K_{1}}).

Similarly, by Lemma
1.5.7
, §
2.5.5
, and Lemma
5.1.2
(ii), we have

{\rm Gal}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}/\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}})\cong K^{p}_{1},

and we obtain a faithful exact
\otimes
-functor

(5.1.4.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb L^{p}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}}G\longrightarrow\Lisse_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}})

by viewing
W\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
as a continuous
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-representation
of
{\rm Gal}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}/\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}})
for each
W\in{\mathrm{Rep}}{Q}G
.

The functors
\mathbb L_{p}
and
\mathbb L^{p}
have complex analytic analogues defined using the complex uniformization. For each
W\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{G}}
, define a subspace
{\mathcal{E}}(W)\subset W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\times X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
by

{\mathcal{E}}(W)=\left\{(w,h,(g_{v})_{v})\mid w\in g_{p}W\subset W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right\}.

We let
G(\mathbb{Q})
act on
{\mathcal{E}}(W)
on the left by

g\cdot(w,h,(g_{v})_{v})=(gw,gh,(gg_{v})_{v}),

and let
K
act on
{\mathcal{E}}(W)
on the right by

(w,h,(g_{v})_{v})\cdot k=(w,h,(g_{v})_{v}\cdot k).

Let
\mathcal{L}_{p}(W)
be the sheaf on the complex manifold
\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C})
consisting of local sections of

G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash{\mathcal{E}}(W)/K_{1}\to\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C})=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1},\quad[(w,h,(g_{v})_{v})]\mapsto[h,(g_{v})_{v}].

Similarly, for each
W\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}}G
, let
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{Q}}(W)
be the sheaf on
\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C})
consisting of local sections of

G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash W\times X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}\to\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C}),\quad[(w,h,(g_{v})_{v})]\mapsto[h,(g_{v})_{v}],

where on the left hand side
G(\mathbb{Q})
acts diagonally on the three factors and
K_{1}
acts by right multiplication on
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}).

We obtain faithful exact
\otimes
-functors

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{p}

\displaystyle:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{G}}\longrightarrow\left\{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\text{-local systems on }\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}}G\longrightarrow\left\{\mathbb{Q}\text{-local systems on }\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\right\}.

For each
W\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{G}}
, there is a natural isomorphism between
{\mathcal{L}}_{p}(W)
and the analytification
\mathbb L_{p}(W)^{\mathrm{an}}
of
\mathbb L_{p}(W)
which is compatible with tensor products. Similarly, for each
W\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}}G
, there is a natural isomorphism between
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbb{Q}}(W)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
and the analytification
\mathbb L^{p}(W)^{\mathrm{an}}
of
\mathbb L^{p}(W)
which is compatible with tensor products. These observations go back to Langlands
[
Lan73
, §3]
, cf. for instance
[
Mor05
, §2.1.4]
.

5.1.5
.

Denote the structure morphism
{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}
by
h
, and denote the structure morphism
{\mathcal{A}}|_{\Sh_{K_{1}}}\to\Sh_{K_{1}}
by
h_{\eta}
.

Over the complex manifold
\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C})
, we have a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
\mathcal{V}_{B,p}
(resp. a
\mathbb{Q}
-local system
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}
) given by the first relative Betti cohomology of the analytification of
h_{\eta}
with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(resp.
\mathbb{Q}
). By the moduli interpretation of the complex uniformization of the Siegel Shimura variety, we have canonical identifications

(5.1.5.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{B,p}\cong\mathcal{L}_{p}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}),\quad\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{Q}}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}),

cf.
[
Kis17
, §1.4.11]
.
28
28

28

In
[
Kis17
, §1.4.11]
, the line “
H_{1}({\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})g_{p}\cdot V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\subset V{Q}
” should be corrected to “
H_{1}({\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Z}_{p})g_{p}\cdot V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subset V_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
”.

For each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
, we can view
s_{\alpha}
as a morphism
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})^{\otimes}
between
{\mathcal{G}}
-representations. By the identifications in (
5.1.5.1
) we obtain a tensor
s_{\alpha,B,p}:=\mathcal{L}_{p}(s_{\alpha})
over
\mathcal{V}_{B,p}
and a tensor
s_{\alpha,B,\mathbb{Q}}:=\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{Q}}(s_{\alpha})
over
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}
.

Let

\mathcal{V}_{p}:=R^{1}h_{\eta,\et,*}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\in\Lisse_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sh_{K_{1}}),

and

\mathcal{V}^{p}:=R^{1}h_{\et,*}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\in\Lisse_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}).

Analogous to (
5.1.5.1
), we have canonical identifications

(5.1.5.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{p}\cong\mathbb L_{p}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}),\quad\mathcal{V}^{p}\cong\mathbb L^{p}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}),

arising from the fact that the tower of Siegel Shimura varieties relatively represents the moduli of level structures.

By (
5.1.5.2
), for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
, we obtain a tensor
s_{\alpha,p}:=\mathbb L_{p}(s_{\alpha})
over
\mathcal{V}_{p}
, and a tensor
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}:=\mathbb L^{p}(s_{\alpha})
over
\mathcal{V}^{p}
.

Remark 5.1.6
.

The natural isomorphism
\mathbb L_{p}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})^{\mathrm{an}}\cong\mathcal{L}_{p}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
at the end of §
5.1.4
coincides with the comparison isomorphism
\mathcal{V}_{p}^{\mathrm{an}}\cong\mathcal{V}_{B,p}
, if we identify the two sides with
\mathcal{V}_{p}^{\mathrm{an}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{B,p}
respectively. Since the analytification functor is faithful, we see that
s_{\alpha,p}
is uniquely characterized by the fact that under the comparison isomorphism
\mathcal{V}_{p}^{\mathrm{an}}\cong\mathcal{V}_{B,p}
it corresponds to
s_{\alpha,B,p}
. In a similar way,
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
is characterized by
s_{\alpha,B,\mathbb{Q}}
. This shows that the current definitions of
s_{\alpha,p}
and
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
agree with those in
[
Kis17
, §1.3.6]
.
29
29

29

In
[
Kis17
, §1.3.6]
, only the
l
-adic components of
\mathcal{V}^{p}
and
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
are considered.

5.1.7
.

Let
\kappa
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
containing
E
, and let
\overline{\kappa}
be the algebraic closure of
{\kappa}
in
\mathbb{C}
. For
z\in\Sh_{K_{1}}(\kappa)
, we write
\mathcal{V}_{p}(z)
for the stalk of
\mathcal{V}_{p}
at
z
viewed as a
\bar{\kappa}
-point. Thus
\mathcal{V}_{p}(z)
is a finite free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module equipped with a continuous
{\rm Gal}(\bar{\kappa}/\kappa)
-action, and it is identified with
\coh^{1}_{\et}({\mathcal{A}}_{z,\bar{\kappa}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. For each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
, write
s_{\alpha,p,z}
for the tensor over
\mathcal{V}_{p}(z)
induced by
s_{\alpha,p}
. Then
s_{\alpha,p,z}
is invariant under
{\rm Gal}(\bar{\kappa}/\kappa)
. We write
{\mathcal{G}}_{z}
for the closed subgroup scheme of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-group scheme
{\rm GL}(\mathcal{V}_{p}(z))
fixing
s_{\alpha,p,z}
for all
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
. Thus we have a natural continuous homomorphism
\rho(z):{\rm Gal}(\bar{\kappa}/\kappa)\to{\mathcal{G}}_{z}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Lemma 5.1.8
.

In the setting of §
5.1.7
, there exists a (non-canonical)
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module isomorphism
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{V}_{p}(z)
taking each
s_{\alpha}
to
s_{\alpha,p,z}
. In particular, there is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-group schemes
{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{z}
, canonical up to conjugation by
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Proof.

Let
z_{\mathbb{C}}\in\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C})
be the point induced by
z
. By Remark
5.1.6
, it suffices to show the existence of a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module isomorphism
f
from
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
to the stalk
\mathcal{V}_{B,p}(z{C})
of
\mathcal{V}_{B,p}
at
z{C}
such that
f
takes each
s_{\alpha}
to the tensor on
\mathcal{V}_{B,p}(z{C})
induced by
s_{\alpha,B,p}
. This follows from
[
Kis17
, §1.4.11]
.
∎

Lemma 5.1.9
.

On
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
, there is a canonical isomorphism between the pull-back of
\mathcal{V}^{p}
and the constant lisse
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-sheaf
V^{*}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
. This isomorphism takes
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
to
s_{\alpha}
(viewed as a tensor on the constant sheaf
V^{*}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
) for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
.

Proof.

The composition of
\mathbb L^{p}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}}G\to\Lisse_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}})
with the pull-back functor
\Lisse_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}})\to\Lisse_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}})
is canonically identified with the functor sending each
W\in{\mathrm{Rep}}{Q}G
to the constant sheaf
W\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
. The lemma follows from this fact and the second identification in (
5.1.5.2
).
∎

5.2.
Crystalline tensors

5.2.1
.

Let
A
be an abelian variety up to prime-to-
p
isogeny over
\overline{\mathbb F}_{p}
. We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{0}(A)

\displaystyle:=\coh^{1}_{\cris}(A_{0}/W(k))\otimes_{W(k)}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}},

where
A_{0}
is a model of
A
over some finite field
k\subset\overline{\mathbb F}_{p}
. Then
\mathcal{V}_{0}(A)
has the natural structure of an integral
F
-isocrystal over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
(see §
4.2.1
), and it is independent of the choices of
k
and
A_{0}
up to canonical isomorphism. We denote the Frobenius on
\mathcal{V}_{0}(A)[1/p]
simply by
\varphi
.

For
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, we write
\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)
for
\mathcal{V}_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{x})
. For
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
with
K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}
or
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, we define
\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)
to be
\mathcal{V}_{0}(y)
, where
y\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
is the image of
x
.

5.2.2
.

Let
x
be a closed point of the special fiber of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}
, with residue field
k(x)
. We then obtain the abelian variety
{\mathcal{A}}_{x}
up to prime-to-
p
isogeny over
k(x)
. As a key construction in
[
Kis10
]
and
[
Kis17
]
, for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
we have a tensor
s_{\alpha,0,x}
over the
W(k(x))
-module
\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x}/W(k(x)))
, which is furthermore
\varphi
-invariant (after inverting
p
). Below we recall the construction of
s_{\alpha,0,x}
given in
[
Kis17
, §1.3]
, and explain why the assumption
p>2
in
loc. cit.
can be removed.

Let
F
be a finite extension of
E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
in
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
whose residue field
k
contains
k(x)
. Let
\widetilde{x}\in\Sh_{K_{1}}(F)
be a point that extends (necessarily uniquely) to a point in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
which specializes to
x
. (Clearly such a pair
(F,\widetilde{x})
exists for any prescribed
x
and finite extension
k/k(x)
; one can moreover take
F
to be
W(k(x))[1/p]
.) Since
{\mathcal{A}}_{x,k}:={\mathcal{A}}_{x}\times_{k(x)}k
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x}}
are the special and generic fibers of an abelian scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{F}
, we know that the
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
-representation
\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})[1/p]\cong\coh^{1}_{\et}({\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x},\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is crystalline. Moreover, by the
integral comparison isomorphism
we have a canonical isomorphism of integral
F
-isocrystals

(5.2.2.1)

\displaystyle M_{\cris}(\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x}))=M_{\cris}(\coh^{1}_{\et}({\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x},\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\big)\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x,k}/W(k)),

which refines the crystalline comparison isomorphism

D_{\cris}\big(\coh^{1}_{\et}({\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x},\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\big)\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x,k}/W(k))[1/p].

Here the functor
M_{\cris}
is defined in §
4.4.1
, and recall that
M_{\cris}(L)
is a
W(k)
-lattice in the
W(k)[1/p]
-vector space
D_{\cris}(L\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, for any
L\in{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\Gamma_{K}}^{\cris,\circ}
. This integral comparison isomorphism is proved in
[
Kis10
, Thm. 1.4.2]
(cf.
[
Kis17
, Thm. 1.1.6]
for a correction in the normalization) for
p>2
, and proved in
[
Kim12
, Prop. 4.2]
for
p=2
. An independent proof valid for all
p
is given by Lau
[
Lau14
,
Lau19
]
.
30
30

30

In all these references, the integral comparison isomorphism is proved more generally for
p
-divisible groups over
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. See the proof of
[
KMP16
, Thm. 2.12]
for a historical account of the different proofs. The integral comparison is now known for arbitrary proper smooth formal schemes (under a certain torsion-free assumption) by the work of Bhatt–Morrow–Scholze
[
BMS18
, Thm. 14.6.3 (iii)]
.

Now since
s_{\alpha,p,\widetilde{x}}
is
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
-invariant, we have the
\varphi_{M_{\cris}(\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x}))}
-invariant tensor
M_{\cris}(s_{\alpha,p,\widetilde{x}})
over
M_{\cris}(\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x}))
by the functoriality of
M_{\cris}
. Under (
5.2.2.1
),
M_{\cris}(s_{\alpha,p,\widetilde{x}})
corresponds to a
\varphi
-invariant tensor
s_{\alpha,0,\widetilde{x}}
over
\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x,k}/W(k))
. Note that
\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x,k}/W(k))
is canonically identified with

\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x}/W(k(x)))\otimes_{W(k(x))}W(k).

It is shown in the proof of
[
Kis10
, Prop. 2.3.5]
(cf.
[
Kis17
, Prop. 1.3.9]
) that
s_{\alpha,0,\widetilde{x}}
in fact comes from a tensor
s_{\alpha,0,x}
on
\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x}/W(k(x)))
that depends only on
x
and not on the choices of
F
and
\widetilde{x}
.

For any
x_{1}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
we have a canonical identification (see §
5.2.1
)

\mathcal{V}_{0}(x_{1})\cong\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x}/W(k(x)))\otimes_{W(k(x))}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}},

where
x
is the closed point of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}
given by the image of
x_{1}
. The tensor
s_{\alpha,0,x}
on
\coh^{1}_{\cris}({\mathcal{A}}_{x}/W(k(x)))
thus induces a tensor
s_{\alpha,0,x_{1}}
on
\mathcal{V}_{0}(x_{1})
, for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
. For any
y\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
mapping to
x_{1}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, we have
\mathcal{V}_{0}(y)=\mathcal{V}_{0}(x_{1})
by definition. In this case we also write
s_{\alpha,0,y}
for the tensor
s_{\alpha,0,x_{1}}
on
\mathcal{V}_{0}(y)
.

Lemma 5.2.3
.

For each
x_{1}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, there exists a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
-module isomorphism
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{*}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\mathcal{V}_{0}(x_{1})

which takes
s_{\alpha}
to
s_{\alpha,0,x_{1}}
for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
.

Proof.

This is proved in
[
Kis10
, Cor. 1.3.4]
. Below we recast the proof using the formalism in §
4.4
. Let
x
be the closed point of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}
induced by
x_{1}
, and let

\widetilde{x}\in\Sh_{K_{1}}(F)
be a lift of
x
to some finite extension
F/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
as in §
5.2.2
. Fix an isomorphism
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})
as in Lemma
5.1.8
, and use this isomorphism to view
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
as a
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
-representation. This Galois representation is crystalline, and
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is a Galois-stable lattice. Moreover, each tensor
s_{\alpha}
on
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
-invariant, since each
s_{\alpha,p,\widetilde{x}}
is invariant under
\rho(\widetilde{x})
(see §
5.1.7
). The action map
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)\to{\rm GL}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
thus factors through a
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-valued crystalline representation
\rho:{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)\to{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
k
be the residue field of
F
. By the construction of
s_{\alpha,0,x}
recalled in §
5.2.2
and by Lemma
5.1.8
, we only need to find a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
-module isomorphism

M_{\cris}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\otimes_{W(k)}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}

which takes each
M_{\cris}(s_{\alpha})
to
s_{\alpha}
. For this, it suffices to find a
W(k)
-module isomorphism

\omega_{\rho,0}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})V^{*}_{W(k)}

which takes each
\omega_{\rho,0}(s_{\alpha})
to
s_{\alpha}
. (See §
4.4.2
for
\omega_{\rho,0}
.) For this it suffices to know that
\omega_{\rho,0}
is
\otimes
-isomorphic with
\idfunc_{W(k)}
. This is indeed the case by the fact that
{\mathcal{G}}
satisfies KL (§
4.4.4
) and by Lemma
4.4.5
.
∎

Lemma 5.2.4
.

For each
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, there is an integral
F
-isocrystal with
{\mathcal{G}}
-structure

\Upsilon_{x}:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{G}}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Isoc}}^{\circ}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}},

together with an isomorphism
\iota_{x}:\Upsilon_{x}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{*})\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)
in
{\mathrm{Isoc}}^{\circ}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}
which takes
\Upsilon_{x}(s_{\alpha})
to
s_{\alpha,0,x}
for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
. Moreover, the pair
(\Upsilon_{x},\iota_{x})
is unique up to unique isomorphism, in the same sense as in Lemma
4.2.3
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.2.3
, the object
(D,\varphi_{D}):=\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)
in
{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
, together with the tensors
s_{\alpha,0,x}
on it, satisfies the hypotheses in §
4.2.2
with respect to the defining datum
(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},(s_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}})
for
{\mathcal{G}}
. The lemma then follows from Lemma
4.2.3
.
∎

Definition 5.2.5
.

For each
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, we fix the choice of a pair
(\Upsilon_{x},\iota_{x})
as in Lemma
5.2.4
once and for all. If
y\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
maps to
x
, we also write
(\Upsilon_{y},\iota_{y})
for
(\Upsilon_{x},\iota_{x})
.

5.3.
Kottwitz triples

Definition 5.3.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Define
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}
to be the set of triples
(\gamma_{0},\gamma,\delta)
, where
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q})
,
\gamma=(\gamma_{l})_{l\neq p}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and
\delta\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
, satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

\gamma_{0}
is conjugate to
\gamma
in
G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})
.

(ii)

\gamma_{0}
is conjugate to
\delta\sigma(\delta)\cdots\sigma^{n-1}(\delta)
in
G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
.

(iii)

the image of
\gamma_{0}
in
G(\mathbb{R})
is elliptic over
\mathbb{R}
.

5.3.2
.

Note that for
n,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, there is a natural map

{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{nt},\quad(\gamma_{0},\gamma,\delta)\mapsto(\gamma_{0}^{t},\gamma^{t},\delta).

We set

{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}:=\varinjlim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}}{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n},

where
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
is a directed set under divisibility.

Let
\mathfrak{k}=(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{,}\delta)\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Recall from
[
Kis17
, §4.3]
that
\mathfrak{k}
gives rise to the following objects:

•

a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
I_{0}
of
G
, defined to be the centralizer in
G
of a sufficiently divisible power of
\gamma_{0}
.

•

a
\mathbb{Q}_{l}
-subgroup
I_{l}
of
G
for each finite places
l\neq p
, defined to be the centralizer in
G
of a sufficiently divisible power of
\gamma_{l}
.

•

a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebraic group
I_{p}
defined by

I_{p}(R)=\left\{g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}R)\mid g^{-1}\delta\sigma(g)=\delta\right\}

for any
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra
R
. We shall view
I_{p}
as a subfunctor of the functor
\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(G)
which sends every
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra
R
to the group
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}R)
. When
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
is sufficiently divisible, we have

(5.3.2.1)

\displaystyle I_{p}(R)=\left\{g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{nt}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}R)\mid g^{-1}\delta\sigma(g)=\delta\right\}

for any
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra.

For each finite place
v
, there is a natural equivalence class (see Definition
1.2.1
) of inner twistings

\eta_{v}:I_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\simeq I_{v}
; see
[
Kis17
, §4.3.1]
. The datum
(I_{0},(I_{v})_{v},(\eta_{v})_{v})
depends on
\mathfrak{k}
only through the image of
\mathfrak{k}
in
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}
. In other words, we can attach
(I_{0},(I_{v})_{v},(\eta_{v})_{v})
to any element of
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}
.

Definition 5.3.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{k}\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}
, with associated datum
(I_{0},(I_{v})_{v},(\eta_{v})_{v})
as in §
5.3.2
. A
refinement
of
\mathfrak{k}
is a tuple
(I,\iota_{0},\iota=(\iota_{v})_{v})
, where

•

I
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-group and
\iota_{0}
is an inner twisting
I_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to I_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

•

For each finite place
v
,
\iota_{v}
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-isomorphism
I_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\to I_{v}
such that
\iota_{v}\circ\iota_{0}
as an inner twisting between
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-groups lies in the equivalence class
\eta_{v}
.

•

(I/\iota_{0}(Z_{G}))(\mathbb{R})
is compact.

We denote by
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
the subset of
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}
consisting of elements which admit refinements. Elements of
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
are called
strict Kottwitz triples
.

Definition 5.3.4
.

Two strict Kottwitz triples
\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
are called
equivalent
(resp.
congruent
), written as
\mathfrak{k}\sim\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{k}}\equiv{\mathfrak{k}}^{\prime}
), if there exist
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and respective representatives
(\gamma_{0},\gamma,\delta),(\gamma_{0}^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}
of
\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{k}^{\prime}
, satisfying the following conditions.

•

\gamma_{0}
and
\gamma_{0}^{\prime}
are conjugate in
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
.

•

\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
are conjugate in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
(resp.
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}
).

•

\delta
and
\delta^{\prime}
are
\sigma
-conjugate in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
(resp.
\delta=\delta^{\prime}
).

5.3.5
.

Recall that
\mathbb{A}^{*}_{f}:=\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\times\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}.
Let
g=(g^{p},g_{p})\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})=G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, we have a bijection

{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n},\quad(\gamma_{0},\gamma,\delta)\mapsto(\gamma_{0},(g^{p})^{-1}\gamma g^{p},g_{p}^{-1}\delta\sigma(g_{p})).

These maps for all
n
induce a bijection
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
. In this way we obtain a right action of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
on
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
, which descends to an action on
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
.

Definition 5.3.6
.

We denote the orbit space
({\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
by
{\mathfrak{KT}}
. Elements of
{\mathfrak{KT}}
are called
Kottwitz triples
. (This terminology agrees with
[
Kis17
]
.) The equivalence relation
\sim
on
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
descends to an equivalence relation on
{\mathfrak{KT}}
, still denoted by
\sim
.

Remark 5.3.7
.

The natural map
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\sim}\to{\mathfrak{KT}}/{\sim}
is a bijection.

5.3.8
.

We summarize the various definitions in the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}=\varinjlim_{n}{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\sim}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{KT}}/{\sim}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{KT}}=({\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})}
subset of refinable elts
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1:1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})}

5.3.9
.

As in Definition
3.3.1
, we denote by
\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
the set of special point data for
(G,X)
. Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i,h)
be an element of
\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
. By Lemma
5.1.2
(iii),
T
is a cuspidal torus.

Let
\mu_{h}\in X_{*}(T)
be the Hodge cocharacter of
h.
By Lemma
4.3.9
, we obtain from the image of
-\mu_{h}
in
X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
a canonical
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
. Let
\delta_{T}
be an element of this equivalence class. Then for
n
sufficiently divisible, the element

\gamma_{0,T,n}:=\delta_{T}\sigma(\delta_{T})\cdots\sigma^{n-1}(\delta_{T})

lies in
T(\mathbb{Q})
. Note that the triple

(5.3.9.1)

\displaystyle(i(\gamma_{0,T,n}),(i(\gamma_{0,T,n}))_{l\neq p},i(\delta_{T}))

is an element of
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}
. In fact, condition (iii) in Definition
5.3.1
is satisfied since
T_{\mathbb{R}}
is elliptic in
G_{\mathbb{R}}
, and the other two conditions are trivial. For
n
sufficiently divisible, the image of (
5.3.9.1
) under
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}\to{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}
is an element

{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})={\mathfrak{k}}(T,i,h,\delta_{T})\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}

which depends only on
{\mathfrak{s}}
and
\delta_{T}
, not on
n
.
By
[
Kis17
, Lem. 4.3.11]
,
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})
lies in
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}\subset{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}
.

Note that the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class of
\delta_{T}
is determined by
{\mathfrak{s}}
. If we
\sigma
-conjugate
\delta_{T}
by an element of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
(or even
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
), the element
\gamma_{0,T,n}
remains unchanged as long as
n
is sufficiently divisible. It follows that the image of
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})
in
{\mathfrak{KT}}/{\sim}
is a well-defined invariant of
{\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
.
31
31

31

Note that the same cannot be said for the image of
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})
in
{\mathfrak{KT}}=({\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
. This is because two elements of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
that are
\sigma
-conjugate by an element of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
need not be
\sigma
-conjugate by an element of
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
.
We denote this element of
{\mathfrak{KT}}/{\sim}
by
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}})
.

Definition 5.3.10
.

An equivalence class of Kottwitz triples in
{\mathfrak{KT}}/{\sim}
is called
special
, if it is of the form
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}})
for some
{\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathcal{SPD}
.

5.4.
Isogeny classes

5.4.1
.

For each
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, we write
\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)
for the stalk at
x
of (the pull-back to
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
of)
\mathcal{V}^{p}
. This is a finite free
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-module. For each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
, we write
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},x}
for the tensor on
\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)
induced by the tensor
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
on
\mathcal{V}^{p}
.

Let
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
. Let
R
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, and let

f\in\Hom({\mathcal{A}}_{x},{\mathcal{A}}_{x^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}R

be an
R
-isogeny. Then
f
induces an
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R
-linear isomorphism

f_{\mathcal{V}^{p}}:\mathcal{V}^{p}(x^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R,

since the two sides are identified with
\coh^{1}_{\et}({\mathcal{A}}_{x^{\prime}},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R
and
\coh^{1}_{\et}({\mathcal{A}}_{x},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R
respectively. Similarly,
f
induces a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R
-linear isomorphism

f_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}:\mathcal{V}_{0}(x^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R.

We say that
f

preserves
G
-structures
, if
f_{\mathcal{V}^{p}}
takes
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},x^{\prime}}
to
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},x}
and
f_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}
takes
s_{\alpha,0,x^{\prime}}
to
s_{\alpha,0,x}
for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
. We denote by
I_{x,x^{\prime}}(R)
the set of all such
f
preserving
G
-structures. The functor
R\mapsto I_{x,x^{\prime}}(R)
is represented by a
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme
I_{x,x^{\prime}}
. If
x=x^{\prime}
, then we write
I_{x}
for
I_{x,x^{\prime}}
, which is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group. Two points
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
are said to be
isogenous
, if
I_{x,x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})\neq\emptyset
. This defines an equivalence relation on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, and the equivalence classes are called
isogeny classes
.

Remark 5.4.2
.

We explain that the above definition of isogeny classes is equivalent to the definition in
[
Kis17
]
.
The perfect symplectic form on
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
induces an isomorphism
\iota:V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}^{*}
, from which we obtain an element

s_{\mathrm{pol}}:=\iota\otimes\iota^{-1}\in\Hom(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}},V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}^{*})\otimes\Hom(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}^{*},V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})\subset V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}^{\otimes}.

Note that the scheme-theoretic stabilizer of
s_{\mathrm{pol}}
in the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-group scheme
{\rm GL}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})
is precisely
\mathrm{GSp}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})
. By our maximality assumption on
(s_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}}
in §
5.1.1
,
s_{\mathrm{pol}}
is one of the
s_{\alpha}
. On
{\mathcal{A}}
we have a canonical weak polarization (i.e., a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-orbit of
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-isogenies
{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee}
which can be represented by a polarization) arising from the moduli interpretation of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{K}_{p}\mathbb{K}^{p}_{1}}
. See
[
Kis17
, §1.3.4]
for details. This weak polarization induces an isomorphism
\jmath:\mathcal{V}^{p}(\mathcal{V}^{p})^{*}
, which is well defined up to
(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})^{\times}
. We can then form the tensor
\jmath\otimes\jmath^{-1}
on
\mathcal{V}^{p}
, which is well defined on the nose. Recall that
s_{\mathrm{pol},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
is defined to be
\mathbb L^{p}(s_{\mathrm{pol}})
via the identification (
5.1.5.2
). Since the level structure on the tower of Siegel Shimura varieties
\varprojlim_{\mathbb{K}^{p}}\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{K}_{p}\mathbb{K}^{p}}
respects weak polarizations, we have
s_{\mathrm{pol},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}=\jmath\otimes\jmath^{-1}
. It follows that each
f\in I_{x,x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})
necessarily respects the canonical weak polarizations on
{\mathcal{A}}_{x}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{x^{\prime}}
. Thus
x
and
x^{\prime}
are isogenous in our sense if and only if they satisfy the conditions in
[
Kis17
, Prop. 1.4.15]
. By that proposition, our definition of isogeny classes is equivalent to the definition in
[
Kis17
, §1.4.14]
. In particular, each isogeny class is stable under the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
.

5.5.
Connected components

5.5.1
.

Recall that
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
denotes
G(\mathbb{Q})\cap G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
, where
G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
is the preimage of
G^{\ad}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
in
G(\mathbb{R})
. As in
[
Kis17
, Lem. 3.6.2]
, we set

\pi(G):=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{p},

where
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}
denotes the closure of
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Since
K_{p}
is compact, the projection
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{p}
is a closed map. Hence the image of
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}
in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{p}
is closed. It easily follows that we have canonical isomorphisms

(5.5.1.1)

\displaystyle\pi(G)\cong\varprojlim_{K^{p}}G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{p}K^{p}\cong\varprojlim_{K^{p}}G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{p}K^{p},

where
K^{p}
runs through compact open subgroups of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

Lemma 5.5.2
.

The set
\pi(G)
has the natural structure of an abelian group. The natural map
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\pi(G)
is a surjective group homomorphism.

Proof.

By strong approximation (see
[
Del79
, §2.5.1]
),
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}
contains the image of
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Since this image is a normal subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
with abelian quotient (see
[
Del79
, §2.0.2]
), we see that
\pi(G)
is naturally an abelian group. The second statement follows from
[
Kis10
, Lem. 2.2.6]
(which uses that
K_{p}
is hyperspecial).
∎

Lemma 5.5.3
.

The subgroup
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}
of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is generated by
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
and the image of
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to G(\mathbb{A}_{f}).

Proof.

Write
Q
for the image of
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to G(\mathbb{A}_{f}).
We first show that
G(\mathbb{Q})Q
is closed in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. The proof is similar to the argument in
[
Del79
, §2.0.15]
. By Lemma
5.1.2
,
G^{\ab}
is a cuspidal torus. By Lemma
1.5.5
,
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q})
is discrete in
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. It follows that
G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q})Q
is an open subgroup of
G(\mathbb{Q})Q
. On the other hand, by
[
Del79
, Cor. 2.0.8]
,
G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q})Q
is closed in
G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Hence
G(\mathbb{Q})Q
is indeed closed in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(as it is locally closed).

Consequently,
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}\subset G(\mathbb{Q})Q
. Now by strong approximation applied to
G_{\mathrm{sc}}
and by the connectedness of
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{R})
(Cartan’s theorem), we know that
Q\subset G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}
. Thus we have reduced the proof of the lemma to showing that

G(\mathbb{Q})\cap G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}\subset G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}.

Let
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})\cap G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}
. By
[
Del79
, Cor. 2.0.7]
, there exists an open subgroup
U
of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
U\cap G(\mathbb{Q})\subset G(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. Since
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}
, there exists

g^{\prime}\in gU\cap G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
. Then
g^{-1}g^{\prime}\in U\cap G(\mathbb{Q})\subset G(\mathbb{R})^{+}
, and so
g\in g^{\prime}G(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. Since
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})
and
g^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
, we conclude that
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
as desired. ∎

5.5.4
.

Now consider the set (cf.
[
Del79
, §2.1.3]
)

\pi(G,X):=\varprojlim_{K^{p}}\pi_{0}(\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}(\mathbb{C}))=\varprojlim_{K^{p}}G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\pi_{0}(X)\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}K_{p}\right),

where
K^{p}
runs through compact open subgroups of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.
There is a natural map
\pi_{0}(X)\to\pi(G,X)
, induced by

\pi_{0}(X)\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(X)\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}),\quad C\longmapsto(C,1).

This allows us to speak of the image of an element of
X
or
\pi_{0}(X)
inside
\pi(G,X)
.

By (
5.5.1.1
) we see that
\pi(G,X)
is a
\pi(G)
-torsor. We have a natural
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant map

\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})\longrightarrow\pi(G,X),

defined as the inverse limit of the natural maps

\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p},\overline{\mathbb F}_{p}})\cong\pi_{0}(\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}(\mathbb{C})).

In particular, for each isogeny class
\mathscr{I}
in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
(see §
5.4
), we have a natural
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant map

c_{\mathscr{I}}:\mathscr{I}\longrightarrow\pi(G,X).

By the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariance and Lemma
5.5.2
, the above map is surjective.

In the following definition, recall that
\mathbb{A}^{*}_{f}:=\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\times\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}.

Definition 5.5.5
.

We set
\pi^{*}(G):=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
. This is naturally an abelian group, and is a quotient group of the subgroup
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})/G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
of
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
.

5.5.6
.

By Lemma
5.5.3
, the natural inclusion map
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\hookrightarrow G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
induces a group homomorphism
\pi(G)\to\pi^{*}(G)
. We define the
\pi^{*}(G)
-torsor
\pi^{*}(G,X)
to be the push-out of the
\pi(G)
-torsor
\pi(G,X)
along
\pi(G)\to\pi^{*}(G)
. Thus we have a canonical map
\pi(G,X)\to\pi^{*}(G,X)
, and we shall use this map to speak of the image of an element of
X
or
\pi_{0}(X)
inside
\pi^{*}(G,X)
.

5.6.
Uniformization on the geometric side

Definition 5.6.1
.

Let
\mathscr{I}
be a small connected groupoid category, i.e., a small category where all morphisms are isomorphisms and all objects are isomorphic. Let
H
be a group. By a
right
H
-torsor over
\mathscr{I}
, we mean a functor from
\mathscr{I}
to the category of right
H
-torsors. Let
Y
be such a functor, and let
x
be an object in
\mathscr{I}
. Then
\Aut(x)
naturally acts (on the left) on
Y(x)
via
H
-equivariant automorphisms, and the right
H
-set
\Aut(x)\backslash Y(x)
