is independent of
x
up to canonical
H
-isomorphism. We denote this right
H
-set by
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
.

5.6.2
.

For
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, let

Y(x):=Y_{p}(x)\times Y^{p}(x),

where
Y_{p}(x)
is the right
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{x})
, and
Y^{p}(x)
is the right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-torsor consisting of
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-module isomorphisms
V^{*}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)
taking
s_{\alpha}
to
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},x}
for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
. Thus
Y(x)
is a right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-torsor.
In fact, by Lemma
5.1.9
,
Y^{p}(x)
has a canonical trivialization.

The set
Y_{p}(x)
can be interpreted without reference to
\Upsilon_{x}
as follows. Recall from Definition
5.2.5
that we have a canonical isomorphism
\iota_{x}:\Upsilon_{x}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)
taking
\Upsilon_{x}(s_{\alpha})
to
s_{\alpha,0,x}.
Now each element of
Y(\Upsilon_{x})
gives rise to an isomorphism
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\Upsilon_{x}[1/p](V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
taking
s_{\alpha}
to
\Upsilon_{x}(s_{\alpha})
. Composing this with
\iota_{x}[1/p]
, we obtain an isomorphism
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)[1/p]
taking
s_{\alpha}
to
s_{\alpha,0,x}
. In this way,
Y(\Upsilon_{x})
is in canonical bijection with the set of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}
-linear isomorphisms
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}}\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)[1/p]
taking
s_{\alpha}
to
s_{\alpha,0,x}
. The
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-action on the latter set is given by the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-action on
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}}
.
Note that under this bijection, the subset
Y(\Upsilon_{x})^{\circ}\subset Y(\Upsilon_{x})
corresponds to those isomorphisms
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)[1/p]
that take
s_{\alpha}
to
s_{\alpha,0,x}
and map
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}
to
\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)
.

Now let
y=(y_{p},y^{p})\in Y(x)
. Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
be sufficiently divisible such that the image of
x
in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
comes from a
\mathbb{F}_{p^{n}}
-rational point
x_{n}
(and
\mathbb{F}_{p^{n}}\supset\mathcal{O}_{E,{\mathfrak{p}}}/{\mathfrak{p}}
). We then have the
p^{n}
-Frobenius acting on
\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)\cong\coh^{1}_{\et}({\mathcal{A}}_{x_{n},\overline{\mathbb F}_{p}},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, which fixes
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},x}
for all
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
. Via
y^{p}:V^{*}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)
, this automorphism of
\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)
corresponds to an automorphism of
V^{*}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
fixing all
s_{\alpha}
, namely an element
\gamma_{n}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. On the other hand, attached to the element
y_{p}\in Y_{p}(x)=Y(\Upsilon_{x})
we have the element
\delta_{y_{p}}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
as in §
4.2.5
. More concretely,
y_{p}
gives rise to an isomorphism
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)[1/p]
as in the above paragraph, and the Frobenius acting on the right hand side corresponds to
\delta_{y_{p}}\sigma
acting on the left hand side.

It is shown in
[
Kis17
, §2.3]
that up to replacing
n
by a multiple, the pair
(\gamma_{n},\delta)
extends to an element
(\gamma_{0,n},\gamma_{n},\delta)\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}
whose image in
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}
lies in
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
. The image of
(\gamma_{0,n},\gamma_{n},\delta)
in
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
depends only on
y
and not on the choices of
n
and
\gamma_{0,n}
. Thus we have obtained a map

(5.6.2.1)

\displaystyle Y(x)\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv},\quad y\mapsto{\mathfrak{k}}(y).

This map is easily seen to be
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariant. (See §
5.3.5
for the right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-action on
{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
.)

5.6.3
.

Now let
\mathscr{I}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
be an isogeny class. We view
\mathscr{I}
as a small groupoid, where the set of morphisms between
x
and
x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{I}
is given by
I_{x,x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})
defined in §
5.4
. Then
\mathscr{I}
is a connected groupoid category. For each
f\in I_{x,x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})
, we have isomorphisms
f_{\mathcal{V}^{p}}:\mathcal{V}^{p}(x^{\prime})\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)
and
f_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}:\mathcal{V}_{0}(x^{\prime})[1/p]\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)[1/p]
as in §
5.4
. By definition,
f_{\mathcal{V}^{p}}
takes
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},x^{\prime}}
to
s_{\alpha,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p},x}
, and so it induces a
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant bijection

Y^{p}(f):Y^{p}(x)Y^{p}(x^{\prime}).

Similarly,
f_{\mathcal{V}_{0}}
takes
s_{\alpha,0,x^{\prime}}
to
s_{\alpha,0,x}
, and so it induces an isomorphism
\Upsilon_{x}[1/p]\Upsilon_{x^{\prime}}[1/p]
by Lemma
4.2.4
. By functoriality, this then induces a
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-equivariant bijection

Y_{p}(f):Y_{p}(x)Y_{p}(x^{\prime}).

We define
Y(f)
to be the bijection

(Y_{p}(f),Y^{p}(f)):Y(x)Y(x^{\prime}).

The associations
\mathscr{I}\ni x\mapsto Y(x)
and
I_{x,x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})\ni f\mapsto Y(f)
define a functor from
\mathscr{I}
to the category of right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-torsors. In other words, we have obtained a right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-torsor
Y
over
\mathscr{I}
in the sense of Definition
5.6.1
. As in that definition, we obtain a right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-set
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
, together with canonical isomorphisms

(5.6.3.1)

\displaystyle I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y(x)\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})

for all
x\in\mathscr{I}
.

For
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{I}
and
f\in I_{x,x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})
, we claim that the bijection
Y(f):Y(x)Y(x^{\prime})
commutes with the maps
Y(x)\to{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
and
Y(x^{\prime})\to{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
as in (
5.6.2.1
). In fact, since
Y_{p}(f):Y(\Upsilon_{x})Y(\Upsilon_{x^{\prime}})
is induced by an isomorphism
\Upsilon_{x}[1/p]\Upsilon_{x^{\prime}}[1/p]
, it commutes with the maps
Y_{p}(x)\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),y\mapsto\delta_{y}
and
Y_{p}(x^{\prime})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),y\mapsto\delta_{y}
in §
4.2.5
. For sufficiently divisible
n
, the isogeny
f
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{p^{n}}
, and so the element
\gamma_{n}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
attached to any
y\in Y^{p}(x)
is equal to its counterpart attached to
Y^{p}(f)(y)\in Y^{p}(x^{\prime})
. Our claim follows.

It follows that for each
x\in\mathscr{I}
, the map
Y(x)\to{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
descends to a map

I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y(x)\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv},

which is independent of
x
if we identify the left hand side with
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
as in (
5.6.3.1
). Hence we have obtained a canonical map

(5.6.3.2)

\displaystyle\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv},\quad\bar{y}\longmapsto{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}).

For
x\in\mathscr{I}
and
y\in Y(x)
, the left
I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})
-action on the right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-torsor
Y(x)
gives rise to a homomorphism

(5.6.3.3)

\displaystyle\iota_{y}:I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})

defined by

j\cdot y=y\cdot\iota_{y}(j),\quad\forall j\in I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}).

Thus we have a map
\iota_{y,v}:I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
for each prime
v\neq p
, and a map
\iota_{y,p}:I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
. These maps have the following extra structures, by the results in
[
Kis17
, §2.3]
. Let
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(y)\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
be a representative of the image of
{\mathfrak{k}}(y)\in{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}.
Let
(I_{0},(I_{v})_{v},(\eta_{v})_{v})
be the datum attached to
{\mathfrak{k}}
as in §
5.3.2
. For each prime
v\neq p
, the map
\iota_{y,v}
comes from an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-groups
I_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}I_{v}
, which we still denote by
\iota_{y,v}
. Also, the map
\iota_{y,p}
comes from an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-groups
I_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}I_{p}
, which we still denote by
\iota_{y,p}
. (Here recall that
I_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
for
v\neq p
and
I_{p}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
.) In particular, the map (
5.6.3.3
) is injective. Moreover, the isomorphisms
\iota_{y,v}
for all primes
v
can be extended to a refinement of
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
of the form
(I_{x},\iota_{0},(\iota_{y,v})_{v})
. (See Definition
5.3.3
for the notion of a refinement.) This in particular implies that
I_{x}
is a reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
I_{x,\mathbb{R}}
is anisotropic mod center.

5.6.4
.

Let
\mathscr{I}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
be an isogeny class. Set

\mathscr{I}^{*}=\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}).

Then we have a natural right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action on
\mathscr{I}^{*}
. It is immediate that the map (
5.6.3.2
) induces a map

(5.6.4.1)

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{*}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{KT}}=({\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}).

For each
x\in\mathscr{I}
, inside

Y(x)/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\cong(Y(\Upsilon_{x})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}))\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})

we have a canonical base point, whose first coordinate is given by the image of the
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{x})^{\circ}\subset Y(\Upsilon_{x})
, and whose second coordinate is
1\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. This base point determines an element
x^{*}
of
\mathscr{I}^{*}\cong I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y(x)/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
(where the canonical isomorphism is induced by (
5.6.3.1
)). Sending
x
to
x^{*}
, we have obtained a map

(5.6.4.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\longrightarrow\mathscr{I}^{*}.

By
[
Kis17
, Prop. 2.1.3]
, the map (
5.6.4.2
) is injective and
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant. The image of (
5.6.4.2
) is described as follows. As in §
2.4.1
, we choose
\mu_{X}\in\bbmu_{X}(E_{\mathfrak{p}})
such that
\mu_{X}
extends to a cocharacter of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}_{E,\mathfrak{p}}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E,{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Let

\upsilon=\sigma(-\mu_{X})

Let
x\in\mathscr{I}
. Recall from Definition
4.2.7
that inside
Y(\Upsilon_{x})
we have the
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-stable subset
Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
, and that we denote the quotient
Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
by
X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
. The subset
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})p^{\upsilon}{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
depends on
\upsilon
only via its
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-conjugacy class, and the latter is independent of the choice of
\mu_{X}
. Hence
Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
and
X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
are independent of the choice of
\mu_{X}
(cf. the independence of
\mu_{X}
in §
2.4.1
). Moreover, if
x^{\prime}
is another element of
\mathscr{I}
and if
f\in I_{x,x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})
, we have seen in §
5.6.3
that the isomorphism
Y_{p}(f):Y(\Upsilon_{x})Y(\Upsilon_{x^{\prime}})
commutes with the maps
Y(\Upsilon_{x})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),y\mapsto\delta_{y}
and
Y(\Upsilon_{x^{\prime}})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}),y\mapsto\delta_{y}
. It follows that
Y_{p}(f)
induces a bijection
Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x^{\prime}})
. Therefore inside
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
we have a canonical subset of the form

\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}\cong I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})\times Y^{p}(x),

which is independent of the choice of
x
.

Proposition 5.6.5
.

The image of (
5.6.4.2
) is equal to the image of
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}
under the projection
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\to\mathscr{I}^{*}
.

Proof.

By
[
Kis17
, §1.4.1]
, we have

(5.6.5.1)

\displaystyle Y(\Upsilon_{x})^{\circ}\subset Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x}),\quad\forall x\in\mathscr{I}.

It follows that the image of (
5.6.4.2
) is contained in
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}
. The reverse containment follows from the definition of isogeny classes in
[
Kis17
, §1.4.14]
, which we have seen is equivalent to our definition (see §
5.4
).
∎

Remark 5.6.6
.

Keep the setting of §
5.6.4
.
From Proposition
5.6.5
, we see that the choice of an element
x\in\mathscr{I}
gives rise to a bijection

\displaystyle I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})\times Y^{p}(x)\mathscr{I}.

As explained in Remark
4.2.8
, if we choose
y\in Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
, then
X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
is identified with the affine Deligne–Lusztig set
X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})
. The natural action of
I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})
on
X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
corresponds to the natural action of
\iota_{y,p}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}))
on
X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})
. (Recall from §
5.6.3
that
\iota_{y,p}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))
is the
\sigma
-centralizer of
\delta_{y}
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. This group acts on
X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})
by left multiplication). Similarly, under the canonical identification
Y^{p}(x)\cong G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, the natural action of
I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})
on
X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})\times Y^{p}(x)
corresponds to the left-multiplication action of
\iota_{y}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}))
on
X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Thus we obtain a bijection

(5.6.6.1)

\displaystyle\iota_{y}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}))\backslash X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\mathscr{I}

associated with the choices of
x
and
y
.
This bijection is the same as the map
[
Kis17
, (2.1.4)]
.

.

5.6.8
.

It follows from the Cartan decomposition that the Kottwitz homomorphism
w_{G}:G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to\pi_{1}(G)
induces a bijection

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\pi_{1}(G)

and an injection

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(G)^{\Gamma_{p}}=\pi_{1}(G)^{\sigma}.

By
[
Kot97
, §7.7]
(cf.
[
Kis17
, Lem. 1.2.3]
), the above injection is also a bijection. It follows that the natural map

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\longrightarrow G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})

is injective, and that its image is precisely the preimage of
\pi_{1}(G)^{\sigma}
in

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}).

From this observation, we see that each element
r_{p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
satisfying
w_{G}(r_{p})\in\pi_{1}(G)^{\sigma}
uniquely determines an element
r_{p}^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Lemma 5.6.9
.

Let
\mathscr{I}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
be an isogeny class. Let
y\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
. Let
r=(r^{p},r_{p})\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})=G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, and let
y^{\prime}=yr\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
. Assume that both
y
and
y^{\prime}
lie in
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}
. Then the following statements hold.

(i)

The element
r_{p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
satisfies
w_{G}(r_{p})\in\pi_{1}(G)^{\sigma}
. In particular,
r_{p}
determines an element
r_{p}^{\prime}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
as in §
5.6.8
. By Lemma
5.5.3
, the image of
(r^{p},r_{p}^{\prime})
in
\pi(G)
is well defined. We denote this image by
r_{\pi(G)}
.

(ii)

The images of
y
and
y^{\prime}
under the composite map

\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}\longrightarrow\mathscr{I}\xrightarrow{c_{\mathscr{I}}}\pi(G,X)

differ by the element
r_{\pi(G)}\in\pi(G)
in part (i). More precisely,
c_{\mathscr{I}}(y^{\prime})=c_{\mathscr{I}}(y)\cdot r_{\pi(G)}
.

Proof.

Since (
5.6.4.2
) is
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant, so is the map
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}\to\mathscr{I}
. The map
c_{\mathscr{I}}
is also
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant. The proof is thus reduced to the case
r^{p}=1
. In the following we assume
r^{p}=1
, and write
r
for
r_{p}
.

Let
x\in\mathscr{I}
be the image of
y
. Then
under the identification

\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\cong I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y_{p}(x)\times Y^{p}(x)=I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y(\Upsilon_{x})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}),

the element
y
is represented by
(y_{p},1)\in Y(\Upsilon_{x})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
with
y_{p}\in Y(\Upsilon_{x})^{\circ}
. The element
y^{\prime}
is represented by
(y_{p}r,1)
.

Let
\delta=\delta_{y_{p}}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Let
\upsilon
be as in §
5.6.4
. By (
5.6.5.1
), we have
y_{p}\in Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
. Since
y^{\prime}\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}
, we also have
y_{p}r\in Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
by the discussion in §
5.6.4
. As in Remark
4.2.8
, we have an identification
X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})=Y_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
, under which the image of
y_{p}
(resp.
y_{p}r
) in
X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})
corresponds to the element
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
(resp.
r{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
) of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Clearly all elements of
X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
have the same image under the composite map

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\xrightarrow{w_{G}}\pi_{1}(G)\xrightarrow{1-\sigma}\pi_{1}(G).

Since
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
and
r{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
both lie in
X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
, statement (i) follows.

Let
C
denote the composite map

X_{\upsilon}(\delta)\cong X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})\xrightarrow{z\mapsto(z,1)}I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cong\mathscr{I}\xrightarrow{c_{\mathscr{I}}}\pi(G,X).

We are left to show that
C(r)
and
C(1)
differ by
r_{\pi(G)}
.

We follow
[
Kis17
, §§1.2–1.4]
closely. Let
\mathscr{G}
be the
p
-divisible group
{\mathcal{A}}_{x}[p^{\infty}]
over
\overline{\mathbb F}_{p}
. By
[
Kis17
, §1.2.16, Lem. 1.2.18]
, for any finite extension
K/\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and any
{\mathcal{G}}_{\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p}}
-adapted lifting
\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}
of
\mathscr{G}
to
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, there is an associated map (which is denoted by
g\mapsto g_{0}
in
loc. cit.
)

f_{\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}}:G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\longrightarrow X_{\upsilon}(\delta).

In fact, in the language of §
4.4
, the choice of
\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}
gives rise to an element
[\rho_{\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}}]\in\Crys_{G}
, and the map
f_{\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}}
by definition sends each
\lambda\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
to the element
\lambda_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
associated with
[\rho_{\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}}]
and
\lambda
as in §
4.4.14
.

By
[
Kis17
, Prop. 1.2.23]
, we can choose
\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}
such that the composite map

G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{f_{\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}}}X_{\upsilon}(\delta)\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(X_{\upsilon}(\delta))

is surjective. Here
\pi_{0}(X_{\upsilon}(\delta))
is the set of
connected components
of the affine Deligne–Lusztig set
X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
, defined in
[
CKV15
]
(cf.
[
Kis17
, §1.2]
). We fix such a choice of
\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}
, and choose
g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
f_{\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}}(g)
lies in the same connected component of
X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
as
r
. In what follows we write
g_{0}
for
f_{\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}}(g)\in X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
.

By
[
Kis17
, Cor. 1.4.12]
, we have

C(g_{0})=C(1)\cdot g.

Here on the right hand side we again write
g
for the natural image of
g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
in
\pi^{*}(G)
.
Thus we are left to show that

C(r)=C(g_{0})\cdot g^{-1}r_{\pi(G)}.

Since
r
and
g_{0}
lie in the same connected component of
X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
, we have
C(r)=C(g_{0})
.
32
32

32

In fact, if one modifies the setting of
[
Kis17
, §1.4.10]
by replacing the integral model of the Shimura variety with a connected component of the geometric special fiber, then exactly the same argument there shows that the map
X_{\upsilon}(\delta)\cong X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})\to I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X_{\upsilon}(\Upsilon_{x})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cong\mathscr{I}
sends each connected component of
X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
into one fiber of
c_{\mathscr{I}}
.
Hence it suffices to show that the image of
g
in
\pi(G)
is equal to
r_{\pi(G)}
. For this, it suffices to show that
w_{G}(g)=w_{G}(r)
.

The fact that
r
and
g_{0}
lie in the same connected component of
X_{\upsilon}(\delta)
implies that
w_{G}(r)=w_{G}(g_{0})
, in view of
[
CKV15
, Lem. 2.3.6]
. By
[
Kis17
, Lem. 1.2.18]
or Proposition
4.4.15
, we have
w_{G}(g_{0})=w_{G}(g)
. Hence
w_{G}(g)=w_{G}(r)
as desired. ∎

Proposition 5.6.10
.

Let
\mathscr{I}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
be an isogeny class. There is a unique map

c_{\mathscr{I}}^{*}:\mathscr{I}^{*}\longrightarrow\pi^{*}(G,X)

such that the diagram

\textstyle{\mathscr{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref {eq:I to I^*})}
\scriptstyle{c_{\mathscr{I}}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{I}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\mathscr{I}}^{*}}
\textstyle{\pi(G,X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}(G,X)}

commutes, and such that the composite

\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\to\mathscr{I}^{*}\xrightarrow{c_{\mathscr{I}}^{*}}\pi^{*}(G,X)

is
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariant. Here
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
acts on
\pi^{*}(G,X)
via the natural homomorphism
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G)
.

Proof.

We have
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}\cdot G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})=\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
, from which the uniqueness of
c_{\mathscr{I}}^{*}
follows. To show the existence, we fix
x\in\mathscr{I}
. The choice of
x
gives an identification
I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y(x)\cong\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
. As discussed in §
5.6.3
, the left action of
I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})
on
Y(x)
can be reconstructed as the composition of the inversion map
I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\to I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})
, the embedding
\iota_{y}:I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\to G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
(see (
5.6.3.3
)), and the right multiplication of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
on itself. By Lemma
5.6.9
, the composed homomorphism
I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{\iota_{y}}G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G)
is trivial. By this fact and by Lemma
5.6.9
, we know that the map
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}\to\pi^{*}(G,X)
, obtained as the composition of the map
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})^{\natural}\to\pi(G,X)
considered in Lemma
5.6.9
and the natural map
\pi(G,X)\to\pi^{*}(G,X)
, extends to a
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariant map
c:\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\to\pi^{*}(G,X)
. Now
c
necessarily factors through the projection
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\to\mathscr{I}^{*}=\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, because
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
acts trivially on
\pi^{*}(G,X)
. This finishes the proof. ∎

5.7.
Special points on the geometric side

5.7.1
.

Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
be a special point datum. From
{\mathfrak{s}}
we can produce a canonical element

x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})

as follows. For each neat compact open subgroup
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the subgroup
i^{-1}(K)\subset T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is neat compact open. We have the Shimura variety
\Sh_{i^{-1}(K)}(T,h)
, which is a zero-dimensional
E(T,h)
-scheme. We write
\Sh_{K}(T,h)
for
\Sh_{i^{-1}(K)}(T,h)
, while we still write
\Sh_{K}
for
\Sh_{K}(G,X)
. We write
\mu
for the Hodge cocharacter
\mu_{h}\in X_{*}(T)
associated with
h
. The reflex field of the Shimura datum
(T,h)
is by definition the field of definition of
\mu
, and we shall denote it by
E_{\mu}\subset\mathbb{C}
in accordance with the notation in §
4.3.13
.

We have
E\subset E_{\mu}
. Let
K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}
.
The morphism
i:(T,h)\to(G,X)
between Shimura data induces an
E_{\mu}
-scheme morphism

(5.7.1.1)

\displaystyle\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}(T,h)\longrightarrow\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}\times_{\Spec E}\Spec E_{\mu}.

For each neat compact open subgroup
U\subset T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
we have the finite abelian extension
E_{\mu,U}/E_{\mu}
as defined in §
4.3.13
. By the explicit description in §
1.5.3
of the Shimura varieties associated with
(T,h)
, we know that all geometric connected components of the
E_{\mu}
-scheme
\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}(T,h)
have the same field of definition
E_{\mu,i^{-1}(K_{p}K^{p})}
. To simplify notation, we write
E_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}}
for
E_{\mu,i^{-1}(K_{p}K^{p})}
. The restriction of (
5.7.1.1
) to the neutral geometric connected component, namely the one corresponding to the neutral
\mathbb{C}
-point

1\in\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}(T,h)(\mathbb{C})=T(\mathbb{Q})\backslash T(\mathbb{A}_{f})/i^{-1}(K_{p}K^{p}),

gives rise to an
E
-scheme morphism

\widetilde{x}_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}}:\Spec E_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}}\longrightarrow\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}.

Let
F_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}}
be the topological closure of
E_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}}
inside
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
(with respect to the fixed embedding
E_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
). We thus have a tower of field extensions
(F_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}})_{K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}}
, and we let
F_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
be the union of these fields. Note that for every place
w
of
E_{\mu}
above
p
, the kernel of the map

\mathcal{O}_{E_{\mu},w}^{\times}\to E_{\mu}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb A_{E_{\mu}}^{\times}\xrightarrow{(\ref {eq:ST law})}T(\mathbb{Q})\backslash T(\mathbb A_{f})\to T(\mathbb{Q})\backslash T(\mathbb A_{f})/i^{-1}(K_{p}K^{p})

is independent of
K^{p}
, which follows easily from the discreteness of
T(\mathbb{Q})
in
T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(by Lemma
1.5.5
and Lemma
5.1.2
(iii)) and the neatness of
i^{-1}(K^{p})
. This implies that the transition maps in the tower
(F_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}})_{K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}}
are unramified field extensions. In particular,
\mathcal{O}_{F_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}
is a regular local ring.

The morphisms
\widetilde{x}_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}}
are compatible when
K^{p}
varies in
\mathscr{K}^{p}
, so they give rise to a morphism of
E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
-schemes

(5.7.1.2)

\displaystyle\Spec F_{{\mathfrak{s}}}=\varprojlim_{K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}}\Spec F_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}_{K_{p},E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}.

Since
\mathcal{O}_{F_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}
is a regular local ring, the extension property of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}
implies that (
5.7.1.2
) extends to a unique
\mathcal{O}_{E,{\mathfrak{p}}}
-morphism
\Spec\mathcal{O}_{F_{\mathfrak{s}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}.
Passing to the special fiber we obtain a point

x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p}).

5.7.2
.

Keep the setting and notation of §
5.7.1
. We write
K_{1}
for
K_{p}K^{p}_{1}
as in §
5.1.3
. Fix
{\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
. To simplify notation, we write
F
for
F_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}_{1}}
, and write
\widetilde{x}\in\Sh_{K_{1}}(F)
for the point induced by
\widetilde{x}_{{\mathfrak{s}},K^{p}_{1}}
. We also simply write
x
for
x_{\mathfrak{s}}
. By construction, the image of
x
in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}
is the specialization of
\widetilde{x}
.

We write
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
for the stalk of
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}
at
\widetilde{x}
, viewed as a point in
\Sh_{K_{1}}(\mathbb{C})
. Thus
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})\cong\coh^{1}_{B}({\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x}}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Q})
. For each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
, we write
s_{\alpha,B,\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{x}}
for the tensor over
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
induced by the tensor
s_{\alpha,B,\mathbb{Q}}
over
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}
(see §
5.1.5
).

Since
\widetilde{x}
comes from the neutral point
1\in\Sh_{K_{1}}(T,h)(\mathbb{C})
, there is a canonical
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism

\mathrm{triv}_{\mathfrak{s}}:V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x}).

This satisfies the following properties:

(i)

\mathrm{triv}_{\mathfrak{s}}
takes
s_{\alpha}
to
s_{\alpha,B,\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{x}}
for each
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
.

(ii)

\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
restricts to a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-module isomorphism
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\coh^{1}_{B}({\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x}}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})
.

(iii)

We view
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
as a faithful representation of
T
via
\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
and the representation
T\xrightarrow{i}G\to{\rm GL}(V^{*}{Q})
. Then the action of the Mumford–Tate group of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x}}
on
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
is via an embedding into
T
. The Hodge structure on
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
is given by
h:\mathbb S\to T_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Using the comparison isomorphisms, we obtain from
\mathrm{triv}_{\mathfrak{s}}
canonical isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}},p}

\displaystyle:V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}

\displaystyle:V^{*}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\mathcal{V}^{p}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}).

We use these isomorphisms to define a
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representation on
\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})[1/p]
and a
T_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
-representation on
\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)
.
The isomorphism
\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}},p}
induces an isomorphism

(5.7.2.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\widetilde{x}}

which lies in the canonical
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-conjugacy class of such isomorphisms as in Lemma
5.1.8
. (Here
{\mathcal{G}}_{\widetilde{x}}
is defined as in §
5.1.7
.) The isomorphism
\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
coincides with the stalk at
x
of the canonical isomorphism in Lemma
5.1.9
.

Since the Mumford–Tate group of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x}}
is contained in
T
, we know that
{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{x}}
has complex multiplication by some CM field
H
, and that the action of
H^{\times}
on
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
induces embeddings of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic groups

T\hookrightarrow\Res_{H/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\hookrightarrow{\rm GL}(\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})).

In particular, we have a canonical embedding of
T
into the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic group of self-isogenies of
{\mathcal{A}}_{x}
. This embedding factors through
I_{x}
, since the action of
T
on
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
fixes
s_{\alpha,B,\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{x}}
for all
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
. Thus we have a canonical embedding

(5.7.2.2)

\displaystyle T\hookrightarrow I_{x}.

5.7.3
.

Keep the setting and notation of §
5.7.1
and §
5.7.2
.

Analogous to the functor (
5.1.4.1
), we have a faithful exact
\otimes
-functor

\displaystyle\mathbb L^{\prime}

\displaystyle:{\mathrm{Rep}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}T\longrightarrow\Lisse_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\Sh_{K_{1}}(T,h)).

Analogous to (
5.1.5.2
), we have a canonical isomorphism between the pull-back to
\Sh_{K_{1}}(T,h)_{E}
of
\mathcal{V}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and (the pull-back of)
\mathbb L^{\prime}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. In particular, we have a canonical
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
-equivariant
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear isomorphism

(5.7.3.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})[1/p]\cong\mathbb L^{\prime}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})(\widetilde{x}),

where the right hand side denotes the stalk of
\mathbb L^{\prime}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
at
\widetilde{x}
viewed as a
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
-point of
\Sh_{K_{1}}(T,h)
. Using (
5.7.3.1
), for each
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-invariant tensor
r_{\beta}\in(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\otimes}
, we obtain a tensor
r_{\beta,p,\widetilde{x}}
over
\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})[1/p]
induced by the tensor
\mathbb L^{\prime}(r_{\beta})
over
\mathbb L^{\prime}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. It is not hard to see that the isomorphism

\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}},p}[1/p]:V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})[1/p]

takes each
r_{\beta}
to
r_{\beta,p,\widetilde{x}}
. It follows that the image of the embedding

T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\longrightarrow{\rm GL}(\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})[1/p])

coincides with the stabilizer of all
r_{\beta,p,\widetilde{x}}
. In particular, the
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
-action on
\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})[1/p]
is via a homomorphism

\rho_{(T,h)}:{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)\longrightarrow T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

We now give an explicit description of
\rho_{(T,h)}
. Let
\mu=\mu_{h}\in X_{*}(T)
, and let
U=i^{-1}(K_{1})\subset T(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. As in §
4.3.13
, we have the field
E_{\mu,U,v}
, and the homomorphism

r(\mu)_{U,p,\mathrm{loc}}:{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/E_{\mu,U,v})\longrightarrow T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Note that
E_{\mu,U,v}\subset F
. The homomorphism
\rho_{(T,h)}
is equal to the restriction of
r(\mu)_{U,p,\mathrm{loc}}
to
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
. This fact is just another way to look at the Shimura–Taniyama reciprocity law, and it easily follows from the explicit description of the tower of Shimura varieties attached to
(T,h)
as in §
1.5.3
. Also cf.
[
Pin92a
, §(5.5)]
.

Note that the hypothesis on
U
in Proposition
4.3.14
is satisfied in the current situation. By that proposition and by the above discussion, we know that
\rho_{(T,h)}
is crystalline, and that the element
[\rho_{(T,h)}]\in\Crys_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is equal to
\mathscr{M}_{T}^{-1}(-\mu_{h})\in\Mot_{T}
.

5.7.4
.

Keep the setting and notation of §§
5.7.1
–
5.7.3
. From
[\rho_{(T,h)}]\in\Crys_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
, we obtain an element
[i\circ\rho_{(T,h)}]\in\Crys_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
.
Denote by
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}
the connected Néron model of
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Applying §
4.5.1
to
[\rho]=[i\circ\rho_{(T,h)}]
and
[\rho_{T}]=[\rho_{(T,h)}]
, we obtain
\Upsilon_{[\rho(T,h)]}\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}\text{-}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
and
\Upsilon_{[i\circ\rho_{(T,h)}]}\in{\mathcal{G}}\text{-}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
. To simplify notation we denote them by
\Upsilon_{(T,h)}
and
\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
respectively. As in §
4.5.1
, we have a natural injection from the
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})
to the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}})
.

As before we write
x
for the point
x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
. We have a canonical isomorphism
\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}}\cong\Upsilon_{x}
, which we now explain.

Letting
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
act on
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
via

{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)\xrightarrow{\rho_{(T,h)}}T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\xrightarrow{i}G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to{\rm GL}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}),

we have a canonical
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}/F)
-equivariant isomorphism
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\cong\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})[1/p]
induced by
\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}},p}
. Since this isomorphism takes
V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
to
\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x})
, it induces an isomorphism of integral
F
-isocrystals

(5.7.4.1)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})M_{\cris}(\mathcal{V}_{p}(\widetilde{x}))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F_{0}}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}},

where
F_{0}
denotes the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
F
as usual. The isomorphism (
5.7.4.1
) takes the tensor
\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}}(s_{\alpha})
over the left hand side to the tensor
M_{\cris}(s_{\alpha,p,\widetilde{x}})
over the right hand side, since
\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}},p}
takes
s_{\alpha}
to
s_{\alpha,p,\widetilde{x}}
. Now the right hand side of (
5.7.4.1
) is identified with
\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)
via the integral comparison isomorphism (
5.2.2.1
), and under this identification
s_{\alpha,p,\widetilde{x}}
is identified with
s_{\alpha,0,x}
by the discussion in §
5.2.2
. Hence we obtain a canonical isomorphism of integral
F
-isocrystals

(5.7.4.2)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\mathcal{V}_{0}(x),

which takes
\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}}(s_{\alpha})
to
s_{\alpha,0,x}
for all
\alpha\in\bm{\alpha}
. It then follows from Lemma
5.2.4
that there is a unique isomorphism

(5.7.4.3)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}}\Upsilon_{x}

in
{\mathcal{G}}\text{-}{\mathrm{Isoc}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}^{\circ}
taking the isomorphism (
5.7.4.2
) to the isomorphism
\iota_{x}:\Upsilon_{x}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)
.

Via (
5.7.4.3
), we have a canonical isomorphism
Y(\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}})\cong Y(\Upsilon_{x})
. Composing this with the canonical injection

Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})\hookrightarrow Y(\Upsilon_{{\mathfrak{s}}})
, we obtain an injection

(5.7.4.4)

\displaystyle Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})\hookrightarrow Y(\Upsilon_{x}).

Recall from §
5.6.2
that
Y(x)=Y_{p}(x)\times Y^{p}(x)
, where
Y_{p}(x)=Y(\Upsilon_{x})
, and
Y^{p}(x)
is canonically identified with
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. We write
1
for the canonical base point of
Y^{p}(x)
.

Recall from §
4.2.5
that inside the
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})
we have the subset of integral points
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})^{\circ}
, which is a
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-torsor.

Definition 5.7.5
.

Let
{\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
, and let
x=x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
. Let
y_{p}\in Y_{p}(x)
be an element of the image of
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})^{\circ}
under (
5.7.4.4
), and let
y=(y_{p},1)\in Y(x)
. We call
y
an
integral special point
33
33

33

The images of the integral special points under
Y(x)\to\mathscr{I}^{*}
, where
\mathscr{I}
is the isogeny class of
x
, are called “integral special points” in the Introduction.
associated with
{\mathfrak{s}}
.

In the next proposition we prove fundamental properties of integral special points.

Proposition 5.7.6
.

Let
x=x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
and let
y=(y_{p},1)\in Y(x)
be an integral special point as in Definition
5.7.5
. The following statements hold.

(i)

Let
\delta_{y_{p}}
be the image of
y_{p}
under the map
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})\to T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),z\mapsto\delta_{z}
as in §
4.2.5
. Then
\delta_{y_{p}}
lies in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
, and the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class of
\delta_{y_{p}}
corresponds to the image of
-\mu_{h}
in
X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
under the bijection (
4.3.9.1
).

(ii)

By (i), the element
\delta_{y_{p}}
satisfies the assumptions on
\delta_{T}
in §
5.3.9
. By the construction in §
5.3.9
, we obtain an element
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{y_{p}})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
. The image
{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
of
y
under the map
Y(x)\to{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
as in (
5.6.2.1
) is equal to the image of
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{y_{p}})
.

(iii)

Let
\mathscr{I}
be the isogeny class of
x
. The image of
y
under the composite map

Y(x)\to I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y(x)/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\cong\mathscr{I}^{*}\xrightarrow{c^{*}_{\mathscr{I}}}\pi^{*}(G,X)

is equal to the image of
i\circ h\in X
in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
. (See Proposition
5.6.10
for
c^{*}_{\mathscr{I}}
.)

Proof.

We have seen in §
5.7.3
that
[\rho_{(T,h)}]=\mathscr{M}_{T}^{-1}(-\mu_{h})\in\Mot_{T}
. Statement (i) follows from this fact and Corollary
4.4.12
.

For (ii), tracing the definitions we see that the
\delta
-component of
{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
is
i(\delta_{y_{p}})
. By the Shimura–Taniyama reciprocity law, for sufficiently divisible
n
the geometric
p^{n}
-Frobenius in
I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})
is given by an element of
T(\mathbb{Q})
, if we view
T
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
I_{x}
as in (
5.7.2.2
). This element has to be
\gamma_{0,T,n}
introduced in §
5.3.9
. (Recall that
\gamma_{0,T,n}
depends only on
(T,h)
and
n
.) Now the composition

T_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\xrightarrow{(\ref {eq:T to I_x})}I_{x,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\to{\rm GL}(\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)){\rm GL}(V^{*}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}),

where the last isomorphism is induced by
\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathfrak{s}},\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}:V^{*}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\mathcal{V}^{p}(x)
, is equal to the base change to
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
of
i:T\to G
. Hence the
\gamma
-component of
{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
is represented by
i(\gamma_{0,T,n})
at level
n
. Statement (ii) follows.

For (iii), let
y_{p}^{\prime}
be an element of
Y(\Upsilon_{x})^{\circ}\subset Y(\Upsilon_{x})=Y_{p}(x)
, and set
y^{\prime}:=(y_{p}^{\prime},1)\in Y_{p}(x)\times Y^{p}(x)=Y(x).
Then the image of
y^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{I}^{*}
is equal to the image of
x
under the canonical injection
\mathscr{I}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{I}^{*}
. See §
5.6.4
for details. By the construction of
x=x_{\mathfrak{s}}
in §
5.7.1
, for each
K^{p}\in\mathscr{K}^{p}
the image of
x
in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
is the reduction of a point of
\Sh_{K_{p}K^{p}}
whose induced
\mathbb{C}
-point is the image of
(i\circ h,1)\in X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Hence
x
and
i\circ h\in X
have the same image in
\pi(G,X)
(cf. §
5.5.4
), and
a fortiori
they have the same image in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
. Therefore we only need find
y^{\prime}
as above such that
y
and
y^{\prime}
have the same image in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
. By the second statement in Proposition
4.5.2
, we can find
y^{\prime}_{p}
such that it lies in the
G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-orbit of
y_{p}
. But then
y
and
y^{\prime}
have the same image in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
, since the map
Y(x)\to\pi^{*}(G,X)
in question is
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariant, and since the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-action on
\pi^{*}(G,X)
restricts to the trivial action of
G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
.
∎

5.7.7
.

Let
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, and let
T
be a maximal torus in the
\mathbb{Q}
-reductive group
I_{x}
. For each
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
, we define
\bar{\mu}^{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\in X_{*}(T)
as in §
3.3.5
(with respect to the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-torus
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
).

Let
y\in Y(x)
. Then we have a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-isomorphism
\iota_{y,p}:I_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}I_{p}
, where
I_{p}\subset\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
is the reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
associated with
{\mathfrak{k}}(y)\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
. Let
\delta_{y}
be the
\delta
-component of
{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
, and let
\nu_{\delta_{y}}
be the Newton cocharacter of
\delta_{y}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
Then
\nu_{\delta_{y}}
can be viewed as a central fractional cocharacter of
I_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
via
\iota_{y,p}
. In particular we can view
\nu_{\delta_{y}}
as an element of
X_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. We say that a cocharacter
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
is
x
-admissible
, if
the composition

(5.7.7.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{m,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\xrightarrow{\mu}T_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{y,p}}I_{p,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}

lies in
\bbmu_{X}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
, and if
\bar{\mu}^{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}=\nu_{\delta_{y}}
as elements of
X_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. Here the map
I_{p,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
is induced by the map
(\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G)_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
induced by the inclusion
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
. It is straightforward to check that the definition of
x
-admissible cocharacters is independent of the choice of
y
. (Note the analogy between this definition and the definition in §
3.3.8
.) The following theorem is the geometric analogue of Theorem
3.3.9
.

Theorem 5.7.8
.

Let
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, and let
T
be a maximal torus in
I_{x}
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. The following statements hold.

(i)

There exists
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
that is
x
-admissible.

(ii)

Let
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
be an
x
-admissible cocharacter. Then there exists a special point datum of the form
{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i,h)
satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

\mu=\mu_{h}
.

(b)

The points
x
and
x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
lie in the same isogeny class. Moreover, there exists
g\in I_{x,x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}(\mathbb{Q})
such that the isomorphism
g_{*}:I_{x}I_{x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}
induced by
g
has the property that the composition

T\hookrightarrow I_{x}\xrightarrow{g_{*}}I_{x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}

is equal to the canonical embedding
T\hookrightarrow I_{x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}
as in (
5.7.2.2
).

Proof.

Part (i) is proved in
[
Kis17
, Lem. 2.2.2]
, and part (ii) is proved in
[
Kis17
, Cor. 2.2.5]
.
∎

5.8.
Uniformization on the gerb side

5.8.1
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\rightarrow\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism (see Definition
2.4.2
). Set

\displaystyle Y_{p}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{UR}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p}),

\displaystyle Y^{p}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=X^{p}(\phi),

\displaystyle Y(\phi)

\displaystyle:=Y_{p}(\phi)\times Y^{p}(\phi).

See Definition
2.2.3
and §
2.4.7
for the notations. Thus
Y_{p}(\phi)
is a right
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-torsor,
Y^{p}(\phi)
is a right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-torsor, and
Y(\phi)
is a right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-torsor.

The construction in
[
Kis17
, §4.5.1]
gives rise to a map

(5.8.1.1)

\displaystyle Y(\phi)\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{KT}}.

In fact, the construction is more precise, as we now explain. Let
y=(y_{p},y^{p})\in Y(\phi)
. Write
\phi(p)_{y}
for the morphism
\Int(y_{p})^{-1}\circ\phi(p):\mathfrak{Q}(p)\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}(p)
. The
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
-subgroup
\im(\phi(p)_{y}^{\Delta})\subset G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
. Let

\mathscr{U}_{\phi,y}=\mathscr{U}_{y}:=\im(\phi(p)_{y}^{\Delta})(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\cap{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),

where the intersection is inside
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. The construction in
loc. cit.
attaches to
y
a
\mathscr{U}_{y}
-orbit in
{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
, which we denote by

[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]\subset{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}.

(Here
\mathscr{U}_{y}
acts on
{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
by the embedding
\mathscr{U}_{y}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
and the
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-action in Definition
5.3.6
.) If we just remember the
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-orbit in
{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
induced by
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]
, then we obtain the map (
5.8.1.1
). (However, we caution the reader that there is no well-defined map
Y(\phi)\to{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
, which is unlike the situation in §
5.6.2
.)

Let
{\mathfrak{k}}\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]
and let
\delta_{y}
be the
\delta
-component of
{\mathfrak{k}}
. Thus
\delta_{y}
is canonical up to
\sigma
-conjugation by
\mathscr{U}_{y}
. It follows easily from the construction in
[
Kis17
, §4.5.1]
that
\delta_{y}
satisfies the following conditions. (Note that both the conditions are invariant when we
\sigma
-conjugate
\delta_{y}
by
\mathscr{U}_{y}
.)

(i)

Define
b_{y}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
such that the morphism
\mathfrak{D}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
underlying
\phi(p)_{y}\circ\zeta_{p}:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}(p)
sends
d_{\sigma}
to
b_{y}\rtimes\sigma
, cf. Definition
2.2.5
. Given any neighborhood
\mathscr{O}
of
1
in
\im(\phi(p)_{y}^{\Delta})(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
(for the
p
-adic topology), there exists
u\in\mathscr{U}_{y}
such that

u\delta_{y}\sigma(u)^{-1}\in\mathscr{O}\cdot_{\sigma}b_{y}.

Here the right hand side denotes

\left\{ab_{y}\sigma(a)^{-1}\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\mid a\in\mathscr{O}\right\},

where each element of
\mathscr{O}
is viewed as an element of
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
In particular,
b_{y}
and
\delta_{y}
are
\sigma
-conjugate by an element of
{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\cap\im(\phi(p)_{y}^{\Delta})(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.

(ii)

The canonical homomorphism
I_{\phi(p)_{y},\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
, and induces an isomorphism

I_{\phi(p)_{y}}(R)=\left\{g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}R)\mid g\delta_{y}\sigma(g)^{-1}=\delta_{y}\right\}

for each
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra
R
. (Here we view the left hand side as a subgroup of
I_{\phi(p)_{y}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}R)
, which maps to
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}R)
via the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
-homomorphism
I_{\phi(p)_{y},\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}\to G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}}
.)

By property (ii) above, we know that although
\delta_{y}
is only well defined up to
\sigma
-conjugation by
\mathscr{U}_{y}
, the
\sigma
-centralizer of
\delta_{y}
in
G
is unambiguous as a subfunctor of
\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
.

Let
y
be as above. Let
g\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
. Then we have
y\cdot g\in Y(\phi)
. Recall from §
5.3.5
that
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
acts on
{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
on the right. By inspecting the construction in
[
Kis17
, §4.5.1]
, we see that:

(iii)

There exists
{\mathfrak{k}}\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]
such that
{\mathfrak{k}}\cdot g\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(y\cdot g)]
.

5.8.2
.

Let
\mathscr{J}
be a conjugacy class of admissible morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. We make
\mathscr{J}
into a small category, where morphisms
\phi\to\phi^{\prime}
are given by elements
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\phi^{\prime}=\Int(g)\circ\phi
. The composition of morphisms
\phi\xrightarrow{g}\phi^{\prime}\xrightarrow{h}\phi^{\prime\prime}
is given by
\phi\xrightarrow{hg}\phi^{\prime\prime}
. Then
\mathscr{J}
is a connected groupoid category. Each morphism
g:\phi\to\phi^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{J}
induces a
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-map
Y(g):Y(\phi)\to Y(\phi^{\prime})
given by the left multiplication by
g
. This makes
Y
a right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-torsor over
\mathscr{J}
in the sense of Definition
5.6.1
. As in that definition, we obtain a right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-set
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})
, together with canonical isomorphisms

\displaystyle I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y(\phi)\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})

for all
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
.

Let
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
and
y\in Y(\phi)
. It is easy to see that the subgroup
\mathscr{U}_{\phi,y}\subset{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
depends only on the image
\bar{y}
of
y
in
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})
, not on
\phi
and
y
. We therefore denote it also by
\mathscr{U}_{\bar{y}}
. Moreover, the
\mathscr{U}_{\phi,y}
-orbit
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]
in
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
attached to
y
as in §
5.8.1
depends only on
\bar{y}
. We thus have a canonical
\mathscr{U}_{\bar{y}}
-orbit in
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
attached to each
\bar{y}\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})
, which we denote by
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})]
.

The following discussion is completely analogous to §
5.6.3
. For
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
and
y=(y_{p},y^{p})\in Y(\phi)
, the left
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
-action on the right
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-torsor
Y(\phi)
gives rise to a homomorphism

(5.8.2.1)

\displaystyle\iota_{y}:I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})

defined by

j\cdot y=y\cdot\iota_{y}(j),\quad\forall j\in I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q}).

Thus we have a map
\iota_{y,v}:I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
for each prime
v\neq p
, and a map
\iota_{y,p}:I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
. For
v\neq p
, clearly
\iota_{y,v}
is induced by
\Int(y_{v}^{-1})
, where
y_{v}\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
is the image of
y^{p}
under
G(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f}^{p})\to G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
. Similarly
\iota_{y,p}
is induced by
\Int(y_{p}^{-1})
. Let
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(y)\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
be an arbitrary element whose image in
{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
belongs to
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]
. Let
(I_{0},(I_{v})_{v},(\eta_{v})_{v})
be the datum attached to
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
as in §
5.3.2
. For each prime
v\neq p
, the map
\iota_{y,v}
comes from an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-groups
\iota_{y,v}:I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}I_{v}
, which is still induced by
\Int(y_{v}^{-1})
. Also, the map
\iota_{y,p}
comes from an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-groups
\iota_{y,p}:I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}I_{p}
. The isomorphism
\iota_{y,p}
is induced by
\Int(y_{p}^{-1})
, in the sense that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y,p}}
\textstyle{G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Int(y_{p}^{-1})}
\textstyle{G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}}
\textstyle{I_{p,\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G)_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Here the bottom arrow is the base change to
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
of the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-embedding
I_{p}\hookrightarrow\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
, and the vertical arrow on the right is given by the map
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}R)\to G(R)
induced by
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}R\to R,a\otimes a^{\prime}\mapsto aa^{\prime}
for all
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
-algebras
R
, . (This description of
\iota_{y,p}
is just a reformulation of property (ii) in §
5.8.1
.) Moreover, the isomorphisms
\iota_{y,v}
for all primes
v
can be extended to a refinement of
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
of the form
(I_{\phi},\iota_{0},(\iota_{y,v})_{v})
.

With the above notation, note that
\mathscr{U}_{\phi,y}
is canonically identified with a subgroup of
Z_{I_{p}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. If we change the choice of
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
, then both
I_{p}
(as a subfunctor of
\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
) and the map
\iota_{y,p}:I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}I_{p}
do not change. Thus for every prime
v
, the reductive group
I_{v}
and the map
\iota_{y,v}:I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}I_{v}
depend only on
y
, not on the choice of
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
.

The following lemma holds in our current setting of Hodge type.

Lemma 5.8.3
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism, and let
\tau\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Let
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
be a neat compact open subgroup. Then the action of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}:=\Int(\tau)(I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q}))\subset I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on
X(\phi)/K^{p}
is free. Moreover, the natural map

I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}\backslash X(\phi)\longrightarrow S_{\tau}(\phi)

is a bijection.

Proof.

Suppose
\gamma\in I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}
has a fixed point in
X(\phi)/K^{p}
. By Lemma
3.7.2
(i), we have
\gamma\in Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{p}K^{p}
. By Lemma
1.5.7
and Lemma
5.1.2
(ii), we have
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{p}K^{p}=\left\{1\right\}
. Hence
\gamma=1
. This proves the first part.

By the definition of
S_{\tau}(\phi)
(see §
2.4.7
), we have a natural surjection

I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}\backslash\bigg(\varprojlim_{K^{p}}X(\phi)/K^{p}\bigg)\longrightarrow S_{\tau}(\phi).

By the previous part this surjection is a bijection. Since
X^{p}(\phi)
is a torsor under the locally profinite group
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, the natural map
X(\phi)\to\varprojlim_{K^{p}}X(\phi)/K^{p}
is a bijection. This proves the second part.
∎

5.8.4
.

Let
\mathscr{J}
be a conjugacy class of admissible morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. Set

S^{*}(\mathscr{J})=\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}).

For each
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
, by Lemma
5.8.3
we have

S(\phi)\cong I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X(\phi)=I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X_{p}(\phi)\times X^{p}(\phi).

In the future we shall view this as an equality. Recall from §
2.2.7
and §
2.4.1
that

X_{p}(\phi)=X_{-\mu_{X}}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})=Y_{-\mu_{X}}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),

where
Y_{-\mu_{X}}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
is a subset of
\mathcal{UR}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})=Y_{p}(\phi)
. Here
\mu_{X}
is as in §
2.4.1
, and the subset
Y_{-\mu_{X}}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})\subset Y_{p}(\phi)
is independent of the choice of
\mu_{X}
. We have a natural injection

(5.8.4.1)

\displaystyle S(\phi)\hookrightarrow S^{*}(\mathscr{J}).

If
g:\phi\to\phi^{\prime}
is a morphism in
\mathscr{J}
, then the bijection
Y_{p}(g):Y_{p}(\phi)\to Y_{p}(\phi^{\prime})
restricts to a bijection
Y_{-\mu_{X}}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})\to Y_{-\mu_{X}}(\phi^{\prime}(p)\circ\zeta_{p})
. It follows that inside
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})
we have a canonical subset of the form

\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})^{\natural}\cong I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y_{-\mu_{X}}(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})\times Y^{p}(\phi),

which is independent of the choice of
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. The image of the injection (
5.8.4.1
) is equal to the image of
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})^{\natural}
under the projection
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})\to S^{*}(\mathscr{J})
, namely
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})^{\natural}/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. In particular, we have a canonical bijection

S(\phi)\cong\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})^{\natural}/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}).

5.8.5
.

Recall the following constructions in
[
Kis17
, §§3.6–3.7]
. Associated with each admissible morphism
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{G}_{G}
, we have a
\pi(G)
-torsor
\pi(G,\phi)
, together with a
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant map

\widetilde{c}_{\phi}:X(\phi)\longrightarrow\pi(G,\phi).

(Here
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
acts on
\pi(G,\phi)
via the natural surjection
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\pi(G)
as in Lemma
5.5.2
.) For each
\tau\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the map
c_{\phi}
descends to a
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant map

c_{\phi,\tau}:S_{\tau}(\phi)\longrightarrow\pi(G,\phi).

See
[
Kis17
, Cor. 3.6.4, Lem. 3.7.4]
for more details. It follows from the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariance that the maps
\widetilde{c}_{\phi}
and
c_{\phi,\tau}
are surjective.

By
[
Kis17
, Prop. 3.6.10]
, for each admissible morphism
\phi
there is a canonical isomorphism of
\pi(G)
-torsors

(5.8.5.1)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\phi}:\pi(G,\phi)\cong\pi(G,X).

In the following we shall use the above identification freely, sometimes omitting it from the notation.

Lemma 5.8.6
.

Let
\mathscr{J}
be a conjugacy class of admissible morphism
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. Let
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. The composite map

(5.8.6.1)

\displaystyle\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})^{\natural}\to\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})^{\natural}/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\cong S(\phi)\xrightarrow{c_{\phi,1}}\pi(G,\phi)\xrightarrow{\vartheta_{\phi}}\pi(G,X)

depends only on
\mathscr{J}
and not on
\phi
.

Proof.

The proof is just by collecting various facts from
[
Kis17
, §3.6, §3.7]
. By
[
Kis17
, Cor. 3.6.4]
, the
\pi(G)
-torsor
\pi(G,\phi)
depends on
\phi
only via the conjugacy class of
\phi
(in fact, only via the conjugacy class of the composite morphism
\mathfrak{Q}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathfrak{G}_{G}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\ad}}
), up to canonical isomorphism. Also, by the characterization of
\vartheta_{\phi}
in
[
Kis17
, Prop. 3.6.10]
,
\vartheta_{\phi}
depends on
\phi
only via its conjugacy class. By the definition of
\widetilde{c}_{\phi}
(see
[
Kis17
, Lem. 3.7.4]
), it is functorial in
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. Namely, if
g:\phi\to\phi^{\prime}
is a morphism in
\mathscr{J}
, then we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X(\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X(g)}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{c}_{\phi}}
\textstyle{X(\phi^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{c}_{\phi^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\pi(G,\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi(G,\phi^{\prime})}

where the top map is the functorial map induced by
g
and the bottom map is the canonical isomorphism mentioned above. The lemma follows from these facts.
∎

Lemma 5.8.7
.

Let
\mathscr{J}
be a conjugacy class of admissible morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. Let
y\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})
. Let
r\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
, and let
y^{\prime}=yr\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})
, Assume that both
y
and
y^{\prime}
lie in
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})^{\natural}
. Then the images of
y
and
y^{\prime}
under the composite map

\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})^{\natural}\xrightarrow{(\ref {eq:c from bar Y(phi)})}\pi(G,X)\to\pi^{*}(G,X)

differ by the image of
r
in
\pi^{*}(G)
under the natural map
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G)
. Here the sign is similar to the one in Lemma
5.6.9
(ii).

Proof.

This follows from the proofs of
[
Kis17
, Lem. 3.6.2, Cor. 3.6.4]
.
∎

Proposition 5.8.8
.

Let
\mathscr{J}
be a conjugacy class of admissible morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. There is a unique map

c^{*}_{\mathscr{J}}:S^{*}(\mathscr{J})\longrightarrow\pi^{*}(G,X)

such that for each
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
the diagram

\textstyle{S(\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref {eq:embed S(phi)})}
\scriptstyle{c_{\phi,1}}
\textstyle{S^{*}(\mathscr{J})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\mathscr{J}}^{*}}
\textstyle{\pi(G,X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}(G,X)}

commutes, and such that the composite

\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})\to S^{*}(\mathscr{J})\xrightarrow{c^{*}_{\mathscr{J}}}\pi^{*}(G,X)

is
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariant. Here
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
acts on
\pi^{*}(G,X)
via the natural homomorphism
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G)
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Proposition
5.6.10
. One applies Lemma
5.8.7
instead of Lemma
5.6.9
.
∎

5.9.
Special points on the gerb side

5.9.1
.

Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
be a special point datum. From
{\mathfrak{s}}
we obtain a morphism
\Psi_{T,\mu_{h}}:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{T}
as in §
2.2.9
. To simplify notation we write
\Psi_{T,h}
for
\Psi_{T,\mu_{h}}
. As in Definition
3.3.2
, we write
\phi({\mathfrak{s}})=\phi(T,i,h)
for the morphism
i\circ\Psi_{T,h}:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. This morphism is admissible, as recalled in Theorem
3.3.3
.

We make the following definitions which are analogous to §
5.8.1
.

\displaystyle Y_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{UR}(\Psi_{T,h}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}),

\displaystyle Y^{p}(\Psi_{T,h})

\displaystyle:=X^{p}(\Psi_{T,h}),

\displaystyle Y(\Psi_{T,h})

\displaystyle:=Y_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})\times Y^{p}(\Psi_{T,h}).

By Lemma
2.2.4
,
Y_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
is a
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor. The definition of
X^{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
is as in §
2.4.7
, but with
G
replaced by
T
.
A priori

X^{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
is either empty or a
T(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-torsor. By
[
Kis17
, Prop. 3.6.7]
, it is a
T(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-torsor.

There is a canonical injection

(5.9.1.1)

\displaystyle Y(\Psi_{T,h})\hookrightarrow Y(\phi({\mathfrak{s}}))

induced by
i
.

Each
t\in Y_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
determines an element
b_{t}^{T}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
such that the morphism
\mathfrak{D}\to\mathfrak{G}_{T}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
underlying the unramified morphism
\Int(t^{-1})\circ\Psi_{T,h}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}
maps
d_{\sigma}:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{T}(p)
to
b_{t}^{T}\rtimes\sigma
, cf. Definition
2.2.5
. Define

X_{p}(\Psi_{T,h}):=\left\{t\in Y_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})\mid w_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(b_{t}^{T})=[-\mu_{h}]\in X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}\right\}.

Here
w_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}:T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}
is the Kottwitz map. By
[
Kis17
, Prop. 3.6.7]
, the set
X_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
is a
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}){\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor, where
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}
is the connected Néron model of
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
34
34

34

In
[
Kis17
, §3.6.6, Prop. 3.6.7]
, what is denoted by
X_{p}(\psi_{\mu_{T}})
is
X_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})/{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
in our notation.
We set

\displaystyle X(\Psi_{T,h})

\displaystyle:=X_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})\times X^{p}(\Psi_{T,h}).

Thus
X(\Psi_{T,h})
is a
T(\mathbb{A}_{f}){\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-torsor.

By Lemma
1.5.5
and Lemma
5.1.2
(iii),
T(\mathbb{Q})
is discrete in
T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and hence closed in
T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. By this fact and by
[
Kis17
, Prop. 3.6.7]
, there is a canonical
T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-equivariant bijection

T(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X(\Psi_{T,h})/{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})T(\mathbb{Q})\backslash T(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

We denote by
X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}
the
T(\mathbb{Q}){\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
-orbit in
X(\Psi_{T,h})
corresponding to the double coset of
1\in T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
in the right hand side. (The subscript stands for “neutral”.)

Elements of the image of
X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}
under (
5.9.1.1
) play a parallel role as the integral special points in Definition
5.7.5
. In order to avoid complicated terminology we do not give these elements a name parallel to “integral special points”. The fundamental properties of these elements are proved in the following proposition.

Proposition 5.9.2
.

Keep the setting of §
5.9.1
. Let
y\in X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}
. We denote the image of
y
in
Y(\phi({\mathfrak{s}}))
under (
5.9.1.1
) still by
y
. The following statements hold.

(i)

Write
\mathscr{J}
for the conjugacy class of
\phi({\mathfrak{s}})
. The image of
y
under the composite map

(5.9.2.1)

\displaystyle Y(\phi({\mathfrak{s}}))\to\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})\to S^{*}(\mathscr{J})\xrightarrow{c^{*}_{\mathscr{J}}}\pi^{*}(G,X)

is equal to the image of
i\circ h\in X
in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
. (See Proposition
5.8.8
for
c^{*}_{\mathscr{J}}
.)

(ii)

The
\mathscr{U}_{y}
-orbit
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]\subset{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
(see §
5.8.1
) has a representative in
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
of the form
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})
, for some
\delta_{T}
lying in the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
determined by
-\mu_{h}
. (See §
5.3.9
for
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
.)

Proof.

As is explained in
[
Kis17
, §3.6.8]
, there is a natural map
f:X(\Psi_{T,h})\to\pi(G,X)
. (More precisely, the target is
\pi(G,\phi({\mathfrak{s}}))
, but we identify it with
\pi(G,X)
via (
5.8.5.1
).) The composition of
f
with the natural map
\pi(G,X)\to\pi^{*}(G,X)
is equal to the composite map

X(\Psi_{T,h})\subset Y(\Psi_{T,h})\xrightarrow{(\ref {eq:inj from Y_T})}Y(\phi({\mathfrak{s}}))\xrightarrow{(\ref {eq:map to pi*})}\pi^{*}(G,X).

By
[
Kis17
, Prop. 3.6.10 (2)]
,
f
sends
y
to the image of
i\circ h
in
\pi(G,X)
. Statement (i) follows.

We now prove (ii). We view
i:T\hookrightarrow G
as the inclusion and omit it from the notation. Write
\phi
for
\phi({\mathfrak{s}})
. Define
b_{y}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
as in property (i) in §
5.8.1
(with respect to
\phi
). Since
y
comes from
X_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
, we have
b_{y}=b_{y}^{T}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, where
b_{y}^{T}
is determined by
y\in X_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
as in §
5.9.1
. By the definition of
X_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
, we have
w_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(b_{y})=[-\mu_{h}]\in X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{p,0}}.

Keep the notation
\phi(p)_{y}
as in §
5.8.1
. As a subgroup of
G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
,
\im(\phi(p)_{y}^{\Delta})
is contained in
T_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}}
.

Let
{\mathfrak{k}}
be an arbitrary element of
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]\subset{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
. By the construction in
[
Kis17
, §4.5.1]
(also cf.
[
Kis17
, §4.3.9]
),
{\mathfrak{k}}
has a representative in
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}
(for suitable
n
) of the form
(\gamma_{0},(\gamma_{0})_{l\neq p},\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}})
, where
\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
and
\gamma_{0}=\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}\sigma(\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}})\cdots\sigma^{n-1}(\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}})\in T(\mathbb{Q})\subset T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Moreover,
\gamma_{0}
is a
p
-unit in
T(\mathbb{Q})
, so in particular
\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
. Note that
\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
is uniquely determined by
{\mathfrak{k}}
, which justifies our notation.

Now
1
has an open neighborhood

\mathscr{O}:=\im(\phi(p)_{y}^{\Delta})(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})

in
\im(\phi(p)_{y}^{\Delta})(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
By property (i) in §
5.8.1
, there exists
{\mathfrak{k}}\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]
such that
\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}\in\mathscr{O}\cdot_{\sigma}b_{y}
. Thus
\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
is
\sigma
-conjugate to
b_{y}
by an element of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
, and in particular,
w_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}})=w_{T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(b_{y})=[-\mu_{h}]
. Therefore
\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
lies in the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
determined by
-\mu_{h}
. Letting
\delta_{T}=\delta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
, we know from the previous paragraph that the current
{\mathfrak{k}}\in{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
is the image of
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
. ∎

Lemma 5.9.3
.

Let
{\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}
be two special point data of the form
{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i,h)
and
{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}=(T,i_{1},h)
. Let
y\in X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}
. We still write
y
for the image of
y
in
Y(\phi({\mathfrak{s}}))
under (
5.9.1.1
), and we write
y_{1}
for the image of
y
in
Y(\phi({\mathfrak{s}}_{1}))
under the obvious analogue of (
5.9.1.1
). Then there exists
\delta_{T}
lying in the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
determined by
-\mu_{h}
such that
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
is a representative of (an element of)
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]\subset{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
and
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{1},\delta_{T})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
is a representative of (an element of)
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y_{1})]\subset{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
.

Proof.

Let
b_{y}^{T}
be the element of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
determined by
y\in X_{p}(\Psi_{T,h})
as in §
5.9.1
. By the construction in
[
Kis17
, §4.5.1]
(also cf.
[
Kis17
, §4.3.9]
), for every neighborhood
\mathscr{O}_{T}
of
1
in
T(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
contained in
i^{-1}({\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p}))\cap i_{1}^{-1}({\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p}))
, there exists

\delta_{T}
lying in the intersection of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
and

\mathscr{O}_{T}\cdot_{\sigma}b_{y}^{T}=\left\{ab_{y}^{T}\sigma(a)^{-1}\mid a\in\mathscr{O}_{T}\right\}

satisfying the following conditions.

•

For sufficiently divisible
n
,
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]
has a representative in
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}
of the form
(i(\gamma_{0}),(i(\gamma_{0}))_{l\neq p},i(\delta_{T}))
, where

\gamma_{0}=\delta_{T}\sigma(\delta_{T})\cdots\sigma^{n-1}(\delta_{T})\in T(\mathbb{Q})\subset T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Moreover,
\gamma_{0}
is a
p
-unit.

•

For sufficiently divisible
n
,
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y_{1})]
has a representative in
{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n}
of the form
(i_{1}(\gamma_{0}),(i_{1}(\gamma_{0}))_{l\neq p},i_{1}(\delta_{T}))
, where
\gamma_{0}
is as above.

In fact, write
\theta_{y}^{T,{\mathrm{ur}}}
for the morphism
\mathfrak{D}\to\mathfrak{G}_{T}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
underlying the unramified morphism
\Int(y^{-1})\circ\Psi_{T,h}(p)\circ\zeta_{p}:\mathfrak{G}_{p}\to\mathfrak{G}_{T}(p)
. In
[
Kis17
, §4.5.1]
, choose the element
c^{\prime}
sufficiently close to
c
such that
\theta_{y}^{T,{\mathrm{ur}}}(c^{\prime}c^{-1})\in\mathscr{O}_{T}
. Write
a
for
\theta_{y}^{T,{\mathrm{ur}}}(c^{\prime}c^{-1})
. We can then take
\delta_{T}
to be
ab_{y}^{T}\sigma(a)^{-1}
. Here the key point is that
c^{\prime}
is sufficiently close to
c
with respect to both
\phi({\mathfrak{s}})
and
\phi({\mathfrak{s}}_{1})
in the sense of
loc. cit.
, since
\mathscr{O}_{T}
is contained in
i^{-1}({\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p}))\cap i_{1}^{-1}({\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p}))
.

We now take
\mathscr{O}_{T}
to be sufficiently small such that it is also contained in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
, and choose
\delta_{T}
with respect to
\mathscr{O}_{T}
as above. As in the proof of Proposition
5.7.6
(ii), this
\delta_{T}
necessarily lies in the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
determined by
-\mu_{h}
. The above two conditions imply that
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}},\delta_{T})
represents
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y)]
and that
{\mathfrak{k}}({\mathfrak{s}}_{1},\delta_{T})
represents
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y_{1})]
.
∎

Remark 5.9.4
.

The analogue of Lemma
5.9.3
for finitely many special point data of the form
(T,i,h),(T,i_{1},h),\cdots,(T,i_{k},h)
is also true. The choice of
\delta_{T}
is not intrinsic to the Shimura datum
(T,h)
and the point
y\in X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}
, but depends on the given finite list of embeddings
i,i_{1},\cdots,i_{k}
of
T
into
G
.

5.10.
Markings and amicable pairs

Definition 5.10.1
.

Let
\mathscr{I}
be an isogeny class in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, and let
\mathscr{J}
be a conjugacy class of admissible morphisms
\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
. By a
marking
of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
, we mean a pair

(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\times\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})

such that

{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime})].

Here
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})\in{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
is the image of
\bar{y}
under (
5.6.3.2
), and
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime})]
is the
\mathscr{U}_{\bar{y}^{\prime}}
-orbit in
{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
attached to
\bar{y}^{\prime}
as in §
5.8.2
. We say that the marking
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
is
\pi^{*}
-compatible
, if the image of
\bar{y}
under

(5.10.1.1)

\displaystyle\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\longrightarrow\mathscr{I}^{*}\xrightarrow{c^{*}_{\mathscr{I}}}\pi^{*}(G,X)

equals the image of
\bar{y}^{\prime}
under

(5.10.1.2)

\displaystyle\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})\longrightarrow S^{*}(\mathscr{J})\xrightarrow{c^{*}_{\mathscr{J}}}\pi^{*}(G,X).

See Proposition
5.6.10
and Proposition
5.8.8
for
c^{*}_{\mathscr{I}}
and
c^{*}_{\mathscr{J}}
respectively.

We call the pair
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})

weakly amicable
(resp.
amicable
) if a it admits a marking (resp. a
\pi^{*}
-compatible marking).

Lemma 5.10.2
.

Let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
be a weakly amicable pair. For every
\bar{y}^{\prime}\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})
, there exists
\bar{y}\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
such that
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
is a marking of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
. Moreover, if
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
is amicable, then we can choose
\bar{y}
such that
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
is a
\pi^{*}
-compatible marking.

Proof.

Let
(\bar{z},\bar{z}^{\prime})
be a marking of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
. Let
u\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
be such that
\bar{z}^{\prime}\cdot u=\bar{y}^{\prime}
. By assumption,
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{z})\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{z}^{\prime})]
. By property (iii) in §
5.8.1
, there exists
{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{z}^{\prime})]
such that
{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}\cdot u\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime})]
. Since
{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}
and
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{z})
lie in the same
\mathscr{U}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}
-orbit, and since
\mathscr{U}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}\subset{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
, there exists
u_{0}\in{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
such that
{\mathfrak{k}}_{0}={\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{z})\cdot u_{0}
. Then we have

{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{z})\cdot u_{0}u\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime})].

Let
\bar{y}=\bar{z}\cdot u_{0}u\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})
. By the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariance of the map (
5.6.2.1
), we have
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})={\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{z})\cdot u_{0}u
. Thus we have

{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})\in[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime})],

which means that
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
is a marking of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
.

If we assume that
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
is amicable, then we can choose
(\bar{z},\bar{z}^{\prime})
as above to be
\pi^{*}
-compatible. It remains to show that the marking
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
produced above is
\pi^{*}
-compatible. But this follows from the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariance of the maps (
5.10.1.1
) and (
5.10.1.2
), and the fact that
u_{0}
has trivial image in
\pi^{*}(G)
(since
u_{0}\in{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
).
∎

5.10.3
.

Let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
be a weakly amicable pair, and let
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
be a marking of it. Fix
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
representing
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}
, and let
(I_{0},(I_{v})_{v},(\eta_{v})_{v})
be the datum attached to
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})
as in §
5.3.2
. Let
x\in\mathscr{I}
and
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. We choose
y\in Y(x)
lifting
\bar{y}
, and choose
y^{\prime}\in Y(\phi)
lifting
\bar{y}^{\prime}
. Recall from §
5.6.3
and §
5.8.2
that there exist inner twistings
\iota_{0}:I_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to I_{x,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
and
\iota_{0}^{\prime}:I_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
of
\mathbb{Q}
-groups such that the tuples
(I_{x},\iota_{0},(\iota_{y,v})_{v})
and
(I_{\phi},\iota_{0}^{\prime},(\iota_{y^{\prime},v})_{v})
are both refinements of
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})
.
By the Hasse principle for adjoint groups and by the fact that
I_{x}
and
I_{\phi}
are both compact mod center at the place
\infty
, there is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}
-groups

f:I_{x}I_{\phi}

such that for each finite place
v
the two
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-maps
\iota_{y,v}\circ f^{-1}:I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\to I_{v}
and
\iota_{y^{\prime},v}:I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\to I_{v}
differ by an inner automorphism of
I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
. The isomorphism
f
is uniquely determined by
(x,\phi,y,y^{\prime})
, up to composing with inner automorphisms defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. (Note that
I_{v}
and the maps
\iota_{y,v},\iota_{y^{\prime},v}
depend only on
(x,\phi,y,y^{\prime})
, not on the choice of the lifting
\dot{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})
of
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y})
; see the last paragraph of §
5.8.2
.) In Remark
5.10.10
below, we will see that
f
is in fact uniquely determined by
(x,\phi)
up to composing with inner automorphisms defined over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Now there is an element

\tau=(\tau_{v})_{v}\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})

such that for each finite place
v
we have

\iota_{y,v}\circ f^{-1}=\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ\Int(\tau_{v}):I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\longrightarrow I_{v}.

Clearly
\tau
is uniquely determined by
(x,\phi,y,y^{\prime})
and
f
. Moreover, the image of
\tau
in
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
is determined by
(x,\phi,y,y^{\prime})
and independent of the choice of
f
. We denote this element by

\tau_{x,\phi,y,y^{\prime}}\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q}).

The image of
\tau_{x,\phi,y,y^{\prime}}
in

I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
is determined by
(\phi,\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
and independent of the choices of
x,y,y^{\prime}
. We denote this element by
