\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}\in I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q}).

If
\phi^{\prime}
is another element of
\mathscr{J}
, then we have a canonical identification

I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})\cong I_{\phi^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I_{\phi^{\prime}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi^{\prime}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})

induced by
\Int g
for any
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
conjugating
\phi
to
\phi^{\prime}
. (This identification is indeed independent of
g
, since
g
is unique up to right multiplication by
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})
.) If we identify
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
for all
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
in this way, then
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}
is independent of
\phi
. This justifies our notation.

Let
\xi_{y}
be the unique
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariant bijection
Y(x)G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
taking
y
to
1
, and let
\xi_{y^{\prime}}
be the unique
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
-equivariant bijection
Y(\phi)G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
taking
y^{\prime}
to
1
.
Let
\delta_{y}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
be the
\delta
-component of
{\mathfrak{k}}(y)\in{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
, and let
b_{y^{\prime}}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
be the element attached to
y^{\prime}
as in property (i) in §
5.8.1
.
By the defining property of a marking and by property (i) in §
5.8.1
, there exists

e\in{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\cap\im(\phi(p)_{y}^{\Delta})(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\subset G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

such that
e\delta_{y}\sigma(e)^{-1}=b_{y^{\prime}}
. Fix such
e
. Define

\mathbf{f}_{1}:G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\longrightarrow G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}),\quad g\longmapsto\sigma^{-1}(\delta_{y}^{-1}g).

This is a well-defined bijection because
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
is
\sigma
-stable. Recall from Lemma
1.6.8
that
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\cong G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
. Using this, we define

\mathbf{f}_{2}:G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\cong G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{g\mapsto ege^{-1}}G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})\cong G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}).

Here the middle map is a well-defined bijection because
e\in{\mathcal{G}}(\breve{\mathbb}{Z}_{p})
. Let
\xi_{y,y^{\prime},e}
be the composite bijection

Y(x)/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\xrightarrow{\xi_{y}}G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\xrightarrow{(\id_{G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})},~\mathbf{f}_{2}\circ\mathbf{f}_{1})}G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\xrightarrow{\xi_{y^{\prime}}^{-1}}Y(\phi)/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}).

Proposition 5.10.4
.

Keep the setting and notation of §
5.10.3
.
The map
\xi_{y,y^{\prime},e}
descends to a bijection

(5.10.4.1)

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{*}I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}\backslash Y(\phi)/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}).

Here
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}
is the image of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\xrightarrow{\Int\tau}I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Moreover, (
5.10.4.1
) restricts to a bijection

(5.10.4.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}S_{\tau}(\phi),

which is compatible with the actions of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and the
q
-Frobenius
\Phi
on the two sides.

Proof.

The map
\xi_{y}
induces a bijection

\bar{\xi}_{y}:I_{x}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Y(x)/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\iota_{y}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}))\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}).

Similarly,
\xi_{y^{\prime}}
induces a bijection

\bar{\xi}_{y^{\prime}}:I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}\backslash Y(\phi)/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\iota_{y}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}))\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}).

Here we have used that the image of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{\tau}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\xrightarrow{\iota_{y^{\prime}}}G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
is equal to
\iota_{y}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}))\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
. To show that
\xi_{y,y^{\prime}}
descends to (
5.10.4.1
) it remains to show that for
i=1,2
we have

\mathbf{f}_{i}(hg)=h\mathbf{f}_{i}(g)

for all
g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
and all
h\in I_{p}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Now
\mathbf{f}_{1}
satisfies this because
I_{p}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the
\sigma
-centralizer of
\delta_{y}
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Meanwhile
\mathbf{f}_{2}
satisfies this because in
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
,
e
commutes with every element in the image of
\iota_{y^{\prime},p}:I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, and this image is equal to
I_{p}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

We now show that (
5.10.4.1
) induces
\mathscr{I}S_{\tau}(\phi)
. The image of
\mathscr{I}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{I}^{*}
is described in Proposition
5.6.5
and Remark
5.6.6
. By that description we know that the image of
\mathscr{I}
under
\bar{\xi}_{y}
is

(5.10.4.3)

\displaystyle\iota_{y}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}))\backslash X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}).

In fact, the bijection from
\mathscr{I}
onto the above set induced by
\bar{\xi}_{y}
is just the inverse of (
5.6.6.1
). By the definition of
S_{\tau}(\phi)
and by the discussion in §
2.2.7
, the image of
S_{\tau}(\phi)
under
\bar{\xi}_{y^{\prime}}
is

(5.10.4.4)

\displaystyle\iota_{y}(I_{x}(\mathbb{Q}))\backslash X_{-\mu}(b_{y^{\prime}})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}),

with
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}
. It remains to show that the bijection
\mathbf{f}_{2}\circ\mathbf{f}_{1}:G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})/{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
restricts to a bijection

(5.10.4.5)

\displaystyle X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})X_{-\mu}(b_{y^{\prime}}).

Since
\upsilon=\sigma(-\mu)
(see §
5.6.4
), it is immediate that
\mathbf{f}_{1}
induces
X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})X_{-\mu}(\delta_{y})
. Using the presentation of affine Deligne–Lusztig sets as in (
2.2.7.1
), we see that
\mathbf{f}_{2}
induces
X_{-\mu}(\delta_{y})X_{-\mu}(b_{y^{\prime}})
.

Finally, we need to show that (
5.10.4.2
) is compatible with the actions of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
\Phi
.
The compatibility with
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is clear. The compatibility with
\Phi
boils down to the following three compatibilities. Firstly, the bijection from
\mathscr{I}
to (
5.10.4.3
) induced by
\bar{\xi}_{y}
is compatible with
\Phi
on
\mathscr{I}
and the operator
(\delta_{y}\rtimes\sigma)^{r}
on
X_{\upsilon}(\delta_{y})
(with
r=[\mathbb{F}_{q}:\mathbb{F}_{p}]
). As we have remarked above, this bijection is just the inverse of (
5.6.6.1
), which is the map
[
Kis17
, (2.1.4)]
. The compatibility follows from
[
Kis17
, Prop. 1.4.4]
, cf.
[
Kis17
, Cor. 1.4.13, Prop. 2.1.3, Prop. 4.4.14]
. Secondly, the bijection (
5.10.4.5
) induced by
\mathbf{f}_{2}\circ\mathbf{f}_{1}
is compatible with

(\delta_{y}\rtimes\sigma)^{r}
on the left hand side and
(b_{y^{\prime}}\rtimes\sigma)^{r}
on the right hand side. This is immediate from the definitions. Thirdly, the bijection from
S_{\tau}(\phi)
to (
5.10.4.4
) induced by
\bar{\xi}_{y^{\prime}}
is compatible with
\Phi
on
S_{\tau}(\phi)
and
(b_{y^{\prime}}\rtimes\sigma)^{r}
on
X_{-\mu_{X}}(b_{y^{\prime}})
. This follows from the discussion in §
2.2.7
.
∎

5.10.5
.

Let
\phi:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
be an admissible morphism. Since
\mathfrak{Q}
satisfies the assumption on
\mathfrak{H}
in the last paragraph of §
2.1.14
, we have reductive
\mathbb{Q}
-groups
\widetilde{I}_{\phi}
and
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
associated with
\phi
. Recall that
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
is identified with the natural
\mathbb{Q}
-homomorphism
I_{\phi}\to G^{\ab}
. Note that
I_{\phi}\to G^{\ab}
is surjective, because
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
contains a maximal torus in
G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
We write
Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}
for the center of
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
. By Lemma
1.2.10
(i) applied to the map
I_{\phi}\to G^{\ab}
, we have

(5.10.5.1)

\displaystyle Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}=Z_{I_{\phi}}\cap I_{\phi}^{\dagger},

and the embedding
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}
induces an isomorphism between the adjoint groups.

Recall from §
2.6.11
that the canonical
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-embedding
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
and the set
{\mathcal{W}}=\left\{g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})\mid\Int g\circ\phi\text{ is gg}\right\}
form an inner transfer datum from
I_{\phi}
to
G
. It follows that the canonical
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-embedding
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow G_{\mathrm{der},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
and the set
{\mathcal{W}}\cap G_{\mathrm{der}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
(which is clearly non-empty, given the non-emptiness of
{\mathcal{W}}
) form an inner transfer datum from
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
to
G_{\mathrm{der}}
. We use this inner transfer datum to define the map

(5.10.5.2)

\displaystyle\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi}^{\dagger})\longrightarrow\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},G_{\mathrm{der}}),

as well as to define

\Sha_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},H)
for any
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
H\subset I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
, as in §
1.2.5
.

Consider the boundary map

(5.10.5.3)

\displaystyle I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\longrightarrow\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})

arising from the short exact sequence
1\to Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}\to I_{\phi}^{\dagger}\to I_{\phi}^{\ad}=(I_{\phi}^{\dagger})^{\ad}\to 1
. As we explained in §
2.6.18
,
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{R})
is connected, and hence
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\mathbb{R})\to I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{R})
is surjective. Therefore the boundary map
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{R})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})
arising from the same short exact sequence is zero. It follows that (
5.10.5.3
) descends to a map

(5.10.5.4)

\displaystyle I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})/\Sha^{\infty}_{I_{\phi}^{\dagger}}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}).

Consider the boundary map
\partial:G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})
arising from the short exact sequence
1\to Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}\to Z_{I_{\phi}}\to G^{\ab}\to 1
. We define the abelian group

(5.10.5.5)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi):=\cok({\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\partial}\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})/\Sha^{\infty}_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})).

The map (
5.10.5.4
) induces a map

(5.10.5.6)

\displaystyle I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi).

Suppose that
\phi_{1}:\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
is another admissible morphism satisfying
\phi\approx\phi_{1}
as in §
2.6.16
. Then the abelian groups
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
and
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{1})
are canonically isomorphic. Indeed,
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
clearly depends only on the two-term complex
Z_{I_{\phi}}\to G^{\ab}
, and similarly for
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{1})
. Since
\phi\approx\phi_{1}
, there is a canonical equivalence class of inner twistings between
I_{\phi}
and
I_{\phi_{1}}
, and they all induce the same isomorphism from the complex
Z_{I_{\phi}}\to G^{\ab}
to the complex
Z_{I_{\phi_{1}}}\to G^{\ab}
. Thus we have a canonical isomorphism
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi){\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{1})
.

5.10.6
.

Now let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
be a weakly amicable pair, and let
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. For any marking
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
, recall that
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}
is an element of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
. Note that the map (
5.10.5.6
) factors through
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
. (In fact, suppose that
g_{1}\in I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
lifts an element of
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
and
g_{2}\in I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\bar{\mathbb}{A}_{f})
lifts an element of
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Then for
\rho\in\Gamma
we have
(g_{1}g_{2})^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})=(g_{2}g_{1})^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}(g_{2}g_{1})
since
g_{1}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}g_{1}
and
g_{2}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}g_{2}
are central in
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
. This shows that
g_{1}g_{2}
and
g_{2}g_{1}
have the same image under (
5.10.5.6
).) We denote by

\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}^{{\mathfrak{H}}}\in{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)

the image of
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}
in
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
under the map induced by (
5.10.5.6
). Recall from §
5.10.3
that the set
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
is independent of
\phi
up to canonical bijection, and that the element
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}
of this set is independent of
\phi
. In a similar sense,
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}^{{\mathfrak{H}}}
depends only on
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
and is independent of
\phi
. More precisely, if
\phi^{\prime}
is another element of
\mathscr{J}
, then we have a canonical isomorphism of abelian groups
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)\cong{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi^{\prime})
which is induced by
\Int g
for any
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
conjugating
\phi
to
\phi^{\prime}
. (This is a special case of the canonical isomorphism discussed in §
5.10.5
as
\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}
.) If we identify
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
for all
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
in this way, then the element
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}^{{\mathfrak{H}}}
depends only on
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
and not on
\phi
.

Recall from §
2.6.13
that we defined

{\mathcal{H}}(\phi):=I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q}).

The element
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}
has a natural image in
{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
, which we denote by

\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}\in{\mathcal{H}}(\phi).

Again, for different
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
, the abelian groups
{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
are canonically identified (cf. §
2.6.16
). Under such identifications the element
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}
is independent of
\phi
.

Lemma 5.10.7
.

Let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
be an amicable pair. Let
(\bar{y}_{i},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{i}),i=1,2,
be two
\pi^{*}
-compatible markings of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
. Then

\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}_{\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{1}}=\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}_{\bar{y}_{2},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{2}},

where the two sides are defined in §
5.10.6
.

Proof.

Fix
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. We view
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}_{\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{1}}
and
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}_{\bar{y}_{2},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{2}}
as elements of
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
. Pick
x\in\mathscr{I}
, and pick
y_{i}\in Y(x)
lifting
\bar{y}_{i}
for
i=1,2
. Also pick
y^{\prime}_{i}\in Y(\phi)
lifting
\bar{y}^{\prime}_{i}
for
i=1,2
.

Let
u\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
be such that
y_{2}^{\prime}=y_{1}^{\prime}\cdot u
. As in the proof of Lemma
5.10.2
, there exists
u_{0}\in\mathscr{U}_{\phi,y_{1}^{\prime}}
such that
(\bar{y}_{1}\cdot u_{0}u,\bar{y}_{2}^{\prime})
is a marking of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
. Using that
u_{0}
has trivial image in
\pi^{*}(G)
, it is easy to see that
(\bar{y}_{1}\cdot u_{0}u,\bar{y}_{2}^{\prime})
is
\pi^{*}
-compatible. For every finite place
v
, let
I_{v}
denote the reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
associated with
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}_{1})\in{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
. Since
(\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}_{1}^{\prime})
is a marking, we know that
I_{v}
is also the reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
associated with any element of
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}_{1}^{\prime})]
. Recall that
\mathscr{U}_{\phi,y_{1}^{\prime}}
is canonically embedded into the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
-points of the center of
I_{p}
(cf. the last paragraph of §
5.8.2
). It follows that
\iota_{y_{1}\cdot u_{0}u,v}=\iota_{y_{1}\cdot u,v}
as maps
I_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\to I_{v}
for every finite place
v
. From this, it is easy to see that
\tau_{\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}_{1}^{\prime}}=\tau_{\bar{y}_{1}\cdot u_{0}u,\bar{y}_{2}^{\prime}}
.
We have thus reduced the proof of the lemma to the case where
\bar{y}_{1}^{\prime}=\bar{y}_{2}^{\prime}
, since we can replace
(\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}_{1}^{\prime})
by
(\bar{y}_{1}\cdot u_{0}u,\bar{y}_{2}^{\prime})
.

We now assume that
\bar{y}_{1}^{\prime}=\bar{y}_{2}^{\prime}
, and write
\bar{y}^{\prime}
for this element. Obviously we can arrange that
y_{1}^{\prime}=y_{2}^{\prime}
. For each finite place
v
, the reductive groups over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
associated with
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}_{1}),{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}_{2})
and any element of
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime})]
are all the same, and we denote it by
I_{v}
.

Write
y_{1}=y_{2}h
for
h=(h_{v})_{v}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
. Then
h_{v}
lies in
I_{v}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
for
v\neq p
, and
h_{p}
lies in

\mathscr{U}_{\phi,y^{\prime}}\cdot I_{p}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset\big(Z_{I_{p}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\cap{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\big)I_{p}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Thus we can write
h=\Int(y^{\prime})^{-1}(s)\cdot t
, with
s\in I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
t\in Z_{I_{p}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\cap{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
. (Here we view
y^{\prime}
and
s
both as elements of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
in writing
\Int(y^{\prime})^{-1}(s)
. The element
\Int(y^{\prime})^{-1}(s)\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
in fact lies in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
.) Then the elements
\tau_{x,\phi,y_{1},y^{\prime}}
and
\tau_{x,\phi,y_{2},y^{\prime}}
of
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
differ by left multiplication by
s
. More precisely, if
f_{1}:I_{x}I_{\phi}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-isomorphism and
\tau_{1}=(\tau_{1,v})_{v}
is an element of
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
satisfying that

\iota_{y_{1},v}\circ f_{1}^{-1}=\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ\Int(\tau_{1,v}):I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\longrightarrow I_{v},

then we have

(5.10.7.1)

\displaystyle\iota_{y_{2},v}\circ f_{1}^{-1}=\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ\Int(s\tau_{1,v}):I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\longrightarrow I_{v}.

It remains to show that the image of
s
under (
5.10.5.6
) is zero.
Since
y_{1},y_{2},y^{\prime}
all have the same image in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
, the image of
h\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
in
\pi^{*}(G)
must be trivial. Note that
t
has trivial image in
\pi^{*}(G)
since
t\in{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
. Hence
\Int(y^{\prime})^{-1}(s)
has trivial image in
\pi^{*}(G)
. The natural map

G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\longrightarrow G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})/{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})

factors through
\pi^{*}(G)
. Therefore the image
s^{\ab}\in G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
of
s
under
I_{\phi}\to G^{\ab}
lies in
[G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}]^{\ab}{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
, where
[G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}]^{\ab}
denotes the image of
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\to G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Since
s^{\ab}
in fact lies in
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we have
s_{\ab}\in[G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}]^{\ab}{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. By Lemma
1.2.10
applied to
I_{\phi}\to G^{\ab}
, the composite maps

I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\rightarrow I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\xrightarrow{\delta^{1}}\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})

and

I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\rightarrow G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\xrightarrow{\delta^{2}}\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})

differ by a sign. Here
\delta^{1}
is associated with the short exact sequence
1\to Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}\to I_{\phi}^{\dagger}\to I_{\phi}^{\ad}\to 1
, and
\delta^{2}
is associated with
1\to Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}\to Z_{I_{\phi}}\to G^{\ab}\to 1
.
To prove our desired statement that the image of
s
under (
5.10.5.6
) is zero, it suffices to prove that the image of
[G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}]^{\ab}
under the boundary map
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},Z^{\dagger}_{\phi})
analogous to
\delta^{2}
is contained in
\Sha^{\infty}_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})
.

In fact, a stronger statement is true, namely that the image of
[G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}]^{\ab}
under the boundary map
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{G_{\mathrm{der}}})
associated with
1\to Z_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}\to Z_{G}\to G^{\ab}\to 1
is contained in
\Sha^{\infty}_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}).
(This is indeed stronger, as
Z_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}\subset Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}
.) This statement follows from Corollary
1.2.11
applied to
I=G
.
∎

Lemma 5.10.8
.

Let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
be a weakly amicable pair. Let
(\bar{y}_{i},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{i}),i=1,2
be two markings of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
. Then

\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{1}}=\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\bar{y}_{2},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{2}},

where the two sides are defined in §
5.10.6
.

Proof.

Fix
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. We view
\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{1}}
and
\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{\bar{y}_{2},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{2}}
as elements of
{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
. Pick
x\in\mathscr{I}
. By the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
5.10.7
, we reduce to the case where
\bar{y}^{\prime}_{1}=\bar{y}^{\prime}_{2}=\bar{y}^{\prime}
. In this case, pick
y_{i}\in Y(x)
lifting
\bar{y}_{i}
for
i=1,2
, and pick
y^{\prime}\in Y(\phi)
lifting
\bar{y}^{\prime}
. Then by the same argument we know that the elements
\tau_{x,\phi,y_{1},y^{\prime}}
and
\tau_{x,\phi,y_{2},y^{\prime}}
of
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
differ by left multiplication by an element of
I_{\phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. But this immediately implies what we want.
∎

Definition 5.10.9
.

Let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
be an amicable pair. Let
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. We define

\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})\in{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)

to be
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}^{{\mathfrak{H}}}
(see §
5.10.6
) for any
\pi^{*}
-compatible marking
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
. By Lemma
5.10.7
this is well defined. Similarly, we define

\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})\in{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)

to be
\tau_{\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}
(see §
5.10.6
) for any marking
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
. This is well defined by Lemma
5.10.8
. If we identify
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
(resp.
{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
) for all
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
as discussed in §
5.10.6
, then
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
(resp.
\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
) is independent of
\phi
.

Remark 5.10.10
.

The proofs of Lemma
5.10.7
and Lemma
5.10.8
also show that the isomorphism
f:I_{x}I_{\phi}
in §
5.10.3
indeed depends only on
(x,\phi)
, up to composing with inner automorphisms defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. In fact, suppose we have two pairs
(y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime})
and
(y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime})
in
Y(x)\times Y(\phi)
, whose images
(\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}_{1}^{\prime})
and
(\bar{y}_{2},\bar{y}_{2}^{\prime})
in
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I})\times\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J})
are markings of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
. For
i=1,2
, the tuple
(x,\phi,y_{i},y_{i}^{\prime})
gives rise to an isomorphism
f_{i}:I_{x}I_{\phi}
, which is well defined up to composing with inner automorphisms defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. In order to show that
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
differ only by an inner automorphism, we argue in the same way as in the proof of Lemma
5.10.7
and Lemma
5.10.8
to reduce to the case where
\bar{y}_{1}^{\prime}=\bar{y}_{2}^{\prime}
. In this case, clearly replacing
(y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime})
by
(y_{1},y_{2}^{\prime})
does not change
f_{1}
, so we further reduce to the case where
y_{1}^{\prime}=y_{2}^{\prime}
. Then as we showed in the proof of Lemma
5.10.7
(see especially (
5.10.7.1
)), we can choose
f_{2}
to be equal to
f_{1}
.

5.11.
Gauges

Definition 5.11.1
.

By a
special fork
, we mean an ordered pair
({\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
consisting of two special point data
{\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
of the form
{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i,h)
and
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}=(T,i^{\prime},h)
, satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

The points
i\circ h
and
i^{\prime}\circ h
lie in the same connected component of
X
.

(ii)

The maps
i:T\to G
and
i^{\prime}:T\to G
are conjugate by
G^{\ad}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
.

When we want to make explicit the ingredients, we also write
(T,h,i,i^{\prime})
for a special fork.

5.11.2
.

Given a special fork
(T,h,i,i^{\prime})
, the two composite maps
T\xrightarrow{i}G\to G^{\ab}
and
T\xrightarrow{i^{\prime}}G\to G^{\ab}
are equal. We denote the kernel by
T^{\dagger}
. The two maps
\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\dagger})\to\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},G_{\mathrm{der}})
induced by
i
and
i^{\prime}
are equal (since
\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},G_{\mathrm{der}})\cong\Sha^{\infty}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q},G_{\mathrm{der}})
and since
i,i^{\prime}
are conjugate by
G^{\ad}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
), and we denote the kernel by
\Sha^{\infty}_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\dagger})
. Similarly, we define
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T)
to be the kernel of
\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T)\to\Sha^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},G)
induced by either
i
or
i^{\prime}
.

Clearly there exists
g\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\Int(g)\circ i=i^{\prime}
. Write
T^{\prime}
for
i^{\prime}(T)\subset G
write
T^{\prime\dagger}
for
T^{\prime}\cap G_{\mathrm{der}}=i^{\prime}(T^{\dagger})
. Since
T^{\prime}
is self-centralizing in
G
, the cocycle
(g{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}g^{-1})_{\rho\in\Gamma}
defines an element
\alpha_{i,i^{\prime}}\in\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\prime\dagger})
which is independent of the choice of
g
.

Lemma 5.11.3
.

The element
\alpha_{i,i^{\prime}}
lies in
\Sha^{\infty}_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\prime\dagger})
.

Proof.

The only non-trivial condition to check is that
\alpha_{i,i^{\prime}}
has trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},T^{\prime\dagger})
. The argument is similar to the proof of
[
Kis17
, Prop. 4.4.13]
. Let
K_{\infty}^{\prime}
be the
\mathbb{R}
-algebraic group that is the stabilizer of
i^{\prime}\circ h
in
G_{\mathrm{der},\mathbb{R}}
. Since
i\circ h
and
i^{\prime}\circ h
lie in the same connected component of
X
, they are conjugate by an element of
G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{R})
(or even
G_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{R})
). It follows that
\alpha_{i,i^{\prime}}
has trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},K_{\infty}^{\prime})
. By
[
Kis17
, Lem. 4.4.5]
applied to
H^{\prime}=T^{\prime\dagger}
and
H=K_{\infty}^{\prime}
, the only element of
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},T^{\prime\dagger})
having trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},K_{\infty}^{\prime})
is the trivial element. Hence
\alpha_{i,i^{\prime}}
must have trivial image in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},T^{\prime\dagger})
, as desired. ∎

In the sequel, given any torus
T
over
\mathbb{Q}
, we write
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}
for the connected Néron model of
T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

Definition 5.11.4
.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be a tuple
(T,h,i,i^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}),
where

•

(T,h,i,i^{\prime})
is a special fork.

•

y
is an element of the
{\mathcal{T}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
-torsor
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})^{\circ}
(see §
5.7.4
).

•

y^{\prime}
is an element of
X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}
(see §
5.9.1
).

We define the following objects associated with
{\mathfrak{g}}
.

•

Let
x_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be the point
x_{(T,i,h)}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
(see §
5.7.1
), and let
\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be the isogeny class of
x_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
.

•

Let
\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be the admissible morphism
\phi(T,i^{\prime},h):\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
(see Definition
3.3.2
and Theorem
3.3.3
), and let
\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be the conjugacy class of
\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
.

•

Let
\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be the image of
y
under
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})^{\circ}\to Y_{p}(x_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\hookrightarrow Y(x_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\to\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
, where the first map is as in (
5.7.4.4
), and the second map sends
y_{p}
to
(y_{p},1)
where
1
is the canonical base point of
Y^{p}(x_{\mathfrak{g}})\cong G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

•

Let
\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be the image of
y^{\prime}
under
X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}\to Y(\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\to\bar{Y}(\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
, where the first map is as in (
5.9.1.1
).

•

Let
\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be the element of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
attached to
y
as in Proposition
5.7.6
(i).

We say that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a
quasi-gauge
, if the following condition is satisfied:

•

The element
{\mathfrak{k}}(T,i^{\prime},h,\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
defined as in §
5.3.9
represents an element of
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})]\subset{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
, where
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})]
is the
\mathscr{U}_{\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
-orbit in
{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
associated with
\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
as in §
5.8.2
. (Note that
{\mathfrak{k}}(T,i^{\prime},h,\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is indeed defined, since
\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
lies in the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
corresponding to
-\mu_{h}
by Proposition
5.7.6
(i).)

Lemma 5.11.5
.

Let
(T,h,i^{\prime})
and
(T,h,i_{1}^{\prime})
be two elements of
\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
. Let
y^{\prime}\in X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}
. Then there exists
y\in Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})^{\circ}
satisfying the following conditions.

(i)

For every special fork of the form
(T,h,i,i^{\prime})
, the tuple
(T,h,i,i^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})
is a quasi-gauge.

(ii)

For every special fork of the form
(T,h,i_{1},i_{1}^{\prime})
, the tuple
(T,h,i_{1},i_{1}^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})
is a quasi-gauge.

Proof.

We still write
y^{\prime}
for the image of
y^{\prime}
under the map

X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}\longrightarrow Y(\phi(T,i^{\prime},h))

as in (
5.9.1.1
). We write
y_{1}^{\prime}
for the image of
y^{\prime}
under the analogous map

X(\Psi_{T,h})_{\ntr}\to Y(\phi(T,i^{\prime}_{1},h)).

By Lemma
5.9.3
there exists
\delta_{T}
in the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
determined by
-\mu_{h}
such that
{\mathfrak{k}}(T,i^{\prime},h,\delta_{T})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
represents
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y^{\prime})]
and
{\mathfrak{k}}(T,i^{\prime}_{1},h,\delta_{T})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
represents
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y^{\prime}_{1})]
. By Proposition
5.7.6
(i), the image of
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})^{\circ}
under the map
Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})\to T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}),z\mapsto\delta_{z}
is precisely the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence of
\delta_{T}
in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
. Thus we can find
y\in Y(\Upsilon_{(T,h)})^{\circ}
such that
\delta_{y}=\delta_{T}
. This
y
is our desired element.
∎

Definition 5.11.6
.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be a quasi-gauge. By a
rectification of
{\mathfrak{g}}
, we mean an element
u\in\ker(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G))
satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

The pair
(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot u,\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is a marking of
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
.

(ii)

Let
u_{p}
be the component of
u
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. We have

i^{\prime}(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})=u_{p}^{-1}i(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\sigma(u_{p}).

We call
{\mathfrak{g}}
a
gauge
, if it admits a rectification.

Lemma 5.11.7
.

A quasi-gauge
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a gauge if and only if
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is an amicable pair.

Proof.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{g}}
has a rectification
u
. Write
{\mathfrak{g}}=(T,h,i,i^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})
. To show that
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is amicable, we only need to check that the marking
(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot u,\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is
\pi^{*}
-compatible. By Proposition
5.7.6
(iii) and by the fact that
u\in\ker(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G))
, the image of
\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot u
in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
equals that of
i\circ h
. By Proposition
5.9.2
(i), the image of
\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
equals that of
i^{\prime}\circ h
. But
i\circ h
and
i^{\prime}\circ h
lie in the same connected component of
X
by assumption, so they have the same image in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
. Thus
(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot u,\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is indeed
\pi^{*}
-compatible.

Conversely, suppose that
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is an amicable pair. By Lemma
5.10.2
, there is a
\pi^{*}
-compatible marking of
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
of the form
(\bar{z},\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}})
for some
\bar{z}\in\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
. Find
w\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
such that
\bar{z}=\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot w
. Again by Proposition
5.7.6
(iii), Proposition
5.9.2
(i), and the assumption that
i\circ h
and
i^{\prime}\circ h
lie in the same connected component of
X
, we know that
\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\bar{y}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}}
have the same image in
\pi^{*}(G,X)
. Since
(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot w,\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is
\pi^{*}
-compatible, we must have
w\in\ker(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G))
.

By the defining property of a quasi-gauge,
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})]\subset{{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}/{\equiv}}
contains the image of
{\mathfrak{k}}(T,i^{\prime},h,\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
. Since
(\bar{z},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is a marking of
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
, we know that
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})]
also contains
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{z})
, which is equal to
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\cdot w
. By Proposition
5.7.6
(ii), the
\delta
-component of
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is
i(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
. By definition, the
\delta
-component of
{\mathfrak{k}}(T,i^{\prime},h,\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is
i^{\prime}(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
. Thus there exists
w_{0}\in\mathscr{U}_{\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
such that

(w_{p}w_{0})^{-1}i(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\sigma(w_{p}w_{0})=i^{\prime}(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}}),

where
w_{p}
denotes the component of
w
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
.

Let
u=ww_{0}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
. Since
w\in\ker(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G))
and
w_{0}\in{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
, we have
u\in\ker(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G))
. It is straightforward to check that
u
is a rectification of
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
∎

Definition 5.11.8
.

Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i,h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
. We write
\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
for the isogeny class of
x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
, and write
\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
for the conjugacy class of the admissible morphism
\phi({\mathfrak{s}}):\mathfrak{Q}\to\mathfrak{G}_{G}
.

Corollary 5.11.9
.

Let
{\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
. Then
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{s}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{s}}})
is an amicable pair.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.11.5
, we can extend the special fork
({\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{s}})
to a quasi-gauge of the form
{\mathfrak{g}}=(T,h,i,i,y,y^{\prime})
. By the defining property of a quasi-gauge, the fact that the two embeddings in
{\mathfrak{g}}
are both
i
, and Proposition
5.7.6
(ii), we know that
(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is a marking of
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
. It follows that
u=1
is a rectification of
{\mathfrak{g}}
, and therefore
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{s}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{s}}})=(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is amicable by Lemma
5.11.7
.
∎

Definition 5.11.10
.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}=(T,h,i,i^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})
be a gauge. We have a natural
\mathbb{Q}
-embedding
T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
whose composition with
I_{\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is
i^{\prime}
, and a natural
\mathbb{Q}
-embedding
T\hookrightarrow I_{x_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
as in (
5.7.2.2
). We say that a
\mathbb{Q}
-isomorphism
f:I_{x_{\mathfrak{g}}}I_{\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
is
{\mathfrak{g}}
-adapted
, if it satisfies the following conditions.

(i)

By Lemma
5.11.7
,
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is (weakly) amicable, so there is canonical
I_{\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
-conjugacy class of isomorphisms
I_{x_{\mathfrak{g}}}I_{\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
as in §
5.10.3
(cf. Remark
5.10.10
). We require that
f
lies in this conjugacy class.

(ii)

f
commutes with the natural
\mathbb{Q}
-embeddings
T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi_{\mathfrak{g}}}
and
T\hookrightarrow I_{x_{\mathfrak{g}}}
.

5.11.11
.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}=(T,h,i,i^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})
be a gauge. By Lemma
5.11.7
, the pair
(\mathscr{I}_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathscr{J}_{\mathfrak{g}})
is amicable. Therefore by Definition
5.10.9
we have a well-defined element

\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\in{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)=\cok({\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})/\Sha^{\infty}_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{\phi}^{\dagger})).

Define
T^{\dagger}
and
\Sha^{\infty}_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\dagger})
as in §
5.11.2
. Then we have
Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}\subset T^{\dagger}\subset I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
as subgroups of
I_{\phi}
. Thus we have a natural map from
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
to

(5.11.11.1)

\displaystyle\cok\bigg({\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},T^{\dagger})/\Sha_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\dagger})\bigg).

Here the map in the definition of the cokernel is the restriction of the boundary map
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T^{\dagger})
arising from the short exact sequence
1\to T^{\dagger}\to T\to G^{\ab}\to 1
.

Proposition 5.11.12
.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}=(T,h,i,i^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})
be a gauge, and assume that there exists a
{\mathfrak{g}}
-adapted isomorphism
f:I_{x_{\mathfrak{g}}}I_{\phi_{\mathfrak{g}}}
. Then the image of
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
in (
5.11.11.1
), as explained in §
5.11.11
, is trivial.

Proof.

(I) Finding a representative of
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{g},\mathscr{J}_{g})
.

In this part, we find an element
\tau\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
representing
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathscr{J}_{\mathfrak{g}})
. Write
\phi
and
x
for
\phi_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
x_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
. We denote the image of
y
in
Y(x)
still by
y
, and denote the image of
y^{\prime}
in
Y(\phi)
still by
y^{\prime}
. Fix a rectification
u
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Let
z:=y\cdot u\in Y(x)
, and let
\bar{z}
be the image of
z
in
\bar{Y}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
. For each finite place
v
, let
I_{v}
be the
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-group associated with
{\mathfrak{k}}(y)
, and let
I^{\prime}_{v}
be the
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-group associated with (any element of)
[{\mathfrak{k}}(y^{\prime})]
. Then
I_{v}^{\prime}
is also the
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-group associated with
{\mathfrak{k}}(z)
since
(\bar{z},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is a marking. Fix a
{\mathfrak{g}}
-adapted isomorphism
f:I_{x}I_{\phi}
, which exists by our assumption. Then there exists
\tau=(\tau_{v})_{v}\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that

(5.11.12.1)

\displaystyle\iota_{z,v}\circ f^{-1}=\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ\Int(\tau_{v}):I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\to I_{v}^{\prime}.

As we showed in the proof of the “only if” direction in Lemma
5.11.7
, the marking
(\bar{z},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is
\pi^{*}
-compatible. Hence
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
is the image of
\tau
. In the rest of the proof we show that the image of
\tau
in (
5.11.11.1
) is trivial.

(II) Constructing an element
t_{v}\in T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
.

Choose
g\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that

(5.11.12.2)

\displaystyle\Int(g)\circ i=i^{\prime}.

Denote the natural embeddings
T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}
and
T\hookrightarrow I_{x}
(cf. Definition
5.11.10
) by
j^{\prime}
and
j
respectively. Then
\iota_{y,v}\circ j=i
and
\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ j^{\prime}=i^{\prime}
. Let
v
be a finite place, and let
t\in T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
be a test element. Write
u_{v}
for the component of
u
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
(resp.
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
) at
v\neq p
(resp.
v=p
). When
v\neq p
, we have the following equalities between elements of
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
:

(5.11.12.3)

\displaystyle\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\big(\tau_{v}j^{\prime}(t)\tau_{v}^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=\iota_{z,v}\circ f^{-1}(j^{\prime}(t))

by (
5.11.12.1
)

\displaystyle=\iota_{z,v}(j(t))

since
f
is
{\mathfrak{g}}
-adapted

\displaystyle=\Int(u_{v})^{-1}\circ\iota_{y,v}(j(t))

\displaystyle\text{since }z=y\cdot u

\displaystyle=\Int(u_{v})^{-1}\circ i(t).

When
v=p
, the above computation is still valid if we interpret the equalities as between elements of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
, and view
u_{p}
as an element thereof via the map
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\to G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
induced by
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p},a\mapsto a\otimes 1
.

Choose
\widetilde{\tau}_{v}\in I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
lifting
\tau_{v}\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
, and write
\widehat{\tau}_{v}
for
\iota_{y^{\prime},v}(\widetilde{\tau}_{v})\in I_{v}^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
. Then we have

(5.11.12.4)

\displaystyle\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\big(\tau_{v}j^{\prime}(t)\tau_{v}^{-1}\big)=\Int\big(\widehat{\tau}_{v}\big)\big(\iota_{y^{\prime},v}(j^{\prime}(t))\big)=\Int\big(\widehat{\tau}_{v}\big)(i^{\prime}(t))=\Int\big(\widehat{\tau}_{v}g\big)(i(t)).

For every finite place
v
, set

s_{v}=u_{v}\widehat{\tau}_{v}g.

Comparing the computations (
5.11.12.3
) and (
5.11.12.4
), we have

(5.11.12.5)

\displaystyle i(t)=\Int(s_{v})(i(t)),\quad\forall t\in T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}).

When
v\neq p
,
s_{v}
is an element of
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
. It follows from (
5.11.12.5
) that
s_{v}\in i(T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}))
. When
v=p
,
s_{p}
is
a priori
an element of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. Now
\widehat{\tau}_{p}
lies in
I_{p}^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
, and
I_{p}^{\prime}
is the
\sigma
-centralizer of
i^{\prime}(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
. In the following computation, we let
\sigma
act on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
only via the first factor
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
. We have

\displaystyle s_{p}i(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\sigma(s_{p})^{-1}

\displaystyle=u_{p}\widehat{\tau}_{p}gi(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})g^{-1}\sigma(\tau_{p})^{-1}\sigma(u_{p})^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{because }\sigma(g)=g

\displaystyle=u_{p}\widehat{\tau}_{p}i^{\prime}(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\sigma(\widehat{\tau}_{p})^{-1}\sigma(u_{p})^{-1}

by (
5.11.12.2
)

\displaystyle=u_{p}i^{\prime}(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})\sigma(u_{p})^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{because }\widehat{\tau}_{p}\in I^{\prime}_{p}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=i(\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}})

\displaystyle\text{by (ii) in Definition \ref {defn:gauge rec}}.

Comparing this with (
5.11.12.5
), we see that
s_{p}
is in fact
\sigma
-invariant, i.e., it lies in
G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
(which is embedded into
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
via
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p},a\mapsto 1\otimes a
). It then follows from (
5.11.12.5
) that
s_{p}\in i(T(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}))
.

We have seen that
s_{v}\in i(T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}))
for every finite place
v
. Write

s_{v}=i(t_{v}),\quad t_{v}\in T(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}).

(III) Relationship between
t_{v}
and
\tau
.

In this part, we show that the image of
\tau
in (
5.11.11.1
) is represented by
(t_{v})_{v}
in a suitable sense.

In the sequel, for every
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra

R
and every
r\in G(R)
, we write
r^{\ab}
for the image of
r
in
G^{\ab}(R)
. Note that
s_{v}^{\ab}=u_{v}^{\ab}
. Since
u_{p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
and since
s_{p}
is
\sigma
-invariant, we have
u_{p}^{\ab}\in G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
35
35

35

This fact also follows directly from condition (ii) in Definition
5.11.6
.
Thus
u^{\ab}\in G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Since
u\in\ker(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G))
, we can write
u=u^{(0)}u^{(1)}
, with
u^{(0)}\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
and
u^{(1)}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{A}^{*}_{f}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Since
u^{\ab}\in G^{\ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we have
u^{(1),\ab}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

We view the embeddings
j^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}
and
i^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G
both as inclusions and omit them from the notations. For each finite place
v
, the cocycle

(\widehat{\tau}_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widehat{\tau}_{v})_{v\in\Gamma_{v}}
is valued in
T^{\dagger}
, and the collection of these cocycles for all
v
represents the image of
\tau
in (
5.11.11.1
). Let
v
be a finite place and let
\rho\in\Gamma_{v}
. In the following computation, if
v=p
we let
\rho
act on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
only via the second factor
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
. In particular
u_{v}
is
\rho
-invariant even for
v=p
. We compute

(5.11.12.6)

\widehat{\tau}_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widehat{\tau}_{v}=gs_{v}^{-1}u_{v}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}u_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}s_{v}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}g^{-1}=gs_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}s_{v}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}g^{-1}\\
=gi(t_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}t_{v})g^{-1}g{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}g^{-1}=t_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}t_{v}g{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}g^{-1}.

Here for the last equality we used (
5.11.12.2
), and in the last term we wrote
t_{v}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}t_{v}^{-1}
for
i^{\prime}(t_{v}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}t_{v}^{-1})
as we explained above. By Lemma
5.11.3
, the cocycle
(g{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}g^{-1})_{\rho\in\Gamma}
represents an element of
\Sha_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\dagger})
. Therefore the cocycle
(t_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}t_{v})_{\rho\in\Gamma_{v}}
represents an element
\beta_{v}\in\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},T^{\dagger})
, and the collection
(\beta_{v})_{v}
represents the image of
\tau
in (
5.11.11.1
).

(IV) Finishing the proof.

To finish the proof it suffices to show that
(\beta_{v})_{v}
has trivial image in (
5.11.11.1
).
Now
\beta_{v}
is equal to the image of
t_{v}^{\ab}=s_{v}^{\ab}=u_{v}^{\ab}\in G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
under the boundary map
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},T^{\dagger})
associated with the short exact sequence
1\to T^{\dagger}\to T\to G^{\ab}\to 1
. We have
u^{\ab}=u^{(1),\ab}u^{(0),\ab}
. By Corollary
1.2.11
, the image of
u^{(0),\ab}
in
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},T^{\dagger})
comes from
\Sha^{\infty}_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\dagger})
. We have also seen that
u^{(1),\ab}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(with trivial components away from
p
). Hence the image of
(\beta_{v})_{v}
in (
5.11.11.1
) is indeed zero.
∎

Proposition 5.11.13
.

Assume that we have two quasi-gauges of the form

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{g}}=(T,h,i,i^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}),\quad{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}=(T,h,i_{1},i^{\prime}_{1},y,y^{\prime}).

Let
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Assume the following conditions.

(i)

We have
i_{1}=\Int(\lambda)\circ i
and
i_{1}^{\prime}=\Int(\lambda^{\prime})\circ i^{\prime}
.

(ii)

We have
\lambda\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}i(T^{\dagger}(\mathbb{C}))
, and
\lambda^{\prime}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}i^{\prime}(T^{\dagger}(\mathbb{C}))
.

(iii)

Define
T^{\dagger}
to be the kernel of the common map
T\to G^{\ab}
induced by
i,i^{\prime},i_{1},i_{1}^{\prime}
. There exists a cocycle
\beta_{0}(\cdot)\in Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\dagger})
such that

(5.11.13.1)

\displaystyle i(\beta_{0}(\rho))=\lambda^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda,\quad i^{\prime}(\beta_{0}(\rho))={\lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda^{\prime},\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma.

(iv)

There exists a rectification
u
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
such that
u\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. In particular,
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a gauge.

(v)

There exists a
{\mathfrak{g}}
-adapted isomorphism
f:I_{x_{\mathfrak{g}}}I_{\phi_{\mathfrak{g}}}
. (This notion makes sense since
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a gauge.)

Then
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
admits a rectification
u_{1}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, and there exists a
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
-adapted isomorphism
f_{1}:I_{x_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}}}I_{\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}}}
.

Remark 5.11.14
.

By condition (ii), for each
\rho\in\Gamma
we have
\lambda^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda\in i(T^{\dagger})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and
\lambda^{\prime-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda^{\prime}\in i^{\prime}(T^{\dagger})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. In view of this, condition (iii) is equivalent to the requirement that
i^{-1}(\lambda^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda)=i^{\prime-1}(\lambda^{\prime-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda^{\prime})
for all
\rho\in\Gamma
.

Proof.

(I) Some notations.

We write
x
and
x_{1}
for the points
x_{{\mathfrak{g}}}=x_{(T,i,h)}
and
x_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}}=x_{(T,i_{1},h)}
in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}(\overline{\mathbb F}_{p})
respectively, and write
\phi
and
\phi_{1}
for the admissible morphisms
\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}}=\phi(T,i^{\prime},h)
and
\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}}=\phi(T,i^{\prime}_{1},h)
respectively. Let
j:T\hookrightarrow I_{x}
,
j^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}
,
j_{1}:T\hookrightarrow I_{x_{1}}
, and
j_{1}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi_{1}}
be the canonical
\mathbb{Q}
-embeddings.
We denote the image of
y
in
Y(x)
still by
y
, and denote the image of
y
in
Y(x_{1})
by
y_{1}
. Similarly, we denote the image of
y^{\prime}
in
Y(\phi)
still by
y^{\prime}
, and denote the image of
y^{\prime}
in
Y(\phi_{1})
by
y^{\prime}_{1}
.

By construction
\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}}=\delta_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
. We write
\delta_{T}
for this element. To simplify notation, for
\varpi\in\left\{i,i^{\prime},i_{1},i_{1}^{\prime}\right\}
, we write
{\mathfrak{k}}(\varpi)
for
{\mathfrak{k}}(T,\varpi,h,\delta_{T})\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
(see §
5.3.9
). Since
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
are quasi-gauges, we know that

{\mathfrak{k}}(i^{\prime})
(resp.
{\mathfrak{k}}(i_{1}^{\prime})
) represents
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})]
(resp.
[{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}})]
). By Proposition
5.7.6
(ii), we know that
{\mathfrak{k}}(i)
(resp.
{\mathfrak{k}}(i_{1})
) represents
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
(resp.
{\mathfrak{k}}(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}})
).

Let
v
be a finite place. Let
I_{v},I_{v}^{\prime},I_{v,1},I_{v,1}^{\prime}
be the reductive groups over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
associated with
{\mathfrak{k}}(i),{\mathfrak{k}}(i^{\prime}),{\mathfrak{k}}(i_{1}),{\mathfrak{k}}(i_{1}^{\prime})
respectively. Write
u_{v}
for the component of
u
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
(resp.
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
) for
v\neq p
(resp.
v=p
). Then we have a
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-isomorphism
\Int(u_{v})^{-1}:I_{v}I_{v}^{\prime}
, and we have

(5.11.14.1)

\displaystyle\iota_{y\cdot u,v}=\Int(u_{v})^{-1}\circ\iota_{y,v}:I_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}I_{v}^{\prime},

cf. the third equality in (
5.11.12.3
).
36
36

36

As in the proof of Proposition
5.11.12
, when
v=p
we view
u_{p}
as an element of
(\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. The isomorphism
\Int(u_{p}):I_{p}I_{p}^{\prime}
is understood as the isomorphism between two subfunctors of
\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
induced by the inner automorphism
\Int(u_{p})
of
\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
.
If
I
is one of the four groups
I_{v},I_{v}^{\prime},I_{v,1},I_{v,1}^{\prime}
, there is a canonical
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-map
I\to G^{\ab}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
, and we denote the kernel by
I^{\dagger}
.

(II) Commutative diagrams involving
u
.

As in §
5.10.3
, let
\tau=(\tau_{v})_{v}\in I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be the element associated with
(x,\phi,y\cdot u,y^{\prime})
and
f
. Namely, for each finite place
v
we have

(5.11.14.2)

\displaystyle\iota_{y\cdot u,v}\circ f^{-1}=\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ\Int(\tau_{v}):I_{\phi,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\longrightarrow I_{v}^{\prime}.

The diagram

\textstyle{I_{v}^{\dagger}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Int(u_{v})^{-1}}
\textstyle{I_{v}^{\prime,\dagger}}
\textstyle{I_{x}^{\dagger}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y,v}}
\scriptstyle{\Int(\tau_{v})\circ f}
\textstyle{I_{\phi}^{\dagger}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y^{\prime},v}}

consists of
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
-isomorphisms, and it commutes by (
5.11.14.1
) and (
5.11.14.2
). Since
\Int(\tau_{v})
induces the identity on
\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{\phi}^{\dagger})\cong\coh^{1}_{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{\phi}^{\dagger})
, and since
f\circ j=j^{\prime}
(as
f
is
{\mathfrak{g}}
-adapted), we obtain from (
5.11
) a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{v}^{\dagger})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Int(u_{v})^{-1}}
\textstyle{\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},I_{v}^{\prime,\dagger})}
\textstyle{\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},T^{\dagger})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y,v}\circ j}
\textstyle{\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},T^{\dagger})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ j^{\prime}}

Since
\iota_{y,v}\circ j=i
and
\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ j^{\prime}=i^{\prime}
, the above commutative diagram implies that there exists
\Delta_{v}\in I_{v}^{\prime,\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
such that

(5.11.14.7)

\displaystyle u_{v}^{-1}i(\beta_{0}(\rho))u_{v}=\Delta_{v}\cdot i^{\prime}(\beta_{0}(\rho))\cdot{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\Delta_{v}^{-1},\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma_{v}.

In view of (
5.11.13.1
), we can rewrite (
5.11.14.7
) as

(5.11.14.8)

\displaystyle u_{v}^{-1}\lambda^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda u_{v}=\Delta_{v}{\lambda^{\prime}}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda^{\prime}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\Delta_{v}^{-1},\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma_{v}.

(III) Constructing
u_{1}
.

We shall construct a rectification
u_{1}
of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
. Let
u_{1,v}:=\lambda u_{v}\Delta_{v}\lambda^{\prime-1}.
Then
u_{1,v}\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
for
v\neq p
and
u_{1,p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
. Here when
v=p
we view
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in G(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
as inside
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
via
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p},a\mapsto 1\otimes a
, and view
u_{p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
as inside
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
via
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p},a\mapsto a\otimes 1
. When
v=p
, we let
\Gamma_{p}
act on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
only via the second factor
\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p}
. Thus
u_{v}
is
\Gamma_{v}
-invariant for every finite place
v
. It then immediately follows from (
5.11.14.8
) that
u_{1,v}
is
\Gamma_{v}
-invariant. Thus we have
u_{1,v}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
for
v\neq p
and
u_{1,p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. We may and shall also assume that the elements
\Delta_{v}
have been chosen such that the element
(\Omega_{v})_{v\neq p}:=(\iota_{y^{\prime},v}^{-1}(\Delta_{v}))_{v\neq p}\in\prod_{v\neq p}I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
comes from
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. We then see that the element
u_{1}:=(u_{1,v})_{v}\in\prod_{v\neq p}G(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\times G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
lies in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})
.

For
v\neq p
, we have
u_{v}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
by our assumption (i), and so
u_{1,v}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
. Again by the assumption (i), the image of
u_{p}
in
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
lies in
{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
. Since
u_{p}
and
u_{1,p}
have the same image in
G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
, we have
u_{1,p}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
by the surjectivity of
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})\to{\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})
(which follows from Lang’s theorem applied to
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{der}}
; see the proof of Proposition
4.5.2
). We have thus constructed an element
u_{1}\in G_{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}){\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}})
.

(IV) Proof that
u_{1}
is a rectification of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
.

Clearly
u_{1}\in\ker(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{*})\to\pi^{*}(G))
. For a sufficiently divisible
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, let
\gamma_{0,T,n}=\delta_{T}\sigma(\delta_{T})\cdots\sigma^{n-1}(\delta_{T})\in T(\mathbb{Q})
(see §
5.3.9
). Then
{\mathfrak{k}}(\varpi)\in{\mathfrak{KT}}^{\mathrm{str}}
is represented by

(\varpi(\gamma_{0,T,n}),(\varpi(\gamma_{0,T,n}))_{v\neq p},\varpi(\delta_{T}))\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{\mathrm{str}}_{n},

for
\varpi\in\left\{i,i^{\prime},i_{1},i_{1}^{\prime}\right\}
. Clearly
\varpi(\gamma_{0,T,n})
for all four choices of
\varpi
are conjugate in
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. For a finite place
v\neq p
, we have

(5.11.14.9)

\displaystyle\Int(u_{1,v})^{-1}(i_{1}(\gamma_{0,T,n}))

\displaystyle=\Int\big(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{v}^{-1}u_{v}^{-1}\lambda^{-1}\big)(i_{1}(\gamma_{0,T,n}))

\displaystyle=\Int\big(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{v}^{-1}u_{v}^{-1}\big)(i(\gamma_{0,T,n}))

\displaystyle=\Int\big(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{v}^{-1}\big)(i^{\prime}(\gamma_{0,T,n}))

\displaystyle=\Int\big(\lambda^{\prime}\big)(i^{\prime}(\gamma_{0,T,n}))

\displaystyle=i_{1}^{\prime}(\gamma_{0,T,n}).

Here the second equality is because
i_{1}=\Int(\lambda)\circ i
, the third equality is because
{\mathfrak{k}}(i)\cdot u\in{\mathfrak{k}}(i^{\prime})\cdot\mathscr{U}_{\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
, the fourth equality is because
\Delta_{v}\in I_{v}^{\prime}
centralizes
i^{\prime}(\gamma_{0,T,n})
, and the fifth equality is because
i_{1}^{\prime}=\Int(\lambda^{\prime})\circ i^{\prime}
.
For
a,b\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
, we write
\Int_{\sigma}(a)(b)
for
ab\sigma(a)^{-1}
, where
\sigma
acts on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
only via the first factor
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}
. Analogously as in the above computation, we have

(5.11.14.10)

\displaystyle\Int_{\sigma}(u_{1,p})^{-1}(i_{1}(\delta_{T}))

\displaystyle=\Int_{\sigma}\big(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{p}^{-1}u_{p}^{-1}\lambda^{-1}\big)(i_{1}(\delta_{T}))

\displaystyle=\Int_{\sigma}\big(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{p}^{-1}u_{p}^{-1}\big)(i(\delta_{T}))

\displaystyle=\Int_{\sigma}\big(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{p}^{-1}\big)(i^{\prime}(\delta_{T}))

\displaystyle=\Int_{\sigma}\big(\lambda^{\prime}\big)(i^{\prime}(\delta_{T}))

\displaystyle=i_{1}^{\prime}(\delta_{T}).

Here the second equality is because
i_{1}=\Int(\lambda)\circ i=\Int_{\sigma}(\lambda)\circ i
, the third equality is by condition (ii) in Definition
5.11.6
, the fourth equality is because
i^{\prime}(\delta_{T})
is
\sigma
-centralized by
\Delta_{p}\in I_{p}^{\prime}
, and the fifth equality is because
i_{1}^{\prime}=\Int(\lambda^{\prime})\circ i^{\prime}=\Int_{\sigma}(\lambda^{\prime})\circ i^{\prime}.

The fact that
i_{1}(\gamma_{0,T,n})
and
i_{1}^{\prime}(\gamma_{0,T,n})
are conjugate in
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, together with (
5.11.14.9
) and (
5.11.14.10
), implies that
u_{1}
is a rectification of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
.

(V) Constructing
f_{1}
.

We have a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-isomorphism
\psi:I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I_{\phi_{1},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
induced by
\Int(\lambda^{\prime})
. Write
\phi(q_{\rho})=g_{\rho}\rtimes\rho
and
\phi_{1}(q_{\rho})=g_{1,\rho}\rtimes\rho
. Then
g_{\rho}\in i^{\prime}(T)(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and
g_{1,\rho}=\Int(\lambda^{\prime})(g_{\rho})
. We compute

{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\psi(\cdot)

\displaystyle=g_{1,\rho}\rho[\Int(\lambda^{\prime})(g_{\rho^{-1}}\rho^{-1}(\cdot)g_{\rho^{-1}}^{-1})]g_{1,\rho}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Int(\lambda^{\prime}g_{\rho}\lambda^{\prime-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda^{\prime}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}g_{\rho^{-1}})(\cdot)

\displaystyle=\Int(\lambda^{\prime}g_{\rho}\lambda^{\prime-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\lambda^{\prime}g_{\rho}^{-1})(\cdot),

where in the last step we use that
g_{\rho}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}g_{\rho^{-1}}
is central in
I_{\phi}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. By (
5.11.13.1
) and the fact that
g_{\rho}\in i^{\prime}(T)
, we have

{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\psi(\cdot)=\Int({\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\lambda^{\prime}).
Thus
\psi
is an inner twisting satisfying

(5.11.14.11)

\displaystyle\psi^{-1}\circ{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\psi=\Int(j^{\prime}(\beta_{0}(\rho)))\in\Aut(I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}),\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma.

We now construct an inner twisting
\chi:I_{x,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I_{x_{1},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
analogous to
\psi
. As in §
5.7.2
, we let
\widetilde{x}=\widetilde{x}_{(T,i,h),K_{1}^{p}}
and
\widetilde{x}_{1}=\widetilde{x}_{(T,i_{1},h),K_{1}^{p}}
. These are points of
\Sh_{K_{1}}(F)
for some finite extension
F/E_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Under the canonical isomorphisms
\mathrm{triv}_{(T,i,h)}:V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
and
\mathrm{triv}_{(T,i_{1},h)}:V^{*}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})
as in §
5.7.2
, the element
\lambda\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
induces an isomorphism of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
-Hodge structures
\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{V}_{B,\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{x}_{1})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
which sends the tensors
s_{\alpha,B,\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{x}}
to
s_{\alpha,B,\mathbb{Q},\widetilde{x}_{1}}
. Thus
\lambda
induces an element
\theta\in I_{x_{1},x}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, satisfying

(5.11.14.12)

\displaystyle\theta\circ\rho(\theta^{-1})=j(\beta_{0}(\rho))\in I_{x}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}),\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma.

Define
\chi:I_{x,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}I_{x_{1},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
to be the isomorphism induced by
\theta
. From (
5.11.14.12
) it immediately follows that
\chi
is an inner twisting satisfying

(5.11.14.13)

\displaystyle\chi^{-1}\circ{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\chi=\Int(j(\beta_{0}(\rho)))\in\Aut(I_{x,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}),\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma.

We define
f_{1}
by the commutative diagram

\textstyle{I_{x,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\textstyle{I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I_{x_{1},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{I_{\phi_{1},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}}

By (
5.11.14.11
), (
5.11.14.13
), and the fact that
f\circ j=j^{\prime}
(as
f
is
{\mathfrak{g}}
-adapted), we know that
f_{1}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-isomorphism. Moreover,
\chi
and
\psi
commute with the canonical embeddings of
T
. Hence
f_{1}\circ j_{1}=j_{1}^{\prime}
.

(VI) Constructing
\tau_{1}
.

Let
v
be a finite place. From the constructions of
\psi
and
\chi
, we have the commutative diagrams

\textstyle{I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y^{\prime},v}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{(I_{v}^{\prime})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Int(\lambda^{\prime})}
\textstyle{I_{\phi_{1},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y^{\prime}_{1},v}}
\textstyle{(I_{v,1}^{\prime})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}}

and

\textstyle{I_{x,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y,v}}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\textstyle{(I_{v})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Int(\lambda)}
\textstyle{I_{x_{1},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{y_{1},v}}
\textstyle{(I_{v,1})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}}

As in part (III), let
\Omega_{v}:=\iota_{y^{\prime},v}^{-1}(\Delta_{v})\in I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
. Let
\widetilde{\tau}_{v}\in I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
be a lift of
\tau_{v}
.
Let

\widetilde{\tau}_{1,v}:=\psi(\Omega_{v}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{v})\in I_{\phi_{1}}^{\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}).

Using
\iota_{y,v}\circ j=i
and
\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ j^{\prime}=i^{\prime}
, we can rewrite (
5.11.14.7
) as

\displaystyle\Int(u_{v})^{-1}\circ\iota_{y,v}(j(\beta_{0}(\rho)))=\Delta_{v}\cdot\iota_{y^{\prime},v}(j^{\prime}(\beta_{0}(\rho)))\cdot{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\Delta_{v}^{-1},\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma_{v}.

By (
5.11.14.1
) and (
5.11.14.2
), we deduce from the above equality
that

\displaystyle\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ\Int(\tau_{v})\circ f(j(\beta_{0}(\rho)))=\Delta_{v}\cdot\iota_{y^{\prime},v}(j^{\prime}(\beta_{0}(\rho)))\cdot{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\Delta_{v}^{-1},\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma_{v}.

Using
f\circ j=j^{\prime}
and the definition of
\Omega_{v}
, we get

(5.11.14.22)

\displaystyle\Int(\tau_{v})(j^{\prime}(\beta_{0}(\rho)))=\Omega_{v}(j^{\prime}(\beta_{0}(\rho))){\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\Omega_{v}^{-1},\quad\forall\rho\in\Gamma_{v}.

Write
\beta_{\rho}
for
j^{\prime}(\beta_{0}(\rho))
.
We compute

(5.11.14.23)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{1,v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{1,v}

\displaystyle=\psi(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}\Omega_{v})\cdot({\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\psi)({\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\Omega_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{v})

\displaystyle=\psi(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}\Omega_{v})\cdot\psi(\beta_{\rho}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\Omega_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{v}\beta_{\rho}^{-1})

by (
5.11.14.11
)

\displaystyle=\psi(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}\Omega_{v}\beta_{\rho}{\vphantom{\rho}}^{\rho}\Omega_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{v}\beta_{\rho}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\psi(\beta_{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{v}\beta_{\rho}^{-1})

by (
5.11.14.22
)

\displaystyle=\psi(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{v})

\displaystyle\text{because $\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}{\vphantom{\rho}}{}^{\rho}\widetilde{\tau}_{v}\in Z_{\phi}^{\dagger}$}.

Since
\psi
is an inner twisting, it follows from (
5.11.14.23
) that the image of
\widetilde{\tau}_{1,v}
in
I_{\phi_{1}}^{\ad}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
lies in
I_{\phi_{1}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
. We denote this element by
\tau_{1,v}
. Recall from part (III) that
(\Omega_{v})_{v\neq p}
comes from an element of
I_{\phi}^{\dagger}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. It easily follows that
\tau_{1}:=(\tau_{1,v})_{v}\in\prod_{v}I_{\phi_{1}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
is in fact an element of
I_{\phi_{1}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

(VII) Proof that
\tau_{1}
is associated with
(x_{1},\phi_{1},y_{1}\cdot u_{1},y_{1}^{\prime})
and
f_{1}
.

We compute

\displaystyle\iota_{y_{1}^{\prime},v}

\displaystyle=\Int(\lambda^{\prime})\circ\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ\psi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Int(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{v}^{-1})\circ\iota_{y^{\prime},v}\circ\Int(\Omega_{v})\circ\psi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Int(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{v}^{-1})\circ\iota_{y\cdot u,v}\circ f^{-1}\circ\Int(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}\Omega_{v})\circ\psi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Int(\lambda^{\prime}\Delta_{v}^{-1}u_{v}^{-1})\circ\iota_{y,v}\circ f^{-1}\circ\Int(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}\Omega_{v})\circ\psi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Int(u_{1,v}^{-1}\lambda)\circ\iota_{y,v}\circ f^{-1}\Int(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}\Omega_{v})\circ\psi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Int(u_{1,v}^{-1})\circ\iota_{y_{1},v}\circ\chi\circ f^{-1}\circ\Int(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}\Omega_{v})\circ\psi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Int(u_{1,v}^{-1})\circ\iota_{y_{1},v}\circ f_{1}^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\Int(\widetilde{\tau}_{v}^{-1}\Omega_{v})\circ\psi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Int(u_{1,v}^{-1})\circ\iota_{y_{1},v}\circ f_{1}^{-1}\circ\Int(\tau_{1,v})

\displaystyle=\iota_{y_{1}\cdot u_{1},v}\circ f_{1}^{-1}\circ\Int(\tau_{1,v}).

Here the first equality is by (
5.11
), the second by the definition of
\Omega_{v}
, the third by (
5.11.14.2
), the fourth by (
5.11.14.1
), the fifth by the definition of
u_{1,v}
, the sixth by (
5.11
), the seventh by the definition of
f_{1}
, and the ninth by the analogue of (
5.11.14.1
).
This shows that
f_{1}:I_{x_{1}}I_{\phi_{1}}
is the canonical isomorphism (up to
I_{\phi_{1}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
-conjugation) as in §
5.10.3
, and that
\tau_{1}
is the element of
I_{\phi_{1}}^{\ad}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
associated with
(x_{1},\phi_{1},y_{1}\cdot u_{1},y_{1}^{\prime})
and
f_{1}
. We have already seen in part (V) that
f_{1}\circ j_{1}=j_{1}^{\prime}
. We conclude that
f_{1}
is
{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
-adapted.
∎

5.11.15
.

Keep the assumptions of Proposition
5.11.13
. Then
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
both admit rectifications, and so the pairs
(\mathscr{I}_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
and
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}})
are amicable by Lemma
5.11.7
. Thus we have the elements

(5.11.15.1)

\displaystyle(\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}),\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}))\in{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)\oplus\mathcal{H}(\phi)

and

(5.11.15.2)

\displaystyle(\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}}),\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}}))\in{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{1})\oplus\mathcal{H}(\phi_{1})

as in Definition
5.10.9
.
Note that we have
\phi\approx\phi_{1}
, where
\approx
is defined in §
2.6.16
. Thus the abelian groups
\mathcal{H}(\phi)
and
\mathcal{H}(\phi_{1})
are canonically identified by §
2.6.16
, and similarly the abelian groups
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
and
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{1})
are canonically identified by §
5.10.5
.

Proposition 5.11.16
.

The canonical identification
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)\oplus\mathcal{H}(\phi)\cong{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{1})\oplus\mathcal{H}(\phi_{1})
sends (
5.11.15.1
) to (
5.11.15.2
).

Proof.

Let
\tau
and
\tau_{1}
be as in the proof of Proposition
5.11.13
. As we showed in the proof of Lemma
5.11.7
, the marking
(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot u,\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
of
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
and the marking
(\bar{y}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}}\cdot u_{1},\bar{y}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}})
of
(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}})
are in fact
\pi^{*}
-compatible. Hence
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
and
\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}})
are the images of
\tau
in
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
and in
{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
respectively, while
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}})
and
\tau^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}},\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}})
are the images of
\tau_{1}
in
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{1})
and in
{\mathcal{H}}(\phi_{1})
respectively. It remains to show that the natural image of
\tau
in
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)\oplus{\mathcal{H}}(\phi)
corresponds to the natural image of
\tau_{1}
in
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{1})\oplus{\mathcal{H}}(\phi_{1})
. This follows from (
5.11.14.23
).
∎

5.12.
Galois cohomological properties of amicable pairs

5.12.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
be an amicable pair. Let
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. For any maximal torus
T\subset I_{\phi}
, we write
T^{\dagger}
for
T\cap I_{\phi}^{\dagger}=\ker(T\to G^{\ab})
, which is a subtorus of
T
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. As in §
5.10.5
, we define
\Sha_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},H)
for any
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
H\subset I_{\phi}^{\dagger}
, in particular for
H=T^{\dagger}
.

We have a boundary map
\partial:G^{\ab}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\coh^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T^{\dagger})
arising from the short exact sequence
1\to T^{\dagger}\to T\to G^{\ab}\to 1
. Recall from Definition
5.10.9
that there is a canonical element
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})\in{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
. There is a natural homomorphism from
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
to the group

(5.12.1.1)

\displaystyle\cok\bigg({\mathcal{G}}^{\ab}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\partial}\coh^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f},T^{\dagger})/\Sha_{G_{\mathrm{der}}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},T^{\dagger})\bigg)

Theorem 5.12.2
.

In the setting of §
5.12.1
, the image of
\tau^{{\mathfrak{H}}}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
in (
5.12.1.1
) is trivial.

Proof.

Let
j^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}
be the inclusion map. Pick a
\pi^{*}
-compatible marking
(\bar{y},\bar{y}^{\prime})
of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
. Pick
x\in\mathscr{I}
. Pick
y\in Y(x)
lifting
\bar{y}
, and pick
y^{\prime}\in Y(\phi)
lifting
\bar{y}^{\prime}
. Choose an isomorphism
f:I_{x}I_{\phi}
as in §
5.10.3
and Remark
5.10.10
. Let
j
be the composition
T\xrightarrow{j^{\prime}}I_{\phi}\xrightarrow{f^{-1}}I_{x}
. Via
j
we view
T
also as a maximal torus in
I_{x}
.

We claim that a cocharacter
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
is
\phi
-admissible in the sense of §
3.3.8
if and only if it is
x
-admissible in the sense of §
5.7.7
. In fact, using Theorem
3.3.9
, Proposition
5.9.2
, Theorem
5.7.8
, and Proposition
5.7.6
, one can show that the elements
(\phi(p)\circ\zeta_{p})^{\Delta}
and
\nu_{\delta_{y}}
of
X_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
(introduced in §
3.3.8
and §
5.7.7
respectively) are both equal to the Newton cocharacter of
[\delta_{T}]\in\B(T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, where
\delta_{T}
is any element of the
\overset{\circ}{\sim}
-equivalence class in
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{{\mathrm{ur}}})^{\mot}
corresponding to
-\mu_{h}
. It is also clear that for
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
the composition (
5.7.7.1
) lies in
\bbmu_{X}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
if and only if
j^{\prime}\circ\mu
lies in
\bbmu_{X}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
.
The claim follows.

By the above claim, Theorem
3.3.9
, and Theorem
5.7.8
, we find the following objects:

•

Two special point data of the form
{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i,h),{\mathfrak{s}}=(T,i^{\prime},h)\in\mathcal{SPD}(G,X)
such that
\mathscr{I}=\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
and
\mathscr{J}=\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}
.

•

An isomorphism
w:I_{x}I_{x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}
which is induced by some element of
I_{x,x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}(\mathbb{Q})
, satisfying that the composition
w\circ j:T\to I_{x_{{\mathfrak{s}}}}
is the canonical embedding (i.e., the embedding (
5.7.2.2
)).

•

An isomorphism
w^{\prime}:I_{\phi}I_{\phi({\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})}
which is induced by some element of
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
conjugating
\phi
to
\phi({\mathfrak{s}})
, satisfying that the composition
w^{\prime}\circ j^{\prime}:T\to I_{\phi({\mathfrak{s}})}
is the canonical embedding (i.e., the one whose composition with
I_{\phi({\mathfrak{s}}),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is
i^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
).

We claim that we can choose the above objects such that
(T,h,i,i^{\prime})
is a special fork (see Definition
5.11.1
). In fact, from the fact that
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
is weakly amicable it already follows that
i
and
i^{\prime}
are conjugate by
G^{\ad}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
for each finite place
v
. Then
i
and
i^{\prime}
are conjugate by
G^{\ad}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, since
i(T)
and
i^{\prime}(T)
are
\mathbb{Q}
-maximal tori in
G
and since the absolute Weyl group of
i(T)
in
G
is the same when considered over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}
and when considered over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. Also note that we can freely replace
i^{\prime}
by
\Int(g)\circ i^{\prime}
for
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})
. By the real approximation theorem, we can choose
g
such that
\Int(g)\circ i^{\prime}\circ h
lies in the same connected component of
X
as
i\circ h
. The claim is proved.

The canonical isomorphism
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi){\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}})
commutes with the natural map from
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi)
to (
5.12.1.1
) induced by
j^{\prime}:T\to I_{\phi}
and the natural map from
{\mathfrak{H}}(\phi_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}})
to (
5.12.1.1
) induced by the canonical embedding
T\to I_{\phi({\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})}
. Thus we can reduce the theorem to the following situation:

•

We have a special fork
({\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})=(T,h,i,i^{\prime})
such that
\mathscr{I}=\mathscr{I}_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
and
\mathscr{J}=\mathscr{J}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}
. Moreover,
\phi=\phi({\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
, and the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow I_{\phi}
is the canonical embedding, namely the one whose composition with
I_{\phi,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is
i^{\prime}
.

By Lemma
5.11.5
and Lemma
5.11.7
, we can extend the special fork
({\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
to a gauge
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Moreover, tracing the above reduction steps we see that there exists a
{\mathfrak{g}}
-adapted isomorphism
f:I_{x_{\mathfrak{g}}}I_{\phi_{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
. (This comes from the initial definition of
j
in the first paragraph of the proof.) The theorem then follows from Proposition
5.11.12
.
∎

5.12.3
.

Let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{J})
be a weakly amicable pair. Fix
x\in\mathscr{I}
and
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
. Recall from §
5.10.3
and Remark
5.10.10
that we have an isomorphism
f:I_{x}I_{\phi}
which is canonical up to
I_{\phi}^{\ad}(\mathbb{Q})
-conjugation. In particular, we have a canonical isomorphism between the abelian groups
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})
and
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{x})
that is independent of all choices. Now fix an element
\beta\in\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{\phi})
, also viewed as an element of
\Sha_{G}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q},I_{x})
. As in Definition
2.1.17
and Proposition
2.6.12
, we obtain the twisted admissible morphism
\phi^{\beta}
, which is well defined up to conjugacy. We denote the conjugacy class of
\phi^{\beta}
by
\mathscr{J}^{\beta}
. Note that for different choices of
\phi\in\mathscr{J}
, the abelian groups
