LS90
, §2.4]
. The same
a
-data and
\chi
-data may be chosen in the two cases to compare transfer factors, as the centralizer of
\gamma_{0,1}
determines the same root system in
G_{1}
and
G^{\prime}_{1}
respectively. Then one sees that each of
\Delta_{I}
,
\Delta_{II}
,
\Delta_{III_{1}}
,
\Delta_{III_{2}}
, and
\Delta_{IV}
is the same by inspecting the definition in
[
LS87
, §3]
.

Putting all this together, we deduce that
SO_{\gamma^{\prime}_{H_{1}}}(f^{H^{\prime}_{1}})
equals

\displaystyle\int_{Z^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\lambda^{-1}_{H^{\prime}_{1}}(z^{\prime})SO_{z^{\prime}\gamma_{H^{\prime}_{1}}}((\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}_{1})^{*}(f^{\prime}_{1}))dz^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\lambda^{-1}_{H^{\prime}_{1}}(y)\sum_{\delta^{\prime}_{1}}e(I_{y\delta^{\prime}_{1}})\Delta_{0}(N(y)\gamma^{\prime}_{H_{1}},y\delta^{\prime}_{1})TO_{y\delta^{\prime}_{1}}(f^{\prime}_{1})dy.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta^{\prime}_{1}}e(I_{\delta^{\prime}_{1}})\Delta_{0}(\gamma^{\prime}_{H_{1}},\delta^{\prime}_{1})\int_{Y_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}TO_{y\delta^{\prime}_{1}}(f^{\prime}_{1})dy.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{[b^{\prime}_{1}]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0,1},G^{\prime}_{1};\mathbb{Q}_{p})}e(I_{\delta_{[b^{\prime}_{1}]}})\Delta_{0}(\gamma^{\prime}_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0,1})\langle\beta_{p}(\gamma_{0,1},[b^{\prime}_{1}]),s^{\prime}_{1}\rangle TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(f).

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})}e(I_{\delta_{[b]}})\Delta_{0}(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0})\langle\beta_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s\rangle TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(f).

This finishes the proof in the unramified case.

The general case (when either
G_{1}
or
{\mathfrak{e}}
is ramified) works in the same way. If
z\gamma_{H_{1}}
is not a norm from
R_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
for any
z\in Z_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
then
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})=0
as before. Otherwise we assume that
\gamma^{\prime}_{H,1}:=z\gamma_{H,1}
is a norm for some
z
. Then we repeat the preceding argument, with the difference occurring in the choice of
f^{H^{\prime}_{1}}
. Namely by
[
Mor10
, Prop. A.3.14]
, there exists
f^{H^{\prime}_{1}}\in{\mathcal{H}}(H^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))
such that (
7.4.14.1
) holds true with
f^{H^{\prime}_{1}}
in place of
(\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}_{1})^{*}(f^{\prime}_{1})
, and such that
SO_{\gamma^{\prime}_{H_{1}}}(f^{H^{\prime}_{1}})=0
if
\gamma
is not a norm. Setting
f^{H^{\prime}_{1}}(h^{\prime}):=\int_{Z_{H^{\prime}_{1}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\lambda^{-1}_{H^{\prime}_{1}}(z^{\prime})f^{H^{\prime}_{1}}(z^{\prime}h^{\prime})dz^{\prime}
, we see from the above computation that Proposition
7.4.13
holds. The proof is complete.
∎

7.4.15
.

The last part of Proposition
7.4.13
can be slightly generalized, following
[
Kot90
, p. 181]
. Assume that
H
and
G
are unramified, thus choose
H_{1}
and
G_{1}
to be unramified, but allow
{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}
to be ramified. In that case, one can write
\eta_{1}=c\cdot\eta^{\circ}_{1}
with a continuous 1-cocycle
c:W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow Z(\widehat{H}_{1})
such that
(H_{1},{\mathcal{H}}_{1},s_{1},\eta^{\circ}_{1})
is unramified. (To see this, apply
[
Lan79c
, Prop. 1]
for an unramified extension to find
\eta^{\circ}_{1}
, and observe that
\eta_{1}
and
\eta^{\circ}_{1}
must differ by a continuous 1-cocycle valued in
Z(\widehat{H}_{1})
.) Via local class field theory,
c
determines a smooth character
\chi_{c}:H_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Writing
f^{H_{1},\circ}:=\widetilde{\eta}^{\circ,*}_{1}(f)
, we see that
f^{H_{1}}:=\chi_{c}\cdot f^{H_{1},\circ}
satisfies the desired condition of Proposition
7.4.13
. This follows from the fact that
\Delta_{0}(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0,1})
gets multiplied by
\chi_{c}(\gamma_{H,1})
when changing from
\eta^{\circ}_{1}
to
\eta_{1}
. (This factor comes from
\Delta_{2}
in
[
LS87
, §3.5]
as the 1-cocycle
a
there is replaced with
ac
.)

Proposition 7.4.16
.

In the setting of Proposition
7.4.13
, assume that
G
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
f\in{\mathcal{H}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}(R(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))
. If
H
is ramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
then we can take
f^{H_{1}}=0
(i.e., the right hand side of (
7.4.13.1
) always vanishes).

Proof.

The untwisted analogue of this proposition is proven in
[
Kot86
, Prop. 7.5]
. We adapt this proof, referred to as “
loc. cit.
” below, to our twisted setting. (A proof in the twisted case is alluded to on p. 189 of
[
Kot90
]
. We are elaborating on the details.) We write
G^{\prime}
for the group
G_{1}
in
loc. cit.
, as we reserve the symbol
G_{1}
to stand for a
z
-extension.
Via
z
-extensions we reduce to the case where
G_{\mathrm{der}}=G_{\mathrm{sc}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}={}^{L}H
. Recall we are also assuming
s\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{p}}
.

Fix
[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and a representative
b\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
of
[b]
. Fix
c\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
as in condition
KP1
of Definition
1.6.5
so that

c^{-1}\gamma_{0}c=\delta\sigma(\delta)\cdots{\sigma^{n-1}}(\delta)

with
\delta=c^{-1}b\sigma(c)\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
(which is also denoted
\delta_{[b]}
). As in the proof of Lemma
7.3.6
, the following sets are in natural bijections with each other:

(i)

the set of
\sigma
-conjugacy classes in the stable
\sigma
-conjugacy class of
\delta
,

(ii)

\ker(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\delta})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},R))
, and

(iii)

{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

To go from (i) to (ii), let
\delta^{\prime}=x\delta\theta(x)^{-1}
with
x\in R(\overline{\mathbb Q}_{p})
, where
\theta
denotes the automorphism of
R
induced by
\sigma\in{\rm Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Sending
\delta^{\prime}
to the cocycle
z_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}:\tau\mapsto x^{-1}{\vphantom{x}}{}^{\tau}x
induces the bijection from (i) onto (ii). To go from (i) to (iii), take

c^{\prime}\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
such that

(c^{\prime})^{-1}\gamma_{0}c^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}\sigma(\delta^{\prime})\cdots{\sigma^{n-1}}(\delta^{\prime})
.
Then we send
\delta^{\prime}
to
[c^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}\sigma(c^{\prime})^{-1}]\in B(I_{0})
, which lies in the set (iii).
Moreover we have the following compatibility: it follows from the bottom commutative diagram in
[
Kot97
, p. 273]
(with
I_{\delta}
and
I_{0}
in place of
J^{h}
and
H
there, and the cocycle
h
determined by
\sigma\mapsto c\delta\sigma(c)^{-1}
) that the image of
z_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}
under the composite map

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\delta})\cong\pi_{1}(I_{\delta})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}\cong\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p},\tors}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}

coincides with the image of
[b^{\prime}]-[b]
under the composite map

{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})\hookrightarrow\B(I_{0})\xrightarrow{\kappa_{I_{0}}}\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}.

Write
\textup{inv}(\delta,\delta^{\prime})
for the image in
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}
.
With the preparation so far, we follow
loc. cit.
to construct an exact sequence of unramified reductive groups over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}

1\rightarrow G\rightarrow G^{\prime}\rightarrow C\rightarrow 1,

where
C
is a non-trivial unramified torus. Define
R^{\prime}:=\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G^{\prime}
. Via the dual map
\widehat{G}^{\prime}\rightarrow\widehat{G}
, we can pull-back
\eta:\widehat{H}\rightarrow\widehat{G}
to define
\widehat{H}^{\prime}
equipped with an embedding
\widehat{H}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}^{\prime}
. We equip
\widehat{H}^{\prime}
with a
\Gamma_{p}
-action as in
loc. cit.

Write
I^{\prime}_{\delta}
for the connected
\sigma
-centralizer of
\delta
in
R^{\prime}
. As in
loc. cit.
we have an exact sequence

1\rightarrow I_{\delta}\rightarrow I^{\prime}_{\delta}\rightarrow C\rightarrow 1
, whose dual exact sequence fits in the following commutative diagram, where rows are
\Gamma_{p}
-equivariant and exact:

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(\widehat{H}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(\widehat{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(\widehat{I}^{\prime}_{\delta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(\widehat{I}_{\delta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}

We have
s\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{p}}\subset Z(\widehat{I}_{\delta})^{\Gamma_{p}}
. Write
\chi_{s}
for the image of
s
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widehat{C})
under the connecting homomorphism arising from the first row. Then
\chi_{s}
determines a smooth character
C(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, still denoted by
\chi_{s}
. As in the proof of
[
Kot86
, Prop. 7.3.5]
, the character
\chi_{s}
is
ramified
, i.e., non-trivial on
C(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. (As
C
is unramified, it extends uniquely to a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-torus.) This is crucial for our proof.

Now consider
g_{1}\in G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and put
\delta^{\prime}:=g_{1}\delta\theta(g_{1})^{-1}=g_{1}\delta g_{1}^{-1}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p^{n}})
. We claim that
\delta^{\prime}
is stably
\sigma
-conjugate to
\delta
. Indeed, writing
c_{1}\in C(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
for the image of
g_{1}
, take a lift
g_{2}\in I^{\prime}_{\delta}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
of
c_{1}^{-1}
via the surjection
I^{\prime}_{\delta}\rightarrow C
. Set
g:=g_{1}g_{2}\in R(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Then we have
\delta^{\prime}=g\delta\theta(g)^{-1}
, which proves the claim.

Let
[b^{\prime}]
denote the image of
\delta^{\prime}
under the bijection from (i) to (iii) above.
Then we assert that

\langle\beta_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b^{\prime}]),s\rangle\langle\beta_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s\rangle^{-1}=\langle\textup{inv}(\delta,\delta^{\prime}),s\rangle=\chi_{s}(c_{1}^{-1}).

The first equality follows from the aforementioned compatibility. The second equality
comes from
[
Kot86
, Lem. 1.6]
(applied to
I=C
and
G=I_{\delta}
),
where the image of
c_{1}^{-1}
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\delta})
is represented by the cocycle
z_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}
. Indeed, after applying the injection from
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},I_{\delta})
into
B(I_{\delta})=H^{1}(W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},I_{\delta}(\overline{\breve{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}))
, they are represented by the same cocycle since
g_{2}^{-1}{}^{\tau}g_{2}=g^{-1}{}^{\tau}g
for
\tau\in W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

For
f\in{\mathcal{H}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))
, define
f_{0}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))
by
f(g_{1}xg_{1}^{-1})
with
g_{1}
as above. (The analogue of
f_{0}
is denoted by
f_{1}
in
loc. cit.
)
Write
f^{H}
and
f_{0}^{H}
for their twisted transfers to
H
. On the one hand, we have
f=f_{0}
by the argument of
loc. cit.
, so we can take
f^{H}=f_{0}^{H}
. On the other hand, comparing the right hand sides of (
7.4.13.1
), we have

SO_{\gamma_{H}}(f_{0}^{H})=\langle\beta_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b^{\prime}]),s\rangle\langle\beta_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s\rangle^{-1}SO_{\gamma_{H}}(f^{H})=\chi_{s}(c_{1}^{-1})SO_{\gamma_{H}}(f^{H})

if
\gamma_{H}
is a norm of some
\delta
as above, and
SO_{\gamma_{H}}(f^{H})=0
if
\gamma_{H}
is not a norm of any such
\delta
. In order to verify that the stable orbital integral of
f^{H}
is identically zero, it is thus enough to exhibit a suitable
c_{1}
such that
\chi_{s}(c_{1}^{-1})\neq 1
.

To this end, let
T
be a maximally split maximal
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-torus in
G
, and take
T^{\prime}
to be the centralizer of
T
in
G^{\prime}
. The resulting exact sequence of unramified tori

1\rightarrow T\rightarrow T^{\prime}\rightarrow C\rightarrow 1

extends uniquely to an exact sequence of tori over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, with
1\rightarrow T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow T^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow 1
exact.
Fix any
c_{1}\in C(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
such that
\chi_{s}(c_{1})\neq 1
and choose
g_{1}\in T^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
to be a lift of
c_{1}
.
Running through the above argument, we conclude that the stable orbital integral of
f^{H}
vanishes everywhere.
∎

7.4.17
.

Finally we drop the assumption that
s\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{p}}
and consider the general case where
s\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{p}}Z(\widehat{G})
.
Write
s=s^{\prime}s^{\prime\prime}
with
s^{\prime}\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{p}}
and
s^{\prime\prime}\in Z(\widehat{G})
. As in §
7.2.14
,
(H,{\mathcal{H}},s^{\prime},\eta)\in E(G)
yields a twisted endoscopic datum
(H_{1},{}^{L}H_{1},\widetilde{s}^{\prime}_{1},\widetilde{\eta}^{\prime}_{1})
for
(R,\theta)
, with
s^{\prime}
playing the role of
s
.

Consider the setting of §
1.8.2
, where
\phi_{n}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{{\mathrm{ur}}}(R(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))
was introduced. If
{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}
is unramified, then take
f_{n}^{H_{1}}:=\mu^{-1}(s^{\prime\prime})\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\prime,*}(\phi_{n})
. If
H
is unramified but
{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}
is ramified, then we take
f_{n}^{H_{1}}
as in §
7.4.15
. If
H
(thus also
H_{1}
) is ramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
then take
f_{n}^{H_{1}}:=0
. We check that Proposition
7.4.13
extends to this case.

Corollary 7.4.18
.

Let
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{(G_{1},H_{1})\textup{-reg}}
. We have
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f_{n}^{H_{1}})=0
if
\gamma_{H_{1}}
is not a norm from
R_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. If
\gamma_{H_{1}}
is a norm of
\delta_{1}\in R_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
then denoting by
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
an image of
\gamma_{H_{1}}
, we have

SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f_{n}^{H_{1}})=\sum_{[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})}e(I_{\delta_{[b]}})\Delta_{0}(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0})\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s\rangle TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(\phi_{n}),

where the summand is understood to be zero if
TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(\phi_{n})=0
.
If
TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(\phi_{n})\neq~0
then
[b]
satisfies
KP0
and
KP1
in Definition
1.6.5
so
\widetilde{\beta}_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b])
is well defined as explained in §
1.7.5
.

Proof.

By definition
[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
always satisfies
KP1
. Let
\mu\in\bbmu_{X}^{{\mathcal{G}}}
as in §
2.4.1
.
If
TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(\phi_{n})\neq 0
for some
[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
then
X_{-\mu}(\delta_{[b]})
is non-empty by definition (
2.2.7.1
), so
[\delta_{[b]}]\in\B(G,-\mu)
by
[
RR96
]
. Since
[b]=[\delta_{[b]}]
in
\B(G)
, we see that
KP0
holds true.
Thus

\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s\rangle=\mu^{-1}(s^{\prime\prime})\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s^{\prime}\rangle=\mu^{-1}(s^{\prime\prime})\langle\beta_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s^{\prime}\rangle.

(The second equality holds because
s^{\prime}\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{p}}\subset Z(\widehat{I}_{0})^{\Gamma_{p}}
and
\beta_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b])
is an element of
\pi_{1}(I_{0})_{\Gamma_{p}}
.)
Now the proof follows from Proposition
7.4.13
with
\phi_{n}
and
s^{\prime}
in place of
f
and
s
, respectively, as
\mu^{-1}(s^{\prime\prime})\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
cancels out.
∎

8.
Stabilization

We return to the point counting formula for Shimura varieties. Taking Conjecture
1.8.8
for granted, we carry out the stabilization of the formula (
1.8.8.1
), with a view towards a representation-theoretic description of the cohomology.

8.1.
Initial steps

8.1.1
.

We start by fixing a central character datum, rewrite the coefficients in (
1.8.8.1
), and apply a Fourier transform on the finite abelian group
{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})
.

We freely use the setting of §
1.8
. Throughout stabilization, we fix an unramified Shimura datum
(G,X,p,{\mathcal{G}})
, which determines
K_{p}={\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and an open compact subgroup
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
K=K_{p}K^{p}
is a neat subgroup.

8.1.2
.

Recall that
Z
is the center of
G
. We endow
A_{Z,\infty}=A_{G,\infty}
, which is isomorphic to a finite product of copies of
\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}
, with the standard multiplicative Haar measure.
Fix Haar measures on
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
Z(\mathbb{R})
, thereby also on
{\mathfrak{X}}:=(Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K)\cdot Z(\mathbb{R})
and
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathfrak{X}}/A_{Z,\infty}
, relative to the counting measure on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
(which is discrete and compact in
{\mathfrak{X}}/A_{Z,\infty}
). With respect to the set of places of
\mathbb{Q}
, we have the decomposition
{\mathfrak{X}}={\mathfrak{X}}^{p,\infty}{\mathfrak{X}}_{p}{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}
.
We put a Haar measure on
Z(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z(\mathbb{A})/{\mathfrak{X}}
via the following exact sequence of topological groups

1\rightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathfrak{X}}/A_{Z,\infty}\rightarrow Z(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z(\mathbb{A})/A_{Z,\infty}\rightarrow Z(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z(\mathbb{A})/{\mathfrak{X}}\rightarrow 1,

where the space in the middle is given the Tamagawa measure.

We promote
{\mathfrak{X}}
to a central character datum
({\mathfrak{X}},\chi)
by defining the finite part
\chi^{\infty}
to be trivial on
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K
and the infinite part
\chi_{\infty}:=\omega_{\xi}^{-1}
, where
\omega_{\xi}
is the central character of
\xi
on
Z(\mathbb{R})
.

For a connected reductive subgroup
G_{0}
of
G
over
\mathbb{Q}
containing
Z
such that
A_{G_{0}}=A_{Z}
, we use the quotient measure to define

\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G_{0}):=\mathrm{vol}(G_{0}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G_{0}(\mathbb{A})/{\mathfrak{X}})

by viewing the double coset space as the quotient of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G_{0}(\mathbb{A})/A_{G_{0},\infty}
by
{\mathfrak{X}}{Q}\backslash{\mathfrak{X}}/A_{Z,\infty}
, where the former is equipped with the Tamagawa measure and the latter with the measure explained above. For any inner form
G_{0}^{\prime}
of
G_{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}
we make sense of
\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G_{0}^{\prime})
by transporting the measure. Since the Tamagawa volumes are equal for
G_{0}
and
G_{0}^{\prime}
by
[
Kot88
]
, we have
\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G_{0})=\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G_{0}^{\prime})
.

Once and for all, fix a
z
-extension
G_{1}
of
G
over
\mathbb{Q}
which is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. This is possible by Lemma
7.2.5
(recalling that
G
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
). Write
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}\subset Z_{G_{1}}(\mathbb{A})
for the preimage of
{\mathfrak{X}}
under
G_{1}\rightarrow G
.
Write
\chi_{1}
for the pull-back of
\chi
to
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}
(thus the finite part of
\chi_{1}
is trivial). Then
({\mathfrak{X}}_{1},\chi_{1})
is a central character datum for
G_{1}
, and
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}={\mathfrak{X}}_{1}^{p,\infty}{\mathfrak{X}}_{1,p}{\mathfrak{X}}_{1,\infty}
analogously as the decomposition of
{\mathfrak{X}}
.
We put the unique Haar measure on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}
, and thus also on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1,\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}/A_{G_{1},\infty}
similarly as above, such that

(8.1.2.1)

\mathrm{vol}({\mathfrak{X}}_{1,\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}/A_{G_{1},\infty})/\mathrm{vol}({\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathfrak{X}}/A_{G,\infty})=\tau(G_{1})/\tau(G),

so that

(8.1.2.2)

\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}}(G_{1})=\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(G).

Let
{\mathfrak{c}}=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Recall from §
1.7.11
that
{\mathfrak{c}}
determines an inner form
I_{v}
of
I_{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
. Note that
I_{p}
depends only on
\gamma_{0}
and
[b]
(not on
a
). Since
I_{\infty}
is always a compact-mod-center inner form of
I_{0,\mathbb{R}}
we often write
I_{0}^{{\rm cpt}}
for
I_{\infty}
. If the Kottwitz invariant
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
(defined in §
1.7.5
) vanishes, then we have an inner form
I
of
I_{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}
which localizes to
I_{v}
at each
v
(Proposition
1.7.12
). We defined the constants
c_{1}({\mathfrak{c}},K^{p},di_{p}di^{p})
and
c_{2}(\gamma_{0})
in §
1.8.6
.

Lemma 8.1.3
.

If
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
vanishes, then

c_{1}({\mathfrak{c}},K^{p},di_{p}di^{p})c_{2}(\gamma_{0})=\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)\cdot|{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})|\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z(\mathbb{R})\backslash I_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))^{-1}.

Proof.

With the choice of measures as above,

\displaystyle c_{1}({\mathfrak{c}},K^{p},di_{p}di^{p})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}(I(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I(\mathbb{A})/Z_{K}I_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{vol}(I(\mathbb{Q})\backslash I(\mathbb{A})/Z_{K}Z(\mathbb{R}))}{\mathrm{vol}(Z(\mathbb{R})\backslash I_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))}=\frac{\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(I_{0})}{\mathrm{vol}(Z(\mathbb{R})\backslash I_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))}.

On the other hand one deduces as in
[
Kot86
, p. 395]
that

c_{2}(\gamma_{0})=\tau(I_{0})^{-1}\tau(G)|{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})|=\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(I_{0})^{-1}\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)|{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})|.

We conclude by taking product of the two equations.
∎

8.1.4
.

Let
{\mathfrak{c}}=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
. Define
e({\mathfrak{c}}):=\prod_{v}e(I_{v})\in\{\pm 1\}
, the product of Kottwitz signs over all places. If
\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})
vanishes, then
e({\mathfrak{c}})=1
since
I_{v}
’s come from a
\mathbb{Q}
-group
I
. Hence

(8.1.4.1)

\sum_{\kappa\in{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})}e({\mathfrak{c}})\langle\alpha({\mathfrak{c}}),\kappa\rangle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}|{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})|,&\mbox{if}~\alpha({\mathfrak{c}})=0,\\
0,&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Applying Lemma
8.1.3
and (
8.1.4.1
) to (
1.8.8.1
), we have

(8.1.4.2)

T(\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m},f^{p}dg^{p})=\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G)\sum_{\gamma_{0}\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{X}},\mathbb{R}\text{-}\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\overline{\iota}_{G}(\gamma_{0})^{-1}\sum_{\kappa\in{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})}\sum_{(a,[b])}N(\gamma_{0},\kappa,a,[b]),

where the third sum runs over
{\mathfrak{D}}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and

N(\gamma_{0},\kappa,a,[b])=\langle\alpha(\gamma_{0},a,[b]),\kappa\rangle e(\gamma_{0},a,[b])\frac{O_{\gamma_{a}}(f^{p})TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(\phi_{n}){\rm tr}\,\xi(\gamma_{0})}{\mathrm{vol}(Z(\mathbb{R})\backslash I_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))}.

This is straightforward possibly except for the following point. In (
1.8.8.1
), every
{\mathfrak{c}}=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])
is a
p^{n}
-admissible Kottwitz parameter. In the formula above, since condition
KP0
is not imposed on
[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, a priori
(\gamma_{0},a,[b])
may not be a Kottwitz parameter. The necessary observation is that
[b]
satisfies
KP0
as soon as
\delta_{[b]}
lies in the support of
\phi_{n}
.

8.2.
Local transfer of orbital integrals

8.2.1
.

Here we prove adelic orbital integral identities for each
{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in E_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
. The fixed
z
-extension
G_{1}
(which is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
) gives rise to a central extension
H_{1}
of
H
via Lemma
7.2.6
. In particular, if
H
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
then so is
H_{1}
.
As before,
{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}
is the preimage of
{\mathfrak{X}}\subset Z_{G}(\mathbb{A})\subset Z_{H}(\mathbb{A})
under
Z_{H_{1}}\rightarrow Z_{H}
.
We have a continuous character
Z^{0}_{G_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{0}_{G_{1}}(\mathbb{A})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
corresponding to the global parameter

W_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow{}^{L}H_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta_{1}}}{{\rightarrow}}{}^{L}G_{1}\rightarrow{}^{L}Z_{G_{1}}^{0}
as in Lemma
7.4.6
. Restricting to
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}
, we obtain a character

(8.2.1.1)

\lambda_{H_{1}}:{\mathfrak{X}}_{1,\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times},

which can be viewed as a character of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}
via
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}\cong{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}
. According as
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}={\mathfrak{X}}_{1}^{p,\infty}{\mathfrak{X}}_{1,p}{\mathfrak{X}}_{1,\infty}
we decompose
\lambda_{H_{1}}=\lambda_{H_{1}}^{p,\infty}\lambda_{H_{1},p}\lambda_{H_{1},\infty}
. With the Haar measure on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{1}
transferred to
{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}
via the isomorphism, the analogue of (
8.1.2.2
) holds.

Lemma 8.2.2
.

We have
\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(H_{1})=\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(H)
.

Proof.

Once we prove that
\tau(G_{1})/\tau(G)=\tau(H_{1})/\tau(H)
, the lemma follows in the same way as (
8.1.2.2
) is implied by (
8.1.2.1
).
By
[
Kot84b
, (5.2.3), §5.3]
(
\tau_{1}(\cdot)
therein is
\tau(\cdot)
by
[
Kot88
]
), we have

\displaystyle\tau(G_{1})/\tau(G)

\displaystyle=\left|\cok(X_{*}(Z(\widehat{G}_{1}))^{\Gamma}\rightarrow X_{*}(\widehat{Z}_{1})^{\Gamma})\right|,

\displaystyle\tau(H_{1})/\tau(H)

\displaystyle=\left|\cok(X_{*}(Z(\widehat{H}_{1}))^{\Gamma}\rightarrow X_{*}(\widehat{Z}_{1})^{\Gamma})\right|.

So it is enough to show that the two cokernels are isomorphic.
Consider the commutative diagram below, where the rows are coming from the exact sequence of
[
Kot84b
, Cor. 2.3]
.

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{*}(Z(\widehat{G}))^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{G}}
\textstyle{X_{*}(Z(\widehat{G}_{1}))^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{*}(\widehat{Z}_{1})^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{*}(Z(\widehat{H}))^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{H}}
\textstyle{X_{*}(Z(\widehat{H}_{1}))^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{*}(\widehat{Z}_{1})^{\Gamma}}
\textstyle{X_{*}({Z(\widehat{H})}/{Z(\widehat{G})})^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{*}({Z(\widehat{H}_{1})}/{Z(\widehat{G}_{1})})^{\Gamma}}

We obtain
\cok i_{G}\cong\cok i_{H}
by diagram chase, and thereby the two cokernels above are isomorphic.
∎

8.2.3
.

For the moment we assume that
s\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{p}}
, and we are going to drop this assumption in §
8.2.6
below.
By Proposition
7.4.11
there exists a transfer

f^{H_{1},p,\infty}\in{\mathcal{H}}(H_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p,\infty}),\chi_{1}^{-1}\lambda_{H_{1}}^{p,\infty})

of
f^{p,\infty}\in{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}){\sslash}K^{p})
with the following property. For each

\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})_{(G_{1},H_{1})\textup{-reg}},

if it has no image in
G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})_{\mathrm{ss}}
then
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1},p,\infty})=0
.
If there exists an image
\gamma_{0,1}\in G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})_{\mathrm{ss}}
of
\gamma_{H_{1}}
, then writing
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
for the projection of
\gamma_{0,1}
, we have

(8.2.3.1)

\displaystyle SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1},p,\infty})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a_{1}}e(a_{1})\Delta(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0,1,a_{1}})O_{\gamma_{0,1,a_{1}}}(f^{p,\infty})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a_{1}}e(a_{1})\Delta(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0,1})\langle\beta^{p,\infty}(\gamma_{0,1},a_{1}),s_{1}\rangle O_{\gamma_{0,1,a_{1}}}(f^{p,\infty})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a}\left(\prod_{v\neq p,\infty}e(I_{v})\right)\Delta(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0,1})\langle\beta^{p,\infty}(\gamma_{0},a),s\rangle O_{\gamma_{0,a}}(f^{p,\infty}),

where the sums for
a_{1}
and
a
run over
{\mathfrak{D}}(I_{0,1},G_{1};\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
{\mathfrak{D}}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
respectively. Recall that
\beta^{p,\infty}(\cdot,\cdot)
was introduced in §
1.7.6
.
The second equality above follows from a basic property of transfer factors regarding the change of
\gamma_{0,1}
within its stable conjugacy class as stated in
[
Kot86
, Conj. 5.5]
, which can be proved by arguing as in the proof of
[
LS87
, Lem. 4.1.C ]
in the
G_{1}
-regular case and extended to the
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular case by
[
LS90
]
.

8.2.4
.

From §
7.4.17
and Corollary
7.4.18
, we obtain
f^{H_{1}}_{p}\in{\mathcal{H}}(H_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\lambda_{H_{1},p})
(renaming
f^{H_{1}}_{n}
) with the following property. Let
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{(G_{1},H_{1})\textup{-reg}}
. If
\gamma_{H_{1}}
is not a norm from an element of
R_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
then
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}_{p})=0
. If it is a norm, there exists
\gamma_{0,1}\in G_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
whose conjugacy class matches
\gamma_{H_{1}}
. Writing
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
for the projection of
\gamma_{0,1}
, we have

(8.2.4.1)

SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}_{p})=\sum_{[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})}e(I_{\delta_{[b]}})\Delta_{0}(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0,1})\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s\rangle TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(\phi_{n}).

By definition
I_{\delta_{[b]}}=I_{p}
if
[b]
comes from
{\mathfrak{c}}=(\gamma_{0},a,[b])\in{\mathfrak{KP}}_{\adm}(p^{n})
.
Thanks to Proposition
7.4.13
we may and will take
f^{H_{1}}_{p}=0
if
H
is ramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

8.2.5
.

Our starting point for real orbital integrals is the argument of
[
Kot90
, §7]
based on Shelstad’s real endoscopy
[
She82
]
and the pseudo-coefficients of Clozel–Delorme
[
CD90
]
. We incorporate central characters and
z
-extensions.

We have the characters
\chi_{1,\infty}
and
\lambda_{H_{1},\infty}
on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1},\infty}
. We will drop
\infty
from the subscript and write
\lambda_{H_{1}}
and
\chi_{1}
when the context is clearly local archimedean. Let
\xi_{1}
be the irreducible representation of
G_{1}
obtained from
\xi
via the surjection
G_{1}\rightarrow G
.
As in §
7.3.8
, fix a Shimura datum
(G_{1},X_{1})
and
\mu_{1}\in\bbmu_{X_{1}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. For each elliptic
\gamma_{0,1}\in G_{1}(\mathbb{R})
we have an element
\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0,1})\in\pi_{1}(I_{0,1})
which maps to
[\mu_{1}]\in\pi_{1}(G_{1})
, cf. §
1.7.6
.

We simply set
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}:=0
if elliptic maximal tori of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
do not come from those of
H_{\mathbb{R}}
via transfer (equivalently, if elliptic maximal tori of
G_{1,\mathbb{R}}
do not come from those of
H_{1,\mathbb{R}}
). In particular
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}=0
if
H_{\mathbb{R}}
contains no elliptic maximal tori. From now on, we assume that
H_{\mathbb{R}}
contains elliptic maximal tori and they transfer to elliptic maximal tori of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
. In particular
Z_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}^{0}
and
Z_{H_{\mathbb{R}}}^{0}
have the same
\mathbb{R}
-rank.

Following Kottwitz, we construct a smooth function
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}
on
H_{1}(\mathbb{R})
that is compactly supported modulo center, by taking a suitable finite linear combination of pseudo-coefficients of discrete series representations (explicitly as on page 186 of
[
Kot90
]
with our
H_{1}
and
G_{1}
playing the roles of his
H
and
G
) such that the following hold:
if
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathrm{ss}}
is elliptic and
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular, which by the above assumption has an elliptic element
\gamma_{0,1}\in G_{1}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathrm{ss}}
as image, then

(8.2.5.1)

SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}_{\infty})=\frac{e(I_{0,1}^{{\rm cpt}})\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0,1}),s_{1}\rangle\Delta_{\infty}(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0,1}){\rm tr}\,\xi_{1}(\gamma_{0,1})}{\mathrm{vol}(Z_{G_{1}}(\mathbb{R})\backslash I_{0,1}^{{\rm cpt}}(\mathbb{R}))},

whereas if
\gamma_{H_{1}}
is non-elliptic and
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular then
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}_{\infty})=0
. If
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{R})_{\mathrm{ss}}
is not
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular, then by
[
Mor10
, Prop. 3.3.4, Rem 3.3.5]
adapted to our setting we have

SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}_{\infty})=0
.
The
\chi_{1}^{-1}\lambda_{H_{1}}
-equivariance of
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}
follows from (
8.2.5.1
) and the equivariance of transfer factors (§
7.4.7
), recalling that the central character of
\xi_{1}
is
\chi_{1}^{-1}
.

We claim that if
\gamma_{H_{1}}
is elliptic and
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular then (
8.2.5.1
) implies that

(8.2.5.2)

SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}_{\infty})=\frac{e(I_{0}^{{\rm cpt}})\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0}),s\rangle\Delta_{\infty}(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0}){\rm tr}\,\xi(\gamma_{0})}{\mathrm{vol}(Z_{G}(\mathbb{R})\backslash I_{0}^{{\rm cpt}}(\mathbb{R}))},

where
\gamma_{0}
is the image of
\gamma_{0,1}
in
G(\mathbb{R})
. It is routine to check term-by-term equalities between the right hand sides of (
8.2.5.1
) and (
8.2.5.2
). We illustrate the idea by showing that
\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0,1}),s_{1}\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0}),s\rangle
, leaving the rest to the reader. Let us write
s
for
s_{1}
since the latter is the image of
s
under the inclusion
\widehat{G}\rightarrow\widehat{G}_{1}
. We have a decomposition
s=s^{\prime}s^{\prime\prime}
with
s^{\prime}\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{\infty}}
and
s^{\prime\prime}\in Z(\widehat{G})
. By definition (
[
Kot90
, §2]
) the character
\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0})
(resp.
\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0,1})
) restricts to a character on
Z(\widehat{I}_{0})^{\Gamma_{\infty}}
(resp.
Z(\widehat{I}_{0,1})^{\Gamma_{\infty}}
) and a character on
Z(\widehat{G})
(resp.
Z(\widehat{G}_{1})
), each of which is determined by
\mu
(resp.
\mu_{1}
). They are related via the following commutative diagrams, from which it is obvious that
\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0,1}),s^{\prime}s^{\prime\prime}\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0}),s^{\prime}s^{\prime\prime}\rangle
.

\textstyle{s^{\prime}\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{\infty}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(\widehat{I}_{0})^{\Gamma_{\infty}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0})}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime\prime}\in Z(\widehat{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0})}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
\textstyle{Z(\widehat{H}_{1})^{\Gamma_{\infty}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(\widehat{I}_{0,1})^{\Gamma_{\infty}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0,1})}
\textstyle{Z(\widehat{G}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0,1})}

8.2.6
.

So far we have constructed the function
f^{H_{1}}:=f^{H_{1},p,\infty}f^{H_{1}}_{p}f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}
on
H_{1}(\mathbb A)
.
If
H
is ramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or if elliptic tori of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
do not come from those of
H_{\mathbb{R}}
, then we have
f^{H_{1}}=0
since
f^{H_{1}}_{p}=0
or
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}=0
in each case. When neither is the case,

f^{H_{1}}
depends only on the image of
s
modulo
Z(\widehat{G})
. To see this, suppose that
s
is replaced with
sz
for
z\in Z(\widehat{G})
. Then
f^{H_{1},p,\infty}
as well as the identity (
8.2.3.1
) remains unchanged as
\prod_{v\neq p,\infty}\widetilde{\beta}_{v}(\gamma_{0},a)
is trivial on
Z(\widehat{G})
. The function
f^{H_{1}}_{p}
is multiplied by
\mu(z)^{-1}
according to §
7.4.17
. Since
\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0}),s\rangle
is the only term in (
8.2.5.2
)
to change and it is multiplied by
\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0}),z\rangle=\mu(z)
, the function
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}
is multiplied by
\mu(z)
to keep (
8.2.5.2
) valid. All in all,
f^{H_{1}}
indeed remains invariant.

8.2.7
.

Let us summarize the above results in terms of adelic orbital integral identities. To this end, we slightly extend the definition of
N(\gamma_{0},\kappa,a,[b])
in §
8.1.4
from rational to adelic elements. Let
{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
.
Let
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{A})
and suppose that
\gamma_{0}
is an image of
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{A})_{(G_{1},H_{1})\textup{-reg}}
at every place
v
. Define a similar quantity
N^{\prime}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{H_{1}},s,a,[b])
for
s\in Z(\widehat{H})
,
a\in{\mathfrak{D}}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and
[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by

N^{\prime}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{H_{1}},s,a,[b])=\langle\widetilde{\beta}^{p,\infty}(\gamma_{0},a),s\rangle\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{p}(\gamma_{0},[b]),s\rangle\langle\widetilde{\beta}_{\infty}(\gamma_{0}),s\rangle\Delta{A}(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0})\\
\times O_{\gamma_{a}}(f^{p})TO_{\delta_{[b]}}(\phi_{n}){\rm tr}\,\xi(\gamma_{0})\mathrm{vol}(Z(\mathbb{R})\backslash I_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))^{-1}.

To compare with (
8.1.4
), if
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathbb{R}\textup{-ell}}
then

(8.2.7.1)

N^{\prime}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{H_{1}},s,a,[b])=N(\gamma_{0},s,a,[b])

by definition of

\alpha(\gamma_{0},a,[b])
and the product formula that
\Delta{A}(\gamma_{H_{1}},\gamma_{0})=1
.

Lemma 8.2.8
.

Let
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{A})_{(G_{1},H_{1})\textup{-reg}}
. If
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{A})_{\mathrm{ss}}
is
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic and an image of
\gamma_{H_{1}}
at every place
v
then

SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})=\sum_{a\in{\mathfrak{D}}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\atop[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})}N^{\prime}(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{H_{1}},s,a,[b]).

If no
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{A})_{\mathrm{ss}}
is an image of
\gamma_{H_{1}}
then

SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})=0
.
If
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ss}}
is not
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular then again
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})=0
.

Proof.

If elliptic maximal tori of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
do not come from those of
H_{\mathbb{R}}
then no
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic element of
G(\mathbb{R})
is an image of an element of
H(\mathbb{R})
. Then
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}=0
by construction, so the lemma holds. If
\gamma_{H_{1},\infty}
is not
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular then we saw
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1},\infty}}(f^{H_{1}}_{\infty})=0
, so in particular
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})=0
for non-
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular elements
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ss}}
.

From now on, let
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{A})_{(G_{1},H_{1})\textup{-reg}}
, and assume that elliptic maximal tori of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
come from those of
H_{\mathbb{R}}
. If
\gamma_{H_{1}}
is not
\mathbb{R}
-elliptic (or equivalently if
\gamma_{H_{1}}
has no image in
G(\mathbb{R})_{\mathrm{ell}}
) then

SO_{\gamma_{H_{1},\infty}}(f^{H_{1}}_{\infty})=0
by
[
Kot92a
, Lem. 3.1]
(asserting that the orbital integrals of pseudocoefficients of discrete series representations are supported on elliptic elements). Thus the lemma is verified is this case.
If
\gamma_{H_{1},v}
does not have an image in
G_{1}(F_{v})_{\mathrm{ss}}
at some finite place
v
then
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}_{v})=0
by §
8.2.3
and §
8.2.4
so the lemma is again true.
In the remaining case, there exists
\gamma_{0}
as in the lemma.
Then the desired equality follows from (
8.2.3.1
), (
8.2.4.1
), and (
8.2.5.2
).
∎

8.3.
Final steps

8.3.1
.

Resuming from the formula (
8.1.4.2
),
we apply the adelic transfer of orbital integrals to finish stabilization.
To re-parametrize the sum over
(\gamma_{0},\kappa)
in (
8.1.4.2
),
consider the set of equivalence classes

\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G):=\{(\gamma_{0},\kappa)\mid\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ell}},~\kappa\in{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})\}/{\sim},

where
(\gamma_{0},\kappa)\sim(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\kappa^{\prime})
if there exists
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that (i)
g\gamma_{0}g^{-1}=\gamma^{\prime}_{0}
, (ii)
g^{-1}{}^{\tau}g\in I_{0}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
for every
\tau\in\Gamma
, and (iii) the inner twisting
I_{0,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong I_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
induced by
\mathrm{Int}(g)
carries
\kappa
to
\kappa^{\prime}
. Define another set of equivalence classes

{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(G):=\{({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H}):~{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in E_{\mathrm{ell}}(G),~\gamma_{H}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)_{(G,H)\text{-}{\rm reg}}\}/{\sim},

where
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)_{(G,H)\text{-}{\rm reg}}
is the set of stable conjugacy classes of
(G,H)
-regular semi-simple elements of
H(\mathbb{Q})
, and
({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\sim({\mathfrak{e}}^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}_{H})
if there is an isomorphism between endoscopic data
{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)
and
{\mathfrak{e}}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime},s^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})
such that
\gamma_{H}
is carried to
\gamma^{\prime}_{H}
. (In particular
({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\sim({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma^{\prime}_{H})
if there is an outer automorphism of
{\mathfrak{e}}
mapping
\gamma_{H}
to
\gamma^{\prime}_{H}
.) Define an analogous set with the obvious surjection

(8.3.1.1)

{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G):=\left\{({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\mid\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G),\\
\gamma_{H}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)_{(G,H)\text{-}{\rm reg}}\end{array}\right\}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(G).

The outer automorphism group
{\rm Out}_{F}({\mathfrak{e}})
acts transitively on each fiber of the map.
Let us define a map

\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}:{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)\longrightarrow\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)\cup\{\emptyset\}.

We explained in §
7.4.2
how

({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})
determines either a stable conjugacy class of
\gamma_{0}\in G(\mathbb{Q})
or
\emptyset
(when there is no matching conjugacy class in
G(\mathbb{Q})
).
In the latter case, set
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}:({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\mapsto\emptyset
. In the former, we map
({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})
to
(\gamma_{0},\kappa)
, where
\kappa\in{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{0}/\mathbb{Q})
is determined by the image of
s
under the composition
Z(\widehat{H})\hookrightarrow Z(\widehat{I}_{\gamma_{H}})\cong Z(\widehat{I}_{0})
. Here the canonical isomorphism comes from the fact that
I_{0}
is an inner form of
I_{\gamma_{H}}
. The ellipticity of
\gamma_{0}
follows from that of
\gamma_{H}
. Letting
Z(\mathbb{Q})
act on each of
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
and
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)
by multiplication, we see that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}
is
Z(\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant.

By
[
Kot86
, Lem. 9.7]
, when
G_{\mathrm{der}}=G_{\mathrm{sc}}
, the map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}
factors through a unique map
{\mathfrak{E}}:{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
, and the image of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}
contains
\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
. When
G_{\mathrm{der}}
is not simply connected, write
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}
for the analogous maps for the
z
-extension
G_{1}
. We have a commutative diagram (
a priori
without
{\mathfrak{E}}
)

(8.3.1.2)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(G_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G_{1})\cup\{\emptyset\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\,{\mathfrak{E}}}
\textstyle{\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)\cup\{\emptyset\},}

where the vertical maps are induced by
G_{1}\rightarrow G
, using
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G_{1})\cong{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
(Lemma
7.2.11
) and part (i) of Lemma
7.3.2
. We add that the right vertical is required to send
\emptyset
to itself. By the
Z_{G_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})
-equivariance of
{\mathfrak{E}}_{1}
, there exists a unique
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant map
{\mathfrak{E}}
making the entire diagram commute. (The image of
{\mathfrak{E}}
does not contain
\emptyset
if
G
is quasi-split over
\mathbb{Q}
, since every
\gamma_{H}
then has an image in
G(\mathbb{Q})
.)

Lemma 8.3.2
.

The map
{\mathfrak{E}}
is
Z(\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant and contains
\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
in its image. Moreover for
(\gamma_{0},\kappa)\in\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
and
{\mathfrak{e}}\in E_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
,

\overline{\iota}_{G}(\gamma_{0})^{-1}=\lambda({\mathfrak{e}})^{-1}\sum_{\gamma_{H}\in\Sigma(H)~\textup{s.t.}\atop{\mathfrak{E}}:({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\mapsto(\gamma_{0},\kappa)}\overline{\iota}_{H}(\gamma_{H})^{-1}.

Remark 8.3.3
.

We remark that, in the strongly regular case the lemma follows from
[
KS99
, Lem. 7.2.A]
, which covers twisted endoscopy.

Proof.

The
Z(\mathbb{Q})
-equivariance was observed above.
Since the right vertical map is surjective in (
8.3.1.2
) by Lemma
7.3.2
,
the containment of
\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
in the image of
{\mathfrak{E}}
reduces to the case for
G_{1}
, which is proved in
[
Kot86
, Lem. 9.7]
(since
G_{1,\mathrm{der}}=G_{1,\mathrm{sc}}
).
Finally the equality asserted in the lemma follows from
[
Lab04
, Cor. IV.3.6]
.
∎

8.3.4
.

Recall that
K^{p}
is a neat subgroup and that
{\mathfrak{X}}=(Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K)\cdot Z(\mathbb{R})
. In particular

(8.3.4.1)

{\mathfrak{X}}\cap G_{\mathrm{der}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})=\{1\}.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)
denote the quotient set of
{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
by the obvious multiplication of
{\mathfrak{X}}{Q}={\mathfrak{X}}\cap Z(\mathbb{Q})
. Likewise, define
\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(H)
and
\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)
from
\Sigma(H)
and
\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
.
Let

\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{X}}}:{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)\rightarrow\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)\cup\{\emptyset\}

denote the map
{\mathfrak{E}}
pre-composed with (
8.3.1.1
) modulo the
{\mathfrak{X}}{Q}
-action.

Corollary 8.3.5
.

The image of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is equal to
\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)
if
G
is quasi-split and contains
\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)
in general. Moreover for fixed
(\gamma_{0},\kappa)
and
{\mathfrak{e}}
,

\overline{\iota}_{G}(\gamma_{0})^{-1}=\lambda({\mathfrak{e}})^{-1}\sum_{\gamma_{H}\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{X}}(H)~\textup{s.t.}\atop{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{X}}:({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\mapsto(\gamma_{0},\kappa)}\overline{\iota}_{H}(\gamma_{H})^{-1}.

Proof.

The only non-trivial point is to deduce the equality from Lemma
8.3.2
. Let
{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in E_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
,
\gamma_{H},\gamma^{\prime}_{H}\in H(\mathbb{Q})_{(G,H)\text{-}{\rm reg}}
. Suppose that
{\mathfrak{E}}
maps both
({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})
and
({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma^{\prime}_{H})
to
(\gamma_{0},\kappa)
. What we need to show is that, if
\gamma^{\prime}_{H}
is stably conjugate to
z\gamma_{H}
for some
z\in{\mathfrak{X}}
, then
\gamma^{\prime}_{H}
is stably conjugate to
\gamma_{H}
.

By assumption,
{\mathfrak{E}}
maps
({\mathfrak{e}},z\gamma_{H})
to
(z\gamma_{0},\kappa)
. Hence
z\gamma_{0}
is stably conjugate to
\gamma_{0}
. In particular
gz\gamma_{0}g^{-1}=\gamma_{0}
for some
g\in G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Thanks to (
8.3.4.1
) the element
z
is trivial, so
\gamma^{\prime}_{H}
is stably conjugate to
\gamma_{H}
as desired.
∎

Lemma 8.3.6
.

Let
f^{H_{1}}
be as in §
8.3.4
.
Assume that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{X}}
maps
({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})
to
\emptyset
.
Then
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})=0
for every lift
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
of
\gamma_{H}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})\neq 0
. By Lemma
8.2.8
there exists
\gamma_{0,1}\in G_{1}(\mathbb{A})
such that
\gamma_{0,1,v}
is an image of
\gamma_{H_{1},v}
at every place
v
. To show that
({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})
is not mapped to
\emptyset
under
{\mathfrak{E}}
, it suffices to show the existence of
\gamma^{\prime}_{0,1}\in G_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
which is stably conjugate to
\gamma_{0,1}
in
G_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
for every place
v
.
Indeed,
({\mathfrak{e}}_{1},\gamma_{H_{1}})
then does not map to
\emptyset
, thus
({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})
does not either, cf. (
8.3.1.2
). Thus the proof boils down to the case where
G=G_{1}
and
H=H_{1}
(with
G_{\mathrm{der}}=G_{\mathrm{sc}}
). Henceforth we will write
\gamma_{0}
for
\gamma_{0,1}
,
\gamma_{H}
for
\gamma_{H_{1}}
, and so on.

Let
G^{*}
denote a quasi-split inner form of
G
. Write
\gamma^{*}_{0}\in G^{*}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ss}}
for the image of
\gamma_{H}
under (
7.4.2.1
).
Now recall that Labesse (see
[
Lab99
, §2.6]
, with
L=G,H=G^{*}
) constructs a non-empty subset

\mathrm{obs}_{\gamma_{0}^{*}}(\gamma_{0})\subset\mathfrak{E}(I_{\gamma_{0}^{*}},G^{*};\mathbb A/\mathbb{Q}){\mathrel{\to}^{\mathrm{Cor.~\ref {cor:compare with Kottwitz}}}}{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{\gamma^{*}_{0}}/\mathbb{Q})^{D},

generalizing the construction of Kottwitz in
[
Kot86
]
.

As in the second paragraph of
[
Kot90
, p. 188]
, the Chebotarev density theorem implies that the natural map
{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{\gamma^{*}_{0}}/\mathbb{Q}_{w})^{D}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{\gamma^{*}_{0}}/\mathbb{Q})^{D}
is surjective for some finite place
w
.
In Labesse’s construction, if we twist
\gamma_{0,w}
within its stable conjugacy class by a class
c\in{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{\gamma^{*}_{0}}/\mathbb{Q}_{w})^{D}
, then
\mathrm{obs}_{\gamma_{0}^{*}}(\gamma_{0})
gets shifted by the image of
c
in the abelian group
{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{\gamma^{*}_{0}}/\mathbb{Q})^{D}
. Hence, if we replace
\gamma_{0,w}
by a stably conjugate element and keep the components of
\gamma_{0}
outside
w
unchanged, then we may arrange that

0\in\mathrm{obs}_{\gamma_{0}^{*}}(\gamma_{0})\subset{\mathfrak{K}}(I_{\gamma^{*}_{0}}/\mathbb{Q})^{D}
. By
[
Lab99
, Thm. 2.6.3]
, the
G(\mathbb{A})
-conjugacy class of
\gamma_{0}
contains an element of
G(\mathbb{Q})
, which we can take to be
\gamma_{0}^{\prime}
.
∎

8.3.7
.

For each
{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
, set

(8.3.7.1)

\iota(G,H):=\tau(G)\tau(H)^{-1}\lambda({\mathfrak{e}})^{-1}=\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G)\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(H)^{-1}\lambda({\mathfrak{e}})^{-1}.

Given
\gamma_{H}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(H)
we define
\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(\gamma_{H})
to be the group of
z\in{\mathfrak{X}}{Q}
such that
z\gamma_{H}=\gamma_{H}
in
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(H)
. This group is finite by the same argument as in §
7.1.4
showing the finiteness of
\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(\gamma_{H})
.

Let us introduce the stable analogue of
T_{\mathrm{ell}}
(see §
7.1.6
) for
H_{1}
with respect to the central character datum
({\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}},\chi_{H_{1}})
, where
\chi_{H_{1}}:=\chi_{1}\lambda_{H_{1}}^{-1}
. To check that it is indeed a central character datum, note that both
\chi_{1}
and
\lambda_{H_{1}}
are trivial on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1},\mathbb{Q}}
by construction, cf. §
8.1.2
and (
8.2.1.1
).
For
h\in{\mathcal{H}}(H_{1}(\mathbb{A}),\chi_{H_{1}}^{-1})
, set

(8.3.7.2)

ST^{H_{1}}_{\mathrm{ell},\chi_{H_{1}}}(h):=\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(H_{1})\sum_{\gamma_{H_{1}}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(H_{1})}|\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(\gamma_{H_{1}})|^{-1}SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(h).

There is no need for the factor
\overline{\iota}(\gamma_{H_{1}})^{-1}
, which is equal to 1 since
H_{1}
has simply connected derived subgroup.

Lemma 8.3.8
.

For
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{Q})_{(G_{1},H_{1})\textup{-reg}}
,

|\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(\gamma_{H_{1}})|=|\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(\gamma_{H})|\overline{\iota}_{H}(\gamma_{H}).

Proof.

It suffices to construct a short exact sequence of groups

1\rightarrow(H_{\gamma_{H}}/H^{0}_{\gamma_{H}})(\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(\gamma_{H_{1}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(\gamma_{H})\rightarrow 1.

The third arrow from the left is the map induced by the projection
{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}
and is clearly surjective. To construct the second arrow, given
\overline{h}\in(H_{\gamma_{H}}/H^{0}_{\gamma_{H}})(\mathbb{Q})
, choose a lift
h\in H_{\gamma_{H}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and a further lift
h_{1}\in H_{1}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then
x_{1}:=h_{1}\gamma_{H_{1}}h_{1}^{-1}\gamma_{H_{1}}
belongs to
Z_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
, and moreover
x_{1}\in\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(\gamma_{H_{1}})
since
h_{1}\gamma_{H_{1}}h_{1}^{-1}=x_{1}\gamma_{H_{1}}
. The assignment
\overline{h}\mapsto x_{1}
is a well-defined homomorphism. To check this map is injective, suppose
h_{1}\gamma_{H_{1}}h_{1}^{-1}=\gamma_{H_{1}}
. Then
h_{1}
lies in the centralizer of
\gamma_{H_{1}}
in
H_{1}
, which is connected. (To see this, choose a place
v
such that
G_{1,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
is quasi-split, so that
\gamma_{H_{1},v}
has an image
\gamma_{1,v}\in G_{1,\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
. Since the centralizer of
\gamma_{1,v}
is connected, and since
\gamma_{H_{1}}
is
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular, the same is true for
\gamma_{H_{1},v}
by
[
Kot86
, Lem. 3.2]
.) Thus the image of
h_{1}
in
H_{\gamma_{H}}
lies in
H^{0}_{\gamma_{H}}
, implying that
\overline{h}
is trivial.

The composition of the two maps above is clearly trivial.
Finally suppose that
x_{1}\in\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(\gamma_{H_{1}})
maps trivially into
\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(\gamma_{H})
. Then
x_{1}\in Z_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
. To check that
x_{1}
comes from
(H_{\gamma_{H}}/H^{0}_{\gamma_{H}})(\mathbb{Q})
, choose
h_{1}\in H_{1}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
h_{1}\gamma_{H_{1}}h_{1}^{-1}=x_{1}\gamma_{H_{1}}
. Its image
h
in
H(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
clearly centralizes
\gamma_{H}
. Writing
\overline{h}
for the image of
h
in
(H_{\gamma_{H}}/H^{0}_{\gamma_{H}})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
, we see that
\overline{h}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-rational and maps to
x_{1}
by construction.
∎

Remark 8.3.9
.

In our situation
|\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(\gamma_{H})|=1
. Indeed the stabilizer group is a finite subgroup of
K
via
{\mathfrak{X}}{Q}\subset Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}\subset K
, but
K
has no non-trivial torsion elements as
K
is neat.

Theorem 8.3.10
.

Assume that Conjecture
1.8.8
is true (cf. Remark
1.8.10
). With
f^{H_{1}}
constructed as in §
8.3.4
for each
{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
, we have

\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}{\rm tr}\,\bigg({\Phi}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m}\times(f^{p}dg^{p})\mid\coh_{c}^{i}(\Sh_{\overline{E}},\xi)^{K_{p}}\bigg)=\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\iota(G,H)ST^{H_{1}}_{\mathrm{ell},\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

for all sufficiently large
m
.

Proof.

We compute the right hand side as follows.

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\iota(G,H)ST^{H_{1}}_{\mathrm{ell},\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H}}(G)\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\lambda({\mathfrak{e}})^{-1}\sum_{\gamma_{H_{1}}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(H_{1})}|\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(\gamma_{H_{1}})|^{-1}SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{H}}(G)\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\lambda({\mathfrak{e}})^{-1}\sum_{\gamma_{H}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(H)}\overline{\iota}(\gamma_{H})^{-1}SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G)\sum_{({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\in{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)}\lambda({\mathfrak{e}})^{-1}\overline{\iota}(\gamma_{H})^{-1}SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G)\sum_{(\gamma_{0},\kappa)\in\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)}\sum_{({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\in{\mathcal{E}}\Sigma^{\sim}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)\atop{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{X}}:({\mathfrak{e}},\gamma_{H})\mapsto(\gamma_{0},\kappa)}\lambda({\mathfrak{e}})^{-1}\overline{\iota}(\gamma_{H})^{-1}SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathfrak{X}}(G)\sum_{(\gamma_{0},\kappa)\in\Sigma{\mathfrak{K}}_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(G)\atop\gamma_{0}:\,\mathbb{R}\textrm{-elliptic}}\sum_{a\in{\mathfrak{D}}(I_{0},G;\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\atop[b]\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}(\gamma_{0},G;\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\overline{\iota}(\gamma_{0})^{-1}N(\gamma_{0},\kappa,a,[b]).

In the third, fourth, and fifth lines,
\gamma_{H_{1}}\in H_{1}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ss}}
is an arbitrary lift of
\gamma_{H}\in H(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{ss}}
. Each summand is independent of the choice since
f_{1}
transforms under the character
\chi_{H_{1}}^{-1}
, which is trivial on
Z_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
.

We justify these equalities. The first equality uses Lemma
8.2.2
and (
8.3.7.1
).
The next one is based on Lemma
8.3.8
and the bijection
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}}(H_{1})\rightarrow\Sigma_{\mathrm{ell},{\mathfrak{X}}}(H)
induced by the surjection
H_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow H(\mathbb{Q})
. We also used
|\mathrm{Stab}_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(\gamma_{H})|=1
, and the vanishing of the summand if
\gamma_{H_{1}}
is not
(G_{1},H_{1})
-regular by Lemma
8.2.8
.
To continue, the third equality is justified by Lemma
8.2.8
telling us that only
(G,H)
-regular
\gamma_{H}
contributes to the sum.
The fourth equality follows from Lemma
8.3.6
. The last equality is deduced from Lemma
8.2.8
Corollary
8.3.5
, and (
8.2.7.1
), noting that
\gamma_{0,1}
can be taken from
G_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
whenever
SO_{\gamma_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})\neq 0
as shown in the proof of Lemma
8.3.6
.

The proof is complete as the last expression in the displayed formula is exactly the left hand side of the theorem by §
8.1.4
.
∎

Theorem 8.3.11

(cf. Theorem
1
in the Introduction)
.

Assume that
(G,X)
is of abelian type. With notation as in Theorem
8.3.10
, we have

\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}{\rm tr}\,\bigg({\Phi}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m}\times(f^{p}dg^{p})\mid\coh_{c}^{i}(\Sh_{\overline{E}},\xi)^{K_{p}}\bigg)=\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\iota(G,H)ST^{H_{1}}_{\mathrm{ell},\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

for all sufficiently large
m
.

Proof.

This follows from Theorems
6.3.6
and
8.3.10
.
∎

9.
Spectral interpretation

In order to read off spectral information from Theorem
8.3.10
, we need to turn the geometric stable distribution into a spectral expansion.
After discussing the stable trace formula in §
9.1
when the test function is stable cuspidal at
\infty
, we will remark on the prospect for unconditional spectral interpretation in §
9.2
.

9.1.
The stable trace formula

9.1.1
.

Let
G
be a quasi-split connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
with a fixed
z
-extension
G_{1}
. (We do not assume that
G
is part of a Shimura datum in §
9.1
.)
Let
({\mathfrak{X}},\chi)
be a central character datum such that
{\mathfrak{X}}\supset A_{Z,\infty}
. For each elliptic endoscopic datum
{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)
, choose a central extension
H_{1}
and define
{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G_{1})
as well as characters
\chi_{1}
and
\lambda_{H_{1}}
as at the start of §
8.2
. We have a central character datum
({\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}},\chi_{H_{1}})
with
\chi_{H_{1}}:=\chi_{1}\lambda_{H_{1}}^{-1}
as in §
8.3.7
.
Let us recall relationships between certain stable distributions on
G(\mathbb{A})
.

9.1.2
.

Set
{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}:=A_{Z,\infty}
. Define
\chi_{0}:A_{Z,\infty}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
to be the restriction of
{\mathfrak{X}}
to
{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}
.
Then
({\mathfrak{X}}_{0},\chi_{0})
is a central character datum.
Let
S_{\chi_{0}}=S^{G}_{\chi_{0}}
denote Arthur’s stable distribution on
{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}),\chi_{0}^{-1})
inductively defined in
[
Art02
, §9]
. Write
S_{\disc,\chi_{0}}
for the discrete part of
S_{\chi_{0}}
(see (7.11) in
loc. cit.
). Define
S_{\chi}
and
S_{\disc,\chi}
in terms of
S_{\chi_{0}}
and
S_{\disc,\chi_{0}}
exactly as in (
7.1.9.1
). The equality defining
S^{G}_{\chi_{0}}
inductively leads to the analogous equality (compare with
[
Art13
, (3.2.3)]
)

(9.1.2.1)

S^{G}_{\chi}(f)=I^{G}_{\chi}(f)-\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)\atop H\neq G}\iota(G,H)S^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}),

where
I^{G}_{\chi}
means either
I^{G}_{{\rm spec},\chi}
or
I^{G}_{{\rm geom},\chi}
, which are equal, and
f^{H_{1}}\in{\mathcal{H}}(H_{1}(\mathbb{A}),\chi^{-1}_{H_{1}})
denotes a Langlands–Shelstad transfer of
f
(Proposition
7.4.11
). If
f_{\infty}
is stable cuspidal then
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}
is also stable cuspidal (possibly trivial). Indeed, this is reduced via
z
-extensions to the case that
G_{\mathrm{der}}=G_{\mathrm{sc}}
, where this fact follows from work of Shelstad and Clozel–Delorme by the argument as in
[
Kot90
, pp. 182–186]
. (This argument is also at the basis of constructing
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}
in §
8.2.5
.)

Likewise the analogue of (
9.1.2.1
) holds true with
S_{\disc}
and
I_{\disc}
in place of
S
and
I
.
What follows is the stable version of Proposition
7.1.12
.

Lemma 9.1.3
.

Let
f=f^{\infty}f_{\infty}\in{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}),\chi^{-1})
with
f_{\infty}
stable cuspidal. Then

S^{G}_{\chi}(f)=S^{G}_{\disc,\chi}(f).

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
7.1.12
, which implies that

I_{{\rm spec},\chi}(f)=I_{\disc,\chi}(f)

via the inductive definition above.
∎

9.1.4
.

We are going to state a stabilization of the geometric side. Let
({\mathfrak{X}},\chi)
be a central character datum for
G
. Write
A_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
for the maximal
\mathbb{R}
-split torus in
Z_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
. (In general
A_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\neq(A_{G})_{\mathbb{R}}
.)
Consider the following hypotheses:

(H1)

G_{\mathbb{R}}
contains an elliptic maximal torus,

(H2)

{\mathfrak{X}}={\mathfrak{X}}^{\infty}\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}
with
{\mathfrak{X}}^{\infty}\subset Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
A_{G{R},\infty}\subset{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\subset Z(\mathbb{R})
.

The two conditions are satisfied by the groups contributing to the right hand side of Theorem
8.3.10
, so (H1) and (H2) are harmless to assume for our purpose.

We adapt the definition of the stable distribution
ST^{G}_{M}
in
[
Mor10
, §5.4]
to the case of fixed central character.
Let
T_{\infty}
be an elliptic maximal torus of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
and write
T_{\textup{sc},\infty}\subset G_{\textup{sc},\mathbb{R}}
for the preimage. Write
G^{\textup{cpt}}
for an inner form of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
which is anisotropic modulo
A_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
; such a
G^{\textup{cpt}}
exists by (H1).
The Haar measure on
G^{\textup{cpt}}(\mathbb{R})
is always chosen to be compatible with that of
G(\mathbb{R})
.
Define

\displaystyle k(G_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle|\textup{im}(\coh^{1}(\mathbb{R},T_{\textup{sc},\infty})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{R},T_{\infty}))|,

\displaystyle\overline{v}(G_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle e(G^{\textup{cpt}})\mathrm{vol}(G^{\textup{cpt}}(\mathbb{R})/A_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}(\mathbb{R})^{0}).

The two numbers depend only on
G_{\mathbb{R}}
and a Haar measure on
G(\mathbb{R})
.

Let
M_{\mathbb{R}}\subset G_{\mathbb{R}}
be an
\mathbb{R}
-rational Levi subgroup containing an elliptic maximal torus. (So the torus is anisotropic modulo
A_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}
.) Let
\Pi
be a discrete series
L
-packet of
G(\mathbb{R})
with fixed central character
\chi_{\infty}
on
{\mathfrak{X}}
, and write
\Theta_{\Pi}
for the associated stable character (either as a function on regular elements or as a distribution on the space of test functions). Let
D^{G_{\mathbb{R}}}_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}
denote the Weyl discriminant. Write
\Phi^{G_{\mathbb{R}}}_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}(\cdot,\Theta_{\Pi})
for the unique function on the set of elliptic elements in
M(\mathbb{R})
which extends the function
\gamma\mapsto|D^{G_{\mathbb{R}}}_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}(\gamma)|^{1/2}\Theta_{\Pi}(\gamma)
on
M(\mathbb{R})\cap G(\mathbb{R})_{\textup{reg}}
; such an extension exists by
[
Art89
, Lem. 4.2]
.
Let
f_{\infty}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(G(\mathbb{R}),\chi_{\infty}^{-1})
.
For elliptic elements
\gamma
in
M(\mathbb{R})
, define

S\Phi^{G_{\mathbb{R}}}_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}(\gamma,f_{\infty}):=(-1)^{\dim A_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}/A_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}}\cdot\overline{v}(M^{0}_{\mathbb{R},\gamma})^{-1}\frac{k(M{R})}{k(G{R})}\sum_{\Pi}\Phi^{G_{\mathbb{R}}}_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}(\gamma^{-1},\Phi_{\Pi})\Theta_{\Pi}(f_{\infty}),

where
M_{\mathbb{R},\gamma}^{0}
is the connected centralizer of
\gamma
in
M_{\mathbb{R}}
, and the sum runs over discrete series
L
-packets with central character
\chi_{\infty}
. Set
S\Phi^{G_{\mathbb{R}}}_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}(\gamma,f_{\infty})=0
if
\gamma\in M(\mathbb{R})
is not elliptic.

Turning back to the global setting, assuming (H1) and (H2) for
G
, let
M
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-rational Levi subgroup of
G
, which is said to be
G
-cuspidal
if
M_{\mathbb{R}}
contains an elliptic maximal torus and if
\dim A_{M}/A_{G}=\dim A_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}/A_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}
. This relativizes the notion of cuspidal reductive groups over
\mathbb{Q}
. Note that
M=G
is always
G
-cuspidal even if
G
is not cuspidal over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Let
f=f^{\infty}f_{\infty}
with
f^{\infty}\in{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}),(\chi^{\infty})^{-1})
and
f_{\infty}\in{\mathcal{H}}(G(\mathbb{R}),\chi_{\infty}^{-1})
. Denote by
f^{\infty}_{M}\in{\mathcal{H}}(M(\mathbb{A}_{f}),(\chi^{\infty})^{-1})
the constant term of
f^{\infty}
defined by
[
GKM97
, (7.13.2)]
. (The same definition works regardless of fixed central character. As explained therein, it is not
f^{\infty}_{M}
itself but its orbital integral that has well-defined values.) If
M
is
G
-cuspidal, put

ST^{G}_{M,\chi}(f):=\tau_{{\mathfrak{X}}}(M)\sum_{\gamma}\overline{\iota}_{M}(\gamma)^{-1}SO_{\gamma}(f^{\infty}_{M})S\Phi^{G_{\mathbb{R}}}_{M_{\mathbb{R}}}(\gamma,f_{\infty}),

where the sum runs over the set of stable semi-simple conjugacy classes in
M(\mathbb{Q})
. (The summand is zero unless
\gamma
is elliptic in
M(\mathbb{R})
.) Define
ST^{G}_{M,\chi}
to be identically zero if
M
is not
G
-cuspidal. Finally, define

ST^{G}_{\chi}(f):=\sum_{M}|(N_{G}(M)/M)(\mathbb{Q})|^{-1}ST^{G}_{M,\chi}(f),

where
M
runs over the set of
G(\mathbb{Q})
-conjugacy classes of
\mathbb{Q}
-rational Levi subgroups.

Lemma 9.1.5
.

Let
G
be a quasi-split reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
with central character datum
({\mathfrak{X}},\chi)
.
Assume (H1) and (H2) above, and let
f=f^{\infty}f_{\infty}
as above with
f_{\infty}
stable cuspidal.
Then

S^{G}_{\chi}(f)=ST^{G}_{\chi}(f).

Proof.

As in (
9.1.2.1
), we have

I^{G}_{\chi}(f)=\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\iota(G,H)S^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}).

The assertion is trivial if
G
is a torus.
We induct on the semi-simple rank. Then

S^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})=ST^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

for all
{\mathfrak{e}}

such that
H\ncong G
.
Indeed, if
H_{1,\mathbb{R}}
contains no elliptic maximal torus or if
A_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}\subsetneq A_{H_{\mathbb{R}}}
, then the transfer
f^{H_{1}}_{\infty}
vanishes so the equality holds trivially. Otherwise
H_{1,\mathbb{R}}
and
{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}
satisfy the analogue of (H1) and (H2), so the above equality is true by the induction hypothesis.

To conclude, it is enough to show that

I^{G}_{\chi}(f)=\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}=(H,{\mathcal{H}},s,\eta)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\iota(G,H)ST^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})

when
f_{\infty}
is a stable cuspidal function. This was proven by Peng
[
Pen19
, Thm. 9.2]
without fixed central character. The desired equality follows from it by averaging with respect to
({\mathfrak{X}},\chi)
.
(When
G
is cuspidal over
\mathbb{Q}
with simply connected derived subgroup, Peng’s result was obtained in an unpublished manuscript by Kottwitz
[
Kot
, Thm. 5.1]
, cf.
[
Mor10
, Thm. 5.4.1]
.)
∎

9.2.
Speculations

9.2.1
.

We return to the setting of Theorem
8.3.10
for the compactly supported cohomology of Shimura varieties, where (H1) and (H2) hold true for
G
and
{\mathfrak{X}}
as well as for
H_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{X}}_{H_{1}}
contributing non-trivially to the right hand side.

The analogue of Theorem
8.3.10
is expected to be true for the intersection cohomology of the Baily–Borel compactification. Writing
T^{\textup{IH}}(\Phi^{m}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},f^{p}dg^{p})
for the intersection cohomology analogue of
T(\Phi^{m}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},f^{p}dg^{p})
, the conjectural stabilization should have the form (cf.
[
Kot90
, (10.1)]
)

(9.2.1.1)

T^{\textup{IH}}(\Phi^{m}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},f^{p}dg^{p})=\sum_{{\mathfrak{e}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)}\iota(G,H)ST^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}).

The point is that the non-elliptic terms in
ST^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}
(coming from proper Levi subgroups) should be accounted for exactly by the boundary strata of the Baily–Borel compactification. For non-proper Shimura varieties, (
9.2.1.1
) is known for certain special orthogonal group and unitary similitude groups in addition to general symplectic groups in
[
LR92
,
Mor08
,
Mor10
,
Mor11
,
Zhu18
]
.
On the other hand, Lemmas
9.1.3
and
9.1.5
imply that

(9.2.1.2)

ST^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})=S^{H_{1}}_{\disc,\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}).

Combined with (
9.2.1.1
), this yields a trace formula for the intersection cohomology in terms of the stable distributions
S^{H_{1}}_{\disc,\chi_{H_{1}}}
, which are of a spectral nature. Then one can follow Kottwitz
[
Kot90
, §§9–10]
to unravel
S^{H_{1}}_{\disc,\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})
to obtain a conjectural description of the intersection cohomology (in each degree, by purity) as a
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times{\rm Gal}(\overline{E}/E)
-module, in terms of automorphic representations of
G(\mathbb{A})
and their endoscopic classification; see p. 201 therein.
49
49

49

We do not reproduce Kottwitz’s argument or his conjectural description here. We content ourselves with remarking that the destabilization process in
[
Kot90
]
may also be carried out by applying the conjectural stable multiplicity formula, cf.
[
Art13
, Thm. 4.1.2, (4.8.5)]
. Still the key computation at
p
and
\infty
of
[
Kot90
, §9]
is irreplaceable as it reflects the features of test functions at
p
and
\infty
specific to the context of Shimura varieties.

The endoscopic classification for classical groups is worked out in
[
Art13
,
Mok15
,
KMSW14
,
Taï19
]
, in the quasi-split case and some more. However, little is known for groups of higher rank beyond classical groups, except for partial results on general symplectic and orthogonal groups in
[
Xu18
,
Xu21
]
.

9.2.2
.

We return to compactly supported cohomology.
In the special case that
G/Z
is anisotropic over
\mathbb{Q}
, the Shimura variety
\Sh_{K}
is proper over
E
for each
K
. Thus the intersection cohomology coincides with the compactly supported cohomology.
In particular
T^{\textup{IH}}(\Phi^{m}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},f^{p}dg^{p})=T(\Phi^{m}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},f^{p}dg^{p})
, and the above consideration suggests that

(9.2.2.1)

ST^{H_{1}}_{\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}S^{H_{1}}_{\mathrm{ell},\chi_{H_{1}}}(f^{H_{1}}).

We stress that this equality is not intrinsic to
H_{1}
. Indeed, a quasi-split inner form
G^{*}
of
G
over
\mathbb{Q}
shares the same elliptic endoscopic data as
G
. When
G^{*}
can be promoted to a Shimura datum, (
9.2.2.1
) would be false for
f^{H_{1}}
constructed in the context of the Shimura variety for
G^{*}
(since the latter does have a non-empty boundary).
Once (
9.2.2.1
) is verified, we obtain (
9.2.1.1
), and the preceding paragraph explains how to extract the spectral information for the compactly supported cohomology in this case.

If
G/Z
is isotropic over
\mathbb{Q}
, then the description of the
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times{\rm Gal}(\overline{E}/E)
-module structure on the compactly supported cohomology is expected to be very complicated. Indeed, this is confirmed by Franke’s formula
[
Fra98
]
even if the Galois action is forgotten. See also
[
Lau97
]
for the case of
\textup{GSp}_{4}
. Moreover, there may be cancellations between different degrees since the compactly supported cohomology need not be pure. We think that it is better to study the intersection cohomology by proving (
9.2.1.1
) in this case.

9.2.3
.

We end by summarizing the prospect of unconditional results on the cohomology of Shimura varieties associated with
(G,X)
. In the case of abelian type, our main result is that the identity in Theorem
8.3.10
holds unconditionally whenever
K_{p}
is a hyperspecial subgroup of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
m
is sufficiently large. When
G/Z
is anisotropic over
\mathbb{Q}
, in order to make the conjectural description in the style of
[
Kot90
, p. 201]
unconditional, the two main missing ingredients are the endoscopic classification of automorphic representations (for
G
and the groups
H_{1}
’s contributing to the stabilization) and the equality (
9.2.2.1
).
When
G/Z
is isotropic, instead of (
9.2.2.1
), one should attempt to prove (
9.2.1.1
) by extending the methods of
[
Mor10
]
and
[
Zhu18
]
. On top of that, the same endoscopic classification is needed to arrive at the final description of cohomology.
